# Decisions so far

Working notes for the `pali` iteration. Not the contract: that evolves during
development and gets written down as it stabilises. Platform detail is in
`notes/linux.md` (marko, Hyprland, bundle) and `notes/matching.md` (nucleo vs
fzf-for-js benchmarks); this file links to them instead of repeating.

## State at 2026-09-17 morning

- Branch: `main` is the rewrite (v1 is `v1`); 64 commits between 716a53d (Tuesday evening) and 8ec4e65 (Wednesday 09:00), 1,002 files, +194k lines. The daily app on hornet is the head, rebuilt from the clean worktree `~/proj/pal-build` (`CARGO_TARGET_DIR=~/proj/pal-build/target make app`); marko runs the head too.
- Shell: Tauri v2, panel pre-painted, `pal://` links = CLI twins with extension routes and Copy deep link; the bar: menu bar and sketchybar renderers, appearance defaults with per-item overrides, rich popovers as compact views, live pushes (`view.update`) and pulls (`refresh`/`on`); instances (`gmail@work`) with one Bun worker per instance; lazy palettes; concealed copy; selection text; OCR; Large Type; snippet expansion; dialog jump; `pal pick`; multi-select; compact mode; a theme file; pal's own commands; the log file.
- Core: config with `[instances]` inheritance, ranking tiers and caps with "more in" rows, inline results and fallbacks at the root, search history, the Now/Frequent/Clipboard sections, every tool call under a deadline, the login shell's PATH, MediaRemote stream, CoreWLAN with Location, menu bar AX, window verbs.
- Host and SDK: `@zcag/pal` with shared helpers (rows, text, exec, token, clock, png, terminal, files, md, colors, tabs), manifest as the source of truth (`checkPalettes`, `checkLinks`, `checkIcon`), workers, live views.
- Extensions (55): the 34 of Tuesday plus 2048, wordle, colors (rebuilt), calendar Today with Google, slack, tela, hue, spotify, gmail (multi), obsidian, images, translate, shell, downloads, gifs, maps, speedtest, youtube, whatsapp, menu-bar, generate, shortcuts, store, screenshots; every one with a brand tile, keys table, store fields, screenshots in both themes, tests.
- Site: https://pal.cagdas.io with a real landing page, the store (tiles, links, bar items, multi), the docs (11 pages incl. troubleshooting).
- Tests: cargo 414, vitest 239, host 1199; clippy clean; CI green on both runners once the last Linux-only expectations were fixed.
- Numbers (hornet, 55 extensions, warm): cold start to first paint 380-390 ms, host ready ~400 ms, hotkey to paint 1.5-4 ms, app idle 171 MB, pal-bun 224 MB, per keystroke ~1.1 ms, 4 processes at idle.
- Open: the release (his: version, secrets, tag), pit 13 (skipped), the "Left" lists in the polish rounds, a Linux popover story. Done 2026-09-17: the `ago`/`size` format unification (below).
- Morning 09:15-09:55 (with Cagdas): speedtest names the tool's last line on a failure (fast-cli's Chrome was incomplete on hornet, reinstalled); Spotify's network palettes lazy; bar popovers open on click by default on both targets (hover is opt-in); Tenor retired (API shut down 2026-06-30), gifs on Giphy with his key in the keychain; Slack presence dots on DM rows; one `ago`/`bytes` in the SDK; `notes/sketchybar-parity.md` maps his 22 sketchybar items onto pal's bar (have 1, partial 6, missing 11, not-for-pal 4; 13 gaps in the bar model, work hours and click-acts first). Daily app at e195a4a. Next: the parity plan's B1-B4 in parallel, then E-rows.
- Bar handoff (2026-09-17, from `cde7c26`): **done** — generic `click: "open"` bar-item behavior; Settings-only named mock states from extension manifests (Spotify, Calendar, Timer, Audio, Network, GitHub, Weather, Power and Bluetooth); Spotify timestamped lyric ticker and next/previous scroll; Calendar Upcoming urgency presentation, configuration, direct conference/Calendar opening, and its per-state sizing/placement; Audio Volume and Microphone items (direct actions, scroll, device popover, mocks); Network status (SSID labels, direct settings action, palette popover, mocks); GitHub Pull requests and Issues as independent cache-backed items (semantic state segments, palettes on click, hidden-zero and mocks); standalone Weather (Open-Meteo location/current forecast, configurable comfort logic, condition popover and hidden-notable behavior); Power/Battery (OS gauge plus fresh watcher diagnostics, smart hide thresholds, popover/palette); Bluetooth Battery (connected low-device interruption over the existing source); runtime `background`, separate icon/label sizes, icon width and sketchybar placement; plus sketchybar hover tint, animated colour changes and word-safe clipping. **Next:** nothing on Weather, Power, Bluetooth, Network or Audio — the bar items that exist are at parity. **Not current scope:** automatic theme/work-hours hiding, brackets/separators, native power/audio event sources, tan/stan, cldd, or `clock`/`media_use`. GitHub PR and issue states are separate semantic items, never a grouped bracket.
- Bar live polish (2026-09-17, after `167c003`): the pass against the real bar, which found three things no Settings preview could (a preview renders the mock, not the extension). **Weather was dead on his bar** — Open-Meteo's geocoder takes one bare place name, so the configured (and manifest-suggested) `Istanbul, Turkey` matched nothing and the item sat red; the first segment is now the query and the rest rank rather than filter the answers, because the index stores endonyms a written exonym never equals. **Power drew its level twice**, since `progress` makes the sketchybar renderer prefix an 8-cell rule to the icon; the rule is gone and his 6-step ramp glyph carries the level, with severity left to the colour. **Power never said how long**: the title is now `16% · 1:04 · 9.5W`, ETA appearing five points above the amber threshold so the number that explains the colour arrives first, never four fields wide, and a recalculating pmset `0:00` defers to the watcher. **Bluetooth** moved to his 25%/20%. Two of his workarounds were deliberately not copied: the `󰋋 󰁺` pair would be menu-bar tofu (`icon_kind` calls any multi-char icon `Text`) and is unnecessary when the label leads with the device name, and his `''|0` guard answers a shell limitation that `Option<u8>` does not have.
- Network bar parity (2026-09-17): `icon_only` drops the name, which only works because the glyph was made to carry the answer — a four-step signal ramp on Wi-Fi, its own mark for a hotspot, an open network, wired, or no route. Adding the toggle without the variants would have been a trap: one `󰖩` on every network says nothing. `networks` maps `SSID = kind` **or** `gateway = kind` to `hide` / `hotspot` / `public`; both keys are needed, since macOS redacts the name from a process without Location Services and a cable into the same router has no name at all. `hide` removes the item rather than dimming it, so its presence is the message. An open network is marked on its own (from `security`, not a name list) and a name containing iphone/android/hotspot/tether is taken as a hotspot; an explicit entry covers a phone named anything else — `marvin`, for one. Signal comes from the core's in-process CoreWLAN read on macOS (RSSI needs no Location grant, unlike the name) and `iw dev <dev> link` on Linux, both on `(dBm + 100) * 2`. Two bugs fell out: the tooltip only ever carried the SSID when a *label* was configured, which is fatal once the name lives nowhere else, and the core's `signal: null` passed an `!== undefined` guard to draw a one-bar glyph and a `Signal null%` tooltip.
- Network popover + volume flash (2026-09-17): the last two named bar-polish items. **Network's popover is now its own compact kv view** (`extensions/network/view.ts`) rather than the full Addresses palette: the item answers "what am I connected to", so the popover is the named head row plus interface, ip, gateway, signal and dns — five lines, not a machine-wide address book; `cmd+a` pushes to that palette when the full list *was* what you wanted (`push` is a legal bar-action effect, `bar.ts:50` → `bar/mod.rs:895`). **Audio's level is feedback, not furniture**: a percentage that only changes when you change it is a number you stop reading, so it appears for three seconds after a scroll or a mute and then collapses to the glyph alone, with a `level` setting for `flash`/`always`/`never`. The collapse has to be a `setTimeout` + `bar.refresh` push because `refresh.every` is floored at `MIN_EVERY = 10.0` s (`bar/mod.rs:53`) — the same pattern E13 will need; a `keep: true` effect already re-renders the item, so the flash shows instantly on the action itself. The glyph gained a three-step loudness ramp with 0 reading as muted, which exposed a latent bug: pal's old "high" glyph `f075d` is volume-*plus*, not loudness.

## Decided

### Product

- Clean rewrite on the orphan branch `pali`; **`main` since 2026-09-16 20:10** (GitHub rename: v1's `main` became `v1`, `pali` was pushed as `main` and made the default; the old `pali` ref stays until the marko clone is switched). v1 is branch `v1`, checked out at `~/proj/pal-v1`; its tags `v0.1.2`..`v0.2.1` remain.
- Name is `pal`; `pali` is the iteration. A standalone app people download, macOS and Linux. Raycast and Spotlight are the behaviour references; the visual identity is ours.
- Bar: high polish everywhere, no corners cut, minute UX details thought through.
- Root search: one box across everything; palettes are sections and drill-downs, not a root that lists palettes.
- Extensibility, one way: every palette including every default is an extension against the same API, no native tier. In-app code is shell (root search, action panel, settings, store, first run) plus OS capabilities as APIs (clipboard, windows, apps, files, hotkeys).
- Order of work: UI first (brief, component set, keyboard grammar), then protocol and host. Done in that order (`git log`).
- The pit board (pit.cagdas.io) holds Raycast research; its `in` lane is "relevant", never a commitment. Decisions are made here.

### Shell

- Tauri v2, the shape of Raycast 2.0. Go/no-go on hornet, dev build: hotkey to paint ~30 ms steady (706 ms first show, fixed by pre-painting), keystroke to list 14-42 ms over 14.7k rows, 14.7k rows streamed from a child in ~70 ms.
- Hotkey on hornet: Ctrl+Space (Alt+Space is taken); from `general.hotkey`, re-registered on file change (`hotkey.rs`). `general.position`: `top` (20%), `centre`, `last`.
- Settings window: second Tauri window on the same bundle (`index.html?settings`), hidden until asked, closes to hidden; opened by `pal-app settings`, the `pal:settings` root action, `cmd+,`. Escape / cmd+w hide it. Since 2026-09-16 it is built in `settings.rs` (`create`), not tauri.conf.json, so the macOS options stay behind `cfg`: a preferences-window shape after Raycast's, 960 by 640 since the evening of 2026-09-16 (min 800 by 560; 720 by 520 before that, and a saved size under the new minimum gets the new default once, read from the plugin's file since tao applies the restore a run-loop turn later: `tauri-plugin-window-state` remembers size and position for this window only, saved on every hide since a hidden window is where pal is killed), overlay title bar with the title hidden and the traffic lights moved to (13, 20) so they sit mid-band in the page's 52px toolbar, `transparent` plus `window-vibrancy` Sidebar behind `--pal-bg-glass` (Linux: decorated, opaque `--pal-bg`). The page: icon tabs centred on the toolbar (General, Palettes, Extensions, About), one search field on its right whose hits replace the page, forms as elevated cards at a 640px measure (560 before the window grew) with a 160px right-aligned label column; two-pane pages (Palettes, Extensions) put a 220px list left, and the selected palette's pane takes 40% of the window (a fixed 268px before). Built on the first open since that evening, not at startup (below, the performance findings). `theme.ts` also sets the window's own appearance (`setTheme`), else a pinned light theme sits on dark vibrancy and dark scrollbars. Links open through `settings_open_link` (the webview has no `target=_blank` handler; a plain `<a>` did nothing). Verified on hornet with the scratch bundle: note yabai tiles it like any other window (the window fills the space unless a rule floats it, e.g. `yabai -m rule --add app="^pal$" title="^pal Settings$" manage=off`), which is not something pal can fix from inside.
- Settings window IA (2026-09-16, revamp): six pages around what people come to do. **Overview** (first): what needs attention as rows with the fix inline (hotkey not registered with Spotlight's switch or the other app named; each missing permission with Grant; an extension with nothing to work with, `needsSetup` = `required` in the manifest else an empty secret / url whose description offers no fallback; load errors; manifest warnings; config file problems; stale bar items; app and extension updates), "Everything is set" with the version when empty, then four facts. **General** (hotkey, permissions summary listing all four with Grant, appearance, startup with the crash-relaunch note, config file, maintenance incl. Refresh listings). **Palettes**: one grouped table (extension header with icon and tagline, rows "Extension › Palette", on / alias / hotkey / icon inline, a live filter) with the selected palette's pane on the right (crumb, description, id, kind, `tier` select "At the root", keys, `item_hotkeys` read-only, declared settings). **Extensions**: the store's hero (tile, tagline, author, version, source, Store page link), a screenshots strip (from the checkout through `icon://localhost/shot` for a store install, from pal.cagdas.io for a bundled one: shipping the 34 sets would add 8.6 MB), callouts for the load error, manifest warnings and what needs setup, the settings form with the missing ones marked, the palettes as chips into Palettes. **Bar** (new): target with detection and a one-line explanation per choice, peek timings, per-target hover, sketchybar position, and every registered item as a row (on, target, position, hotkey, hover, order) with its live state under the name (badge, hidden, poll, last render, stale). **About** plus Copy Diagnostics. Search indexes every row with an anchor; a hit opens the page and lights the row (`[data-flash]`, System Settings' highlight); `/` focuses it, cmd+1..6 switch pages. `settings_get` grew `bar` (registry snapshot with live state) and `warnings` per extension; `permissions::Status` grew Calendars (EventKit), Full Disk Access (probed by opening the Messages db) and Input Monitoring (IOHIDCheckAccess, the FFI moved here from bar/popover.rs), `watch` polls all of them. `pal settings <page>` and `pal://settings/<page>` take the two new pages. Group titles went from tracked caps to sentence case.
- HUD (`hud.rs`, `panel/{macos,linux}.rs`, `app/src/HudPage.tsx`): a third window `hud` (`index.html?hud`, 480 by 72, transparent, no decorations, `focusable: false`) for the one-line confirmation after the panel hides. macOS: an NSPanel like the main one but never key (`can_become_key_window: false`), status level, all Spaces, mouse ignored, no window shadow (the capsule draws `--pal-shadow-hud`); kept alive hidden the same way (alpha 0, never ordered out, occlusion detection off), so the same screen-lock caveats apply. Linux: a plain always-on-top toplevel, pre-mapped at startup; same class as the panel, so the compositor rule for the HUD keys on its title `pal HUD` (`notes/linux.md`). Placed bottom-centre of the work area of the monitor under the cursor (else the panel's), 8 px up; the capsule sits 24 px inside the window. `hud::show(app, text)` emits `pal://hud` to that window, shows it, and hides it after 120 + 900 + 240 (+ 60) ms; the page runs the brief's motion (in 120, hold 900, out 240 via `--pal-dur-hud-out`) and a new show restarts both clocks. Uses: `copy` shows "Copied" when the envelope hides (not with `paste`, `keep`, `toast`, `push`, `show`, or an own `hud`), `paste` nothing (the target app is the feedback), `toast` unchanged, and an extension's `{ hud: "text" }`. The page is the `Hud` component and nothing else: no list, no host calls. Verified on hornet with a scratch release instance (own identifier, own config, `XDG_DATA_HOME` aside): capsule painted 0.35 s after the trigger, gone by 1.75 s, and two triggers 0.6 s apart kept it up at 1.3 s.
- Menu bar icon (`tray.rs`): there is no Dock icon, so this is the way to Settings and Quit. Menu: Open pal (hotkey as accelerator hint), Settings… (⌘,), Restart extension host, Check for updates… (disabled until the updater flow is in; `updater::check` exists), Quit pal. Left click opens the menu (Raycast does). Image `app/design/tray.svg` (the app icon as one colour: the slab's outline with the caret and the empty query inside): black template `icons/tray/36x36` + `18x18` on macOS, white `22x22` for Linux panels. `general.menu_bar_icon = false` removes it live; `true` default, unset by the view.
- `general.launch_at_login` and crash relaunch share one agent (`autostart.rs`, 2026-09-16). macOS: pal writes the LaunchAgent itself (label = the bundle identifier; `RunAtLoad`, `KeepAlive.SuccessfulExit = false`, `ProcessType Interactive`, `ThrottleInterval 5`, `EnvironmentVariables` for `PAL_CONFIG`/`XDG_*`), to `~/Library/LaunchAgents/` when the key is on and to `<data dir>/launchd/` when off, and a hand-started release build hands itself over to launchd at startup (helper waits for its exit, `bootout` + `bootstrap`, marker gates it to once a minute). `SuccessfulExit` implies `RunAtLoad`, so a plist in LaunchAgents always starts at login: hence the two locations rather than one plist with the key toggled. tauri-plugin-autostart stays for the Linux XDG entry only; its macOS half named the plist `io.cagdas.pal` regardless of the identifier, so a scratch build with the key off removed the daily plist (seen on hornet 2026-09-16). Linux: `pal.service` user unit (`Restart=on-failure`) or a `systemd-run` transient unit, unverified (no Linux box in that session). Verified on hornet with a scratch identifier: SIGTRAP on the supervised process relaunched it in 0.3 s (a `kill -SEGV` is swallowed by WebKit's signal handler, so it is not a crash test), `pal quit` stayed quit for 10 s, both with the key on and off. `crash.rs`: a panic hook writes `<profile>/last-panic.txt`; the newest own `.ips` (header `app_name`, `bundleID` for a bundled build) newer than the previous start is announced once with the HUD ("pal restarted after a crash", `crash.json` remembers) and About lists the last crash and panic with Open / Reveal / Copy Path.
- Quit (tray, `pal quit`): host gets EOF and 2 s (`Host::stop`, no respawn), then `app.exit(0)` runs the `RunEvent::Exit` flush of frecency and the cache saver (`quit  flushed` in the log). `pal quit` with no instance prints `not running`, starts nothing.
- Data keyed by config file: `<data dir>/pal/<profile>/{index, frecency.json}`, `<profile>` = `default` for `~/.config/pal/config.toml` else first 8 hex of sha256 of the canonical path (`ConfigFile::profile`; logged at startup as `profile`). `clipboard.db` stays one level up: history, not a view of one config. Nothing migrated.
- Index persisted (`cache.rs`): every default listing to `<data dir>/pal/index/<extension>/<palette>.json` (wire items, `listed_at`, meta, extension title; atomic, debounced 500 ms per source; >50k items not written). `index::restore_cache` restores all before the host spawns (items `stale`, registry entry, palette row, hotkeys): first query 32 items at ~400 ms cold vs the full ~15.5k at ~400 ms warm. `host/ready` prunes extensions gone from disk (`known`; failed-to-load keeps its cache). Restored order `pal/palettes`, `apps`, then by name, so a cold frecency profile still leads with palettes and apps.
- `ttl` is palette meta (seconds; palette object, manifest `palettes.<key>.ttl`, `scripts` maps v1 `ttl` else its own setting). On `extension/loaded`: no `ttl` = list now; younger than `ttl` = keep restored rows; else one sequential pass 1 s after `host/ready` (`refresh_expired`). `live` still re-lists on show (within the budget under Core). `SourceInfo.stale` = listing pending: footer "updating…" + search sweep. `index_refresh(source?)` / `pal:refresh` (cmd+r) force past `ttl`, the `list` carries `refresh: true` so an extension's own cache steps aside.
- d811024: hotkey apply deadlock, request timeout covering the write, stray rows after an extension leaves, zombie open children, bounded icon threads, poison-tolerant locks, `events`/`registry` modules.

### Core

- Matching in Rust: corpus in the core, `nucleo-matcher` single-thread, per-field scoring (name 100%, keyword 80%, subtitle 50%), top 200 to the webview, frecency hook before the top-N select. Numbers and why: `notes/matching.md`. Wired in 92dc234.
- Windows and system commands are core capabilities (`pal_core::windows`, `pal_core::system`; `core/windows.{list,close,minimize}`, `core/system.{commands,run}`), the `windows`/`system` extensions thin over them. Focus is a pick effect (`{ focus: id }`, effects.rs), like `paste`: the panel hides first so orderOut hands key focus back. `system.run` hides the panel itself.
- Accessibility in `pal_core::ax` (`trusted`/`request`, `AXUIElement` wrapper); the `accessibility_blocked` toast in effects.rs is paste's, the system prompt (`ax::request`) is shown once per run across paste and focus.
- Focus without Accessibility activates rather than refuses: `pal_core::windows::activate(id)` brings the window's app forward (app-level, no AX needed; `focus` reuses it after its AX raise) and returns the app's name; effects.rs hides the panel, activates, and once per run shows the HUD "Switched to <app>; per-window switching needs Accessibility" plus the system prompt. A toast cannot carry that message: the activation makes the panel resign key, which hides it (`panel/macos.rs`). With the permission nothing changed. The decision path is `focus_feedback` (unit-tested); Linux is unaffected (`ax::trusted()` is always true there).
- macOS window list: CoreGraphics (ids, order, bounds, on-screen) matched to AX windows by frame and title; titles from AX (CG needs Screen Recording for other apps' titles); an app answering AX with no windows falls back to CG rows; AX asked per app in parallel (10-30 ms when it naps).
- Linux backends detected, not configured: Hyprland (`hyprctl` + instance signature, newest `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/hypr/*` when the env var is missing), Sway (`SWAYSOCK`), X11 (`DISPLAY` + `wmctrl`). Hyprland has no minimise: parked on `special:minimized`, `focus` brings it back.
- Clipboard retention has one meaning (fcb967a): `max_age_days = 0` = no age limit; `exclude_apps` default in the manifest, so an explicit `[]` no longer drops the Keychain/Passwords protection.
- Exact name over history (`index::EXACT_BONUS = 1000`): an item whose trimmed name, or a keyword (alias), equals the trimmed query case-insensitively (no accent folding) gets +1000 after boost, before the top-N select; among exact hits score (with boost) then name length still order. Sized against the frecency maximum of 200 (`MAX_SCORE * BOOST_SCALE`) plus the fuzzy-vs-keyword-exact spread (nucleo ~16 per char, a keyword hit is 80% of a name hit: 33 apart at `chrome`); the welcome source's 1e9 is on the empty query only, where nothing is exact. Fixture corpus, frecency max on the other item: `ha` was harp 262 / ha 62, now ha 1062 / harp 262; `slack` with `snowflake_check` +200: Slack 1140 first. Cost: `ha` median 520 us, `chrome` 302 us (was 432 / 310). Trade-off, measured: `chrome` puts the nerd icon `chrome` (1166) above a hot Google Chrome (366) that used to lead at +30; `google chrome` or a keyword `chrome` on the app is the way back. No prefix bonus: nucleo scores prefix, word-start and exact alike (`ha`, `hat`, `Claude Code URL Handler` all 62), so a bonus that mattered against a boost would have to be at least the boost, which is the case frecency is designed to win (`term` with `terminal_tmux` +200 stays boosted-first); the length tie-break orders equals.
- `scripts` `ttl` setting defaults to 3600: no v1 plugin on hornet declares one, so every script ran on every start. A table's own `ttl` wins; a `live` table without one is exempt (a `ttl` now skips the show relist, and `otp` reads the Messages db for the current code); one resolved value feeds both the core's across-restarts keep and the extension's in-process cache. Bundled TypeScript extensions keep "no `ttl` = list on load": they are cheap (`docs/extensions.md`).
- Window layouts (`pal_core::windows::layout`, `windows::apply`; `core/windows.{frame,set_frame,displays,focused,layout}`; the `layout` pick effect in effects.rs; `extensions/window-management`): Raycast's set as one static row per layout. The arithmetic is pure and unit-tested on a 1440x900 visible frame and a two-display case; the OS part is `frame`/`set_frame` (macOS `AXPosition`/`AXSize`, the displays from `NSScreen` `frame`/`visibleFrame` flipped into the CG space, called off the main thread the way tao does; Hyprland `movewindowpixel exact`/`resizewindowpixel exact` after `setfloating` on a tiled window, `monitors -j` minus `reserved`; Sway and X11 written to the tools' documented shapes, fixture-tested only) and `focused` (frontmost app's `AXFocusedWindow`, `hyprctl activewindow`). The effect hides the panel first so the focused window is the one behind it, then the HUD names the layout or the refusal. `restore` is per window, in memory, and goes back to the frame before a *run* of pal layouts (a hand move in between starts a new run; `original_of`, unit-tested). Verified with the example binary on hornet (a hidden TextEdit document: seven layouts, restore, gap 10, frames read back exactly) and on marko (a spawned kitty over Hyprland, the focused path included). `palettes.<id>.item_hotkeys = { left_half = "ctrl+alt+left" }` registers per-item global hotkeys that run `index::run_pick` with the panel down (hotkey.rs `Target::Item`; root beats palette beats item in a clash); config-only, not in the Settings table.
- Live relist budget (`index::relist_due`, `LIVE_RELIST_GAP = 2 s`): on show, a live palette whose last show relist was issued under 2 s ago is skipped (double toggle, or a relist still in flight under the 2 s `LIVE_RELIST_TIMEOUT`), and one with a `ttl` relists only once its rows are older than that; no `ttl` = every show, as before. Parallel relist and the 2 s timeout unchanged; skipped palettes are logged (`index\tshow relist\tskipped ...`).

### Extensions and protocol

- Extensions are TypeScript in one Bun host, UI as a render tree from a fixed component set, never HTML. Scripts/data files stay the zero-code tier under the built-in `scripts` extension (runs v1 script plugins and data palettes from a v1 config). Raycast API compatibility: nice to have; keep the component set close enough that a shim stays plausible.
- Manifest is `pal.json` next to `index.ts`, readable without running code (Settings lists an extension whose code fails; host sends the manifest with `extension/error`). Shape (`host/src/protocol.ts` `Manifest`): `name title description version icon author repo`, `settings: SettingSpec[]` (`[extensions.<name>]`), `palettes.<key>.{settings,ttl,title}` (`[palettes.<id>].settings`; title only a fallback while it fails). Settings come from the manifest only (9332904).
- Palette id in the file: the extension's name when the palette is named like it (`emoji`, `apps`), else `<extension>-<palette>` (`clipboard-history`); bare keys, `index::palette_id` resolves.
- Settings reach extensions resolved: core overlays file over manifest defaults (`Config::extension_settings`/`palette_settings`, one level deep, a set key replaces the default whole), pushes `settings/changed` before the first `list` and on every change; `settings.get()` reads it. Caller identity: host async context in `list`/`pick`, file on the stack at import, explicit argument elsewhere (Bun drops context through dynamic import). Changed values relist. `secret` settings arrive resolved (fcb967a).
- `list`/`pick`/`detail` take a trailing `ctx: { filter?, args?, refresh? }`; on the wire `filter`/`args` ride flat.
- Lazy detail: palette method `detail(id, ctx)`, meta `detail: "lazy"`; the open-with-pane flag is `showDetail`. UI asks after 100 ms cursor rest on an item without inline markdown (`Item.lazyDetail`), merges the reply over the inline one, skeleton only past 150 ms more, drops replies for a left item. Host caches per palette+id until relist/reload, UI per level until the index changes. `scripts` runs v1 `preview` there, `preview_max` (default 4): `prs` lists in ~40 ms instead of ~5.9 s, one `gh pr view` per rest (~1 s, cached).
- The root has no scope dropdown (2026-09-16): a palette is picked from the list, the dropdown at the right of the search box was clutter. The dropdown is only a palette's own filters.
- Filters through the core: `PaletteMeta.filters` via `sources()`; Search dropdown, Tab cycles, first is default (core sends `filter: filters[0].id` explicitly). Indexed palette listed per filter (`index::filter`), bucket swapped, each filter's list kept until relist; root sees the filtered bucket while inside, reset to the first on leaving, the next query restores it; the page asks per query and the core no-ops when the bucket already holds that filter. Input palettes get it per keystroke. Verified with `scripts/otp` (4), `ha-states` (10).
- Drill-in: `Effect.push { extension, palette, args? }` pushes a scoped level; with `args` it lists from the extension per keystroke (never the index) and picks are not remembered (frecency is the parent's). `Effect.show: Detail & { title? }` pushes a detail-only level (full width, crumb, Back on Enter/Escape, arrows/PageUp/Down scroll, input read-only). `scripts` maps v1 `{palette, env}` to `push` (env as args, re-exported to the script; rows kept per args so a root pick still works after a drill-in listing) and v1 `show` to `show`.
- `keep` relists an indexed palette (`index::pick`): "stay open and list again" holds for every palette; a closed window is gone next query.
- Live at the root: a `live` non-`input` palette (windows, otp) is indexed and relisted on every `pal://shown` (`index::on_shown`), all at once, 2 s each, one `pal://index`, spawned after the event: paint stayed 1-4 ms; relist ~15-20 ms windows, ~300 ms otp (reads the Messages db); a timeout keeps old rows. `windows` is plain `live` so titles are root results; `system` stays `input: true` (keep-awake row flips), so its rows are not at the root.
- Picks from live and input palettes are not remembered (edc1670).
- `ssh` and `processes` (v1's builtins `ssh`/`psg`) and `files` (mdfind / fd / locate / find) are real extensions (708abf7, c8f478f); the v1 rows under `scripts` for them are gone.
- Palette rows carry the extension title as subtitle (Raycast does), omitted when equal to the palette title (Windows / Windows). Root sections ordered by best hit.
- View level (third protocol pass, driven by `extensions/blackjack`): `Palette.view(ctx)` in place of `list` makes a view palette (`PaletteMeta.view = "view"`, reported `input: true` so the core indexes nothing and the root keeps its row), and `Effect.view` carries a `View { tree, actions, title?, id?, keys?: "actions" }`. The tree is a fixed vocabulary the app draws with the tokens (`ViewNode`: `stack`, `text`, `image` (`icon://` or `data:image/` only), `badge`, `divider`, `spacer`, `progress`, `keycap`), never HTML; unknown node types are skipped by the renderer, not thrown on. Keyed nodes get entrances/exits through the presence wrapper (`Transition { enter: fade|slide-up|flip, exit: fade|none, delay: 0..8 steps of --pal-dur-fast }`); a card is not a primitive, blackjack draws its cards as SVG data urls (`cards.ts`). The Launcher pushes a `{ kind: "view" }` level: no search input (the view's title in its place, `Search title=`), the footer keeps the first action and ⌘K, Escape/⌘⌫ pop, Enter/⌘Enter are actions 0/1, a modifier combo its action; with `keys: "actions"` a bare key (`h`, `space`, `+`, `up`) runs the action carrying it. A pick is `pick(view.id ?? "view", action, ctx)`; `{ view }` back replaces the level's tree in place (from a list row it pushes a level). The host checks every view (`host/src/view.ts`): `pal:` action ids refused, 2000 nodes / 24 deep, duplicate sibling keys. `docs/extensions.md`.
- Form level (`Effect.form`, driven by `extensions/quicklinks` and `extensions/snippets`): `Form { id?, title, fields: FormField[], submit: { id, title }, cancel?, errors? }`, fields `text|textarea|password|select|checkbox` with `label placeholder default required description`. The Launcher pushes `{ kind: "form" }` (the `Form` component in the body, the title in the search row as for a view, the submit in the footer, no ⌘K); Enter submits as `pick(form.id ?? row.id, submit.id, ctx)` with `ctx.values` (on the wire `values` rides flat beside `args`; `index::pick` takes `values: Option<Value>`), `required` is checked in the panel first (field marked and focused, no pick). A `{ form }` answer to the submit replaces the spec in place (the fields keep what was typed, `errors` show under them); any other answer pops the form first. `checkForm` in `host/src/view.ts` refuses empty/duplicate fields, unknown kinds, a `pal:` submit id, errors for no field. `stays_open` in effects.rs does not list `form` yet (only matters for a `copy` sent with a form: the HUD would say Copied). `ctxOf` now drops a `null` `args` (the core sends `args: null` for a plain level; extensions only ever truth-tested it). Vitest interaction tests run under `happy-dom` (added as a devDependency; per-file `@vitest-environment`, the others stay in node).
- Storage: `storage.get/set/remove/keys` in api.ts over `core/storage.*` (`pal_core::storage`, `app/src-tauri/src/storage.rs`): one JSON object per extension in `<data dir>/pal/storage/<extension>.json` (top-level data dir, shared across profiles like the clipboard db), atomic writes, 256 KB cap (a `set` over it rejects and rolls back), keys 1..128 bytes, extension name validated as a file name, a broken file set aside as `.bak`. Blackjack keeps its whole state there (shoe included, ~3 KB), so Escape mid-hand loses nothing.
- 9332904: process-group timeouts in scripts, stable bookmark ids, apps honour refresh, calc import guard, `home()`. 0b48368: extension `console.log` to stderr, non-array `list` is an error, symlinked roots. Linux `apps` (`.desktop`): `notes/linux.md`.
- Files "Open with…" and "Copy file" (`pal_core::apps`, `core/apps.{for_file,open_with}` via `app/src-tauri/src/apps.rs`; the `copy_files` effect in effects.rs over `clipboard::write_files`): `for_file` is `NSWorkspace URLsForApplicationsToOpenURL:` / `URLForApplicationToOpenURL:` on macOS (the documented replacement for `LSCopyApplicationURLsForURL` with `kLSRolesAll`, already reachable through objc2-app-kit's `NSWorkspace` feature, so no new crate; 39-44 ms on hornet for 7-10 apps) and, on Linux, `xdg-mime query filetype|default` plus the `mimeapps.list` (added/removed/default, `<desktop>-mimeapps.list` first) and `mimeinfo.cache` parsers (pure, fixture-tested on every platform), `text/*` also taking `text/plain`'s editors; `open_with` is `open -a` / `gio launch`. The extension pushes the same `files` palette with `args: { open_with: path }` rather than a second palette, so no "Open With" row appears at the root (the crumb and placeholder stay Files'); the level's rows are app paths with `{ app: path }` icons, the default tagged, and a level pick calls `open_with` then hides. `copy_files` writes through the same `platform::write(Content::Files)` the history's `copy` uses (arboard's `text/uri-list` on Linux; a backend without it is a failure toast, since the panel is still up). `.desktop` reading is now one place, `fs::desktop_dirs` / `fs::desktop_entry` (icons.rs and windows.rs used to carry their own). Open: `Effect.copy_files` and typed `apps.forFile`/`openWith` wrappers are not in `sdk/src/{protocol,api}.ts` yet (another agent owns them); the extension goes through `core.call` and the effect literal passes `tsc` only because `pick`'s return is a union of branch types, not a fresh literal against `Effect`.
- Bar items (`docs/design/bar.md`, 2026-09-16, spec only): extensions declare `bar.<id>` in the manifest and `Extension.bar[id].render(ctx) -> BarItem` in the code; one model (`icon title segments badge color urgent stale progress tooltip hidden menu`) rendered by three targets, the macOS menu bar (one Tauri tray icon per item, glyphs prerendered to template PNGs with `ab_glyph` from a bundled Nerd Font TTF since the NSStatusItem title is plain system-font text), sketchybar (batched CLI, names under `pal.` only, the owner's items untouched, `sketchybar --query bar` as detection) and a waybar JSON feed later. `hidden: true` is the owner's "earns its slot" rule and takes no space anywhere. Rich content is pal's own popover (a fourth NSPanel under the anchor rect: `TrayIconEvent::Click.rect` on the menu bar, `bounding_rects` from `sketchybar --query` on sketchybar) drawing a menu level (`menu: nodes`), a palette level or a view tree, the same on every target: popover only, no native NSMenu, since one renderer means parity across bars and one keyboard model and the native menu was the only per-target behaviour (it may return as an opt-in degradation, not a design path). Hover opens a peek after `[bar] hover_delay` (250 ms; `open_on_hover` on for sketchybar, off for the menu bar, per item overridable): non-activating, no keyboard, closes `hover_grace` (400 ms) after the pointer leaves item and popover; a click, the hotkey or any key engages it. Sources: `TrayIconEvent::{Enter, Leave}` and sketchybar `mouse.entered`/`mouse.exited` via `pal bar hover`. sketchybar popups and per-row click scripts are not reproduced. Pushes via `bar.update` over the reverse RPC; `[bar]` config with per-item target/position/hotkey; `pal bar` CLI for click scripts and scripting. Phases: the strip on both targets plus the popover with menu and palette levels, then the view level and hover, then Linux. Open calls listed in the spec (work hours as a pal notion, `target = "auto"` fallback, how a peek sees its engaging key).
- GitHub (`extensions/github`, 2026-09-16): one extension, five palettes (`prs`, `issues`, `repos`, `notifications`, `search`), replacing the v1 `repos`/`gh-reviews` scripts; the personal `prs`/`issues` scripts (sketchybar cache) stay under `scripts`. Auth is a token setting else `gh auth token` (5 min memo), never `gh` for data: one HTTP client for both, so a machine without gh is the same code path. Lists that need per-row facts (checks rollup, review decision, branch, size) are GraphQL, one request per palette with the filters' queries as aliases (`mine`/`reviews`/`merged`), so "All" and each filter share one cached answer and cost one round trip; Repositories and Notifications are REST for the ETags (`If-None-Match`, a 304 is free against the limit). Cache = normalised rows + ETag + age, memory over `storage` (`cache:<key>`), never a raw body (256 KB cap); the palettes' `ttl` and the cache agree (300 s, notifications 60 s live); a loader failure with cached rows logs and shows them, but no token at all is the hint, never stale rows. Mutations (`merge`, `ready`, `close`, `read`, `read-all`, the two create forms) `forget` the key so the `keep` relist fetches. Ids are `owner/repo#n`, `owner/repo`, `@login`, `thread:<id>`; a pick after a restart looks the row up by id (`FindPR`/`FindIssue`) when no table has it. Not possible from an extension: an accessory on the palette's own root row (`PaletteMeta` has none), so the unread count is the first row of Notifications instead. Skipped: `$EDITOR` for Open in editor (a terminal editor cannot be launched from the panel; `code` else the folder), Checkout without gh (would need git in the extension), GraphQL ETags (the API sends none). Tests: a Bun mock of api.github.com dispatching on the GraphQL operation name, reached through `PAL_GITHUB_API`, with `PAL_GITHUB_TOKEN` and a fake `gh` on PATH.

### UI

- Both webviews follow `general.theme` from `pal://config` (`data-theme` on `<html>`).
- Glyph icons: bundled Symbols Nerd Font Mono (woff2, 1.2 MB) scoped to the private-use range; freedesktop icon names mapped (474e670).
- Font, highlight, selection (the three taste calls from the brief): **system font**, not bundled Plex: `--pal-font-ui` is `-apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, "SF Pro Text", "Segoe UI", "Inter", "Noto Sans", system-ui, sans-serif`, `--pal-font-mono` is `ui-monospace, "SF Mono", Menlo, "JetBrains Mono", monospace`. The panel is judged next to Spotlight and Raycast on macOS, both in SF; Linux gets its own desktop's sans. Plex's tabular figures come from `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` on accessories, tags and metadata values instead (`ui.css`), checked in Chrome: `1111` and `0000` measure the same in an accessory, not in a title. No Google Fonts link in the brief any more. **Match highlight stays the amber highlighter** (`--pal-match`) and **selection stays the inset pill** (`--pal-radius-row`, `--pal-list-inset`), both unchanged. `app/design/brief.html`, Type.
- Landed: grapheme highlights, IME and key-repeat guards, hover vs keyboard cursor, overlay key swallowing, focus traps, roles, motion from tokens, exit motion via a presence wrapper, Tab swallowed without a filter; first run = Welcome section on the empty root until dismissed, "Show tips again", empty-state copy.
- Diagnostics strip: unknown keys show the dotted path with no line (serde gives no spans for ignored keys); parse errors have a line.
- `alias` is an extra keyword on the palette row, not a jump; docs say so now. Settings cmd+w also honours Super+W on Linux (both fcb967a).

### Settings and config

- v1 migration on first run (`config::migrate`, effc4ff): a v1-shaped `config.toml` is kept whole as `config.v1.toml` (stale `base` lines under the old `~/proj/pal` checkout rewritten to `pal-v1` when that is where they exist), and the new `config.toml` points `[extensions.scripts] config` and `[extensions.bookmarks] file` at it. The user store lives in `<data dir>/pal/extensions`, nothing is written next to the config (`#:schema` is the raw GitHub URL), `general.extension_dirs` adds dotfiles-managed roots; old pre-profile `frecency.json` is adopted into `default`, a root `index/` cache deleted. `show` levels keep metadata. Lock Screen via the private `SACLockScreenImmediate` (fine outside the App Store); DND needs a Shortcut named "Toggle Do Not Disturb", hidden otherwise, no hint.

- Declarative, file-first TOML: everything the view sets lives in the file the user edits and versions; the UI is a front, never the only path. UI change writes the file, file change is picked up live, formatting and comments kept, JSON schema shipped.
- Extensive settings view; extensions declare their settings, palette extensions also get pal-provided per-palette defaults (hotkey, alias, enabled, ranking behaviour) without declaring them.
- A declared setting equal to its default leaves the file (the view unsets the key); `enabled = true` likewise.
- Secrets: a `secret` field goes to the OS store as `pal/<extension>-<key>` (`SecretStore::set`, `security add-generic-password -U` on macOS), the file gets `keychain:pal/<extension>-<key>`; remove only unsets the key, the keychain item stays.
- Secrets on Linux: Secret Service through the `secret-tool` CLI (libsecret), no D-Bus client of our own: `secret-tool lookup service <s> account <a>` / `store --label="pal <key>"` with the value on stdin (`SecretService` in `core/src/config/secrets.rs`). `secret-tool` exits 1 for everything, so stderr decides: empty = `NotFound`; a bus or `org.freedesktop.secrets` activation complaint = `Unavailable` (new variant, also for no `secret-tool` on PATH); anything else (locked, denied) = `Store`. Mapping unit-tested with a fake `secret-tool` script (runs on macOS too); the real roundtrip (`--ignored secret_tool_set_then_get_roundtrip`) passed on marko against gnome-keyring. `env:` unchanged.
- One config watcher (`settings::install`): reload updates managed state, re-applies root and per-palette hotkeys, removes/lists palettes per `enabled`, pushes `settings/changed`, emits `pal://config` to every window.
- Permissions (`permissions.rs`, `pal_core::ax::open_settings`): every ask for Accessibility is the system prompt (which is what puts pal on the Privacy & Security > Accessibility list) plus System Settings opened on that pane. Askers: the panel's first show on a fresh profile (once per run, `general.ask_permissions_on_start`, default true), the Welcome row (now first while missing, every time), the first refused paste/focus per run, and Settings > General > Permissions (live dot, Grant button). No OS notification for a grant, so `ax::trusted` is polled every 2 s while a pal window is open and the permission is missing; `pal://permissions` on the grant. `settings_get` carries `permissions`.
- Several root hotkeys (2026-09-16 20:45): `general.hotkey` is a string or a list (`pal_core::config::Hotkeys`, `#[serde(untagged)]`, the schema says both); every entry registers as `Target::Root`; `hotkey::apply` is `parse_roots` + `reconcile` (pure: a fake OS in the tests) + `judge`, a root the OS refuses costs the others nothing and only when none of the wanted roots could be had do the previous ones stay. `Outcome` is `{hotkeys: [{wanted, registered, error, spotlight}], registered}` (overall = at least one works). Settings > General draws one recorder row per entry with its presets, Remove and status line, "Add another" (max 3, an unsaved row until a combination lands); Settings writes back the file's spelling and turns a string into a list only when a second entry needs it (`edit::set` swaps the value on the same line, comment kept). The Overview's fact lists all (`⌥␣, ⌃⌥⇧J`), its issue row is per failed entry and names what still opens pal. The menu bar hint and the Welcome tips show the first.
- Hotkey outcome (`hotkey::Outcome`, `hotkey_status`, in `settings_get`, `pal://hotkey` on change), per entry `{wanted, registered, error, spotlight}`. `pal_core::spotlight::hotkey()` reads entry 64 of `com.apple.symbolichotkeys` (`defaults export`, parsed with `plist`; absent = ⌘Space on); a root hotkey equal to it is reported as not registered ("Spotlight takes this key first") and polled every 2 s until Spotlight lets go, then re-applied. Settings > General: recorder plus presets (⌘Space, ⌥Space, ⌃Space, ⌘⇧Space; ⌘Space cannot be recorded, Spotlight opens on the press), a status line, and the guidance with an "Open Keyboard Shortcuts" button (`open_system_settings`).

### Packaging and release

- Sidecar `pal-bun` (pinned release, `app/scripts/fetch-bun.sh`, checksum verified), host and extensions as resources, binary `pal`, bundle targets narrowed, first-run config written with a commented template and schema.
- CI + release workflows, updater with a daily check behind `general.check_updates`, `make release VERSION=x.y.z` (sets the version everywhere, commits, tags, pushes; `DRY_RUN=1` prints the steps; `docs/releasing.md`).
- Toolchain pinned in both workflows (`dtolnay/rust-toolchain@1.97.1`, what hornet's Homebrew rustc is): stable 1.98.1 on the runners added `clippy::chunks_exact_to_as_chunks` on 2026-09-16 and turned CI red on code every box had checked clean. Bump both files together. Release notes are the commit subjects since the previous reachable `v*` tag (GitHub's generated notes list pull requests, and this repo has none); a `manifest` job reads `latest.json` back from the draft and fails on a missing platform (the three matrix jobs merge into that file in parallel; `tauri-action`'s `upload-version-json.ts` downloads, merges, deletes and re-uploads, so two finishing together can drop one).
- macOS stays ad-hoc signed for v0.1.0. Gatekeeper refuses a downloaded ad-hoc app once (`docs/getting-started.md` gives the three ways past it: `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine`, Privacy & Security > Open Anyway, right-click Open on 14 and earlier). The real fix is a Developer ID Application certificate (Apple Developer Program, USD 99/year) plus notarisation (free within the program, `notarytool`, a few minutes per build), `hardenedRuntime: true` with JIT entitlements for the bun sidecar, and the six `APPLE_*` secrets in `release.yml`; steps in `docs/releasing.md`. A Developer ID signature is also what keeps the Accessibility grant across updates; ad-hoc builds lose it on every install.
- `pal install <name>`: a bare name is looked up at `https://pal.cagdas.io/api/extensions/<name>` (`spec` field) in `Spec::resolve` (`core/src/extensions.rs`); `--from SPEC` skips the lookup; an unknown name is `Error::Unknown`, an unreachable site `Error::Registry` (no fallback). The deep link and the Settings form go through `Store::install`, so they take names too.
- Linux bundle (`NO_STRIP=true`, `GDK_BACKEND=wayland,x11` under `APPDIR`, deb depends on `libayatana-appindicator3-1`): `notes/linux.md`.

### Linux

- All in `notes/linux.md`: go/no-go numbers, no global hotkey on Wayland (bind `pal toggle` in the compositor), Hyprland windowrule set, `panel/linux.rs` (hide on focus loss, pre-map at startup), tray via `libappindicator` dlopen, XDG autostart (Hyprland needs `exec-once = pal`).

## How to run it

- Daily instance on hornet since 2026-09-16 12:50: `/Applications/pal.app` on the real `~/.config/pal/config.toml` (migrated; v1 kept as `config.v1.toml`), `pal` on PATH is `~/.local/bin/pal` -> the app binary, v1 uninstalled. Dev instance: `cd app && PAL_CONFIG="$HOME/Library/Application Support/pal/dev.toml" npm run tauri dev` (a second profile, so it never touches the daily one; give it another hotkey in that file). Host and extensions load from the repo and reload on change; README has the installs. `pal toggle|show|hide|settings|quit` drive the running instance.
- `make test`: Rust workspace, app vitest, host bun test.
- Scratch release instance on hornet (a measurement, not the daily one): `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/scratch-<x> TAURI_CONFIG='{"identifier":"io.cagdas.pal.scratch"}' cargo build --release --features tauri/custom-protocol -p pal` (the identifier is baked in; each agent its own, the single-instance socket is `$TMPDIR/../io_cagdas_pal_<id>_si.sock`), stage `scripts/build-extensions.sh` and rsync `app/src-tauri/resources/{host,sdk,extensions}` into `target/scratch-<x>/release/` for the bundled layout (without a staged `host/src/host.ts` there the binary falls back to the repo's `.ts`, which reloads on every edit another agent makes), then run it with its own `PAL_CONFIG` (another hotkey, `menu_bar_icon = false`, `[bar] target = "off"` so it never touches the daily sketchybar) and `XDG_DATA_HOME`. Write `date +%s` to `$XDG_DATA_HOME/pal/launchd/handover` first: a hand-started release build otherwise hands itself to launchd within the minute and its stderr is gone. `pal quit` through the same env ends it.
- Logs: tab-separated marks on stderr (`profile`, `hotkey\t...`, `quit\tflushed`, extension `console.log`), so in dev the `tauri dev` terminal; no log file. Data in `<data dir>/pal/<profile>/`.
- Daily instance on marko since 2026-09-16 17:00: bare release binary `~/.local/share/pal/bin/pal` (+ `pal-bun`), `~/.local/bin/pal` -> it, host and extensions from the `~/proj/pali` clone (`Layout::resolve` repo fallback), v1 uninstalled, `~/proj/pal` kept for the v1 plugins the scripts tier reads. Super+Space is an xremap entry running `pal toggle`; `hyprland.conf` has the title-keyed panel and HUD rules, `exec-once = pal`, and a `$mod, space` bind that only fires when xremap is down. Since 2026-09-17 04:07 it is tonight's build (22.8 MB; the 09-16 one kept as `pal.old`) running under the `pal.service` user unit it wrote (`autostart supervised`), log `~/.local/state/pal/pal.log`; `notes/linux.md` "The daily instance".
- marko: clone `marko:~/proj/pali` (branch `main`). Release: `cargo build --release --features tauri/custom-protocol`; bundle: `NO_STRIP=true npm run tauri build` in `app/`; take `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`/`DISPLAY` from `systemctl --user show-environment` when launching by hand. `notes/linux.md`.

Spotify's `playlists`, `library`, `devices`, `queue` are marked since 09:20. What remains for Cagdas: Calendar's `loadFilters()` still asks Google for the calendar names at load (the filters are static meta), so with the Google source one token command still runs at start.

- **Pit pull, decided 2026-09-16 22:10**: build the triage's top 25 (`notes/pit-triage.md`) except 13 (projects). 15 (rows from the clipboard) must be ultra rich; 9 (empty root) gets the next event, recent files and "common stuff" for the user on top. Batches: A root/search (1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 9, 15, 24) one agent; B1 new palettes (11 generate, 12 Shortcuts, 25 script commands); B2 AX (3 menu bar items, 14 window verbs); B3 files content search (20); C1 core capabilities (6 concealed copy, 19 selection.text, 21 OCR, 22 Large Type); C2 after the Wi-Fi and links agents (17 updater install, 23 settings.set, 10 hotkeys/aliases in Settings); C3 after A (18 multi-select, 8 `pal pick`, 16 dialog jump).
