Not covered: `otp` (v1 reads Android SMS through datak; the extension reads Messages: needs a datak source setting), `today` (v1 reads HA calendar entities; Calendar reads EventKit: an HA Today palette or Google in macOS Calendar), `repos` (local projects: a Projects extension), `pwatch` (a foreground filter on Processes), `cmds` (a data file, stays zero-code), `ss` (kitty sessions, marko), `gl-reviews` (GitLab, if still needed). Waiting on his pick.


Things an agent could not decide alone; each waits for a call.

- Window layouts: should `restore` survive a restart (a small JSON under the profile, keyed by window id, which macOS reuses after an app relaunch) or stay in memory as now? (`item_hotkeys` has its Settings table since 2026-09-16 23:50, "Decided: item hotkeys in Settings" below.) Should the layouts get default hotkeys at all (Raycast ships none bound)? The installed `/Applications/pal.app` is not in the Accessibility list yet, so the palette's `Enter` shows the toast until it is granted; the example binary ran from a trusted terminal.
- macOS `show-desktop`, `lock`, `dark-mode` system commands are the only unverified ones on hornet (window list/focus/minimize/restore were verified after the unlock at 08:10; see Findings). One-liner: `cargo run -q -p pal-core --example system -- run show-desktop` twice.

## Findings

- **PATH from the Dock (2026-09-16 21:00).** Cagdas: "I already did gh auth login, but github palettes doesn't work". The host ran with launchd's `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin` (`ps eww` on `pal-bun`), so `Bun.which("gh")` was null and every CLI-backed extension (gh, op, docker, timer, make, nmcli, sketchybar) failed when pal started from the LaunchAgent and worked from a terminal. `pal_core::env::adopt()` at the top of `run()` reads the login shell's PATH (`$SHELL -l -i -c` with a marker, 120 ms on hornet, 2 s cap, skipped when the inherited PATH already reaches past the system dirs) and merges it with the usual tool dirs; the process env carries it to the host and every `Command`.

- **Empty list with a live count (2026-09-16 20:40).** Cagdas saw the panel with hits counted in the footer ("97 of 30526", the primary action named) and no rows drawn, now and then, self-healing later. Diagnosis (guess, the state was gone): the virtualiser reads the scroll rect once at mount and then only through ResizeObserver, and WebKit holds those callbacks while the panel sits hidden at alpha 0, so a list whose rows changed while hidden could keep a zero or stale rect and draw nothing. Fix: `observeRect` in `app/src/ui/virtual.ts` re-reads the rect on every `pal:shown` (dispatched by App.tsx) and on `visibilitychange`; List and Grid use it. If it shows up again with this build, the guess was wrong: capture `document.visibilityState`, the `.pal-list` client height and `virt.getVirtualItems().length` from the console.
 that changed the design

- Raycast 2.0 is a Rust core with a web UI in the system webview, the shape Tauri v2 gives; that set the go/no-go candidate (Shell, above).
- WebKit suspends rendering (rAF, timers) for a page whose window is ordered out or occluded, so the macOS panel is never ordered out (hidden = alpha 0, mouse ignored) and occlusion detection is switched off via the private `_setWindowOcclusionDetectionEnabled:` Raycast also flips. `app/src-tauri/src/panel/macos.rs:49`.
- `global-hotkey` 0.8 is X11-only on Linux; on Wayland the grab lives on Xwayland and fires only with an X11 client focused (0 of 7 from Chromium). Hence `pal toggle` over single-instance. `notes/linux.md`.
- First key after a re-show lost on WebKitGTK: hiding inside an unhandled keydown leaves a "forward next key" flag stuck. Rule: `preventDefault()` on the key that hides, never hide from an unhandled key event (`ui/keys.ts:110`). `notes/linux.md`.
- Pre-mapping at startup (show then hide, never on screen) took marko's first show from 111-124 ms to 12-20 ms; pre-painting took hornet's 706 ms to 1.7 ms release. `notes/linux.md`, 1db672e.
- The joined-haystack fuzzy match (fzf-for-js, nucleo par) ranks `hand` above the bookmark `ha` and drops Terminal.app from the `term` top 10; per-field weighting fixes it under 0.7 ms per keystroke. `notes/matching.md`.
- `!`, `^`, `'`, `$` are ordinary text in a query, not fzf operators (Spotlight and Raycast have none; a bookmark `!important` must be findable). `core/src/index.rs:263`, 75a7f47.
- Hotkey apply deadlock, found and fixed in d811024 (re-register order first touched in 75a7f47). `app/src-tauri/src/hotkey.rs`.
- WebKitGTK does no PUA font fallback: Nerd Font glyphs were tofu on Linux, so the font is bundled for that range. `notes/linux.md`.
- linuxdeploy exports `GDK_BACKEND=x11`, so the AppImage ran under Xwayland and the windowrule never matched; `main.rs` sets `wayland,x11` under `APPDIR`. `NO_STRIP=true` is load-bearing on Arch. `notes/linux.md`.
- CoreGraphics gives other apps' titles only with Screen Recording, so titles come from AX; AX returns nothing while the screen is locked, and lists only the current Space's windows, so an unmatched CG row is kept and focusing it activates the app (which switches Spaces); `kCGWindowName` lags AX, so matching is frame-first (`core/src/windows.rs`, commit 27d9873).
- Bun does not carry async context through a dynamic import; serde reports no spans for ignored keys. Both shaped the settings and diagnostics paths above.
- macOS never refuses a `RegisterEventHotKey` for a combination another app holds either (checked 2026-09-16 with a Carbon holder on ⌃⌥⇧H, with and without `kEventHotKeyExclusive`: the scratch pal logged `registered` both times, and with the exclusive holder the press went to the holder, not pal). So on macOS a root hotkey "another app holds" reads as registered and does nothing; the Overview's "held by another app (Raycast…)" row can only come from X11 (`XGrabKey` refusal) or the crate's own duplicate check. Verifying the per-entry failure path used Spotlight's key (⌥Space on hornet) as the taken entry.
- macOS accepts a `RegisterEventHotKey` for a combination Spotlight holds (`hotkey registered alt+Space` on hornet, where Spotlight is ⌥Space), and Spotlight still gets the press: the conflict has to come from reading `symbolichotkeys`, not from a refusal. Also: a binary launched from a terminal is Accessibility-trusted through the terminal (TCC's responsible process), so a scratch instance has to be an app bundle launched with `open` (`LSEnvironment` for `PAL_CONFIG`/`XDG_*`) to see a fresh machine's `permissions accessibility false`. And `TAURI_CONFIG` is a build-time input: set at runtime it does nothing and the binary hands over to the daily instance.
- The docs pass (06:30) found six code/doc contradictions (clipboard retention meaning, `exclude_apps = []`, `alias` semantics, unresolved `keychain:` in extension settings, cmd+w on Linux, deb tray dependency); all fixed in fcb967a, not papered over.
- Blackjack pass, limits found in the app/host and fixed there, not in the game: (1) no bare-key command in the grammar (a `keydown` for `h` was typing and went to the search input), so `keys.ts` gained `{ type: "key" }` that a handler may decline, and the ActionPanel, Form and Confirm claim it (`"native"`/swallow) so a game behind an overlay gets nothing; (2) the Launcher's `useKeys` returned early for any resolved command, which would have stopped typing from reaching the input once bare keys resolved, hence the `cmd.type !== "key"` fall-through; (3) fast key presses raced the host reply (two hits in flight, the second on a hand that may be over), so a view level runs one pick at a time and drops keys meanwhile (`viewBusy`), with the search sweep as feedback; (4) a late reply after Escape would have replaced whatever level was on top, so `Effect.view` is applied only when the view level it came from is still the top (`level` ref); (5) action ids could collide with the shell's `pal:*` and an item's actions could smuggle extra fields, so the host refuses `pal:` ids in a view and `toAction` keeps the five wire fields only; (6) `shortcutKeys("+")` split on its own key and drew two empty caps, so a trailing `+` is the plus key; (7) `+`/`-`/`space`/arrows had no shortcut spelling, now `keyName` gives `space`/`up`/`down`/`left`/`right` and format.ts draws them on both platforms; (8) nothing focusable in a view level (no input), so the `.pal-view` document takes focus (`autoFocus`) and the input is refocused on leaving; (9) a gallery with two window-scoped Launchers answered every key twice (two action panels opened on ⌘K): one live launcher at a time there (`LiveSlot`), the app has one anyway; (10) a keyed child leaving a flex row needs its exit to finish before the DOM goes, so `Presence` gained `onExited` and `View` keeps leaving children at their old index under it; a row whose children come and go holds its height with `minHeight`. Not hit: the 5 s bridge timeout (a storage call is well under a millisecond) and tree size (a full table is ~25 nodes and ~20 KB with the SVG cards; the cap is 2000).
- Home Assistant extension (2026-09-16, replaces the v1 `ha-states`/`ha-services` scripts): (1) the entity palette is `live` with no `ttl`, so every show is one `/api/states` (385 KB, ~0.5 s over Cloudflare on the owner's setup); that is the cost of a current state at the root and what the owner had. (2) Areas come from one `/api/template` render (`areas()`/`area_entities()`/`area_name()`, 0.75 s, 164 of 507 entities mapped), cached ten minutes; the entity registry over the websocket was the heavy alternative. (3) HA's REST `/api/services` carries `name`/`description` for almost no core service (10 of 274 on the owner's HA; they live in frontend translations), so the form labels are the title-cased keys and the `example` is the placeholder; `selector` decides the field kind. (4) "Lights tinted when on": the wire has no tint for a glyph, only a hex colour that draws a dot, so an `on` light is a dot in its `rgb_color` (warm white without one) and an `off` one the bulb glyph. (5) Bun's `fetch` drops `Authorization` when a redirect changes the origin, and the owner's `HA_SERVER` is `http://` behind Cloudflare which answers 301 to `https://` for a non-curl user agent, so a valid token read as 401 until the client followed redirects by hand (one hop, token kept, logged once). Set the `https://` URL in pal. (6) The live check ran read-only plus `persistent_notification.create` and `dismiss` through the service form: all ten `input_boolean`s on the owner's HA are AC control flags and every scene sets lights, so nothing there was a harmless toggle.

## otp, onepassword, browser-tabs (2026-09-16)

Three first-class extensions replacing the v1 script palettes `otp`, `op` and `tabs` (`extensions/{otp,onepassword,browser-tabs}/`, docs in `docs/palettes.md`).

- `otp` reads `~/Library/Messages/chat.db` itself over `bun:sqlite` (read-only) rather than the `datak` view v1 curled: the generic product cannot depend on a personal service. Full Disk Access is the whole permission story: SQLite answers `SQLITE_AUTH` ("authorization denied") and `cp` answers EPERM for a process without it (verified from the agent shell on hornet, which has none), so the palette's one hint row opens the `Privacy_AllFiles` pane. A locked database is copied with `-wal`/`-shm` and read from the copy. Since Ventura `message.text` is often NULL and the text sits in `attributedBody` (a typedstream: the string after `NSString`, five framing bytes, a 1/3/5-byte length), decoded in `bodyText`. A code is only taken from a message that names one (a keyword list across English, Turkish, Spanish; suffixes allowed, prefixes not, so `decode` does not count) and is a 4..8 digit run not inside an amount, date, time or phone number; the candidate nearest a keyword wins, 6 digits breaking ties; `G-123456` yields its digits, which is what Google's form takes. Numbers and emails resolve to names through the AddressBook databases (same permission), best effort. Not carried over from v1: the hand-kept sender table with per-bank icons and the money/shop/gov filter; the sender string and a green code tag are enough to identify a row.
- `onepassword` wraps `op` with a `ttl` cache of `op item list` so the per-vault filters and a refresh inside the ttl do not re-run the CLI (and its Touch ID prompt). Secrets are fetched only on a pick (`op item get <id> --fields label=<f> --reveal`, `--otp`) and go straight to `copy`; nothing secret is in a row, the index or a log. "Open in 1Password" is `onepassword://view-item?i=&v=&a=`, not an `op://` url: `op://vault/item/field` is the CLI's secret-reference scheme, while the app registers only `onepassword` and `onepassword8` (`/Applications/1Password.app/Contents/Info.plist`, `CFBundleURLTypes`). Filters come from the `vaults` setting at load, since `PaletteMeta.filters` is static; a change lands at the next host start (documented). Signed out and "no accounts configured" are one hint row; hornet has the CLI (2.39.0) with no account configured, which is the state that was verified live. `op` is looked up on PATH, then the installers' directories, since the app under launchd has a bare PATH.
- `browser-tabs` speaks CDP itself (`cdp.ts`: `/json`, `/json/version`, `/json/activate`, `/json/close`, and one browser WebSocket with id-multiplexed calls so the per-page media probe and window lookup run concurrently: 10 tabs probed in 5 ms on hornet's daily Chrome, the whole listing 83 ms) instead of shelling to `bt`, which is Cagdas's tool, not the product's. Safari and a Chrome without the port go through one `osascript -l JavaScript` run per listing (288 ms for 10 Chrome tabs); a Chromium app already on the port is skipped so tabs do not list twice. Firefox is read from `recovery.jsonlz4` (mozlz4: magic, LE size, one LZ4 block; decoder in `lz4Block`) and is read-only, so only its window can be focused. Focus is the browser-side select (`/json/activate` + `Page.bringToFront`, or `activeTabIndex`/`currentTab` + `index = 1`) followed by the `focus` effect on the OS window from `windows.list` whose title matches the tab's, else the browser's front window, so the panel hides first. Mute is the media-element mute `bt` does (`e.muted = true` on every `video`/`audio`), not Chrome's tab mute, which CDP has no call for without a helper extension; `bt`'s unpacked-extension trick was not ported. The Automation refusal (-1743) is a hint row opening the `Privacy_Automation` pane. Verified live on hornet read-only: the CDP listing of the daily Chrome and the AppleScript listing of the same tabs with the port setting pointed elsewhere; nothing was focused or closed.
- Tests through the harness (`host/test/extensions/{otp,onepassword,browser-tabs}.test.ts`): otp on a fixture `chat.db` and `AddressBook-v22.abcddb` built with `bun:sqlite` (7 tests, 1 platform-skipped); onepassword on a fake `op` first on PATH with a sign-in state file (9); browser-tabs on a Bun server faking the DevTools endpoints and WebSocket, a fake `osascript`, and a hand-compressed mozlz4 session with one LZ4 match (14, 4 macOS-only). 30 in all, 0.6 s to 1.3 s.

## calc rebuilt (2026-09-16)

- `calc` was mathjs over the query and could not do `12 usd to try`. Rebuilt around three parsers tried in order (`extensions/calc/{dates,currency,math}.ts`, `index.ts` the orchestration, `format.ts` the numbers, `row.ts` the shape they answer): dates and time zones first (their keywords are unambiguous), currency second (a currency token on the right or the left), mathjs last with a rewrite pass for the idioms it lacks (`sqrt 2`, `15% of 240`, `240 + 15%` = 276, `72 f to c`, `12 gb to mb`, `100 kph to mph`, `5 ft 3 in`, `3 x 4`, `2 power 10`, `10 mod 3`). mathjs stays for arithmetic and units: its unit table is good, only the spellings were missing (`kb` there is a kilobit, so the byte aliases map to `kB`/`MB`/`GB`; `f`/`c`/`k` are degrees only across a `to`).
- Rates: the ECB reference set through frankfurter (`https://api.frankfurter.dev/v1/latest`; `api.frankfurter.app` now 301s there). Free, no key, 30 currencies, TRY included, no crypto (so `1 btc` lists nothing rather than fetching a second source; no `rate_source` setting until one exists). Fetched lazily on the first currency query, never at load; kept in `storage` under `rates` (`{ base, date, rates, fetched }`, ~1 KB, the one exception to "storage is not a cache") and refreshed after 24 h in the background: `ensureRates` awaits only the one storage read, the fetch is a module-level promise nobody awaits, so the first query without a cache answers an inert `Fetching exchange rates…` row and the next keystroke has the numbers. A stale or offline set answers dated (`rates 2026-09-15` accessory). A failed fetch backs off a minute. `PAL_CALC_OFFLINE=1` (tests, or a machine that should never call out) and `PAL_CALC_RATES_URL` (the test's Bun server) are the two env knobs.
- Rows: the result is the title (Enter copies it as shown), the query as understood the subtitle (`12 USD to TRY`), accessories the rate and date / the unit; a second row when there is another form: the other bases for a number typed as one or a bare integer (not for every integer result, `2+2` stays one row), a fraction, the reverse conversion, the ISO date, the unix time. Row ids are fixed (`result`, `base`, `fraction`, `reverse`, `iso`, `unix`) and `pick` reads the last listing from a map: an input palette relists per keystroke and the ids only need to be stable across those. Actions: Copy result, Paste result (`paste: { text }`), Copy without formatting (`cmd+shift+c`, `583.68` / `1000000`), Copy expression = result (`cmd+shift+e`).
- Silence while typing: every parser returns nothing for a query that does not parse, so no error rows; two exceptions on purpose: a target still being typed (`12 usd to tr`) keeps the source's row (a prefix of a known currency word), and a date-shaped query that is no date (`2026-02-30`) lists nothing instead of falling through to `2026-2-30 = 1994`.
- Formatting through `Intl`: `precision` (default 10, was 14) is significant digits for the fraction only, integers are never rounded (`maximumSignificantDigits` would print `2^40` as `1,099,510,000,000`); units and rates cap at 6 (`22.2222 °C`, not `22.222222222222`). `locale` (default `en`) drives grouping, the decimal separator and dates, and is applied to the input too (`1,5 + 2` under `tr`). `home_currency` empty means the time zone's currency (`Europe/Istanbul` is TRY; the Intl locale is `en-US` under a `LANG=en_US` shell even on an `en_TR` Mac, so the zone is the better signal), then the locale region, then USD.
- Zones: an alias table (abbreviations, ~120 cities and countries) over IANA ids, `utc+3` as `Etc/GMT-3` (whole hours only: no `Etc` zone has minutes), wall-clock to instant by two `Intl.formatToParts` offsets. `ist` is Istanbul (the airport code, and where the machine is), India by name; `cet` follows Berlin's DST. Without a source zone the local one; the time is today's in the source zone; `next day` accessory when the date differs.
- Tests: `host/test/extensions/calc.test.ts`, 23 tests, 168 expects, ~0.8 s: every doc example through the harness with canned rates (seeded through the harness's `stored` map before the host starts, `PAL_CALC_OFFLINE=1`), `process.env.TZ = "UTC"` so the spawned host agrees with bun test's own UTC, and two more hosts against a `Bun.serve` for the no-cache (fetching row, stored set) and stale-cache (dated answer, background refresh) paths.

## docker, services, make (2026-09-16)

Three extensions replacing the v1 script palettes `docker`, `systemd` and `mk` (`extensions/{docker,services,make}/`, docs in `docs/palettes.md`; the table at the top of that page was left as it was, these three are missing from it).

- `docker` is three live palettes over the CLI's `{{json .}}` output (containers, images, Compose projects), `binary` a setting so podman's compatible commands work (`Names` and `Ports` as arrays are tolerated). No daemon and no binary are one inert hint row each. The palette `ttl` comes from the `ttl` setting at import (`PaletteMeta` is static; a change lands at the next reload, documented). Enter toggles start/stop on the state of the last listing (`pick` gets only the id, so each extension keeps a small map from the listing). Verified live on marko against a throwaway `alpine sleep` container: list, logs, stop, start, restart, remove, the images Run form (`docker run -d --name pal-live-form -p 18081:80 alpine`), Compose listing (7 projects, 0.13 s); on hornet, which has no docker, the hint row.
- The core drops a pick unanswered after 10 s (`app/src-tauri/src/host.rs` `REQUEST_TIMEOUT`), which `docker stop` on a process that ignores SIGTERM (docker kills at 10 s) and `compose up` walk straight into. Every action in these three extensions waits at most 8 s (`WAIT_MS`) and then answers a "not done yet" toast while the command goes on (`proc.unref()`, still killed at its own longer limit); the palette lists again on the next show. Make's background mode is the same rule, so its planned `timeout` setting was dropped: nothing above 8 s could ever be honoured.
- Logs are a `show` level (a code fence of the last 200 lines) as specified, but a `show` level has no Refresh: the shell offers `pal:refresh` (⌘R) only at the root and on an indexed palette level (`app/src/Launcher.tsx`, the `actions` memo). Re-picking Logs re-reads. A `view` level would carry a ⌘R action of its own but scrolls only by wheel (no keyboard scroll for a view), which is worse for a tail. Open: a refresh hook for `show` (a `pick` id the shell re-asks on ⌘R).
- `services` picks its backend by platform (`PAL_SERVICES_BACKEND` forces one for the tests). systemd: `systemctl list-units --type=service --all --output=json` (systemd 245+; marko has 261) merged with `list-unit-files` so a disabled unit that never ran still lists; templates, masked and alias files skipped; an instance's enabled state is its template's. A system verb goes `systemctl --no-ask-password`, then `sudo -n`, then `pkexec`, each tried only when the previous was a permission refusal, and a toast quotes all three when none is allowed (verified on marko from the agent shell, where `sudo -n` has no window and `pkexec` has no agent: `alsa-state.service` start refused three ways, nothing changed). A user unit created for the check (`pal-live-test.service`) went through start, logs, restart, enable, disable, stop and was removed after. launchd: `launchctl list` for state, the plists in `agent_dirs` for the rows (a setting rather than a hard-coded pair so the tests point it at a temp folder), `bootstrap`/`bootout gui/$UID`, `kickstart -k`; `application.*` launch jobs are dropped from the Loaded and Running filters (568 rows on hornet, most of them those). Verified on hornet read-only: 16 agent rows across the two folders with the right pids (yabai, skhd, sketchybar, syncthing running; `io.cagdas.pal` not loaded since the app is started by hand).
- `make` walks the `projects` roots itself (depth 2, `readdirSync`) rather than shelling to the owner's `mk` (which is `rg --files -g Makefile` over `~` and `~/proj`, a personal tool). Targets are parsed from the text: `^names:` not followed by `=`, names with `%` or `$` or a leading dot dropped, `.PHONY` names added when no literal rule exists, description from `## text` on the line or the comment line above (the owner's Makefiles use `# comment` above `.PHONY:` + target, so a plain `#` counts and the `.PHONY` line is skipped when looking up). Run wraps the command in `sh -c 'make t; …; read -r _'` so the terminal window survives a quick target (v1 ran in the background and showed long output in a `show`, which the `background` terminal setting keeps). Listing `~/proj` on hornet: 69 targets in 9 projects.
- The terminal launcher is a copy of the ssh extension's (`extensions/{docker,make}/terminal.ts`, identical files), with shell quoting for the AppleScript terminals and a `cwd` (a `cd` in front). A bundled extension may not import a sibling's file (each installs on its own), so the copy stays until `@zcag/pal` grows a terminal helper; that is the place for it, and ssh should then use it too.
- Tests through the harness with fake binaries first on PATH (`host/test/extensions/{docker,services,make}.test.ts`): a `docker` shell script with canned rows, a mode file for "daemon down" and a 12 s `stop` for the pending path (11 tests, one of them 8 s); `systemctl`/`journalctl`/`sudo`/`pkexec` scripts with an `allow` file choosing which escalation works, and `launchctl` plus a temp LaunchAgents tree, both backends in one file (13); a temp projects tree with three Makefile spellings, a too-deep folder and a `node_modules` one, the real `make` for the background path (8). 32 in all. `PAL_TERMINAL_LOG` makes `openTerminal` append the argv to a file instead of spawning.

## Reference extensions: unicode, colors, icons, network (2026-09-16)

The four v1 data palettes (`chars`, `colors`, `icons`/`nerd`, `ip`) became bundled extensions; what was decided along the way:

- **Grids carry `section` on the row and the core keeps runs together**, so a block or a set is one header in the grid. The three reference grids put a "Recent" section first from the extension's storage (the last twelve picks) and drop those rows from their block, since an id may appear once per palette. The core's frecency also lifts a picked tile at the empty query, so between listings a used tile sits at the top under its block's header; the Recent section catches up at the next listing (start, ⌘R). Accepted rather than re-listing on every pick (`keep` would hold the panel open after a copy).
- **Generated tables, committed, with the script beside them.** `unicode/build.ts` (UCD names + WHATWG entities over curated ranges, 1795 rows, 160 KB), `colors/build.ts` (CSS names from `color.ts`, pal tokens read out of `tokens.css`, Tailwind and Material from unpkg, 700 rows, 48 KB), `icons/build.ts` (`glyphnames.json` at the pinned Nerd Fonts release, 10995 glyphs, 282 KB). No fetch at runtime, no UCD in the bundle. The icons listing is ~3.4 MB of JSON per start (11k rows with four actions each) and has no `ttl` so Recent stays fresh; the rows were trimmed to name, code point subtitle and one keyword. If the start-up pass ever shows in the profile, a `ttl` of a day is the knob.
- **Swatch tiles are `{ image: <svg data url> }`**: with `mask: rounded` an image icon fills the tile box, which nothing else in the icon vocabulary does (a hex icon draws a dot). The same data url in the detail's markdown gives the wide swatch; marked only blocks `data:` on links, not images. Unicode's detail draws its big glyph the same way (an SVG `<text>` in a mid grey, `#85869A`, that reads on both themes); Nerd Font glyphs cannot, since an `<img>` SVG cannot use the page's `@font-face`.
- **Colour maths is a dependency-free module** (`colors/color.ts`): parse, sRGB/HSL/HWB/OKLab/OKLCH, WCAG contrast, nearest CSS name in OKLab. Unit-tested directly by importing the file; the harness tests cover the palettes. Pick-from-screen was not built: no core capability samples the screen, and the doc says so rather than shipping a stub.
- **Network rows put the value in the name** (v1 put it in a tag): a list of addresses reads better with the address as the title and the label under it, and the name is what the search matches. Live with `ttl: 60`, so a show relists only when the rows are a minute old; the public IP is one fetch cached ten minutes in the extension, dropped by the shell's Refresh (`ctx.refresh`). Every tool runs with a 3 s timeout and a missing one is silently absent. A pick or detail for an id the extension has not listed (the restored cache after a restart) gathers once more instead of failing. The tunnel interface duplicating the Tailscale addresses is folded away.
- **Skipped, and why**: Flush DNS (sudo, no way to prompt from the host), pick colour from screen (no capability), the big Nerd Font glyph in the detail pane (font reach), v1's wider freedesktop name list (only the SDK's 114 names draw). The macOS SSID is redacted for a process without Location Services (`ipconfig getsummary` says `<redacted>`, `system_profiler` too, verified on hornet); the row is labelled by kind and the detail says why.
- Tests fake the OS with shell scripts on PATH (`fakeBin`) and `PAL_NETWORK_OS` / `PAL_NETWORK_RESOLV` to run the Linux path on a Mac, and a `Bun.serve` on port 0 as the public IP endpoint, counting hits to prove the cache.

## audio, bluetooth, wifi, media (2026-09-16)

Four device capabilities in the core (`pal_core::{audio,bluetooth,wifi,media}`, bridged as `core/audio.*`, `core/bluetooth.*`, `core/wifi.*`, `core/media.*` in `app/src-tauri/src/{audio,bluetooth,wifi,media}.rs`, typed in `sdk/src/api.ts` at the end) and the palettes over them (`extensions/{audio,bluetooth,wifi,media}/`, replacing v1's `audio`, `ble`, `wifi`, `media` script palettes; docs in `docs/palettes.md`). The four modules share one shell-out helper, `pal_core::tool` (`run`, `run_in` for stdin, `run_timeout` through coreutils' `timeout` on Linux, `osascript`), with one `Error` (`Unavailable` / `Failed` / `Io`) each re-exports; the older copies of that helper in `system.rs`, `apps.rs` and `windows.rs` were left where they are (other agents were in those files this round) and are a follow-up to fold into `tool`.

- **audio**: macOS is the CoreAudio HAL in-process through `objc2-core-audio` (`AudioObjectGetPropertyData` on the system object and each device; `std` + `AudioHardware` features only), no tool to install: the UID is the id, a device is an output or an input by which scope has streams, the volume is the virtual main volume (`'vmvc'`, `kAudioHardwareServiceDeviceProperty_VirtualMainVolume`, which the crate does not carry so it is a local constant) with the per-channel scalar on elements 1 and 2 as the fallback, mute is `kAudioDevicePropertyMute` on the main element, and the default is set on the system object. Verified read-only on hornet: 2 outputs (the Bluetooth speaker at 25%, the built-in speakers at 56% default) and the built-in mic at 57%. Linux is `wpctl status` parsed (the `*` in the gutter is the default, `[vol: 0.40 MUTED]` the rest) with `wpctl set-default|set-volume|set-mute`, else `pactl -f json list sinks|sources` + `get-default-sink|source`; verified on marko (3 devices, mute toggled twice on the non-default HDMI sink and read back). The palette pushes itself with `args: { volume: <row> }` for the 0/25/50/75/100 presets rather than a second palette.
- **bluetooth**: macOS lists from `system_profiler SPBluetoothDataType -json` (80 ms on hornet, and the only unprivileged source that has the device type and the battery levels, AirPods' left/right/case included; `blueutil --paired --format json` has neither) and connects/disconnects through `objc2-io-bluetooth` (`IOBluetoothDevice deviceWithAddressString:` then `openConnection`/`closeConnection`, which is what `blueutil` calls), so `blueutil` is not needed at all and there is no hint row for it. Verified read-only on hornet: 9 paired, 2 connected, kinds right (speaker, mouse, headphones, keyboard, gamepad); no device was connected or disconnected. Linux is `bluetoothctl devices Paired` (falling back to `paired-devices` for BlueZ before 5.65) plus `info` per device, every call under `timeout 5` (`connect` 20) and gated on `/sys/class/bluetooth` having an adapter, since without one `bluetoothctl` blocks rather than answering empty (v1's finding); on marko, which has none, that is the `no Bluetooth adapter` row.
- **wifi**: macOS is `networksetup` for the interface, the radio, the preferred list, join, forget; `scutil` (`State:/Network/Interface/en0/AirPort`) and `ipconfig getsummary` for the current link; `security find-generic-password -wa` for a password; `system_profiler SPAirPortDataType -json` for a scan, which takes 9.2 s on hornet, so the core caches a scan for 60 s and `scan` takes a mode (`cached` never runs it, `auto` reuses under the TTL, `fresh`): the palette lists Available from the cache on macOS with a Scan row, and with `auto` on Linux where `nmcli device wifi list` answers from NetworkManager's own cache. Linux is `nmcli -t` throughout, with the `\:` unescaping done once (`nmcli_fields`). Verified read-only on hornet (interface en0, powered, current link on channel 44 with the IP and WPA2_PSK, 90 preferred networks) and on marko (no Wi-Fi interface: the one row). Join, forget, power and the keychain prompt were not exercised on hornet (they change his network); the fixture tests cover the parsers and the extension's flows.
- **media**: macOS asks Spotify and Music over `osascript` only after `NSRunningApplication` says the app runs (a `tell application` to one that does not would launch it: the check is load-bearing), the script returning one line joined with `character id 31`; Spotify gives the artwork url and the `spotify:track:` url, Music neither. `nowplaying-cli` on PATH is one more row (`system`) for any other player, and `NowPlaying.system_wide` tells the palette whether to hint at installing it. Linux is `playerctl -l` then `metadata --format` per player with the same separator, units 1e-6 (MPRIS microseconds) against Spotify's 1e-3. Neither Spotify nor Music was running on hornet and no player on marko, so the live rows were not seen; the parsers are fixture-tested (Spotify, Music, playerctl and nowplaying-cli lines) and the running-check path was exercised (nothing launched).
- Tests: 22 core `#[test]`s on fixtures (`wpctl status`, `pactl` JSON, `system_profiler` Bluetooth and AirPort JSON, `bluetoothctl` list and info, `nmcli -t` lines, `ipconfig`/`scutil`/`networksetup` text, the player lines, `tool`), run on hornet and marko; 26 harness tests across `host/test/extensions/{audio,bluetooth,wifi,media}.test.ts` with canned core replies, run on both (the wifi and media tests assert per `process.platform` where the palettes differ). `core/examples/devices.rs` is the terminal check for a machine.

## Open for Cagdas: device palettes

- (The Wi-Fi names question moved to "Decided: Location Services for the Wi-Fi names" below.)
- Both stay `live` with no `ttl`: a Wi-Fi listing on macOS is CoreWLAN in-process plus `ipconfig` and `networksetup` (`wifi.status` 16-24 ms on hornet, down from 55-58 ms with four CLI calls), Bluetooth one `system_profiler` (80 ms), audio in-process, media one or two `osascript`s. If a show ever feels late, a `ttl` on wifi is the first knob.

## Raycast parity pass on the bundled extensions (2026-09-16)

- **apps**: a `Running` tag on an indexed palette is only as fresh as the last listing; `live` was rejected because it drops frecency ranking, which apps need most. Quit and Hide drop the scan cache and answer `keep`, so the tag follows those two at least. Quit is `osascript quit` (graceful) with a `SIGTERM` fallback, guarded by a `ps` check so a not-running app is never launched by the AppleScript. System Settings panes are a curated table of 35 ids: `/System/Library/ExtensionKit/Extensions` mixes panes with intents and widgets and has no display names. Localised app names are not read (binary `InfoPlist.strings`, a spawn per app). A pre-existing `!seen.add(...)` (always false) let same-named apps repeat across roots; fixed to `has` + `add`.
- **bookmarks**: Safari's plist is read as XML through `plutil -convert xml1` and a 50-line plist reader (`sources.ts`), not `-convert json`: JSON conversion refuses `<date>`/`<data>` values and the Reading List has dates. No npm dependency (a `bplist-parser` would have meant a lockfile per extension for one file). Firefox is `bun:sqlite` over a copy of `places.sqlite`, deleted after. `PAL_BOOKMARKS_HOME` points the profile roots at a temp home for the tests, like `PAL_FILES_BACKEND`. Duplicate urls are folded (first source wins) instead of the old NUL-ordinal ids.
- **emoji**: `live` + `ttl: 30` rather than indexed: a "Recently used" section needs the extension's own order, and an indexed palette re-lists only on start/`⌘R`/settings, so a just-used emoji would have hoisted its whole category above the recents until the next start (sections are contiguous in order of first appearance). The cost is one 1906-row listing per show at most every 30 s and no frecency boost on emoji at the root. `data.json` annotated from unicode-emoji-json (`group`, `skin_tone_support`) joined on the glyph; the names and keywords stayed emojilib's.
- **clipboard**: Links and Colors are text entries narrowed on this side (the core knows three kinds); "Delete all unpinned" deletes one by one over pages because the core's only bulk operation is `clear`. A colour's icon is the hex string, which the UI already draws as a tinted dot.
- **files**: recents come from `mdfind -attr kMDItemLastUsedDate` (sortable, unlike plain `mdfind`) or `recently-used.xbel`; `PAL_RECENT_XBEL` lets the tests feed the xbel path on macOS too. The empty query of the Files palette shows them in place of the hints (Raycast's empty state); `recent` is a second palette in the same extension sharing rows, actions and detail.
- **processes**: `:port` is a query mode inside the palette rather than a filter, so a port can be typed; `ss` first, else `lsof -F pcn` (field output, since the table form truncates commands). "Open in Activity Monitor" types the pid through System Events (there is no URL scheme); best effort.
- **ssh**: the section is the file the host came from; `ping -c 1` with `-W` in ms on macOS and seconds on Linux.
- **quicklinks/snippets**: import/export is a form with a path field; `{selection}` maps to the clipboard, `{cursor}` documented as unsupported (pal pastes whole).
- Tag colours are token names only (`amber` replaced processes' `orange`).
- Docs for this pass are an appended section of `docs/palettes.md` (the earlier per-palette sections were left as they were, by the brief); the tests are `host/test/extensions/*.test.ts`, every new pure module (`apps/desktop.ts`, `bookmarks/sources.ts`, `emoji/skin.ts`, `files/recent.ts`, `processes/ports.ts`) tested without a host.

## calendar: My Schedule (2026-09-16)

- `pal_core::calendar` (`core/calendar.{permission,request,open_settings,calendars,events,create,delete,open}` in `app/src-tauri/src/calendar.rs`, typed at the end of `sdk/src/api.ts`, palette `extensions/calendar`, docs in `docs/palettes.md`): EventKit in-process on macOS through `objc2-event-kit` (store, events, calendars, participants, `block2` for the access prompt's completion, the calendar colour off `CGColor`'s components), `khal` on Linux. Every call makes and drops its own `EKEventStore` on the bridge's blocking thread (EventKit: the store is thread-safe, fetched objects are not shared), which costs nothing visible: 96 ms for the calendars, 81 ms for a week of events on hornet, the store included. Times are unix ms on the wire; a recurring event's occurrences share the id, so `occurrence` (the start) names one for `delete` (fetched by a one-second predicate around it, `EKSpan::ThisEvent`) and `open` (`ical://ekevent/<stamp>/<id>?method=show&options=more`, the UTC stamp MeetingBar uses).
- Permission: `authorizationStatusForEntityType` answers without a prompt (`write_only` maps to `denied`: it cannot list); `request` calls `requestFullAccessToEventsWithCompletion` when the selector exists (macOS 14+) else the deprecated `requestAccessToEntityType`, and waits for the completion up to 8 s in the bridge (under the host's 10 s pick timeout). The app's `Info.plist` now carries `NSCalendarsFullAccessUsageDescription` and `NSCalendarsUsageDescription`: without the string TCC ends the process instead of asking. The permission is the extension's own row (`Grant calendar access`, then `Open System Settings` for a denial), not a slot in `permissions.rs`, whose watch/poll is Accessibility-shaped; folding a second permission into it is the next step if a third arrives. From the terminal the grant is the terminal's (verified once on hornet: `NotDetermined`, the prompt, `Granted`), the bundle asks for its own.
- Linux is `khal list -o --json <fields>` (one JSON array per day; khal 0.14 on marko), `printcalendars`, `new`. khal prints dates the way the user's `[locale]` says, so the patterns are *learnt* from `khal printformats`' samples for 2013-12-21 21:45 (`khal::Pattern`, parse and format both) rather than assumed; `%c` samples (`Sat Dec 21 21:45:00 2013`, the default without a `[locale]` section) read fine, but `khal new` cannot parse them back (its `datetimefstr` counts spaces in the format string, not the sample), so a timed create under `%c` is refused with the format to set; all-day goes through `%x`. `khal new` prints nothing even with `--json uid`, so `create` answers an empty id on Linux; `delete` and `open` are `Unavailable` (no khal command for either). Verified on marko against a temp vdir through a `khal` wrapper on PATH: list with `%d.%m.%Y` formats (occurrences, a two-day all-day event, the calendar colour word to hex), a timed create at the right instant, the `%c` refusal.
- The conference link is pure (`conference_url`): `https?://` links pulled from the url field, then the location, then the notes, the first from a known provider with a meeting-shaped path (Zoom `/j/ /my/ /w/ /s/ /wc/`, Meet, Teams `meetup-join`, Webex, Jitsi, Whereby, GoTo); Outlook safelinks unwrapped, `&amp;` unescaped, trailing punctuation dropped. The live check found the recurring SerpApi Zoom call with 2 attendees, the organizer and `accepted`.
- The palette (`calendar-schedule`, "My Schedule") is `live` with `ttl: 60`; rows from the local midnight to `days` ahead, ended ones dropped, `hide_declined` on by default, sections Today / Tomorrow / This week / Later, the first non-all-day upcoming row carries `now` or `in N min`; the calendar colour is the row icon (a hex draws a dot). Filters come from `calendars()` at load (static meta), narrowed by the `calendars` setting (titles or ids). Enter joins the call when there is one, else opens in Calendar (macOS) or copies the details (Linux). New event is a form with a day in words (`fri`, `next mon`, `20 sep`) and times (`2pm`, `1430`), parsed in `schedule.ts`; `calc`'s date parser was not imported (a bundled extension may not import a sibling's file, and its parser is not exported). Tests: 8 core `#[test]`s (the link parser, the UTC stamp, the khal patterns and fixtures), 18 harness tests in `host/test/extensions/calendar.test.ts` with canned `core/calendar.*` replies (108 expects, fixtures relative to the clock, `TZ=UTC`).
- Not built: accept/decline (no public EventKit API for the current user's participant status), a `calendars` palette toggling visibility (an extension cannot write its own settings), reminders. `app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs` was another agent's this round, so the bridge module is declared from the end of `bridge.rs` (`#[path = "calendar.rs"] mod calendar;`); moving that line into lib.rs's list is a one-line follow-up.

## Where a palette is described (2026-09-16)

- One rule, `docs/extensions.md` "Where a palette is described": `pal.json` holds the static, author-facing facts (`title`, `description`, `kind`, `ttl`, `keys`, `rank`, `settings`, the extension's `icon`), the code the behaviour (`list`/`pick`/`detail`/`view`, `filters`, `placeholder`, `showDetail`, `columns`, `live`, `input`). `kind` is stated by the manifest and implied by the code and must agree: `view` for a `view()` palette, `grid` for `view: "grid"`, `input` for `input: true`, `live` for `live: true` without `input`, else `list` (`kindOf` in `sdk/src/manifest.ts`, first match wins). `title` and `ttl` may sit on both sides; the manifest's is served and a differing code value is a warning (before this the code's `ttl` won and the manifest's title was never served).
- `checkPalettes(manifest, ext)` in `sdk/src/manifest.ts` (exported from `@zcag/pal`) is what the host runs on every load; it answers `{ metas, warnings }` and replaced `metas()` in `host.ts`. A code palette the manifest lacks is served from the code with a warning; a manifest palette the code lacks is not served (its meta is not sent) with a warning; a manifest with no `palettes` key is dynamic (`scripts`) and gets no cross-check. Every warning is a `[<ext>] manifest: palettes.<key>: ...` stderr line.
- **Wire addition, for the app side:** `extension/loaded` and each entry of `hello.extensions` now carry `warnings: string[]` next to `palettes` (empty when the manifest and the code agree). Additive: the Rust side already keeps `error?` per extension (`settings::register`, `Ext`); `warnings` is the same shape's non-fatal sibling, meant for the Settings window's extension row. Nothing reads it yet.
- `ManifestPalette` in `protocol.ts` now types `kind` (`PaletteKind`), `keys` (`ManifestKey[]`, `{ keys, title }`) and `rank` (number), which the bundled manifests already carried untyped; `app/src/ui/SettingsTypes.ts` mirrors `Manifest` by hand and does not have them yet.
- `defineExtension(manifest, ext)` is a second overload: `ExtensionFor<M>` maps the manifest's palette keys to the shape each `kind` demands, so a missing or extra key is a type error at the export (an inline `as const` manifest also pins the kind; a JSON import widens it to `string`, keys only). The bundled extensions still use `satisfies Extension`; nothing was rewritten to the new form.
- The sweep over the bundled extensions (`host/test/manifest.test.ts`, "the bundled extensions") found no drift today: every `kind` the content pass guessed matches the code, no title or ttl conflicts. Nine palettes carry a `ttl` in the code that the manifest does not repeat (docker x3 at 10, emoji 30, files/recent 60, home-assistant services/areas 3600, network 60, onepassword/items 300, services 10): served as before, no warning (a code value with no manifest counterpart), and the natural follow-up is to move each into its manifest and drop it from the code.

## 2048 and Wordle (2026-09-16)

Two more view palettes on the blackjack pattern (`extensions/{2048,wordle}/`: pure `game.ts`, `render.ts` for the tree, SVG tiles the extension draws, the state whole in storage after every pick; docs appended to `docs/palettes.md`, store fields and screenshots in `pal.json`, fixtures generated by `extensions/<game>/fixture.ts` into `app/src/gallery/shots/`). What was decided, and what the tree could not do:

- **Tiles are `image` nodes keyed by cell and content, with `exit: "none"`** (superseded the same day by the `tile` node and `move`, next section). A 2048 tile that slid is a new key in its destination cell and enters there; the old key leaves at once, since a fading exit would leave five children in a four-cell row for a frame. Merged tiles `flip`, slid ones `fade`, the spawned one fades with `delay: 2` so it lands after the rest. Wordle's submitted row keys each tile by row, column, letter and mark and flips with `delay: column`, which gives the staggered reveal; a typed letter fades in, a deleted one goes at once. Keying by tile id alone was rejected: a tile moving within its row would keep its DOM and jump without any transition, while one changing rows would animate, so moves would look different by direction.
- **Six rows in Wordle, side by side with the keyboard.** The brief said five rows; the original has six and a five-guess Wordle plays as a different game (the share text's `x/6` is what people recognise). Six rows of 48 px stack to 308 px, which with a keyboard under them does not fit the 358 px body, so the keyboard, the stats and the message sit to the right of the grid instead. If five was meant, `ROWS` in `game.ts` is the one constant.
- **Letters as bare-key actions.** The grammar (`app/src/ui/keys.ts` `resolve`) routes any unmodified printable key to `{ type: "key" }`, which a view with `keys: "actions"` matches against `Action.shortcut`, so a-z work as they are (by `e.code`, so a shifted or caps-locked letter is the same key, and non-QWERTY layouts go by physical key). `n` and `c` are letters mid-game, so New game rides on `cmd+n` there and on a bare `n` once the game is over; copy is `c` (and Enter, as the first action) once the game is over. The 26 letter actions are listed in ⌘K (last, titled `Type A`), since there is no way to hide an action from the panel.
- **Word lists** are built by `extensions/wordle/build.ts` from public domain sources and committed: answers from 12dicts' `3esl` + `6of12` (Alan Beale, released to the public domain) with plurals dropped by a stem check and a short block list, 2551 words; allowed from every public domain 12dicts list plus ENABLE, 8878 words; 69 KB together, inlined by `bun build` through the `*.txt` text loader. The daily is `ANSWERS[(day * 1103) mod n]`, a permutation since 1103 and 2551 are coprime (tested), so the file's order is load-bearing and the build script says so. The original's lists were not used: the NYT's curation is not public domain, and the numbers would not have matched the original's puzzle of the day anyway.
- **Undo is one move** (a `prev` snapshot on the state), allowed after game over as a rescue, and taking a win back restores `won` from the snapshot. Enter mid-game is New game with a confirm, so the footer has something sensible and a stray Enter costs nothing; at 2048 Enter is Keep going, at game over New game without the question.
- Tests: `host/test/extensions/2048.test.ts` (21: line and board slides with the no-double-merge rule, spawn odds at the boundaries and over 5000 draws, undo, the won and over phases, the tree's keys and transitions, the wire with the undo setting) and `wordle.test.ts` (21: marks with duplicates, hard mode, the lists and the daily permutation, the day index, typing and submitting, stats and the streak, sync on a new day, share text, the tree, the wire with `daily` and `hard_mode`). Screenshots through `app/scripts/shots.mjs` from the fixtures.

## Decided: what the view tree lacked for the games, and what landed (2026-09-16)

The eight gaps the games worked around were each closed in the tree's API, and the three renders rewritten on it; none of the workarounds remain. In order:

- **`backspace` and `delete` reach a view.** `resolve` in `app/src/ui/keys.ts` returns `{ type: "key", key: "backspace" | "delete" }` for the unmodified keys, so `shortcut: "backspace"` binds like `space`; `Kbd` already drew ⌫. Held in a field the key keeps its native repeat (the query clears), held on a view it fires once, the same as letters (`useKeys`, the repeat branch; before this a held letter in the search input typed once, a quirk fixed in passing). Wordle's Delete is on `backspace` alone; the `delete-alt` on `left` is gone.
- **`Action.shortcut` is a string or a list.** `["up", "k"]`: `shortcutsOf` / `hasShortcut` (keys.ts, exported from `@zcag/pal` too) match any; the panel draws the first as the key and the rest faintly before it (`.pal-action__alt`, 45%); `checkView` refuses an empty list or a non-string in it. 2048's four moves are four actions with two keys each, not eight; `game.ts` lost its `h`/`j`/`k`/`l` pseudo-actions.
- **`Action.hidden: true` routes and is never listed.** Not in ⌘K (`ActionPanel` filters), not in the footer, and not one of the Enter / ⌘Enter pair, which are the first two *listed* actions (`listed` in Launcher.tsx). `checkView` refuses a hidden action without a shortcut, since nothing could reach it. Wordle's 26 letters are hidden; ⌘K lists Submit and Delete (and New game when it is on offer). Chosen over a `View.keys` map because the action shape already carries the key and the confirm; a second table would have duplicated it.
- **Transitions: three more slides, `pop`, and `move`.** `enter` gained `slide-down`, `slide-left`, `slide-right` (the node arrives from that side, `--pal-motion-slide`, 6 px, which `slide-up` now reads too) and `pop` (a scale from 0.8 with a 6% overshoot, `--pal-motion-pop`); each has a matching exit. `move: true` is a FLIP in `View.tsx` (`useMove`): the `View` keeps a per-tree registry of moving elements; when a new tree arrives it measures every registered element's box in render, before the commit (transform included, so a slide interrupted mid-flight continues from where it is on screen); after the commit each mover's layout effect measures its new box and plays a `translate` from the old one over `--pal-motion-move` with the Web Animations API. It works across parents: a node that unmounts in one cell and mounts in another is one key to the registry, the stack it left draws no exit for it (`Children` consults the tree's moving keys), and the re-mounted node has its `enter` switched off so it does not pop while sliding. A move key must be unique in the whole tree (`checkView`). Measuring and playing happen once per tree, not per render: the first cut ran the effect on every commit and the `busy` flag flipping cancelled the slide it had just started, which is why a probe showed `animate` called and no motion. `reduced()` (presence.tsx, now exported) skips the play; the tokens are zero under reduced motion anyway.
- **A `tile` node.** `{ type: "tile", width, height, text?, sub?, color?, fill? }`: a rounded box drawn with the tokens. `color` is the tag palette plus `neutral` (default) and `accent`; `fill` is `solid` (the colour as background, the panel background as ink, which reads on both themes since the tag colours are dark on light and light on dark), `soft` (the tint, the colour as ink; default) or `outline` (a 1.5 px inset hairline). Solid neutral is paper: the elevated surface with a hairline and the bevel, what a 2 tile and a typed letter want. The type is set inline (`tileType`): the size scales with the box and shrinks with the text's length (a 64 px tile shows "2" at 28 px and "2048" at 21), weight 800 from 20 px, 700 from 14, tabular figures throughout. Under 44 px the box takes the control radius, so a keyboard key is not a pebble. The SVG tile modules of both games (`tiles.ts`) are deleted; 2048's ramp climbs in weight only (`lookOf` in render.ts): paper for 2, the neutral tint for 4, then solid tiles walking the hues warm to cool (amber, red, pink, violet, blue, teal, green), the grey chip at 1024, the accent at 2048 and past it. A first cut alternated soft and solid per hue and read as a sawtooth (512 in the pink tint looked weaker than 256 in solid violet), so no two fills alternate; the palette has no orange, so 16 is red rather than a second amber next to 8.
- **`stack.surface` and `radius`.** `sunken` is `--pal-bg-sunken`, `elevated` is `--pal-bg-elevated` with a hairline and the bevel; `radius` is the tile radius. 2048's and Wordle's boards sit in a sunken well with `padding: 2`, blackjack's table in one with `padding: 3` (the status row stays outside), Wordle's stats on an elevated card.
- **`text.width` / `minWidth` / `align`, `progress.color`.** A text with a width is a column: `flex: none`, clipped with an ellipsis instead of wrapping, `align` inside it. Wordle's distribution is a label of 8 px, a 96 px bar (`value` is the share of the fullest row, `color: "grey"` except the winning row, which keeps the accent) and a count of 20 px right-aligned, in place of the `progress` of computed width.
- **A queue of keys while a pick is in flight.** `viewCommand` in Launcher.tsx is the one entry for a view level's keys (Enter, ⌘Enter, a combo, a bare key); while `busy` it pushes the command onto a FIFO of four and an effect drains one per settled tree, resolving the key against the *new* actions, so a "hit" typed a beat early lands on the hand it was meant for or on nothing. A queued key no action carries is dropped and the next tried. The queue is emptied when the level changes (`levelKey`: depth and palette). A form level still drops a second Enter.

The games on the new API: 2048's cells are stacks (`cell0..15`) each holding one tile, an outline for an empty cell or the tile keyed `t<id>` with `move`; a slid tile glides across the board (rows too), a merged one pops in its cell one step after the slide starts, the spawned one two steps. Wordle's board tiles and keyboard are `tile` nodes (outline empty, paper typed with `pop`, green / amber / grey marks with the staggered `flip`), the letters hidden actions, Backspace routed. Blackjack's player cards are keyed by deal and card (`p-<card>[#n]-<deal>`, a repeated card numbered) with `move`, so a split's second card glides to its new hand; the dealer's keep their slot keys. A `fixture.ts` for blackjack now generates its shots fixture like the other two (it reproduces the states the hand-made JSON had).

Tests: `app/src/ui/__tests__/view.test.tsx` (tile, surfaces, text width, progress colour, the entrances), `move.test.tsx` (the FLIP across cells with a stubbed layout, a new key entering instead, reduced motion), `keys.test.tsx` (Backspace and Delete, `shortcutsOf`, the held-key rule), `app/src/__tests__/view-level.test.tsx` (Backspace and either shortcut routing to picks, hidden out of the panel and footer with the alternative drawn faintly, the queue of four run against the replies, dropped on leaving); `host/test/view.test.ts` (shortcut lists, hidden without a key, every entrance and exit, `move` without a key or with a key used twice, tile / text / progress / stack fields). The game tests were updated to the new trees. The tokens' brief (`app/design/build.py`) was rebuilt; its meta lacked two settings tokens too, added.

Not done, deliberately: the merged tile's two sources vanish at once and the merged tile pops in place, rather than the sources sliding under it as in the original; that would need the game to report which tiles merged into which, and the pop reads well enough. The `--pal-motion-*` names are the first tokens outside the `--pal-dur-*` / `--pal-ease*` pair; they are what the view's motion reads, the shell's own motion keeps the durations.

## Colours: the picker, the sampler, and what the view tree gained for it (2026-09-16)

`extensions/colors` went from two palettes (a 700-swatch grid and a converter) to the extension people would install pal for; Raycast's Color Picker and Digital Color Meter were the behaviour references, the look is the tokens'. What landed, and what it took from the shell:

- **Four palettes over one maths.** `picker` (a view level: the swatch on a sunken well over a hue strip and a saturation/lightness plane, every notation as copyable rows, the nearest Tailwind and Material tokens, contrast on white / black / the previous colour with the WCAG badge, nine tints, nine shades and five harmonies as tiles), `colors` (995 swatches: CSS, Tailwind 3.4, Material 3 tonal palettes generated from the baseline seed with Google's material-color-utilities at build time, Material 2014, Apple light and dark, Catppuccin, Rosé Pine, Nord, Solarized, pal's tokens; a filter per set; the detail says where the token is used), `history` (live, so the root's rows follow every pick and copy: each colour with when and where from, `Pick Colour from Screen` as its first row and so at the root), `convert` (Enter now opens the picker). State in `state.ts` (pure), the tree in `render.ts`, the rows in `rows.ts`, the sets in `sets.ts`; `color.ts` gained CIE Lab against D50 with the CSS Color 4 matrices, display-p3 through XYZ, OKLab strings, gamut-aware OKLCH steps (chroma reduced by a binary search rather than channels clipped), tints, shades, harmonies and the formats. The maths is checked against the spec's reference points (red is `lab(54.29 80.8 69.89)`, `lab(29.2345% 39.3825 20.0664)` is `#7d2329`).
- **The keys.** `←`/`→` hue, `↑`/`↓` lightness, `-`/`+` saturation or chroma, `⇧` big steps, `m` HSL/OKLCH, `⇥` walks the tile rows (arrows along, Enter takes the tile), a digit or `#` opens the text field, `c`/`⌘C` copy, `p` picks from the screen, `h`/`n` the other palettes, `u` undo, `r` random. Saturation has no big step: `⇧-` is `_` and `⇧=` is already `+`, and a third key pair was not worth the table.
- **`core/color.sample`** (`app/src-tauri/src/color.rs`, `color.sample()` in the SDK): hides the panel on the main thread, then `NSColorSampler` (`showSamplerWithSelectionHandler:`, run and answered on the main thread, the colour converted with `colorUsingColorSpace:` sRGB; no permission) or, on Linux, the `Screenshot.PickColor` portal over zbus's blocking API with the `Response` signal subscribed on the request path before the call (`request_path` mangles the unique name; the signal stream has no timeout of its own, so a thread feeds a channel). The bridge thread waits up to 120 s; the SDK call carries the same timeout (`runtime.call` grew an `opts.timeout`, the host bridge's 5 s stays the default). Cancel is `null`. Compiles clean on marko (`cargo check -p pal` over a copy); the portal path itself is unverified on Linux.
- **Why `effects.run` exists.** A pick is bounded by the host's 10 s `REQUEST_TIMEOUT`, and a person aiming a loupe takes longer, so the picker's `p` returns `{ hide: true }` at once and samples in the background; the colour then has to reach the panel from outside a pick. `core/effects.run { effect }` runs an `Effect` envelope as `effects::apply` would (copy with the HUD, open, paste, focus, layout, hud) plus `push`, which is `show_in(app, Some("ext/palette"))` (the page opens the palette afresh, so the picker's `view()` draws the picked colour); the webview-side effects (`toast`, `keep`, `view`, `form`, `show`) are refused with the reason. The root row does `{ copy, hud: "Copied #…" }` the same way. The extension's `view()` awaits an in-flight sample before reading storage, so a panel brought back early still draws the new colour; the sample task itself reads storage without that wait (the first cut deadlocked on its own promise, caught by the harness test).
- **The view tree gained three things.** `tile.color` takes a hex colour (`HexColor`, `HEX_COLOR` in view.ts): the box is that colour whatever the fill, the ink black or white by WCAG luminance (`inkOn`, threshold 0.179 where the two ratios meet), a checker under a translucent one, a hairline in it for `outline`. A **`gradient` node** (a hex `fill` under `layers` of CSS linear gradients whose hex stops may carry alpha, in paint order so a later layer composites over an earlier one, a `marker` ring at fractions of the box drawn white over a dark halo so it reads on any colour) draws the hue strip (seven pure hues) and the classic saturation/value plane exactly (`fill` the pure hue, `#ffffff` to `#ffffff00` rightwards, `#000000` to `#00000000` upwards) with no image at all; the first cut built the plane from discrete opaque stops and read as painted patches. In HSL mode the marker is the colour's HSV coordinates (the arrows still move HSL); in OKLCH mode the same plane stands in with the hue's most chromatic sRGB colour as the fill and the marker at the chroma as a share of the gamut's width at that lightness, smooth rather than exact. The three picker screenshots stay true colour (`raw: true` in the fixture, a per-shot switch added to `shots.mjs`): 256 colours turn a plane into bands, and the wallpaper eats the palette. **`View.input`** turns the search row into a text field: `submit`/`cancel` name actions, Enter picks the submit with `ctx.values.input`, Escape the cancel (or pops), a tree without `input` closes it (query cleared, focus back on the view's document via a `rootRef` on `View`), a `value` that differs from the previous tree's replaces the text, keys that queued before the field opened are typed into it (`viewCommand`'s `key` branch while `spec.input` is set). Chosen over a form: the field sits where the query would and the picker keeps drawing underneath.
- **The grammar gained two things.** A digit key that produced a symbol (`#` on `⇧3`) is named by the symbol, letters stay by physical key (`keyName`); a shifted arrow is the combo `shift+up` (`repeats`, so held it repeats like a move), a view without an action for it runs the plain arrow's, and a list level moves the cursor as before (`shiftedArrow`). Tab reaches a view as the bare key `tab` (`shift+tab` as a combo) where the level has no filter dropdown.
- **Screenshots**: `fixture.ts` renders rigged states (a history behind the colour, the shades row focused, the field open with `#ff88`) into `app/src/gallery/shots/colors.json`; three fixture palettes share the title so the crumb reads the same; the sets shot tabs six times to Catppuccin through `byFilter`. Checked in both themes (a scratch dark run through the same gallery page).
- Tests: 35 in `host/test/extensions/colors.test.ts` (379 expects: the maths against the reference points, the sets' counts and tokens, the state's moves and history, the tree and the actions, the rows, and the wire with the sample and the effect faked), 3 host `checkView` additions, app vitest `view-input.test.tsx` (the field's open, type, submit, cancel, the shifted arrows) plus tile and gradient renders and the grammar cases, 4 cargo tests in `color.rs` (component rounding, the JSON shape, the portal path, the portal result mapping).

## Findings: polish pass over the bundled extensions (2026-09-16)

Every bundled extension (35) was read against the brief, `docs/keyboard.md` and `pal-site/STORE-FIELDS.md`, each screenshot looked at, and what was off fixed in the extension, its fixture (`app/src/gallery/shots/<name>.json`) and its host test; changed sets were re-rendered with `app/scripts/shots.mjs` and looked at again. The same faults repeated across the set, so the rules they settled: no row or popover menu row without an icon (the initial fallback was live in media's transport rows and github's popover, and thin Unicode symbols of mixed weight stood in for glyphs in clipboard, files, system, snippets, quicklinks, onepassword, otp, unicode, window-management, windows, scripts); row glyphs come from the Nerd Font's `md-` set (Octicons in github), so a palette has one weight; action titles are sentence case, palette titles Title Case, and `store.actions` repeats the code's titles; `keys` in `pal.json` lists every shortcut the code declares and never a shell key (`cmd+i`, `cmd+r`, `cmd+k`, `cmd+shift+b`, `cmd+backspace` were all found bound by extensions; `cmd+backspace` cannot even reach an action, the grammar takes it first, so timer's Stop never fired); every `store.tagline` is under 60 characters; `store.permissions` is the store's "needs" and names the binary or token an extension cannot run without (`docker`, `op`, `make`, `timer`, `nmcli`, `token`); every setting has a description or a checkbox text; placeholders say what to type; hint rows end without a period and name the fix. The full sweep (`host/test/extensions`, `host/test/manifest.test.ts`): 468 pass, 1 platform skip; `tsc` over the host tree is clean. Left alone on purpose: row names that are names (`Left Half`, `Turn Wi-Fi Off`), the games' trees, `{ image }` icons not brightening on the selected row (a renderer matter), and the manifests some passes rewrote through `json.dump`, which expanded one-line arrays.

## Decided: icon tiles (2026-09-16)

Every extension and most rows were a grey Nerd Font glyph, drab next to the real app icons and emoji. Raycast is the reference: every extension a coloured rounded square with a white mark, rows carrying the real thing where one exists, glyphs kept for actions and states. What landed:

- **The tile kind** (`sdk/src/icon.ts`, re-exported from `@zcag/pal`; `Icon.tsx` and the new `app/src/ui/icons.css`): `{ tile: { glyph | svg, bg } }`, `tile(bg, mark)` to write it. A rounded square in one of twelve brand colours with the mark white: one Nerd Font glyph at 62% of the side, or SVG path data (`d` only, under 400 bytes, `checkIcon` refuses markup) in a 16 by 16 box at 66%. The side follows the box (24 row, 20 crumb and footer, 32 grid cell, 56 settings pane, 48 in the Extensions hero well), the radius a quarter of it; a hairline inset ring (`--pal-tile-ring`) keeps ink a square on the dark panel. Selection changes nothing on a tile, like an app icon. `protocol.ts` is another pass's file, so `tile()` returns the object typed as the string `PaletteBase.icon` takes and `tinted()` as `Icon`; the three-line widening of `Icon`, `PaletteBase.icon` and `Manifest.icon` to admit `TileIcon | TintedIcon` is open for whoever merges that file, after which the casts go. Rust: `PaletteMeta.icon` is `Option<Value>` now (registry.rs), opaque to the core.
- **The brand palette** (`--pal-brand-*` in tokens.css, light and dark values, white readable on every one): red, orange, amber, green, teal, cyan, blue, indigo, violet, pink, slate, ink. The per-extension table:

| tile | extensions | mark |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ink | github | nf-oct-mark_github |
| blue | onepassword, bluetooth, wifi | md-shield_key, md-bluetooth, md-wifi |
| teal | home-assistant, ssh | md-home_assistant, md-server |
| green | media, otp, wordle | md-music, md-message_text, an SVG 3 by 3 grid |
| amber | timer, 2048, emoji | md-timer_outline, an SVG 2 by 2 grid, md-emoticon_happy |
| violet | clipboard, snippets, audio | md-clipboard_text, md-content_cut, md-volume_high |
| orange | bookmarks, quicklinks, make | md-bookmark, md-link_variant, md-hammer |
| cyan | docker, network, browser-tabs | md-docker, md-lan, md-tab |
| indigo | calc, window-management | md-calculator, md-dock_left |
| pink | icons, unicode | md-shape, md-omega |
| red | calendar, blackjack | md-calendar, md-cards_playing |
| slate | apps, windows, files, processes, services, system, scripts | md-apps, md-dock_window, md-folder, md-memory, md-cog, md-power, md-console |

  `colors` keeps its hex dot for now: `extensions/colors/**` belonged to another pass while this landed; it wants `tile("indigo", md-palette)` or a swatch SVG.
- **Palettes wear the manifest's tile.** `paletteMeta` falls back to the manifest's `icon` when the code's palette has none, so the per-palette `icon:` lines that only repeated the extension's glyph are gone from the code (github's five, home-assistant's three, files' two, and so on); a palette that wants its own still sets one (`scripts` passes a table's own `icon`/`icon_utf`/`icon_xdg` through and otherwise inherits). The root, the crumb, the footer, the Palettes and Extensions pages and the Overview all draw the tile through `iconOf`.
- **Rows: real assets first, then the palette's colour.** App icons, favicons, avatars, artwork, swatches and diagrams as before. A plain Nerd Font glyph row takes its palette's tile colour as a tint (`toItem`, `tintedBy`; `{ kind: "glyph", tint }`), the favicon globe fallback too, so an extension's rows are marked in its colour rather than `--pal-fg-muted`; the tint holds on the selected row. Not tinted: letters (the initial fallback), text glyphs, emoji, hex-coloured glyphs, and any glyph in a catalog or grid palette (there the glyphs are the content: the icons palette stayed pink all over until this rule). Ink-tinted glyphs draw in `--pal-fg` (ink vanishes on the dark panel). `{ glyph, color }` / `tinted(glyph, color)` is a row's own colour: github's PR and issue rows now carry the state's octicon in its colour (open green, merged violet, closed red, draft slate) instead of a dot. calc's `=` became md-equal so it tints; window-management's layout diagrams are drawn in indigo (a data url cannot follow the theme, so a value between the two).
- **Checks and tests.** `checkPalettes` runs `checkIcon` over the manifest's and every palette's icon: a tile off the palette, both or neither mark, a multi-character glyph, an svg over 400 bytes or with markup, a tint colour that is neither a brand nor a hex, each a warning; a bad palette icon is dropped from its meta, a bad manifest icon is not inherited. Tests: `app/src/ui/__tests__/icon.test.tsx` (the renderer at every size and both marks, `iconOf` on every form, the tint rule and its exclusions), `host/test/manifest.test.ts` (the check and the fallback), the meta assertions in twenty extension tests updated to the tiles. Gallery: an "Icon tiles" section reads the manifests live (both themes, rows, crumb, footer, grid cell, hero); `?gallery&shot=<ext>&theme=dark` renders a fixture in dark for looking. Every store screenshot re-rendered and looked at, light and a dark sample.
- **Rules.** One tile per extension, the colour it is known by, slate for the neutral family; a glyph mark from the `md-` set (Octicons for github), an SVG only when no glyph says it (the two game grids). Rows: the real asset, else a glyph in the palette's colour; a state in its own colour; emoji only as content. Section headers, accessories and tags stay text. No emoji next to tiles in one list unless the emoji is the row's content (2048, emoji and blackjack gave theirs up for tiles; wordle's green square too).

## Decided: links (2026-09-16)

Spec `docs/design/links.md`, user doc `docs/links.md`, authors' section in `docs/extensions.md`, `docs/cli.md` updated, `STORE-FIELDS.md` in pal-site tells the site how to render `links`. Built while the surface is small, so every later feature lands with a link.

- **One grammar**: `pal://<route>[/<part>...][?k=v]`, parts percent-decoded one by one (an encoded slash stays inside an id), `+` a space, a repeated key an array, `args` percent-encoded JSON. The first part is a reserved route or an extension's name (`pal://timer/start?duration=25m`); the table order settles a clash (an extension named `open` cannot be linked, documented). `deeplink.rs` is table-driven: `ROUTES: &[Spec { pattern, doc, build }]` with `{name}` parts and a `{spec...}` rest, matched top to bottom, one example per row in the tests (`every_route_in_the_table_parses_to_its_route` fails when a row lacks one); `pal link --list` prints the table.
- **Every link has a CLI twin**: `pal link URL` plus `open run form copy paste hud toast confetti command call` (`Cmd::link()` spells the url, the tests round-trip each through the parser). A twin is handed over as its own argv, the instance spells the link again and runs it **trusted**: no confirm card, the shell is the user's hand. A link the grammar refuses never leaves the CLI process (reason on stderr, exit 2); a handed-over link exits 0 and the outcome is the HUD's (no reply channel over single-instance; documented rather than faked).
- **What is addressable**: the app (`show hide toggle settings[/page] reload quit`, pal's own rows as `commands/<id>` with `run/pal/commands/<id>` kept as an alias), a palette (`open/<ext>/<palette>?q=&filter=`), an item (`run/.../<id>?action=&args=`), a form (`form/.../<id>?field=value`: the same pick, its `form` answer prefilled by `prefill` and shown in the panel, nothing submitted), effects from outside (`copy paste open?url= hud toast confetti`), the store (`install update remove`), a bar item (`bar/<ext>/<id>[?action=]`), extension routes. `pal://extensions` stays as the old spelling; nothing was renamed.
- **A link's answer goes where it belongs** (`deliver`): a `toast` is the HUD's line with the panel down; `push`/`show`/`view`/`form` show the panel and hand the page `pal://deeplink { effect, item }`, which `Launcher.apply` runs through the same `applyEffect` a pick's answer takes (extracted from `pickItem`). `pal://toast` while the panel is up is a toast in it, else the HUD. `pal://confetti` is `hud::celebrate`: the HUD payload grows `celebrate`, the `Hud` component bursts 24 CSS particles (`.pal-hud__confetti`, nth-child over the tag palette, one keyframe, reduced motion zeroes the hold so they never fly). Cheap enough to keep.
- **What asks**: `general.deeplink_confirm` is `pal_core::config::Confirm`, `true`/`false` or a list of extension names trusted without the card (`Confirm::asks(extension)`; the extension is the path's, a bar key's first part). `run`, `form`, a bar action, an extension route obey it; `paste` and `open?url=` (no extension, a page must not type into the front app or launch a file) follow the boolean; `install update remove` always ask; a manifest route with `confirm: true` always asks (system's `run`); the CLI never. Schema regenerated (`cargo run -p pal-core --example schema`).
- **Extension routes**: `pal.json` `links.<route>: { description, confirm?, params: { <name>: { description, required?, type } } }` (`type` one of `string number boolean json string[]`), code `Extension.link(route, params)` answering an Effect. Host method `link`: the manifest gates the route (undeclared = `no route x/y`), `checkLinkParams` (sdk/manifest.ts) refuses a missing required param naming it and coerces by type, `checkLinkEffect` refuses `keep show view form` (the `effects.run` set), then `checkEffect`. `checkLinks(manifest, ext)` runs on every load next to `checkPalettes` and its warnings ride the same `warnings` (a block without a handler, a handler without a block, a bad route name, a bad type). The core reads the manifest's `links.<route>` from `settings::manifest_of` for the card's text and `confirm`. Routes shipped: `quicklinks/open?name=&query=`, `snippets/paste?name=&copy=`, `window-management/layout?name=`, `system/run?id=` (`confirm: true`), `timer/start?duration=&name=&ring=`, `clipboard/copy?index=`. `calendar/join-next` and `colors/pick` are specified in `docs/design/links.md` and left for those extensions' agents (their directories were another agent's this round).
- **Copy deep link** (⌘⇧C, section Link): a shell action on every row (`run/...`, a palette row's `open/...`, a pal row's `commands/<id>`, a drill-in row with `?args=`), inside a palette or view with nothing under the cursor (`open/...?q=`), on a form (`form/...?action=`, the form level now remembers the action that opened it), on a bar menu row (`bar/<key>?action=`). `app/src/links.ts` `linkFor(level, item, query)` is pure and tested; the page writes `pal://` and `link_copy` (a Tauri command) hides the panel, swaps in the bundle's scheme (`plugins.deep-link.desktop.schemes[0]`) and copies with the HUD saying `Copied pal://...`. Not on the settings pages: `app/src/ui/Settings*.tsx` were another agent's this round; the pages are addressable (`pal://settings/<page>`) and the action is one `onLink` call away.
- **Arrival, and a deadlock found live**: the deep-link plugin delivers a link inside a tauri event listener on the main thread (under the listeners' lock); `pal://settings/bar` from `open` built the settings window inline there, which emits events of its own, and hung the instance in `_pthread_mutex_firstfit_lock_slow` (the old code had the same inline path). `enqueue` now hops every link to the async runtime and each route posts back to the main thread; the CLI twins take the same path.
- Verified on hornet with a scratch bundle (`npx tauri build --bundles app --config '{identifier: io.cagdas.pal.scratch.links, schemes: [palscratch], createUpdaterArtifacts: false}'`, the same JSON in `TAURI_CONFIG` for the codegen; copied to `~/Applications/pal-scratch-links.app` and `lsregister -f`, since a second `pal` handler would race LaunchServices with the daily app; the target-dir copy unregistered), own config and `XDG_DATA_HOME`, `[bar] target = "off"`, the timer extension pointed at a stand-in CLI so no real timer fired, the wifi palette off (its list asks for Location on show).
