Seen end to end, from `open -g` and from the CLI twins: `open/emoji/emoji?q=smile` (the query typed), `run/quicklinks/quicklinks/<url>?args={"link":..}&action=copy` (the filled url on the clipboard), `commands/version` (the version copied), `form/quicklinks/quicklinks/create?action=create&name=Demo&url=...` (the form up, both fields filled), `copy?text=` ("Copied"), `hud?text=`, `toast?title=&message=` with the panel up (the toast in it), `confetti?text=Shipped` (the burst, after two rounds: a `span` was clipped by the HUD's ellipsis rule, and a tall burst left the 72 px window, so it is a low arc now), `settings/bar` (the window on Bar, after the deadlock fix), `snippets/paste?name=sig&copy=1`, `timer/start?duration=25m&name=tea` ("Timer started: started tea for 25m" from the stand-in), `window-management/layout?name=restore` ("Restore: nothing to restore: pal has not moved that window", through the core's layout effect), the refusals (`quicklinks/open?name=nope`, `timer/start` without duration: `pal: timer/start: duration is required`), the cards ("“Start a timer” from a link?", "Install wordle from a link?"; the install card read as declined when a newer card replaced it, since a keypress could not be sent while he was at the keyboard), the allowlist (`deeplink_confirm = ["timer", ...]` live-reloaded: no card after it), ⌘⇧C in the quicklinks palette (`palscratch://run/quicklinks/quicklinks/create` on the clipboard: the scheme swapped), and `pal link 'palscratch://nope'` exiting 2 with `unknown route "nope"`. His clipboard was empty before and was cleared after; the daily instance was not touched. Caveat seen: a link that shows the panel takes the keyboard from whatever he was typing in (that is what showing the panel means); scripts that only want the HUD should use `open -g`.

## Findings: performance pass (2026-09-16)

Measured on hornet with a scratch release build (`TAURI_CONFIG` identifier
`io.cagdas.pal.scratch`, own `PAL_CONFIG` and `XDG_DATA_HOME`, `[bar]
target = "off"`, no v1 scripts config), the 36 bundled extensions from the
staged resource tree (`scripts/build-extensions.sh`), warm index cache, the
panel never shown unless the row says so. Stderr lines with `since start`
(`mark`, `profile`, `cache loaded`, `host spawn`, `host ready`, the page's
new `first paint` mark in `Launcher.tsx`) are the clock; `footprint` for
memory (RSS on macOS counts the shared WebKit frameworks and freed heap);
`ps cputime` over 60 s for idle CPU; the core's `corpus_heads` ignored test
(`PAL_CORPUS=<profile>/index`, now printing the restore cost, the
per-keystroke time and the reply's bytes per source) for the index numbers.

| What | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cold start (warm cache) to cache restored | 336 ms (read+parse 35 ms) | 190-204 ms (14 ms) |
| Cold start to first paint | ~580 ms (first query answered at 531 ms, no mark) | 458-513 ms (`first paint` mark) |
| Cold start to `host/ready` | ~590 ms (spawn 355 + 236) | 404-416 ms; 1071 ms on the first run after a build (signature check) |
| `pal show` to paint, after the first show | 2.1-3.3 ms | 1.3-2.2 ms (unchanged) |
| Idle after 2 min, app: RSS / footprint / peak | 353 MB / 99 MB / 237 MB | 171 MB / 62 MB / 93 MB |
| Idle after 2 min, `pal-bun` | 184 MB / 65 MB / 124 MB | 161 MB / 68 MB / 107 MB |
| Idle CPU over 60 s, app / host | 0.02% / 0.2% (bar renders every 10 s with the target off) | 0.02-0.03% / 0.02-0.1%, no renders (daily instance, sketchybar target: 0.02% / 0.02%) |
| `icons/icons` listing (10995 rows) | 3363 KB, 22 ms in the host, 108 ms round trip, cache file 3.47 MB | 1570 KB, 16 ms, 24-25 ms, 1.63 MB |
| `unicode/unicode` (1795) | 767 KB | 250 KB |
| `colors/colors` (700) | 370 KB | 239 KB |
| `apps/apps` (132) | 61 KB | 36 KB |
| `make/make` (69), `icons/freedesktop` (114) | 25 KB, 27 KB | 11 KB, 13 KB |
| `emoji/emoji` (1906), `bookmarks/bookmarks` (321) | 626 KB, 197 KB | unchanged (see below) |
| All 35 default listings | 5654 KB | 3169 KB |
| Index restore (46 files, `corpus_heads`) | 5.9 MB in 61 ms (parse 44, fill 4.6) | 3.4 MB in 23 ms (parse 15, fill 4.2) |
| Per keystroke, core, 16.5k items: `c` / `chr` / `git` / `ha` / `""` | 1.13 / 0.85 / 0.90 / 1.18 / 0.10 ms | 1.08 / 0.83 / 0.89 / 1.17 / 0.10 ms (unchanged; fixture `c` 0.70, `chrome` 0.32, `ha` 0.58) |
| Root reply on the wire: `c` / `chr` / `git` / `""` | 92.7 / 59.1 / 56.0 / 96.8 KB | 87.0 / 54.0 / 51.3 / 71.6 KB |

What was out of line, and the fix:

- **Per-row actions were half of every catalog listing.** Every icons row
  carried the same four `{ id, title }` objects (1.8 MB of 3.4 MB), unicode
  five (517 of 767 KB), colors four, make four, apps six. Now a palette
  declares `actions` once (`PaletteBase.actions` in `sdk/src/protocol.ts`,
  carried in `PaletteMeta` by `paletteMeta` in `sdk/src/manifest.ts`,
  opaque `actions: Option<Value>` on the core's `PaletteMeta` in
  `registry.rs`, so it rides in the cache and `SourceView`), and a row
  without its own gets them in the UI (`toItem` in `app/src/items.ts`,
  which now takes a `PaletteInfo { title, detail, actions }` instead of two
  positional args; `[]` on a row still means inert). icons, unicode, colors,
  make and apps (macOS rows: the running ones and those without a bundle id
  still say their own) moved. emoji stays per row: its order follows
  `paste_by_default`, and a meta is sent once at load. bookmarks stays: the
  browser rows each add "Open in <browser>". github rows are ~1.1-1.2 KB
  each (inline `detail` 460 B plus 300 B of actions) and lead every typed
  root reply's bytes; the palette could go `detail: lazy`, left to its owner.
- **Two full clones of every reply's JSON tree.** `host.rs` cloned
  `msg["result"]` out of the parsed line and `index::fetch` cloned
  `v["items"]` again before `from_value`: a 3.4 MB listing was four trees
  in memory at once (`phys_footprint_peak` 237 MB). Both `take()` now
  (`host.rs` reader loop, `index.rs` `fetch`); the icons round trip went
  108 -> 25 ms with the smaller reply, the peak 237 -> 93 MB.
- **`permissions::install` probed on the main thread during setup**: AX
  trust, EventKit, a file open behind Full Disk Access, IOHIDCheckAccess,
  ~85 ms before the cache restore could start. Now `spawn_blocking`
  (`permissions.rs`); nothing at startup waits on it.
- **Bar items rendered on their timers with nothing to draw them.** With
  `[bar] target = "off"` the host was still asked for otp and timer every
  10 s and media every 30 s (12-14 round trips a minute, the host at 0.2%
  CPU). `Bar::draws(key)` (`core/src/config/mod.rs`: enabled and not aimed
  at `off`) now gates the load render, the timer (`schedule`), the
  triggers and `apply_config` in `bar/mod.rs`; a target flip renders them.
- **The bar popover took the keyboard at launch.** AppKit hands the
  frontmost key-capable window the keyboard when an app activates at
  launch; the popover panel is ordered front last (invisible), so on hornet
  a scratch start ate a user's typing for six seconds (`key->paint "ok
  sent" (0)` from the popover page, then `window_did_resign_key` when they
  clicked away). `set_becomes_key_only_if_needed(true)` on the popover
  panel (`panel/macos.rs`), plus a `bar popover key` log line: three starts
  after the change show none at launch. The engaged path
  (`show_and_make_key`, an explicit `makeKeyWindow`) was not exercised
  this round: verify a click-opened popover still types.

Left as measured, not changed:

- Every show relists ten live palettes with no `ttl` (browser-tabs 44 ms
  over CDP, wifi 79 ms, bluetooth 80-140 ms, windows 15 ms, system,
  audio, media, otp, home-assistant): after the paint, ~80 ms wall in
  parallel, all by their manifests ("device palettes" is open above).
- The clipboard watcher's 250 ms `changeCount` read is in the app's
  0.02%.

Fixed later the same day (the settings window, the update checks, the
sketchybar probe; measured the same way, `[bar] target = "off"`,
`check_updates = false`, warm cache, the before binary the build above):

| What | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Idle after 2 min, app: RSS / footprint | 160-171 MB / 55-64 MB | 153-169 MB / 56-59 MB (three runs each; the app's own share barely moves, the WebContent row is the saving) |
| Idle after 2 min, WebContent processes behind the app | 4 (main 107, HUD 45, popover 74, settings 65 MB RSS; 292 MB) | 3 (108, 44, 69; 222 MB); the fourth appears on the first settings open |
| `pal settings`, first open, to the page's first paint | the page was already loaded, hidden | 134 ms (window built and shown 39 ms, chunk loaded +64, view +19, paint +13); 199 ms before the first `settings_get` skipped the 50 ms debounce |
| `pal settings` once the window exists | `show` | 1.3-6 ms (`settings open shown`) |
| Panel cold start to first paint | 458-513 ms | 399-419 ms (four runs; `main.tsx` no longer renders through `Suspense`) |
| `updater error` at launch with `check_updates = false` | at 0.9 s, every launch | none; nothing runs until the Overview opens, and then only when the setting is on |
| `sketchybar --query bar` forks while the target is `auto` | 4 in 90 s (every 30 s) | 1 at start, +1 per `[bar]` config change, wake or Space change (0 in 95 s idle) |

- **The settings window is built on its first open** (`settings::open_page`
  runs `create` on the main thread when no window exists; `install` no
  longer builds one), then hidden on close as before. The page it opens on
  rides in the URL (`index.html?settings&page=about`, `startPage` in
  `Settings.tsx`), since the `pal://settings` event would land before the
  listener; an existing window still gets the event. Size and position
  restore as before: the plugin restores inside `build`, but tao applies
  the resize a run-loop turn later, so the under-minimum check reads the
  plugin's file (`saved_size`) rather than `inner_size`. Open-to-paint was
  480 ms at first: 300 ms of it was React's fallback throttle on the
  `lazy` + `Suspense` in `main.tsx` (a resolved lazy component is held
  back 300 ms after a fallback showed), so every page's chunk is now
  imported before the render; the rest was the 50 ms `refresh` debounce on
  the first read (`read` runs at once on mount now). The window itself costs 33-55 ms, under the 150 ms that
  would have argued for a prebuild after `host/ready`; the ~100 ms to
  content after it is the page, and a prebuild would have put the fourth WebContent
  process (~70 MB RSS) back at idle. `settings_get` is `async` now
  (`permissions::status` probes the OS for 10-20 ms, and the Overview
  re-reads every 5 s), and `theme.ts` asks `settings_theme` for the one
  key instead of the 123 KB `View` per window at load (four windows did).
- **The update checks live in the core** (`settings::Checks`, in the
  `Settings` state: the app's release check and the store's, each a
  `Checked { at, value?, error? }`). `settings_check_updates { force }`
  runs each when due (`Checks::due`: `general.check_updates` on and the
  last result older than a day, or `force`), remembers, and returns what
  is known; the page calls it when the Overview or Extensions page opens
  and from the Overview's "Check now" (`force`). `updater::check` (the
  daily loop, the tray, the About page, the `pal:check-updates` command)
  and `extensions_check_updates` remember too, and the daily loop asks
  `checks_due` so it never repeats a check the window just ran. The
  Overview's `updatesLine` says when the checks last ran and what they
  said ("Updates checked 2h ago. No release published yet. Automatic
  checks are off."), never as an attention row; a missing `latest.json`
  (`Error::ReleaseNotFound`, the state today) and a manifest without this
  platform (`TargetNotFound`, `TargetsNotFound`) are `UpdateInfo.status`
  facts, not errors. `extensions_update`/`extensions_remove` drop the
  name from the remembered list. Tests: `settings::tests` (due, the
  serialised shape, forget) and `settings-overview.test.tsx` (the line,
  no row for a failed or skipped check, Check now).
- **The sketchybar probe has no timer.** `sketchybar::install` probes
  once; `reprobe(app, why)` runs on a `[bar]` config change
  (`bar::apply_config`), on `NSWorkspaceDidWakeNotification` and on
  `NSWorkspaceActiveSpaceDidChangeNotification` (`triggers::macos`), the
  answer cached in `Bar::sketchybar` as before; `pal bar sync` still
  probes at once. A bar started after pal is seen at the next of those.
  Verified with a `sketchybar` shim on PATH that logs each call and exits
  1: one call at start, one more on a config edit, none over 95 s idle.

## Release v0.1.0: what remains for Cagdas (2026-09-16)

The dry run on 2026-09-16 (this head, `a84c75d` plus the working tree):
every `tauri-action` input in `release.yml` exists in the v1 `action.yml`
(`projectPath`, `tagName`, `releaseName`, `releaseDraft`, `releaseBody`,
`args`, `uploadUpdaterJson`); the updater pubkey in `tauri.conf.json` is
byte-identical to `~/Sync/.secrets/pal/pal-updater.key.pub`; `npm pack
--dry-run` in `sdk/` lists 17 files (`dist/*.d.ts`, `src/*.ts`, README,
LICENSE, package.json), nothing else; `make release VERSION=0.1.0 DRY_RUN=1`
prints the eight steps and refuses `0.2.1` (exists on origin). What an agent
could not do:

1. **Decide the version.** `zcag/pal` already carries the v1 tags `v0.1.2`
   to `v0.2.1` and GitHub's "latest" is `v0.2.1`. `v0.1.0` is free as a tag,
   but it sorts below every v1 release: tick **Set as the latest release**
   when publishing the draft (step 5 in `docs/releasing.md`), or pick
   `v0.3.0`/`v1.0.0` and skip the question (`make release VERSION=` takes
   either; `sdk/package.json` and the Cargo versions follow).
2. **Branch: done.** `main` is the rewrite since 2026-09-16 20:10; the SDK's `main` links are true and the site's specs carry no `@ref`.
3. **Set the two secrets** (repository Settings > Secrets and variables >
   Actions; none is set today, `gh api repos/zcag/pal/actions/secrets` says
   `total_count: 0`):
   - `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY`: the contents of
     `~/Sync/.secrets/pal/pal-updater.key` (`gh secret set
     TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY < ~/Sync/.secrets/pal/pal-updater.key`)
   - `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY_PASSWORD`: the key's password (empty for
     this key per `docs/releasing.md`; set it to the empty string anyway,
     `tauri build` reads the variable when the key is set)
   Without them the build still ships, minus the `.tar.gz`/`.sig` pair and
   `latest.json`, and the `manifest` job skips itself: no pal would ever
   see an update from that release.
4. **CI green first.** `ci.yml` was red on the head (1.98.1 lint in
   `app/src-tauri/src/bar/glyph.rs:71,87,248,253,264`, and Linux dead code in
   `bar/popover.rs:119,575`, `deeplink.rs:258-263`, `crash.rs:177-216`); the
   toolchain pin removes the first, the other agents' working-tree changes
   the rest. Push, wait for `gh run list --branch main --limit 1` to say
   `success`.
5. **Tag**, on a clean tree: `make release VERSION=0.1.0` (dry run first:
   `make release VERSION=0.1.0 DRY_RUN=1`). Then
   `gh run watch` on the release workflow.
6. **Verify the draft**: all four jobs green (`bundle` x3, `manifest`);
   assets `pal_0.1.0_aarch64.dmg`, `pal_0.1.0_x64.dmg`,
   `pal_0.1.0_amd64.AppImage`, `pal_0.1.0_amd64.deb`, the two
   `.app.tar.gz` + `.sig`, `AppImage.sig`, `latest.json` with
   `darwin-aarch64`, `darwin-x86_64`, `linux-x86_64`; the notes read as a
   changelog (edit them). Install the aarch64 dmg on hornet (`xattr -dr
   com.apple.quarantine /Applications/pal.app` first) and the AppImage on
   marko, run each once, `pal --version` says 0.1.0.
7. **Publish** the draft (with "Set as the latest release"). Then
   `curl -sL https://github.com/zcag/pal/releases/latest/download/latest.json
   | jq .version` says `0.1.0`, and the installed pal's tray "Check for
   updates" logs `updater\tup to date`. Note `updater.rs` (another agent's
   file this round) still logs `updater\terror\tCould not fetch a valid
   release JSON from the remote` for a release without `latest.json`
   (`tauri_plugin_updater::Error::ReleaseNotFound`) and
   `TargetsNotFound` for one missing this platform; the quiet "no release
   yet" wants those two variants matched before `to_string()` in
   `updater::check`.
8. **npm**: `cd sdk && npm login && npm publish` (scope `@zcag` must be
   your npm user or an org you own; `publishConfig.access: public` is in
   `package.json`, `prepack` builds `dist/`). `npm view @zcag/pal version`
   says `0.1.0` afterwards. Then the README's "link it from a checkout"
   note is the fallback only.
9. **Later, not for v0.1.0**: Developer ID + notarisation (above, and
   `docs/releasing.md`); the `APPLE_*` secrets stay commented out in
   `release.yml` until the certificate exists.


## media: the MediaRemote adapter replaces nowplaying-cli on macOS (2026-09-16)

- **The fact.** Since macOS 15.4 `mediaremoted` answers only clients with the `com.apple.mediaremote.*` entitlement. On hornet (macOS 26.4, build 25E246) with a YouTube tab playing in Chrome, `nowplaying-cli get title artist playbackRate` printed `null null null`; the `media` palette therefore saw nothing beyond Spotify and Music and told Cagdas to `brew install nowplaying-cli`, which he had. `nowplaying-cli`'s own README says the same for 15.4+.
- **The fix.** pal bundles [mediaremote-adapter](https://github.com/ungive/mediaremote-adapter) (BSD-3-Clause, Jonas van den Berg; `NOTICES.md`): a Perl script and a small Objective-C framework; `/usr/bin/perl` is an Apple-signed, entitled binary with no library validation, so `DynaLoader::dl_load_file` on the framework gives the script the MediaRemote API. `perl mediaremote-adapter.pl <Framework> get --now --no-artwork --allow-missing-title` prints one JSON object (or `null`), `send <MRCommand id>` controls (0 play, 1 pause, 2 toggle, 4 next, 5 previous). `app/scripts/fetch-mediaremote.sh` clones the pinned commit (`73f14ab`), builds with cmake (universal, ad-hoc signed by its own post-build step, ~8 s on hornet, needs `brew install cmake`; the CI macOS image has cmake) into `app/src-tauri/mediaremote/` (gitignored); `build.rs` runs it when the script is missing, `tauri.macos.conf.json` ships the directory as `Contents/Resources/mediaremote` (macOS only: the Linux bundle never sees it). `pal_core::media::configure(dir)` (called from `app/src-tauri/src/media.rs::install` with the bundle's Resources, else the repo dir) is the resolver; the source order is adapter, then `nowplaying-cli` on PATH, then only the osascript apps.
- **What Chrome/YouTube returns on hornet.** `{"bundleIdentifier":"com.google.Chrome","playing":true,"playbackRate":1,"title":"","artist":"","album":"","duration":3963.08,"elapsedTime":2411.41,"elapsedTimeNow":2532.90,"timestamp":"2026-09-16T17:49:16Z","processIdentifier":79336,"contentItemIdentifier":"…"}`: the app, the state and the position, every text key empty. Without `--allow-missing-title` the adapter prints `null` for it ("media without a title is invalid" by its default), so the flag is load-bearing. `elapsedTime` is the position at `timestamp`, which was minutes stale on one read (a 17:34:50Z timestamp on a read made around 17:40, position 1547 s of a 3963 s video); `--now` adds `elapsedTimeNow`, which the row uses. The row is then "Google Chrome" (the running app's `localizedName`, its bundle path for the icon) with "42:12 / 1:06:03" as the subtitle and the `playing` tag; the bar strip says "Google Chrome". Twenty minutes later the same tab read `"title":"Taylor Tomlinson (Full Episode) | Conan O'Brien Needs A Friend","artist":"Team Coco"` (a new `contentItemIdentifier`, timestamp 17:55:49Z): YouTube's media session does reach Chrome's Now Playing, late; the empty keys are the gap, and the title-less row covers it instead of "Nothing playing" beside a `playing` tag. Both captures are fixtures.
- **In the bundle.** tauri's resource copy (`bundle.resources`) does not keep symlinks: the framework arrives with `MediaRemoteAdapter` as a real file at the top and `Versions/A/` beside it, no `Versions/Current` or `Resources` links. The script dlopens `<framework>/MediaRemoteAdapter`, so that is enough; the Mach-O's own ad-hoc signature is untouched (`codesign -dv` reads `com.vandenbe.MediaRemoteAdapter`, adhoc) and Resources is not a nested-code location, so neither the ad-hoc app signing nor `make app`'s `pal-dev` identity (tested: `codesign --force --deep -s pal-dev` on a copy) re-signs or rejects it; `codesign --verify --deep --strict` passes both ways and `get` runs from `Contents/Resources/mediaremote` at 20.6-22.4 ms. `/usr/bin/perl` does no library validation, which is the whole trick; hardened runtime is off anyway.
- **Latency.** `perl` + framework load + `get`: 19-21 ms per run on hornet (10 runs, min 19.0, median 19.9, max 21.2 ms), `send` 18-20 ms; the whole `now_playing()` in a debug build, with the two `NSRunningApplication` checks, 44.6 ms. Well under the 100 ms target, so `get` runs on the existing cadence (the bar's 5 s poll while playing, the 30 s core refresh) and there is no long-lived `stream` process. Option kept for later: one `stream --no-diff --no-artwork` child in the core, answered from its last payload, which would make a track change push-driven instead of polled; nothing needs it at 20 ms. **Superseded the same day: the stream landed (Decided: media stream, below).**
- **Artwork** was off (`--no-artwork`; the stream now carries it, below): the adapter emits it as base64 `artworkData` (a few hundred KB per read) and says it arrives late and unreliably for `get`; a `data:` url on the row at every 5 s poll is not cheap. Spotify's row has its https artwork url anyway; Music's has none either way.
- **Dedupe** is now by bundle id: when the adapter names `com.spotify.client` or `com.apple.Music` and that app's osascript row exists, the system row is dropped (the app row has the urls); the nowplaying-cli fallback still dedupes on title+artist since it has no bundle id.
- **Palette and bar.** A title-less player with a state is a row (app name, position or "Playing"/"Paused" as the subtitle, no Copy); the macOS install hint is gone (the adapter is always in the bundle; a build without it says so), the Linux `playerctl` hint stays. `clock()` and the Chrome fixture are tested in `host/test/extensions/media.test.ts`; `parse_mediaremote` against the captured Chrome JSON, a README-shaped paused track and `null`/`{}` in `core/src/media.rs`; `mediaremote_live` (`#[ignore]`) runs the whole chain against the box.
- **Control test.** `send 2` twice, 1.5 s apart, on Cagdas's Chrome while measuring: playback paused and resumed, `playing` was true again after.

## Decided: Slack (2026-09-16)

- `extensions/slack`: four palettes (`unreads` live, `channels` hourly catalog, `search` input, `status` live) and the bar item `slack/unreads`, replacing the sketchybar `slack` item with `helpers/slack-unreads` and `helpers/slack-auth` (Python, `uv` with cramjam and pycryptodome). The rule it keeps from that item: **addressed, not unread**. A busy workspace is never at zero unread channels (hornet at 22:00: 34 channels, 8 unread, 0 addressed), so the badge counts only DMs + mentions + thread replies, is hidden at zero, and the quiet channels are named in the popover under "Also unread", not counted.
- **Auth `app`** (default) ports the two extractions to TypeScript, no dependency: `leveldb.ts` (SSTable footer, index and data blocks, prefix-compressed entries, snappy raw decoding in forty lines, zlib via `node:zlib`, zstd via `Bun.zstdDecompressSync`; the write-ahead log's fragments reassembled into batches; the newest write picked by sequence number rather than file order) and `cookies.ts` (`bun:sqlite` on a copy of the jar, PBKDF2-SHA1 over `saltysalt`, AES-128-CBC with the 16-space IV, the 32-byte host hash Chromium 130+ prefixes detected by the plaintext not being printable ASCII; the password from `security find-generic-password -s "Slack Safe Storage" -w` on macOS, `secret-tool lookup application Slack` for `v11` or "peanuts" for `v10` on Linux, the Linux half unverified). Both files copied before reading; the session is in memory only, never on disk (the Python helper wrote `slack.creds.json` under TMPDIR, this does not); extracted again once per call on `invalid_auth`. Verified on hornet: 7 ms for the LevelDB, 16 ms for the cookie, no keychain prompt (`security` already had "Always Allow" from the helper), one `client.counts` at 387 ms.
- **Auth `token`** (`xoxp-`) is a bearer against `slack.com`; `auth.test` names the workspace. It has no `client.counts`: `conversations.info` per conversation of the directory gives `unread_count_display` and `last_read` (DMs and unread channels), no mention counts, no threads. Documented as the trade-off, not hidden.
- **Requests**: one `client.counts` per refresh; `conversations.history` only for an addressed conversation, the newest `MAX_DETAIL` (12), memoised by `(conversation, latest)` so a refresh where nothing moved fetches no history; the inbox shared between the bar and the palette for `INBOX_FRESH_MS` (30 s), so a `show` (which fires both) is one fetch; the directory (`users.list` up to 5 pages, `users.conversations`) an hour in memory, `users.info`/`conversations.info` for a miss; a 429 remembered for `Retry-After` and every call until then refused locally (`RateLimited`). Not persisted in `storage`: the rows carry message text and avatars, the cap is 256 KB, and a restart costs one `client.counts`.
- **Rows**: id `<kind>:<team>/<conversation>` (a channel can be a mention and a thread at once); sections Direct messages, Mentions, Threads, Channels; the DM subtitle drops the "who:" when the sender is the row's name; a mention row shows the message naming you (`<@U..>`, `<!here|channel|everyone>`), not the last; joins and leaves never preview; Slack's wire text rendered (`<@U>` to a name, `<#C|name>`, `<url|label>`, entities). Thread rows: `client.counts` gives `unread_count_by_channel` only, so the row is the channel and the count, no text, no Reply, no Mark as read (the thread's ts is not known); reading them needs the client-only `subscriptions.thread.getView`, left out.
- **Deep links**: `slack://channel?team=T&id=C&message=ts` (message-level when the run gave a ts), `https://<domain>.slack.com/archives/C/p<ts without the dot>` for the browser; `slack://open` for Open Slack (was `open -b com.tinyspeck.slackmacgap`, macOS only).
- **Bar popover** is a menu level (a section per kind with the newest five, "Also unread" with up to 8 quiet channels, Open in pal, Mark all read, Open Slack), not `{ palette }`: it mirrors what the sketchybar popup drew, and "Open in pal" is the way to the per-row Reply / Mark as read. A menu row has one action, so the per-row Mark read lives in the palette level.
- **Status presets** are one `list` setting line each, `:emoji: text (expiry)`; `emoji.ts` tables ~100 Slack shortcodes to characters for the row icon (the emoji extension's 300 KB catalog is keyed by Unicode names, not Slack's, and a bundle inlines imports); the current status uses `status_emoji_display_info[0].unicode` from the API. `today` is the next local midnight.
- Tests: `host/test/extensions/slack-auth.test.ts` (synthetic SSTable with a snappy block, a WAL with a record split across the 32 KB boundary, a jar encrypted with and without the host hash; the builders in `slack-fixtures.ts`) and `slack.test.ts` (a Bun mock of the API dispatching on `/api/<method>`, the synthetic app dir through `PAL_SLACK_APP_DIR`, a fake `security` on PATH standing in for the keychain; 24 tests over rows, sections, deep links, the pane, Mark as read, Reply, the badge rules, the 429 back-off, re-extraction on `invalid_auth`, token mode). 30 pass.
- Not done: reading a thread's replies (above); several workspaces for Status and Search (they act on `workspace` else the first); a Linux box running Slack to verify the cookie path.

**Decided: Slack presence dots (2026-09-17).** Cagdas asked for them (09:30), scoped to direct messages: `client.counts` carries no presence, so each DM row's person (the other side of an `im`; in an `mpim` whoever wrote the message shown, `Msg.uid`) gets one `users.getPresence`, and the row a dot, green for `active`, grey for `away`, none for a channel, a failed lookup, or with the `presence` setting off. The mechanisms are the ones already there, no new vocabulary: in the popover `image.dot` on the avatar node (`BarRow.dot`), in the palette a `{ tag: "●", color }` accessory placed after the count tag, since the first tag is the one a narrow row beside the pane keeps (`accessoryDropOrder`). Cost model in one sentence: one call per distinct person among the DM rows listed, eight in flight at once, each answer remembered 60 s (`data.ts presenceOf`, a refusal remembered as none for as long) so a re-list inside the minute makes no call, and a call that fails or runs past 2 s (`call(..., PRESENCE_MS)`, the timeout now a parameter of `call`) leaves its row without the dot rather than holding the inbox. The Channels catalog has no dots: it is indexed hourly, a dot there would be an hour stale. Tests: the mock's `users.getPresence` is a table (Mara active, Tomas away, Erin refused), `slack.test.ts` 30 pass (27 + 3: the dots per row and none on a channel, the refused row still listed, the popover's `image.dot`, no second call inside the minute, `presence = false` making none).


## Decided: Today (2026-09-16)

- **What landed** (`extensions/calendar`): a second live palette **Today** (`today`, the day's events in order with the time, how long, and the state `in 12 min` / `now, 25 min left` / `over`, tomorrow's under a section once no timed event is left, a "Nothing else today" row naming the next timed event's day), the bar item **`calendar/upcoming`** (`Standup in 12m`, `now` while it runs), and a third source, **Google Calendar** per account through a token command. My Schedule stays the week view. Replaces the owner's sketchybar `upcoming` item (`plugins/upcoming.sh` + `helpers/meetings`): the HA `calendar_summary` / `merged_event_N` sensors and the title-matched join links are gone, the extension reads the same two identities from the Calendar API directly, so the link and the event are one row.
- **Source design** (`source.ts`): a `source` setting, `system` (EventKit / khal through `pal_core::calendar`, untouched), `google` (`google.ts`), `auto` = google once an `accounts` entry exists, else system. One cache for every reader: `load(from, to, ids, maxAge)` keeps the last window fetched (local midnight to `days` ahead, two at least) and answers it while younger than the caller's `maxAge` (palettes 60 s, the bar's minute tick 5 min, `refresh` 0), one fetch in flight at a time; a failure with a cache in hand answers it as `stale` with the error (the bar item goes `stale`, Today puts a hint row first), with none it throws (a hint row, the item hidden). Settings changes `forget()` everything, tokens included. Google calendars are `<account>:<id>` with the primary as `<account>:primary`, so two accounts' calendars never collide and the filter dropdown reads `Personal (personal)`.
- **Google through a token command, no OAuth flow in pal.** `accounts` is a `list` setting of `name = command` strings (the Settings window's list control takes strings; the name is one word so a `?aud=calendar` inside a bare command is not one); the config file may also hold tables `{ name, token_command, calendars }` for calendars beyond the primary. The command runs through `sh -c`, its stdout is the token: a bare line, or JSON with `access_token` and `expires_in` / `expiry` (the broker's shape); cached in memory until the stated expiry less a minute (30 min for a bare token), one mint shared by concurrent callers, minted again once on a 401. The secret is the command's; nothing secret sits in the config or on disk. The owner's two identities: `ssh archer "curl -s 'http://127.0.0.1:8776/token?aud=calendar'"` and `aud=calendar-work`, read-only by the broker's audiences (`~/Sync/vault/infra/token-broker.md`); ⚠️ the remote command needs its own quotes, the remote login shell is zsh and `?` globs there (`no matches found`), which is exactly the error the extension surfaced on the first live run. A generic user gets the same mechanism with `gcloud auth application-default print-access-token` after an ADC login carrying the `calendar.readonly` scope; documented as documented, not tested (README, docs/palettes.md say so). Rejected: an OAuth client id shipped with pal (a secret in the bundle, a consent screen to maintain) and a `secret` setting holding a refresh token (pal would then own a credential it cannot rotate).
- **The API read**: `calendarList` per account (colour, title, access role; `minAccessRole=reader`), `events.list` per calendar with `singleEvents`, `orderBy=startTime`, `timeMin`/`timeMax`, `maxResults=250` and a `fields` mask carrying `conferenceData(entryPoints(entryPointType,uri))`, paged on `nextPageToken`; all accounts in parallel (`Promise.allSettled`), one account away logged and skipped, all away is the error. `PAL_GOOGLE_API` points the tests at a local server. Events map to the core's `CalendarEvent` (`toEvent`): `dateTime` as given, `date` as the local midnight (Google's all-day `end.date` is exclusive like the core's), `recurringEventId` → `recurring` + `occurrence`, attendees without resources, `self` → `me` and `my_status`, `needsAction` → `pending`, `htmlLink` as `url`, the description HTML to text (`text`: `<br>` and block ends to newlines, `<li>` to `- `, tags dropped, entities decoded). A Google row opens in the browser (`htmlLink`), no delete, no New event (the tokens may be read-only; the work identity must never create an event).
- **Join link detection** (`joinLink`): `conferenceData` entry point of type `video` (a `more` one when there is no video), else `hangoutLink`, else the first known provider's meeting link in the location then the description, by the same provider rule as `pal_core::calendar::is_conference` ported to TS (`isConference`: Zoom `/j/ /my/ /w/ /s/ /wc/`, Meet not `/new`, Teams `meetup-join` / `/meet/`, Webex, Jitsi, Whereby, GoTo), safelinks unwrapped, `&amp;` unescaped, trailing punctuation dropped. Kept in the extension rather than round-tripped through the bridge per event; the two rules are tested against the same cases.
- **Escalation rules** (`today.ts`, `escalation`): the strip's colour from minutes to the start, boundaries inclusive: `> warn_minutes` (15) `muted`, `<= warn_minutes` `amber`, `<= urgent_minutes` (5) `red`, `start <= now` (running) `green`; `urgent_minutes = 0` means red only at the start. The item is hidden when nothing timed starts within `horizon_hours` (10, the owner's "only the next 10 hours are worth bar space"); `nextEvent` is the first by start that has not ended, timed unless `hide_all_day` is off, not declined unless `hide_declined` is off, starting inside the horizon; a running one counts until it ends. `badge: "dot"` when there is a call (the owner's "click joins" becomes "the dot says Enter joins"). The title is `<title> in 12m` / `<title> in 1h 20m` / `<title> now`, the title cut to 36 chars so the whole stays under the 64-char check. The owner's position move (`q` against the notch inside 15 min) and size jump are not reproduced: the model has no position per state, and colour carries the escalation on both targets (sketchybar maps `muted` / `amber` / `red` / `green` through `bar/colors.rs`).
- **Refresh**: `refresh: { every: 300, on: ["minute", "wake", "network"] }`; `render` fetches on every reason but `minute`, which reads the cache (up to 5 min old) so the count-down ticks without a request. Measured: `bar/render` on a minute tick 0.10 ms round trip through the host in the harness, 0.3 ms live; the first fetch 329 ms for both real identities over ssh (the token mints happened at load, 792 ms), 245 ms for a re-fetch with tokens cached; EventKit's 81 ms for a week is unchanged. The item's `menu` is `{ palette: "today", args: { rest: true } }`: the popover is Today with the over ones dropped and tomorrow always there.
- **Live check on hornet** (2026-09-16 22:00, both identities through the broker, read-only): the filters listed `cagdassalur@gmail.com (personal)` and `cagdas@serpapi.com (work)`; Today showed one all-day event (Home); My Schedule listed Home (today, tomorrow, twice this week), "Weekly Meeting (team terry)" (5 people, Join) and "Cagdas and Terry" (2 people, Join) this week; the bar item was hidden (nothing timed within 10 h of a late evening), which is the rule working. An all-day event does not hold the day open: "Nothing else today" and tomorrow's rows show once the timed ones are done.
- **Tests**: `host/test/extensions/calendar.test.ts` (31: the schedule helpers as before, `today.ts` at the boundaries, `upcomingItem`, Today's rows and the popover form, the day-done and clear-day rows, the bar item's cache on a minute tick with its timing, stale on a failing source, hidden without permission, the horizon and colour settings) and `calendar-google.test.ts` (14: `parseAccounts`, `parseToken`, `joinLink`, `links` / `isConference`, `text`, `toEvent`; then a Bun mock of the API v3 fed by `calendar-google.fixture.json`, shaped like the API with `conferenceData`, its times shifted so the fixture's 10:00 is the test's start and the host's zone a fixed `Etc/GMT±N` so the local clock reads 10:xx; two accounts whose token commands are scripts, one printing a bare token, one the broker's JSON; the token cache, a 401 re-mint, one account away, every account away, `source = system` with accounts still listed). The EventKit / khal paths and their tests are untouched but for the detail's `When` line, which now carries the duration. Gallery: `app/src/gallery/shots/bar-calendar.json` (four states) and a `today` palette in `calendar.json`; `4-today.png` and the four bar shots rendered and listed in the manifest.
- **Open**: the sketchybar item is still in the owner's `sketchybarrc`; retiring it is his `item_hide` / removal plus `[bar] target = "sketchybar"` on the daily instance (this round ran with `target = "off"` and one look at `menubar` on a scratch instance). `PaletteMeta.filters` is static, so a Google account added later shows in the dropdown at the next host start (the rows are right at once). The Settings window shows `accounts` as a list of strings; a table entry from the file has no shape in the list control (`SettingsField.tsx` casts the value to `string[]`; not checked what it draws, a guess is `[object Object]`), which is the reason the string form exists. The scratch binary of the live check embedded a `dist` older than the tinted-glyph support, so Today's rows drew a letter there; the gallery on the current source draws the tinted glyph (`4-today.png`).

## Decided: media stream (2026-09-16)

- **What landed.** `pal_core::media` keeps one `mediaremote-adapter.pl <framework> stream --allow-missing-title --micros --debounce=100` child for the process (`media.rs`, `mod stream`): started by the first `now_playing()` once `configure` found the adapter, read on a `media-stream` thread, each `{"type":"data","diff":bool,"payload":{..}}` line merged into one map (`merge`: a non-diff replaces, a diff patches, a `null` key removes), `artworkData` taken out of the map on arrival and decoded once into `Cover` (bytes capped at `MAX_ARTWORK` 512 KB, else dropped and logged; width and height from the header; an id from the first 8 bytes of the SHA-256 so the same picture on the next line or the next track keeps its `Arc` and id; a 128 px PNG thumb made on the first ask). `now_playing()` answers the system row from that state (`Live::player`), with the position ticked from `elapsedTimeMicros` + (now - `timestampEpochMicros`) x `playbackRate` while `playing` (the adapter's own `getElapsedTimeNow` rule, plus the `playing` gate: QuickTime's pause arrives as `playing:false` one line before the rate drops); `get --now` stays the fallback while the stream is down and `NowPlaying.stream` says which one answered. Restart with backoff (1 s doubling to 60 s, reset after a minute's run). Exit needs no hook: the child runs under `sh -c '"$@" & p=$!; exec >&-; trap .. TERM INT HUP; read -r _; kill $p'`, so the adapter dies when pal's end of the stdin pipe closes, on any exit including a crash; `media::shutdown()` exists for a deliberate stop and is not wired (lib.rs is another agent's file this round, and the pipe already covers it).
- **Change notification.** `media::on_change` takes one listener; `Live::feed` reports a change when the bundle, title, artist, album, `playing` or the cover id moved (a seek or the clock is not one). `app/src-tauri/src/media.rs::install` hands it `bar::trigger(app, "media")`, so an item with `media` in `refresh.on` re-renders at once; `extensions/media/pal.json` declares it. There is no literal `media/changed` line to the host: `host/src/host.ts` has no handler for one and would log it as unknown; the bar trigger is the path, and `BarCtx.reason` in `sdk/src/protocol.ts` (not this round's file) should gain `"media"` when someone is in it. With `stream: true` on the reply the extension stops its own 5 s poll (`follow`), which stays for Linux and for a fallen stream.
- **Artwork to the UI.** `core/media.artwork {id}` (`bridge.rs` route list) answers `{ data: "data:image/png;base64,..", width, height }` for a `Player.artwork_id`; the extension keeps one cover (`coverOf`, one fetch per id: one thing plays at a time) and uses it as the palette row's icon and the popover's new first row (`track`, the title and artist over the cover at row size, a click opens the track, else copies, else inert). The strip keeps the glyph unless the `bar_artwork` setting is on and the cover is square (`width === height`); the bar's `glyph::image_png` decodes the `data:` PNG (the app's `image` crate has only `png`, hence PNG thumbs, never the raw JPEG). No `icon://` route: `icon.rs` is not this round's file, and a data url needs none. When the adapter names Spotify or Music and that app's osascript row exists, the cover id moves to that row when it has no artwork url of its own (Music), instead of the system row being dropped whole. `sdk/src/api.ts` (not this round's file) does not type `artwork_id`, `stream` or `media.artwork` yet; the extension types them locally (`Player`, `Playing`, `Artwork`) and calls `core.call("media.artwork")`.
- **Position in the row.** `progress(p)` (`12:34 / 1:06:03`) is the first accessory of a row with a track and the subtitle of a title-less one, from the position the core ticked at listing time. It does not tick while the palette stays open: a live palette lists on show and on `keep`, and the UI (`app/src/ui/Row.tsx`, not this round's file) has no accessory that counts up; a `{ date }` accessory counts down, not a clock. Noted in the README under what it does not do.
- **Measured on hornet** (macOS 26.4, QuickTime Player at volume 0 on a generated 10 min silent WAV, since nothing else was playing and Chrome is read-only; QuickTime reports no title or artwork, so the cover path is the fixtures' and `artwork_is_bytes..`): the stream child idle over 60 s while playing, nothing changing: `cputime` 0:00.01 to 0:00.02 (10 ms in a minute), `%cpu` 0.0, RSS 22 MB (`ps -o cputime,%cpu,rss`; the adapter blocks in the MediaRemote run loop). `now_playing()` in a debug build: `get` fallback (the first call, which starts the stream) 24.6 ms; from the stream 20 calls min 171 us, median 180 us, max 583 us (`mediaremote_live`, `#[ignore]`, prints both). Bar re-render after a change, on a scratch instance (release build, `TAURI_CONFIG` identifier `io.cagdas.pal.scratch.media` baked in at build time, since `generate_context!` reads it then and a runtime env is ignored; `[bar] target = "menubar"`): `osascript` pause sent at t+0, `now_playing` 0.68 ms and `bar media/now-playing rendered 1.6ms media` at t+103 ms, the menubar item removed at t+104 ms; play sent at t+0, `now_playing` 1.13 ms, `rendered 3.7ms media` at t+106 ms, the item created at t+111 ms. Of that, ~100 ms is the adapter's `--debounce=100` (a track change is a burst of partial lines; one line beats three renders) and the rest is osascript's own round trip; the render itself is under 4 ms. No `now_playing` call from the instance in the 15 s after (the poll is off with `stream: true`). After `pal quit`, `pgrep` found neither the `sh` wrapper nor the perl child: the pipe ended them. Not seen live: a cover (QuickTime sends none for a WAV) and a titled track change; both are fixture-tested, the Chrome capture's keys with a PNG.
- **Tests.** Core: `stream_lines_merge_full_then_diffs_and_a_null_removes` (the hornet Chrome line with `--micros` keys and a PNG, a pause diff, a seek that is no change, nulls, the empty state, noise), `artwork_is_bytes_with_an_id_over_them_capped_and_thumbed` (1x1 and 2x1 PNGs, the cap, a non-picture, the same bytes keeping the `Arc`), `position_ticks_from_elapsed_and_timestamp_at_the_rate`, `stream_answers_while_up_and_falls_back_when_the_child_dies` (a shell fake that prints a state and exits, then a second state and `exec sleep`: the row, `None` while down, the restart, `stop()` closing the pipe; 14 in `media::tests`, 1.4 s). Host: `media.test.ts` 12 (the progress accessory, the cover fetched once per id and the app icon once the core dropped it, the track row and the `media` trigger, `bar_artwork` with a square and a wide cover through `settings/changed`, no poll with `stream: true` on a second host at `PAL_MEDIA_POLL_MS=100`), `media-poll.test.ts` unchanged. `core/Cargo.toml` gains `base64` (already in the lock through the app).

## Decided: Location Services for the Wi-Fi names (2026-09-16)

- **Yes to the prompt.** macOS 15+ hands Wi-Fi network names only to an app with Location Services. `permissions.rs` grew a `location` permission (CoreLocation over `objc2-core-location`): the state is `CLLocationManager`'s class-method `authorizationStatus` (no manager needed; deprecated, but the instance property would want a manager per read), mapped onto the shared `pal_core::permission::Status` (the enum the calendar had; `calendar::Status` re-exports it), and the ask is `requestWhenInUseAuthorization` on the main thread on a manager kept for the run in a `thread_local` with its delegate (`PalLocationDelegate`, `define_class!`): CoreLocation answers asynchronously and only to a manager that still exists, and the delegate's `locationManagerDidChangeAuthorization:` is the log line while `watch` (the 2 s poll, unchanged) carries the grant to the windows. `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription` (and the pre-11 `NSLocationUsageDescription`, same text) went into `app/src-tauri/Info.plist`: "pal reads the names of Wi-Fi networks; macOS only shows them to apps with Location access". `Status.location` is on the wire, in the Overview's attention rows (only with the wifi extension installed, like Calendars with its extension), in General > Permissions ("Location" with the note "Wi-Fi network names"; every row now carries a short note) and in the diagnostics block; `denied` opens `Privacy_LocationServices`.
- **Lazy, and what "lazy" had to mean.** The wifi extension asks through a new bridge capability, `core/permissions.{status, request {which}}` (`permissions::call`, typed as `permissions` in the SDK), from its listing while the state is `not_determined`. But the host lists every palette at startup for the cache, so "the first time the palette lists" is the first run: the app therefore honours an extension's ask only while a pal window is in front of the user (`window_open`: the panel or Settings) and skips it otherwise (`permissions\tlocation\tskipped\tno pal window in front` on hornet's startup load); the extension asks on every listing while `not_determined` (a second `requestWhenInUseAuthorization` while the prompt is up is a no-op), so the first listing the user looks at is the one that prompts. On a fresh profile that is the first show, next to the Accessibility ask. A refusal is a row (**Wi-Fi names need Location access**, Enter opens the pane; `restricted` says a profile forbids it) above the power row.
- **CoreWLAN, in-process, for both the name and the scan.** `core/src/wifi.rs` on macOS now reads the interface (`CWWiFiClient.sharedWiFiClient.interface`: name, `powerOn`, `ssid`, `rssiValue`, `wlanChannel`, `security`) and scans (`scanForNetworksWithSSID:nil`) through `objc2-core-wlan`; `scutil`, `networksetup -getairportnetwork`, `-listallhardwareports`, `-getairportpower` and `system_profiler` are gone (with `parse_airport`/`parse_scutil_airport` and their fixtures; `cw_channel`/`cw_security` are the pure, tested parts now). Measured on hornet: `wifi.status` 16-24 ms in the bundle (55-58 ms before), a scan 9.3 s for 10 networks from the terminal, the same as `system_profiler`'s 9.2 s (the radio walks the channels either way), so the cache and the Scan row stay. **The grant does not reach child processes**: with Location granted to the bundle, CoreWLAN answered the name and `ipconfig getsummary` in the same process still printed `<redacted>` (a terminal's grant covers its tools because the terminal is the responsible process; pal's own does not). The network palette therefore asks `wifi.status()` when its summary is redacted, and its SSID hint names the missing grant. Signal now comes from RSSI (`Current.signal` was `None` on macOS before).
- **Verified on hornet** with a scratch bundle under a fresh identifier (`io.cagdas.pal.scratchloc`, `pal-dev` signed, `LSEnvironment` for the profile): `permissions ... location: NotDetermined` at start, the startup listing's ask skipped, the first `pal show` asked (`requested NotDetermined`, then the delegate's initial `changed NotDetermined`), CoreLocationAgent showed the alert and logged `AuthPrompt response=1` two seconds later (the unified log; Cagdas clicked Allow), `changed Granted`, `watch` emitted the new `Status`, and the next listing had the name (the current network, named, signal from RSSI; 94 root items from wifi). The prompt is per bundle identifier: **the daily `io.cagdas.pal` will ask once, on the first panel show after this build lands**, from the Wi-Fi listing. Other scratch instances built from the tree after this landed ask too (two more `AuthPrompt` rounds that evening). A fresh scan under the grant was not driven from the agent shell (the Scan row needs Enter in the panel; `pal://run/wifi/wifi/scan` through `open -b` did not reach `run_pick` in the instance, unexplained and left), so the named count of a granted scan is unmeasured; the terminal's scan found 10 networks, all nameless without the grant.
- Tests: `permissions::tests` (the `CLAuthorizationStatus` mapping, `complete` with every location state), `wifi::tests::corewlan_labels` (channel spelling, security codes, strongest-first), `host/test/extensions/wifi.test.ts` (the gate, the ask on a listing with the names withheld, the hint row and its pane action, the hidden count's wording per grant), `network.test.ts` (the core's name for a redacted summary, the hint without one), the settings UI tests (the Location row in General and on the Overview, gated on the wifi extension). Docs: `docs/palettes.md` (wifi and network), `docs/getting-started.md` (the permissions paragraph now lists all five), the two READMEs, `extensions/wifi/pal.json` (a feature line and the Open System Settings action).

## Decided: concealed copy, selection, OCR, Large Type, file contents (2026-09-16)

Pit 6, 19, 21, 22, 20 (`notes/pit-triage.md`), the C1 batch. Each is a core capability behind the same `core/<capability>.<fn>` bridge, with the bundled palettes as the first callers.

- **Concealed copy** (pit 6): `Effect.copy` takes a string or `CopyText { text, concealed?, clear_after? }` (`sdk/src/protocol.ts`; `conceal(text, seconds = 30)` in api.ts writes it). The core writes the text plus `org.nspasteboard.ConcealedType` on the same pasteboard item on macOS (`pal_core::clipboard::write_text_concealed`, `platform::write_concealed`) and arboard's `exclude_from_history` (the `x-kde-passwordManagerHint` type) on Linux. Pal's own watcher stays out of it two ways: the type check it already had on macOS (now `platform::concealed()`, run once per change, and on Linux through `wl-paste --list-types` / `xclip -t TARGETS` since arboard cannot list types), and a **suppression window** (`suppress_watch(d)`, a unix-ms `AtomicU64`; the watcher skips every change while it runs) around every write pal makes that is not a copy: the concealed write, its restore, the selection snapshot. `clear_after` runs on a thread (`write_text_concealed_for`): after the delay `restore_plan(now, secret, previous)` decides `Leave` (the user copied meanwhile), `Clear` (nothing preceded it) or `Write(previous)`; the previous contents are read with `read_any`, which is `read` minus the concealed check. The HUD line comes from effects.rs `copied_text`: "Copied, clears in 30 s" unless the envelope has its own `hud`. 1Password (password, one-time code) and Verification Codes (the code, the bar click) copy this way with their own HUD text; the username stays plain. Tests: `clipboard::tests::{observe_skips_a_suppressed_window_and_excluded_apps, restore_plan_after_the_clear_delay}`, the ignored live `concealed_write_live` (run on hornet: the type is on the item, `read` skips it, `read_any` has it, the restore landed 600 ms after a 300 ms clear), app `clipboard::tests::copy_envelope_shapes`, `effects::tests::copied_text_says_when_a_concealed_copy_clears`, host onepassword/otp updated.
- **Selection** (pit 19): `pal_core::selection::text(snapshot)` (`core/selection.text`, `selection.text()` in the SDK). macOS: `AXUIElementCreateSystemWide` then `AXFocusedUIElement` then `AXSelectedText` (`ax::element::Element::{system_wide, focused_element, selected_text}`); Linux: the primary selection over arboard. When the API answers nothing and `general.selection_snapshot` (new key, default `true`, `docs/config.md`) allows, `clipboard::selection_snapshot` saves the clipboard, sends Cmd+C (`platform::copy_key`, the paste key's twin), polls the change count for 300 ms, reads the text, and puts the clipboard back, inside a suppression window. Both need Accessibility on macOS; the refusal asks once per run like paste (`selection.rs` in the app). Snippets' `{selection}` reads it (`placeholders.ts` `Sources.selection`; the clipboard when null or refused, so a paste never fails on it); quicklinks has no `{selection}` today and is another agent's this round. **`Ctx.selection` on show: not done.** Measured on hornet from the agent shell (`selection_read_timing`, ignored): the first AX read took 198 ms, later ones 0 ms, and a hung app can hold the 300 ms messaging timeout; over the 20 ms budget, so the read stays on demand.
- **OCR** (pit 21): `pal_core::ocr::{image(path), bytes(data), available()}` (`core/ocr.image { path | data }`, `ocr.image()` / `ocr.available()` in the SDK). macOS: `objc2-vision` (new dependency, `VNImageRequestHandler initWithURL:options:`, `VNRecognizeTextRequest` accurate, language correction, `automaticallyDetectsLanguage` when the selector exists), lines in Vision's order; Linux: `tesseract <path> stdout` when installed, else `Unavailable`. A PDF is its first page through `pdftoppm -r 200 -singlefile` when installed, else `sips -s format png --resampleWidth 1600` on macOS (72 dpi upsampled; verified sips renders a PDF's first page). The test renders "HELLO PAL 483920" into a PNG through AppKit over `osascript -l JavaScript` (no font rasteriser ships in pal; skipped where that fails), asserts Vision reads both words, and repeats through a `sips`-made PDF: green on hornet. Clipboard History gained "Copy text from image" (`cmd+shift+t`, image entries) and Files "Copy text (OCR)" on images and PDFs; both copy the text as a copy of its own ("Copied text"), concealed when the palette's `ocr_concealed` setting says so (`conceal(text, 0)`, no clear). Searching history by an image's text (the pit card's other half) is not done: it needs OCR text stored per entry.
- **Large Type** (pit 22): `Effect.large_type: string` (effects.rs hides the panel first, then `large::show`). A fourth window `large` (`index.html?large`, `LargePage.tsx`; `large.rs`; `panel::large_{install,show,hide}` in both panels, the same never-ordered-out arrangement as the HUD but key while up, hidden on resign key / focus loss). The shell places it over the work area of the monitor under the cursor: full width, half the height, centred. Dismissal: any key or a click (the page invokes `large_hide`), 8 s (`HOLD`, a show counter like the HUD's), losing key. `app/src/large.ts` is pure: `fit(text, w, h)` (a one-row size against 86% of the window with a 0.56/0.62 advance ratio, or for a single line the larger of that and the area-derived wrapped size; 28 to 260 px), `isCode` (one token with a digit or symbol: monospace, so 0/O tell apart), `groupDigits` (5 to 16 digits: six as 3 3, nine as 3 3 3, else fours). `large.rs::prepare` trims, refuses blank, cuts at 400 characters. Tests: `app/src/__tests__/large.test.ts` (8), `large::tests::prepare_trims_cuts_and_refuses_blank`. **The `⌘L` "Show in Large Type" shell action is not wired**: it lives in `Launcher.tsx`, another agent's file this round; the `large_show { text }` Tauri command is registered for it (hides the panel, shows the text), so the Launcher needs one action (`{ id: "pal:large", title: "Show in Large Type", shortcut: "cmd+l", section: "View" }` when `current.name` is short) and one `run` case (`invoke("large_show", { text: current.name })`).
- **File contents** (pit 20): `extensions/files/content.ts` (pure: `parseQuery`, `contentArgv`, `snippetArgv`, `snippet`). A `'` or `content:` prefix searches contents only; a plain query lists the name matches and then an "In files" section of content matches minus what the names found, each subtitle the first matching line (`rg -n -m1`, else `grep -n -m 1`, eight files at a time). Backends: `mdfind -onlyin ... 'kMDItemTextContent == "*q*"cd'` on macOS (354 ms over `~/proj/pal` on hornet), `rg --files-with-matches` else `grep -rlI` on Linux; 1 s budget, killed after; `PAL_FILES_CONTENT` forces one (the tests use `grep` on their temp folder). `content_search` setting (default on) turns the second section off; the prefix always works. Tests: `host/test/extensions/files.test.ts` (+5: the helpers, the prefix, the second section, the dedupe, the setting, the OCR action).
- **Two things the scratch run found.** (1) A fourth window needs its label in `app/src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` or its page can neither `listen` nor `invoke`: the Large Type window was up (alpha 1, full width, CGWindowList) and blank until `large` joined the list. (2) `NSPasteboard` is one object and not thread-safe: with `core/clipboard.current` reading it on a bridge thread while the watcher polled, a launchd-restarted scratch instance crashed 3 s in, `objc_msgSend < -[NSPasteboard _updateTypeCacheIfNeeded] < -[NSPasteboard _typesAtIndex:combinesItems:]` on a `tokio-rt-worker` (`~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports/pal-2026-09-16-224236.ips`). Every pasteboard access in `clipboard.rs`'s macOS platform now holds one process-wide `Mutex` (`PASTEBOARD`; the poll's sleep stays outside it).
- Docs: `docs/extensions.md` (the effect fields, the three SDK calls), `docs/palettes.md` (Clipboard History, Files, Snippets, Verification Codes, 1Password), `docs/config.md` (`selection_snapshot`).

## Generate, Shortcuts, script commands (2026-09-16)

Three palettes from the triage (pit 11, 12, 25), each to the bundled standard: a tile, a keys table, store fields, a README, screenshots looked at, a host test.

- **Generate** (`extensions/generate/`, amber tile, md-dice_multiple): one input palette. The empty query lists a fresh value of every generator; a mode word narrows and takes parameters (`password 32 alnum`, `lorem 3p`, `number 1-6`); a transform mode works on the text after it or, with none, on the newest clipboard text. Enter copies, cmd+Enter pastes, `cmd+shift+c` copies a group; **regenerate is the shell's `cmd+r`**, not an action: `keys` never lists a shell key, and a fresh listing is exactly what Refresh does for an input palette. Values are made in `list` and kept in a map by row id for `pick` (calc's pattern); a pick against an older listing is a failure toast. The passphrase words are a copy of Wordle's 2551 public-domain answers (`words.txt`) rather than an import across extensions: a user directory named `wordle` replaces the bundled one, and a data file is cheaper to duplicate than to couple. `qr.ts` is a byte-mode encoder after Nayuki's qrcodegen (versions 1 to 40, four levels, the eight masks scored by the standard's penalties), verified two ways: matrices compared with segno at a forced mask (segno pads an extra zero byte when the stream ends on a byte boundary, so only a full-capacity case matches at its own mask), and PNGs decoded by Apple's Vision framework through a scratch Swift tool for versions 1 to 39 (Vision fails on v40 from segno too). One GF(2^8) bug was found that way (`z >>> 7`, not `>>> 8`). The SVG carries `width`/`height` of 224 so the detail pane's markdown image comes at that size instead of the pane's width. The QR row deviates from "Enter copies": Enter shows the code large (a `show` level), `cmd+c` copies the SVG, since the point of a QR is to scan it. JWT rows: header and payload with the compact JSON as the subtitle, a row per time claim, the expiry as a green or red tag, and an inert row saying the signature is not verified.
- **Shortcuts** (`extensions/shortcuts/`, violet tile, an SVG of the two stacked diamonds since no glyph says Shortcuts): `shortcuts list --folders` then `--folder-name <f> --show-identifiers` per folder plus `none`, five calls of about 10 ms each, the folder as the row's section and keyword. The row id is the shortcut's name (readable in `item_hotkeys`), a repeated name gets `(identifier)` appended; runs use the identifier. A run may outlast a pick (a shortcut can show UI and wait), so `pick` answers `{ hide }` and the outcome reaches the HUD through `effects.run` when the process ends (colors' pattern): the output's first line, "Done", or the tool's message. Output goes through `-o <tmpfile>` (no `--output-type`: forcing text fails on a shortcut that outputs an image; a non-UTF-8 file is a Done). Input is a temp file for `-i`. The Shortcuts database is TCC-guarded (`~/Library/Shortcuts` is "Operation not permitted" even to `ls`) and the CLI has no colour or icon, so rows wear the tile's mark. `PAL_SHORTCUTS_BIN` points the tests at a stand-in script; Linux gets one inert row and the run tests skip.
- **Script commands** (`extensions/scripts/commands.ts`, palette `scripts-commands`, "Script Commands"): a `commands` folder setting (`~/.config/pal/commands`), every executable with a `# @pal.title` tag in its first 60 lines is a row; `@raycast.*` tags are aliases (`compact` is `hud`, `fullOutput` is `show`, `argument1..3` JSON, `packageName` the section, `needsConfirmation`, `currentDirectoryPath`, `refreshTime`), so Raycast script commands drop in unchanged. Five modes: `silent`, `hud` (default), `show` (the whole output pushed back as a level with args `{ show, out }`, since `effects.run` refuses `show`), `list` (a level with args `{ list, values }` whose rows are the script's JSON lines, plain lines as a fallback; a row with `url` opens, `copy` copies, else the script runs again with `PAL_PICK`), `inline` (the first line as the subtitle, cached per `@pal.refresh`, a minute by default). The palette is **live**: the folder is watched (`fs.watch`, invalidating the parsed headers), but no channel lets an extension ask the core to relist, so live is what makes a saved file and an inline line show on the next open; the readdir and header parse cost nothing. A confirm on a command with arguments would fire before the form, so `@pal.confirm` applies only without `args` (the form is the confirmation). The row id is the file name. Shared with the v1 tables: `run` (process group, timeout, now with an optional piped stderr and the exit code), `parseLines`, the icon, accessory, detail and action mappers moved from `index.ts` into `shared.ts`, and `Settings`/`S()` with them. The manifest keeps no `palettes` key (the v1 tables are dynamic), so the commands palette's keys are in `store.actions`, the README and the docs rather than a `keys` table. Examples in `examples/commands/`.
- Tests: `host/test/extensions/generate.test.ts` (22), `shortcuts.test.ts` (8), and 11 more in `scripts.test.ts` (26 in the file). The sweep of the bundled manifests passes for all three; the three failures in the full run today (`hue` links, the colors picker tree) are another pass's files.

## Decided: Menu Bar Items, and the window verbs (2026-09-16)

Pit 3 (93, Search Menu Bar Items) and 14 (119, the window size and move commands), `notes/pit-triage.md`.

- **Menu bar** (`core/src/menubar.rs`, `pal_core::menubar::{items, items_of, press}`; `core/menubar.{items, press {pid, id}}` in `app/src-tauri/src/menubar.rs`, declared from bridge.rs like calendar; `extensions/menu-bar`, palette "Menu Bar Items", primary, live). The walk is over the front app's `AXMenuBar` (`NSWorkspace.frontmostApplication`, refused when that is pal), each bar item's `AXMenu`, its `AXMenuItem`s and a submenu item's own `AXMenu`, generic over a `Node` trait so a fake tree tests it (`walk`, `to_items`, `parse_id`, `shortcut`). One round trip per item: `AXUIElementCopyMultipleAttributeValues` reads role, title, enabled, `AXMenuItemCmdChar`, `CmdModifiers`, `CmdGlyph`, `MarkChar` and children at once (`ax::element::Element::attrs`; the wrapper also gained `string`/`boolean`/`element`/`elements`/`set_bool`/`press`/`menu_bar`/`fullscreen`, and `windows`/`title`/`minimized`/`close` now go through them); one more per submenu for the menu's children. Skipped: separators (no title), disabled items, the Apple menu. Caps: `MAX_DEPTH = 4` path segments (`File > Export > As > PDF` listed, its children not), `BUDGET = 150 ms` checked before every item. Ids are the path joined with ` > `, ` (2)` on a repeat (two `Window > Untitled`), and the elements found are kept per pid for `press`, which presses the very element listed (falling back to following the path by title, the nth match, after a restart) after checking it is still enabled; the app-side `press` hides the panel and waits `HIDE_SETTLE` (80 ms) first, the way `system.run` hides, so a sheet the item opens lands in the app (effects.rs is another agent's this round, so no new effect kind). **Measured on hornet** (`cargo run -p pal-core --example menubar -- <pid|name>`, the terminal's Accessibility): Finder in the background 40 items 18 ms, kitty 54 in 7 ms, Raycast 14 in 8 ms, Contexts 7 in 7 ms, Zoom 30 in 51 ms, Preview napping 20 in 224 ms then 22 in 29 ms, Chrome in the background 191 in 67 ms and in front 271 in 81 ms (cut at depth 4 by bookmark folders), TextEdit with its window key 144 in 11 ms warm and 119 ms the first read, and **22 in 188 ms just activated** (the app's main thread is busy for a moment; a second later 144 in 11 ms). No Xcode on hornet to measure. That cold read is why the core keeps the last listing per pid and answers a walk the budget cut short with the previous fuller one of the same app (`platform::items`, `Walk::cut_time`); a stale item is caught by the enabled check at press. Shortcuts are the `Action.shortcut` spelling the panel's key caps draw (`cmd+shift+e`, `cmd+up`, `f5`, `ctrl+cmd+space`): modifiers in macOS's order from the bit mask (shift 1, option 2, control 4, "no command" 8), the key from the Carbon `kMenu*Glyph` code when there is one (arrows, delete, escape, F-keys, page up, eject) else the character (Cocoa's `U+F700..` function keys named, control characters for return / tab / delete / escape); a bare letter with the "no command" bit is drawn `fn+f`, since Accessibility has no Globe bit and that is what Enter Full Screen carries. The row: the item's title, the path above it as the subtitle and as keywords (with the app's name), the app's icon (`{ app }`), a `✓` text accessory when checked, `{ keys }` as the key caps (the panel draws that accessory kind, `app/src/ui/types.ts`; protocol.ts does not list it yet, so the extension casts), the top menu as the section. A pick more than 2 s after the listing, or of an id it lacks (`pal run`, an item hotkey), reads the menus again first so the press lands in the app in front now; a `null` action is Press (`pal run` sends none). Linux and no-Accessibility: one row saying why, Enter on the permission row opens the pane.
- **Window verbs** (`Layout` in `core/src/windows/layout.rs` grew `MaximizeHeight`, `MaximizeWidth`, `Larger`, `Smaller`, `MoveLeft/Right/Up/Down`, `Fullscreen`, `Minimize`, `Unminimize`; `ALL` is 31, ordered for the palette). The frame ones are `target` arithmetic like the rest: maximize one side keeps the other; larger / smaller scale each side by `RESIZE_PERCENT = 10` about the centre, capped at the area, then pushed back inside it where they fit (`inside`); the moves add `Options.step` (new, default 32 px, the `step` setting) and clamp to the area on an axis where the window fits, leaving a wider window alone; all of them go through `set_frame` and the `restore` memory. The three state verbs are not frames (`Layout::is_frame`): `apply` flips `AXFullScreen` (`windows::fullscreen`, a toggle: Hyprland `focuswindow` then `dispatch fullscreen 0` since that dispatcher takes no window, Sway `fullscreen toggle`, X11 `wmctrl -b toggle,fullscreen`), minimises and remembers the id, or unminimises, which is `focus` on every backend (restore is part of raising already) on the window `to_unminimize` picks: the one pal's `minimize` last put away while still minimised, else the frontmost minimised one in `list()`; the row says "Last minimized window" and has no Apply to…, and is not offered for a picked window. `windows.{unminimize, fullscreen}` joined the bridge. The extension's `LAYOUTS` is typed `Verb = WindowLayout | the eleven new names` and casts into the `layout` effect: **`WindowLayout` and `WindowLayoutOptions.step` in `sdk/src/protocol.ts` are open for whoever merges that file** (the same note as `{ keys }` above and the tile icons before). Diagrams in `icons.ts` for each (arrows out of / into the corners, a cell with an arrow beside it, a tall or wide stripe, the filled screen, the Dock sliver with an arrow). `item_hotkeys` for the repeating ones are documented in `docs/palettes.md` (larger `ctrl+alt+=`, the moves `ctrl+alt+shift+arrows`, fullscreen, minimize); none bound by default, as before.
- **Verified on hornet.** Core, from the agent shell on a TextEdit document opened in the background and floated in yabai (a tiled window snaps back: the first run showed every frame returned as the 1800x1131 tile): `set-frame 200,150 900x600`, then larger 990x660 at 155,120, smaller 891x594 at 205,153, move left/right/up/down by 32, maximize height 891x1131 at 205,38, maximize width 1800x1131 at 0,38, center, restore back to 900x600 at 200,150; move_left with gap 100 clamped at 100; minimize (listed `min`), unminimize (listed `on`), fullscreen on (1800x1130 at 0,39, the notch band kept) and off (900x600 back), close. Through a scratch instance (`target/scratch-ax`, `io.cagdas.pal.ax`, own config and data dir, bar off, the resources staged beside the binary; Accessibility granted, the startup listing walked Chrome's 271 items): `pal run 'menu-bar/menu-bar/File > New'` with TextEdit in front pressed it (`core/menubar.press 85.69ms`, an Untitled window appeared, closed by pal), and `pal run window-management/window-management/{larger,move_right,maximize_height,restore,minimize} --args '{"id":…}'` on the scratch window moved it as the core run had. Two things seen on the way: bringing TextEdit to the front got its document window closed within a couple of seconds every time (Cagdas at the machine, or an automation; the verification went to background windows), and the instance's log is `~/Library/Logs/pal/pal.log` for every profile, so a scratch instance interleaves with the daily one there (`pal_core::log::capture` ignores `XDG_DATA_HOME`; left alone).
- Tests: `menubar::tests` (the fake tree: paths, skips, shortcuts, ids and repeats, the depth and time cuts, every glyph and modifier spelling), `layout::tests` (+4: the maximised side, larger / smaller about the centre and against the edges, the moves and their clamps, the state layouts computing nothing), `windows::restore_tests::unminimize_takes_the_last_one_pal_minimised_else_the_front_minimised_window`, `bridge::tests` (the route). Host: `menu-bar.test.ts` (4: meta, rows, press and the HUD, the hint rows and the relist), `window-management.test.ts` updated (31 rows, `step`, Unminimize's shape, the arrange level without it). Gallery: `shots/menu-bar.json` (a TextEdit menu through the real extension), `window-management.json` refreshed plus a `5-verbs` shot; the screenshots re-rendered and looked at. Docs: `docs/palettes.md` (Menu Bar Items, the verbs, `step`, the hotkeys), both READMEs, `pal.json` of both.

## Decided: the root beyond the index (2026-09-16)

Pit 1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 9, 15, 24 of `notes/pit-triage.md`'s top 25, in that order: the root list is no longer only what the index matched.

- **Inline results** (pit 1): a palette declares `match` (a regex, a regex source, or a predicate; the manifest carries the string under `palettes.<key>.match` for the store) and `inline: true` (`PaletteBase` in `sdk/src/protocol.ts`; `paletteMeta` merges them into the meta as `inline`/`match`, `inlineMatches` in `sdk/src/manifest.ts` is the one place that decides). The host matches, never the core: a predicate cannot cross the wire, so the page asks the host's `inline { query }` (host.ts `sections`, every accepting palette's `list(query, { inline: true })` at once, `ROOT_TIMEOUT_MS` 1.5 s each, five rows per palette, a failed or late one logged and left out). The page (`Launcher.tsx`) asks `ROOT_DEBOUNCE` 120 ms after the last keystroke, after the local hits painted, cancels the ask on the next keystroke and drops a reply for a query that moved on (`extra.key`); the rows go first, under the palette's title (`rootHits`). Picks are ordinary: the row's source is the palette, so calc's `pick` finds its `last` table and copies. The bundled matchers: calc (`matches`, a digit next to an operator, a unit or a word, or a date word; a bare number never), colors' convert (`parse(q)`; the first four notations, Enter opens the picker), quicklinks (a web address, one Open row, `open:<url>` ids), files (`PATH_RE`: a path completes rather than searches, `pathRows`, inside the palette too). Trade-off, measured: `1password` reads as a unit and costs a mathjs parse that answers nothing; a name-shaped word never wakes calc.
- **Fallback rows** (pit 2): three kinds, one section "Use “q” with" (`app/src-tauri/src/fallback.rs`, the `fallback(q)` command the page calls with the inline ask). The shell's two rows are the synthetic source `pal/fallback`, never indexed, picked by id here: `web` (`general.search_engine`, `{query}` form-encoded) and `url` (`as_url`: a scheme as typed, or a dotted host with a letters-only last label under `https://`; never a bare word, a number or an email). An "Ask <title>" row per palette with `fallback: "ask"` (a `true` or a title template in the code or manifest) carries `push { extension, palette, query }` in the row, and the page opens the level with the query typed without a pick (`Effect.push.query` is the same door for extensions). A palette with a `fallback(query)` function (`fallback: "rows"` in the meta) answers rows through the host's `fallback` request (quicklinks: every `{query}` link filled). `general.fallbacks` orders by id (`web`, `url`, palette ids), the unnamed after in load order; `fallbacks_always` puts them under the hits too. Frecency never remembers them (`fallback::inert`). Inline rows count as results: with a calc answer on screen no fallback shows.
- **Alias and space** (pit 4): `aliasTarget` in Launcher.tsx, the alias (now on `SourceView.alias`), else the palette name, else a one-word title, each only when exactly one palette answers; never a `pal/*` source or a view palette. Only a word typed forward jumps (`q.length > query.length` and no space before), so a deletion never does; the jump is `enter(key, undefined, rest)`, a normal push with the crumb, the rest typed into the level. `general.alias_space` (default on). A pasted `calc 2+2` (`type()`) lands the same way.
- **Reset ranking** (pit 5): `Frecency::forget` now answers whether there was history; `frecency_forget(source, id)` in index.rs emits the index event so the list re-ranks; the action `pal:forget` ("Reset ranking for this item", section pal) on an indexed or palette row at the root or an indexed level (never a tip, a fallback, a "more" row or a Now row; a Frequent row yes), the toast says which of the two happened.
- **Search history** (pit 7): in the frecency store, since it is the same memory as the per-item queries: `Frecency::{record_history, history, clear_history}`, `history: Vec<String>` in `frecency.json` (`MAX_HISTORY` 20, newest first, once each, trimmed as typed; a file without the key loads as none). Recorded in `index::pick` for every root pick with a query (a calc result or a fallback row included: the query is what is recalled) when `general.search_history`. The page (`recall` in Launcher.tsx): Up at the top of an empty root fetches once per show and walks newest first, Down walks back to an empty box, typing ends the walk, Escape clears as always; the list under a recalled query searches as usual. "Clear Search History" is a pal command (`commands::HISTORY_CLEAR`).
- **The empty root** (pit 9): `rootHits("", …)` orders Welcome, the suggestions, Frequent, Recent Files (`files/recent`, the one palette named in the page), then the rest as the core ordered. Suggestions are extension-driven: `suggest: () => Item[]` on a palette (`suggest: true` in the meta), the host's `suggest` request runs them all on the 1.5 s budget, the page asks on every show of the empty root (`shown()` on the handle, the query emptying, an index bump) and orders the sections by `general.now` (config ids); a row's own `section` names its section, else "Now". Bundled: calendar Today (the strip's `nextEvent`, a Today row with Join first), timer (the most urgent one), media (the playing track), clipboard (below). Frequent is the core's (`index::frequent`): `Frecency::top` over eight times the cap, the keys that name an indexed row of a real palette (palette rows and pal's commands are picked too but stay in their sections; a welcome row is not history), `FREQUENT_MAX` 5, carried as `HitView.group`, and `dedupe` drops them from their own sections. The welcome rows keep the top on a fresh install.
- **Clipboard rows** (pit 15): a second palette of the clipboard extension, `rows` ("Clipboard", `clipboard-rows`; `extensions/clipboard/{rows,now}.ts`), input, with `suggest`.
