Privacy first: the rows are built from `clipboard.current()` (`Clipboard::current`: the pasteboard read as the watcher reads it, hashed, looked up in history by `find_by_hash`), so a concealed copy, one from an excluded app, one over the cap or one deleted from history is not current either; the page-title fetch (`fetchTitle`, 1 s, 64 KB, http(s) only, `fetch_titles` setting, one fetch per url per host life) is the only network call. `analyzeText` reads a text as everything it is at once (a web address, a colour through `../colors/color.ts`, existing paths and file lists, an email, a phone (ten bare digits are a unix time first), JSON, an expression through a 40-line evaluator of its own rather than mathjs in this bundle, a number with its bases, a unix or ISO time, hex or base64 that decodes to readable text, UPS/USPS/FedEx/DHL numbers, a git sha or `owner/repo#12`), `rows` builds one row per reading with the one action it is for on Enter and the rest behind, every row ending in Hide (`⌘⇧H`, storage `rows:hidden` = `id:at`, so a re-copy of the same text brings the section back). The QR code is `qrcode-generator` (MIT, no deps, in `extensions/clipboard/package.json`; bun inlines it) as an SVG data url on the row and large in the detail or a `show` level; Save to Desktop writes the SVG. Private window: `privateArgv` over the installed browsers (Chrome, Brave, Edge, Chromium, Vivaldi, Firefox; `private_browser` prefers one; Safari has no switch). Save as snippet pushes `snippets` with `args.create`, which the snippets palette answers with one row and the form pre-filled. OCR rows appear when `ocr.available()`. `general.now` names `clipboard-rows` last.
- **Pop to root** (pit 24): `general.pop_to_root` is `PopToRoot` (`core/src/config/mod.rs`: `Always | Never | After(secs)`, written as `"always" | "never" | "after 90s"`, `2m`/`1h` read too, schema says string), default `After(90)`. `pop.rs` notes the hide from both panels' `hide` and `show_in` puts `keep` in the `pal://shown` payload; the page then calls `shown()` (suggestions and history asked again) instead of `reset()`. A palette hotkey opens its palette either way; before the first hide of a run nothing is kept.
- Config keys added to `General` (`fallbacks`, `fallbacks_always`, `search_engine`, `alias_space`, `search_history`, `pop_to_root`, `now`), the schema regenerated; `settings_general` hands the page `[general]` and it follows `pal://config` (`usePrefs` in core.ts). Settings pages untouched (config now; Settings can reorder `fallbacks` and `now` later).
- Tests: `index::tests::frequent_rows_lead_the_empty_root_and_leave_their_sections`, `fallback::tests` (5), `pop::tests` (2), `frecency::tests::search_history_is_ordered_deduped_capped_and_persisted`, `clipboard::tests::find_by_hash_is_what_current_looks_up`; `host/test/root-sections.test.ts` (8: the meta merge, `inlineMatches`, the three host requests with a slow and a throwing palette), `clipboard-rows.test.ts` (13), and the inline/fallback cases in `calc`, `colors`, `quicklinks`, `files`, `snippets`; `app/src/__tests__/root-sections.test.tsx` (9: `rootHits` both ways, `aliasTarget`, the jump, the history walk, an Ask row's Enter, the reset action).
- Docs: `docs/getting-started.md` ("The root: inline answers, fallbacks, and the empty list"), `docs/config.md` (the seven keys), `docs/extensions.md` (the three hooks, `Effect.push.query`), `docs/palettes.md` (Calculator, Clipboard, Quicklinks, Files, Colors, Timer, Calendar, Now Playing).

## Decided: tela (2026-09-16)

- `extensions/tela`: nine palettes (`search` and `research` input, `pages`, `spaces`, `new-page`, `decks`, `sheets`, `backlinks` indexed with the manifest's ttl, `comments` live; an `append` palette shipped on the 16th and went the next day, below) and the bar item `tela/inbox`, over one client (`api.ts`) and one data layer (`data.ts`). No `daily` palette: tela has no journal concept (checked against the router: `backend/internal/api/router.go`); its per-user scratchpad is Quick Notes (`POST /api/users/me/quick-notes`, the `/n` shortcut), which stands in at the top of Pages.
- **Two transports, one instance.** REST under `<base>/api/` for lists, pages, search, backlinks, notifications and the writes; the MCP endpoint `<base>/api/mcp` (Streamable HTTP, the Go SDK's stateful handler: `initialize` answers a `Mcp-Session-Id`, a `tools/call` without one is refused, answers are `text/event-stream` with the message on the last `data:` line) for `research` alone, the one thing REST has no shape for: `POST /api/rag/ask` is the LLM answer (credits, `llm_disabled` 503 without a model) and `GET /api/rag/search` bare hits, while the `research` tool returns the assembled `context` (`[n] Space › path › Title` blocks, full page bodies where they matter), `sources`, `disagreements`, `low_confidence`, `considered`, `truncated`. `tool()` opens the session once per process, re-opens it once on a 404/400 or a "session" error, and drops it on a settings change (the session is the token's). Verified live: 3 requests for the first ask (initialize, initialized, tools/call), 8 sources of 14 in 3.2 s.
- **Ids.** A page is `page:<n>` everywhere: the list row, the view it opens as (`View.id`), the form that comments on it, so one `pickPage` answers all three and a pick after a restart looks the page up by id. Spaces `space:<n>`, notifications `notif:<n>`, questions `ask:<text>`, the research view `research`, the commands `cmd:<name>`.
- **Research view** (`render.ts`): flags first (an elevated card with an amber badge for `low_confidence`, a red one with tela's note for `disagreements`), a count line (`8 sources of 14 considered; + asks for more`), the sources as numbered rows (a `tile` per number, the selected one solid accent on an elevated card) and the selected source's excerpt from the grounding rendered inside its card through `md.ts` (headings shifted two levels, 1600 chars, 220 nodes). `keys: "actions"`: `down`/`j`, `up`/`k`, hidden digit actions `go1..go9`, `+`/`=` re-asks with twice the limit (cap 40), `n` sets `View.input` for the next question (the `ask` and `cancel` actions exist only while it is open, since `checkView` refuses a hidden action without a key and a listed Cancel would sit in ⌘K otherwise), `cmd+shift+o` opens tela's Ask page. Enter on a source opens it in tela; `cmd+enter` is `show` with the page's markdown (a detail-only level, so the research stays underneath) rather than `{ view }`, which from a view would replace the tree. Questions live in `storage` (`questions`, twelve).
- **`md.ts`** is the reusable half: `frontmatter`, `inline` (marks reduced, links collected, a whole-run bold/italic/code said), `parseBlocks` (headings, paragraphs with soft and hard breaks, fenced code, `> [!TYPE]` callouts, quotes, tela's `:::quote{cite}` and `:::tabs` / other directives unwrapped, `<details><summary>`, nested bullet, ordered and task lists, pipe tables with alignment and escaped pipes, images, rules, html comments and bare tags dropped), `render` to a column stack (callouts elevated with a coloured badge, code and quotes sunken, tables as `text` columns with widths scaled to the room, list marks in a fixed column, `↗ label` runs under a block for its links, a node budget with a closing line, `shift` for excerpts, `dropTitle` for a leading `#` that repeats the page's title), `outline`, `plain` (what tela anchors a comment on), `excerpt`. The limit met: a view `text` is one run and a row stack does not wrap, so inline bold/italic/code inside a paragraph is flattened to its words; a paragraph that is one such run keeps `weight: semibold` / `style: mono`. Diagram fences (`excalidraw`, `mermaid`, `defter-style`) are a placeholder line. 9 tests of their own (`tela-md.test.ts`).
- **Rows.** Pages at the root: four command rows (New tela page, Search tela, Ask tela unless `research` is off, Quick Notes), favourites (`/api/users/me/favorites`, star icon) then `/api/recent-changes` sectioned by space with the author as an accessory (the subtitle is the breadcrumb alone, the section already says the space); pushed with `{ space }` the space's `tree=1` listing flattened with breadcrumbs, sectioned by top-level page, `props.deck` / `props.sheet` tagged. Spaces count pages from one `/api/pages/all` (278 rows live) rather than an overview call per space. Decks and Sheets are the trees of every space filtered on the flag (no listing endpoint carries props; 14 parallel tree calls, 540 ms live, an hour's ttl); a deck's pane resolves `GET /api/pages/{id}/deck/cover` by hand (`redirect: manual`) to the public content-addressed asset and leads with it. Search hits carry tela's `<mark>` passage: unmarked on the row, as bold in the pane. Comments are the notifications of type `mention` and `comment_reply` (his instance's 181 unread are 94% `user_registered` admin noise, which the inbox never counts); a root comment needs an anchor triple in the page's plain text (`comment_no_anchor` otherwise), so the form's second field is the run, the first line of `plain()` by default.
- **Keys.** `cmd+i` was asked for the outline and is the shell's detail toggle (the polish pass rule), so Outline is `cmd+shift+o`; Copy markdown from the page view `cmd+shift+c`; the rest per the key tables in `pal.json`, which list every shortcut the code declares.
- **Hints** (`failure`): no address, no token (Enter on tela's `/settings?tab=api-keys`, or pal's settings), a 401 ("expired or was revoked"), a 403, a 503 `rag_disabled` (Search still works), anything else with `cmd+r tries again`. The bar item is hidden on `AuthError`, an error on a 401 (the core marks it stale). `cached` keeps stale rows through an outage, never through a missing or refused token.
- **Live check** (his instance through the host harness, read-only, the token from `~/Sync/.secrets/tela.env`, nothing printed but counts): 14 spaces with counts (330 ms), 54 root rows (4 commands, 3 favourites, 7 space sections), 1 hit for `pal` with a 5.6 KB pane and an 84-node view, 3 backlinks, 14 decks and 2 sheets across the trees (540 ms), one deck cover resolved, 0 addressed notifications so the bar item hid, research 8 sources of 14 considered in 3.2 s. Writes were exercised against the mock only.
- Tests: `host/test/extensions/tela.test.ts` (27 after the append removal, against `tela-mock.ts`: a Bun server for the REST routes and the MCP transport with a session id, an event-stream answer, a lost-session re-open, the `rag_disabled` tool error), `tela-md.test.ts` (9). Fixture `extensions/tela/fixture.ts` runs the extension through the harness against the same mock (a few pages added) and writes `app/src/gallery/shots/{tela,bar-tela}.json`; six panel shots and four bar shots rendered and looked at, light and dark.

## Decided: tela, second pass (2026-09-17)

- **Search tela missed what was typed.** Reported: inside the palette `onboard` showed nothing while `onboarding` showed rows, and the harness answered `onboard` with 25 hits. Reproduced on a scratch instance (`io.cagdas.pal.tela`, own config and data dir, the host and the extension from the repo, a `DBG` line per list request and per search call): one `pal://open/tela/search?q=onboar` while the panel was showing produced **ten `list` calls for the one query in 1.1 s, none of which searched**. Two halves. (1) `Launcher.tsx`: the query effect had `version` in its deps, which `useSources` bumps on every `pal://index` event, and a show lands one event per palette listing (lazy palettes on their first show, live ones on every show, a refresh pass, a pick's frecency bump) over several seconds (github/prs 3 s, onepassword 8 s on his profile); an input palette's rows never come from the index, so each of those was another host `list` for the same query. (2) `extensions/tela/index.ts` `searchRows`: one global `searchSeq`, a 250 ms `Bun.sleep`, then `if (seq !== searchSeq) return lastSearch`: every call restarted the wait, so while events came closer than 250 ms apart no call ever reached the request, and every superseded call answered the rows of the last search that did complete (or `[]` before any had). The panel drops a reply older than its newest request, so what stayed on screen was whatever it showed before the keystroke: the previous prefix's "Nothing found" (tela's FTS matches whole words, `onboar` has 0 hits and `onboard` 25), or, entered by alias or link, the root's rows (`key->paint "onboar" (200)` in the log). The core's request ids reached the host in order in every trace, so the echo was never itself painted; the loss was the search never running for as long as the events kept coming.
- **Fix, both halves.** The Launcher keys the query effect on `indexVersion`, which is `version` at the root and in an indexed palette and `0` for a level the host lists (an input palette, a push with args): those list per keystroke and never again on an index event (`app/src/__tests__/input-relist.test.tsx`). The extension keeps the wait (the panel does not debounce input palettes, so it belongs there) but per query: `newest = { q, rows, done }`; a call for the query already waiting joins its promise (no restart), a call a newer query overtook resolves to that newer query's rows once they land (the panel has moved on and drops it; nothing ever answers an older query's list to a newer one), `lastSearch` is gone. `tela.test.ts` types `spawns` letter by letter with the calls overlapping plus one repeat mid-wait: one request, for the last query, and every reply is its rows.
- **Append is gone**: the `append` palette, "Append to page" on the page rows and in the page view, `appendForm`/`saveAppend`, `appendToPage`/`quickNotes` in `data.ts`, Quick Notes' append (Open Quick Notes stays), the mock's `PATCH` and quick-notes routes, the keys and store entries in `pal.json`, the README, `docs/palettes.md`, the gallery fixture re-run and the six shots re-rendered. Cagdas: "append to page is nonsense for pal, that's an MCP capability". The panel reads, opens, comments and creates; a page's body is edited through tela's MCP.
- Seen on the way, not fixed: entering a palette keeps the root's `found` on screen until the palette's first reply (the 200 root rows painted inside Search tela); and the CLI's `open` link shows the panel twice on macOS ("show undone by the activation; showing again").

## Decided: Hue (2026-09-16)

- **What landed** (`extensions/hue`): eight palettes (`rooms`, `lights`, `scenes` as lists; `light` and `setup` as views; `sensors`, `automations`, `entertainment`), the bar item `hue/home`, three links (`toggle`, `scene`, `off`), all over CLIP v2 on the LAN: `api.ts` (the client, discovery, press-link, the SSE reader, the certificate peek), `model.ts` (one `Home` of every bridge's resources, the event merge, the derived rooms/lights/scenes/sensors/automations/areas, slugs), `color.ts` (xy to and from sRGB with the gamut triangle clamp, the Planckian locus for a mirek's white, HSV for the plane, lux), `rows.ts`, `render.ts` (the light/room view and the setup view as trees), `png.ts` (a disc for the bar), `sample.ts` (the home the fixture and the mock share), `fixture.ts`. The v1 API is used for exactly one thing v2 lacks, pairing (`POST /api`).
- **Live means the model, not polling.** One `GET /clip/v2/resource` per bridge at load, then the bridge's event stream (`/eventstream/clip/v2`, `text/event-stream`, one `data:` line per block carrying an array of `{ type: update|add|delete, data: [partial resources] }`, parsed by hand in `parseSse`) patches the map (`Home.merge`: objects merged, arrays replaced, an `update` for an unknown id ignored, an `add` inserted). Reconnect with backoff 1 s to 60 s and a whole re-read after a gap. So a listing, a view or a bar render is drawn from memory; the rows and the bar follow the Hue app or a switch. A write is one `PUT`, applied to the model at once (`applyLocally`, with `effects.effect` written as `effects.status`, the name the bridge reports it under) and confirmed by the stream; `put` keeps one in-flight body per resource and sends the newest queued state after Hue's gap (100 ms per light, 1 s per group), so a held arrow is one command and a room toggle followed by "Turn on" is two, a second apart. Rate guidance from Hue's docs; the coalescing is tested.
- **What the view cannot do**: redraw without a key. There is no channel to push a tree into an open level (`effects.run` refuses `view`); the model is current, so the next key's tree is right, and the README and the palette doc say so. A `view/update` notification would close that gap; it belongs to the shell.
- **Ids are slugs**, unique per kind (`room:living-room`, `scene:living-room/relax`, `light:lamp-2` for a second Lamp; `slug()` folds accents and Turkish dotless i), so a deep link and an `item_hotkeys` entry read without a UUID; a rename changes the id. The bridge's UUIDs never leave the model.
- **TLS, pinned.** Every request is https with Bun's `tls: { ca, checkServerIdentity }`. `ca` is Signify's `root-bridge` CA (`cert.ts`; from the developer site's "Using HTTPS" page, which is behind a login, via github.com/callionica/hue's copy of 2021-09-23; sha256 `F0:BD:8E:65:…`; it verified the owner's bridge with `openssl s_client -CAfile`, return code 0, and the extension's `config()` read that bridge over it in 114 ms) plus the leaf pinned at pairing, which is what makes an older self-signed bridge work; `checkServerIdentity: () => undefined` since the CN is the bridge id, not the address. Measured on Bun 1.4.2: `checkServerIdentity`'s return value and throws are ignored by `fetch`, `rejectUnauthorized: false` skips the callback altogether, and `getPeerCertificate(true)` gives no issuer chain; so the CN check is done at pairing over `node:tls` and the chain check is the protection. `insecure = true` is the escape hatch and is documented as such.
- **Pairing is a view with a background task**, since a pick may not wait thirty seconds: `startPairing` polls `POST /api` every second until the window closes, the view shows the seconds left as of its render (Enter checks at once), and success stores the bridge, connects it and calls `effects.run({ push: setup })` to bring the panel back on "Paired"; a miss is a HUD line. The key lives in storage (`bridges`, with the client key and the pinned PEM); the SDK has no `settings.set`, so the paired view tells the user to copy the key (`c`) into the secret setting for the keychain, and a `bridge` + `application_key` pair in the settings wins over storage for that address. Discovery is the cloud endpoint and mDNS in parallel, then `GET /api/0/config` per candidate; `dns-sd -B`/`-L` never exit, so `runFor` stops them 400 ms after their last line (5.0 s down to 0.8 s on hornet).
- **Rooms' colour**: a mean of a blue strip and two whites is grey, so a room's tile is its lit lights' colours as stripes (an SVG data url, faded by the grouped brightness, an outline when off) and the one colour that stands for the room (the bar's dot, the room view's tile) is the most saturated of them. The bar's dot is a PNG (`png.ts`, 36 px, deflate through `node:zlib`) because the menu bar decodes PNG only; the gallery's bar page draws glyphs only, so the bar fixture shows the bulb in amber and the captions say so.
- **The real bridge, seen but untouched**: `discovery.meethue.com` answered `ecb5fafffe8c0231` at 192.168.1.25, mDNS `Hue Bridge - 8C0231` (`ecb5fa8c0231.local`), `GET /api/0/config` says BSB002, api 1.78.0, sw 1978293000; the certificate's CN is the id, issuer `root-bridge`. No press-link was done and no light was changed (Cagdas holds Hue through Home Assistant today, which has its own key); `/clip/v2` and the event stream answer 403 with an HTML page for an unknown key, which the client maps to "rejected the application key" before parsing. Everything else is verified against the mock.
- **Tests** (`host/test/extensions/hue.test.ts`, 34, 292 expects; `hue-mock.ts` is a Bun https server with an openssl self-signed certificate answering the CLIP v2 routes, press-link armed by `press()`, the stream fed by every PUT and by `change()`, plus the cloud discovery endpoint): the maths (xy round trips, gamut B clamping a deep blue and sRGB red to the triangle, mirek/kelvin, the locus near D65, HSV, lux, the root CA parsing with the recorded fingerprint), the model (slugs and `-2`, lights with rooms/effects/reachability, rooms and zones with grouped lights, the aggregate, scenes' swatches from a palette or from actions, sensors' text and batteries, automations, areas, the merge), the trees through `checkView`, the parsers (SSE, `dns-sd`, `avahi`), then over the wire: the setup row before pairing, discovery and pairing with the pinned leaf and the `pal#host` devicetype, every palette's rows, the light view's keys as PUTs (coalescing under the gap, presets, transition, colour on the plane inside the gamut, scope to the grouped light, effects), the room view, a change from elsewhere reaching rows, view and a bar push with no GET, the bar item and All off, `main_room`/`bar_scenes`, the links, forgetting a bridge closing the stream; a settings-only bridge, a wrong key as the hint row and a `stale` item, the unpinned self-signed certificate refused with the `insecure` hint, a dead address within the timeout.
- **Screenshots**: six panel shots (rooms, the light view, the room view with the scenes row focused, scenes, sensors, the press-link countdown) and the four bar strips from `sample.ts`, looked at in light and dark; the two view shots and the setup are `raw` (gradients). Fixed on looking: the room tile as stripes (was a grey mean), the big tile dimmed less, the presets as named tiles, the plane's marker hidden while the light shows no colour (it sat on the label), single-swatch scene strips as wide as five-swatch ones, a device's battery on its first sensor row only.
- **Open**: a view push channel (above); the gallery's bar strip drawing `{ image }` icons; `PaletteMeta.filters` is static, so Lights has sections rather than a room filter; the Linux mDNS path (`avahi-browse -rpt`) is parser-tested only; a bridge's certificate renewal after pairing would still verify (root-signed), an old self-signed one that changes needs a re-pair, and the row says so.

## Decided: Spotify (2026-09-16)

- **What landed** (`extensions/spotify`): seven palettes and one bar item over the Web API. `search` (input: Tracks, Artists, Albums, Playlists, Podcasts, Episodes as sections, five each, the cover as the icon; Enter plays, `cmd+enter` queues a track or lists a playlist's or album's tracks, `cmd+l` likes, `cmd+s` shuffles a playlist, `cmd+o` opens `spotify:` uris, `cmd+c` copies the link), `playlists` (Yours / Followed by owner id, a drill-in level per playlist or album whose rows play from that point inside the context), `library` (filters Liked Songs newest first, Recently played deduplicated, Top tracks and Top artists `short_term`), `devices` (live: a row per device with the kind's md glyph, volume and `active`; Enter transfers, `cmd+enter` without playing; Volume up / down / Mute rows on the active one), `queue` (live: the playing track, then the queue numbered; Enter is N `next` calls, since the Web API has no remove-from-queue), `commands` (primary: play or pause, next, previous, like, lyrics, sign out, and a "Play <name>" row per `pinned` entry by name or `spotify:playlist:` link, a hint row for one not among the user's playlists), and `now-playing`, the lyrics view. Rows carry `<kind>:<id>` ids, so a pick after a restart derives the uri and the link from the id alone (no lookup table needed). Store: 6 panel shots plus 3 bar strips, README, keys tables.
- **Auth is PKCE against the user's own app**, no bundled client id: pal has no Spotify app of its own to bundle, and a client id in the bundle would be a shared identity for every install. `client_id` and `redirect_port` (27182) are settings; "Sign in to Spotify" (a hint row wherever a listing needs a token, and the view's Enter) starts `Bun.serve` on `127.0.0.1:<port>` for `/callback`, answers `{ open: authorizeUrl, toast }` (the panel stays up; the browser has the consent page), checks `state`, exchanges the code with `code_verifier`, stores the tokens, tells the HUD and asks the bar item to render again; then the listener stops, after a 250 ms linger, since `server.stop(true)` under a reply in flight cut the browser off (the mock caught it: "Unable to connect" on the very request that succeeded). The listener lives for one pending sign-in (5 min at most). The real `accounts.spotify.com/authorize` was read once with curl over the url the code builds (a bogus client id): a 303 to the login page with every parameter carried in `continue=`, so the parameter set is accepted; nothing was opened in the browser. The refresh token lives in `storage` (`auth`): the SDK has no `settings.set` to write a `keychain:` reference through the secret setting, so `<data dir>/pal/storage/spotify.json` holds it as plain JSON (user-only file); documented in the README with the Sign Out row that deletes it. Renewed a minute early and once on a 401, one refresh in flight at a time, the rotated refresh token kept; `invalid_grant` signs out.
- **The API client** (`api.ts`): one `api()` with the bearer; 401 → one refresh and retry; 429 → a `Retry-After` under 2 s waited out inside the call, a longer one remembered in `limitedUntil` and every call until then refused locally (`RateLimited`, "try again in N s"); a fetch failure is `Offline`; Spotify's `{ error: { status, message, reason } }` becomes `ApiError` with `noDevice` (404 `NO_ACTIVE_DEVICE`) and `premium` (403). Listings turn these into one hint row each (`failure`), the view into a status tree with the fix as Enter (sign in, open Spotify, settings, try again).
- **One playback state** (`live`): `/me/player` read through `readLive(maxAge, fresh)`; the position between reads is `progress_ms + (now - at)` while playing (`positionOf`); an action patches the state at once (`patch`, held `HOLD_MS` 1.2 s against the next non-fresh read) so the tree after a key is right before Spotify confirms; a view opening anew, a `show` / `wake` / `media` render and Try again read fresh; `keep` after a popover action (reason `update`) and the timer (`every`, `SYNC_MS` 5 s) take the held or cached state. A skip waits 350 ms and reads fresh (Spotify reports the new track late).
- **The lyrics view** (`view.ts`, pure; the gallery renders fixture states with it): two layouts, `wide` (panel: the 208 px cover at the left, the track, artist and album, a progress row with the times, badges, the lyrics beside it: 3 before, the current, 3 after) and `compact` (the popover at 420: the cover in a header row, 2 before and 2 after). Lines are `stack` rows keyed `l<index>` with `move: true` (`enter: slide-up` for a coming line, `fade` otherwise), so the app's FLIP slides them up as the song advances and fades the one leaving; the current line is `xl semibold` in the text colour, before `lg muted`, after `lg faint`; `minHeight` per line holds the column still; before the first line an `intro` node shows a note and stays as the first "before" line so the key moves rather than re-enters. Unsynced lyrics are a window over the plain text at the position's share of the song; no lyrics is one line and `f` (lrclib's search page); instrumental says so. **The tint**: the vocabulary has no tinted surface, so the cover's dominant colour is a `gradient` node flush under the cover (16 px, the colour at alpha 0.36 fading down; a 6 px band across in compact) and the tag colour of the progress bar (hue bands: red, amber for the oranges, green, teal, blue, violet, pink; grey under 12% saturation); badges stay grey (an orange cover made `repeat` look like an alarm in red). The sampler (`color.ts`): the 300 px JPEG the view shows anyway, decoded by `jpeg-js` (the extension's one dependency, `package.json` like calc's), every pixel binned by hue into 24 buckets weighted by saturation and mid brightness, greys only when nothing has colour. Keys: `space`/Enter play or pause, `left`/`right` seek 10 s, `up`/`down` volume 5, `l` like, `s` shuffle, `r` repeat (all, one, off), `q` and `d` push the queue and the devices over the view, `cmd+right`/`cmd+left` skip, `cmd+c` copies the line playing (the track without synced lyrics), `cmd+o` opens. The 256-colour quantiser turned the light theme's amber bar red (`#874C00` has too few pixels to earn a palette slot next to the cover), so the two lyrics shots are `raw: true` like the picker's.
- **Lyrics** (`lyrics.ts`): lrclib `/api/get` by track, first artist, album and rounded duration, then `/api/search` by track and artist taking the closest duration within 3 s (synced first); LRC parsed with `[mm:ss.xx]`, `.xxx` and `:xx` tags, several per line, id tags and blank lines dropped, a bare tag kept as a pause line; `lineAt` is a binary search for the last line whose time has come (a line starts on its own timestamp, -1 before the first). Cached per track id in memory for the run (100, misses included; storage is capped and "not for a cache"); a failed lookup is remembered 30 s so a key does not re-ask twice. Read live: "Weird Fishes/ Arpeggi" exact (31 lines, "Your eyes" at 70 s), "Nude" with a wrong album through the search fallback (a 255 s synced hit), a made-up title as null; the search endpoint answered one 503 in between, which is what the 30 s throttle is for.
- **The bar item** `spotify/playing`: the track (`title · artist`) or, with `bar_lyrics` (default on), the lyric line playing, cut to 64; hidden unless playing; the popover is `menu: { view }` with the compact tree. **Ticking**: `onShown` (the only "popover is up" signal; there is no close) starts a 1 Hz `bar.update` of the item for `TICK_WINDOW_MS` (5 min), renewed by every `onAction`; the core replaces the popover's level in place on each push (`popover::on_item_changed` → `BarPage` `inPlace`), so the lines slide and the bar ticks there. Outside the window the render sets `refresh` to the seconds until the next lyric line, so the strip changes line on time without polling; the manifest's `every: 30`, `show`, `wake`, `network` and the `media` trigger (the MediaRemote stream: a track or state change re-renders at once) cover the rest. A popover action answers `keep` (the item renders again from the patched state, reason `update`), `q`/`d` push palettes inside the popover, `cmd+c` copies. Measured in the harness with `PAL_SPOTIFY_TICK_MS=2500`: pushes at +1 s and +2 s with the position moved, none after the window, one `bar.refresh` when it closed.
- **What the panel's view cannot do**: tick. There is no channel to push a tree into an open view level (`effects.run` refuses `view`; a `push` would reset and re-mount the level every second and relist every live palette through `show_in`), the same gap the Hue notes name. The view is drawn on open and on every key from the live position, which is right at that moment; the README and the palette doc say so. The proposal for the shell (protocol.ts is another agent's file this round): `View.refresh?: number` like `BarItem.refresh`, the Launcher re-asking `view(ctx)` (or picking a named action) after that many seconds while the level is on top and replacing the tree in place; this extension already answers `view(ctx)` from the live position, so nothing here changes when it lands.
- **Screenshots**: `fixture.ts` writes `spotify.json` (the wide and the compact trees from `render()` over a made-up track, "Harbour Lights" by The Low Tide, with words written for the fixture and SVG covers, since the gallery has no Spotify and a real cover is someone's art; search, playlists, library, queue rows) and `bar-spotify.json`. `1-lyrics`, `2-lyrics-dark` (the driver was run from a scratch copy of `shots.mjs` that adds `&theme=dark` per shot; `app/scripts/shots.mjs` is not this round's file, and a `theme` key on a shot is the one-line addition it wants), `3-search`, `4-playlists`, `5-library`, `6-queue`, `bar-menubar-dark` (the lyric line), `bar-menubar-light` (the `track` state), `bar-sketchybar`; all looked at. No `bar-menubar-popover`: `bar-shot.tsx` draws menu and palette popovers only, and a `{ view }` popover would have drawn the HUD; the compact tree was looked at instead by rendering the `now-playing-compact` fixture palette at 420 px (the popover's width) in a scratch page.
- **Tests**: `host/test/extensions/spotify-lyrics.test.ts` (13: the RFC 7636 S256 vector, the verifier's alphabet, the authorize url; LRC parsing in the three spellings with a repeated tag and a pause line; `lineAt` at every boundary; the window at the intro, mid-song and the end; the sampler on synthetic pixels and on a jpeg-js-encoded JPEG; the wide and compact trees through `checkView`, the keys, the moving keys, plain lyrics, every status) and `spotify.test.ts` (18: three Bun mocks, api.spotify.com dispatching on path with the covers served as real JPEGs, accounts.spotify.com checking S256 of the verifier against the challenge parsed off the authorize url and rotating the refresh token, lrclib.net; signed out everywhere without a request; the sign-in flow end to end through the extension's listener with a wrong state refused; the 401 refresh; the 429 refused locally; search sections, actions and picks; the playlist drill-in and an album's tracks; the four library filters; devices and the volume rows; the queue's skip; the root commands with the pinned rows and Sign out; the view's cover as a data url with the tint, the line at the position, every key with its request and its optimistic tree; the search fallback and the missing-lyrics states; the bar item's title, `refresh`, `bar_lyrics` off, hidden while paused; the popover tick). 31 pass; the whole host suite 822 pass with the one pre-existing colors failure (`vivid(56.5)` off by one in the last hex digit, not this round's).
- **Not done**: Premium-gated calls are surfaced, not worked around (a free account gets "Spotify Premium is needed to control playback"); the Linux side is the same code with nothing platform-specific and was not run there; Spotify's playlist `tracks` field is the older spelling (`/items` is the newer alias, both served); no `links` routes yet (`pal://spotify/play?uri=` and `pal://spotify/toggle` are the obvious two).

## Decided: multi-select, `pal pick`, dialog jump (2026-09-16)

Pit 18 (338), 8 (329) and 16 (358) of `notes/pit-triage.md`.

- **Marked rows** (`app/src/ui/selection.ts`, pure and tested: `toggle`, `mark`, `isMarked`, `markable`, `multiActions`, `pickIds`). A selection is one palette's ids in marking order; marking a row of another palette starts over, since a multi pick is one `pick` to one palette. Kept across queries (gather, filter, gather more), dropped on a level change and after the pick that used them. Marks: `⇧↓`/`⇧↑` mark the cursor row (never unmark) and move (`markAndMove` in Launcher.tsx: a view's own shifted-arrow action still wins), `⌘`-click toggles one (`clickWithModifier` in List.tsx, `onToggle` on List and Grid), `Tab` toggles and steps down in a palette whose meta says `multi: true` (`PaletteBase.multi`, through `paletteMeta` and registry.rs to `SourceInfo.multi`; Files, Recent Files, Windows, none with a filter dropdown) and in the picker, and a bare `x` does the same there while nothing is typed (with a query it stays a letter: `xcode` has to be searchable in Files and Windows, which is why the pit card's `x` is not unconditional). Unmarkable: hints (`actions: []`), palette rows, fallbacks, "more" rows, pushes, disabled rows. A marked row is `data-marked` (the accent tint, a hairline under the cursor, a check accessory `.pal-row__check`), a tile the same with the check in its corner; the footer shows an "N selected" pill. `Escape` clears the marks before the query. With rows marked the action list is the marked row's `Action.multi` actions plus "Clear selection"; Enter runs the first as `pick(id, action, { ...ctx, ids })` with `id` the marked row on screen (the cursor's when marked, else the first marked row in the list) and `ids` every marked id, the cursor's first (`pickIds`); Enter with no multi action is a failure toast, not a single pick. Wire: `PickRequest.ids` (index.rs) to the host's `pick` params, `ctxOf` puts them in `ctx.ids`; frecency records nothing for a pick with `ids`. Opted in: files (open, reveal, copy paths joined by newline, copy files, trash; `multi: true` on both palettes), clipboard (copy joins the entries' text one per line, an image its title, a file list its paths, `hud: "Copied N entries"`; delete each), windows (close, minimize each; focus single; `multi: true`), bookmarks (open: the first is the effect, the rest through the opener). Docs: `docs/getting-started.md` (tour 11), `docs/keyboard.md`, `docs/extensions.md`, `docs/palettes.md`.
- **`pal pick`** (`app/src-tauri/src/pick.rs`, `Cmd::Pick` in cli.rs, `pickLevel` in Launcher.tsx). The CLI process reads stdin (a line is a row; a line that is a JSON object with `id` is a row with `name`, `subtitle`, `icon`; repeats dropped; `parse_rows`), listens on `$TMPDIR/pal-pick-<pid>.sock`, hands `pick --reply <sock> [--title] [--multi] [--query] [--select]` over the single-instance channel (`handover_args`) and waits; the instance connects, reads the rows as one JSON line, `show_in` plus `pal://pick { token, title, multi, query, rows }` to the page, which `start`s a menu level (`kind: "menu"` with `pick: { token, multi }`, rows under the palette key `pick`, one `Pick` action each), writes `{ "ids": [...] }` or `{ "cancel": true }` back as one line. The page answers through the `pick_reply` command (`onPickReply`: Enter with the row's id or every marked one, Escape null) then resets and hides; any hide (`panel::hide` on both platforms calls `pick::on_hidden`) cancels a pending pick and tells the page (`pal://pick/cancel`); a second `pal pick` cancels the first; a dead CLI is EOF on the socket (a reader thread) and the instance drops the level and hides. Exit codes 0 / 1 (Escape, hide) / 2 (no instance, no rows, a bad reply) / 130 (SIGINT: a `libc::signal` handler unlinks the socket and `_exit`s; `libc` added to the app crate). This is the one reply channel over the handover, which itself carries nothing back (the links round's note). `--select <id>` answers from the instance without showing the panel: the plumbing checked from a terminal. Docs: `docs/cli.md` with the three examples (a file pick, a kill menu over JSON rows, a git branch switcher with `-q`).
- **Dialog jump** (`core/src/dialog.rs`, `app/src-tauri/src/dialog.rs`, the `dialog` effect in effects.rs, `dialog.current()` in api.ts, the Files action, the root hint). Detection is the front app's `AXFocusedWindow` classified over a `Win` trait (a fake tree in the tests): AppKit names the panel in `AXIdentifier`, `open-panel` on the standalone Open window (an `AXWindow` with subrole `AXStandardWindow`, not `AXDialog`: measured on TextEdit, macOS 26) and `save-panel` on the Save sheet (`AXSheet`), and that settles it; a sheet or dialog without the identifier is a panel when a file browser (`AXBrowser`/`AXOutline`/`AXTable`) sits under it with the "Where:" popup or the search field, the file-name field (`saveAsNameTextField`, or one titled "Save As:"/"Export As:") making it a save panel; `MAX_DEPTH` 6, `MAX_NODES` 400. The keystroke plan is pure (`plan(Toolkit)`: the field's shortcut `cmd+shift+g` / `ctrl+l`, `SETTLE` 150 ms, the path pasted, 150 ms, Return); `go` runs it through `clipboard::send_key` (new: any key with modifiers in the `Action.shortcut` spelling, `Keystroke::parse`, macOS `kVK` codes and evdev codes for wtype/ydotool; `paste_key`/`copy_key` share `post_key`) and `paste_text`. The app caches one detection per show (`dialog::on_shown` from `show_in`; `current()` for the bridge's `core/dialog.current` and the `dialog_detect` command), ~70 ms on hornet off the main thread. Files asks it at the start of every listing (`refreshDialog`) and leads every row with "Use in TextEdit's open panel" (`⌘G`, `{ dialog: path }`) while one is up; the empty root's "Dialog" section (`dialogHit`, asked with the suggestions, only while Files is loaded) says "Type a path for the open panel of TextEdit" and Enter pushes Files. The effect hides first, then the HUD says "Typed into the open panel of TextEdit" or why not. Linux: `detect` is `None` (no hint, no action), `go` runs the GTK plan blind.
- **Measured on hornet** (`cargo run -p pal-core --example dialog`, the terminal's Accessibility; `dialog go PATH` runs the plan): TextEdit's Open panel `detect` 72 ms, `None` on a document window 68 ms; the Save sheet detected as `save` in 71 ms; `go /Users/cagdas/proj/pal/target` into the Save sheet left the "Where:" popup reading `target` (read back over AX). Through the scratch instance (`target/scratch-ax`, identifier `io.cagdas.pal.pick`, own config with every network and prompting palette off by its `<extension>-<palette>` id, `[bar] target = "off"`, `XDG_DATA_HOME` aside; its log is now under that data dir): `printf 'main\nfeature/x\nfix/y\n' | pal pick -t Branch --select feature/x` printed `feature/x`, exit 0, `pick 3 rows selected feature/x without the panel` in the log; JSON rows with `--multi --select 42` printed `42`; a real `pal pick -t "Switch to" --multi -q fi` showed the level (a screenshot at 0.9 s: the crumb "Switch to", `fi` typed, the one row `fix/y` highlighted, "Pick ↵" in the footer; `pick 3 rows shown token 1`) and `pal hide` a second later cancelled it (`token 1 cancelled`, exit 1, the socket file gone). The dialog jump through the instance: TextEdit activated with its Open panel up (`File > Open…` pressed through the menubar example), `pal run 'files/files//Users/cagdas/proj/pal/docs' -a dialog` returned in 11 ms and the panel's "Where:" popup read `docs` a second later (`deeplink cli pal://run/files/files/...?action=dialog`, `pick files/files ... 0.5ms` in the log); the panel was then dismissed and the terminal brought back. One thing seen: the first show of the scratch instance ran the 1Password listing (`[palettes.onepassword]` was the wrong key; the palette id is `onepassword-items`) and put the 1Password prompt on his screen once.
- Tests: `dialog::tests` (6: the identifiers, the structural open panel and the sheet, the save fields, a document window / an alert / a bare table, the plan), `pick::tests` (4: rows, the handover argv, the reply outcomes, the socket round trip in-process), `cli::tests::pick_parses_its_flags_and_the_hidden_reply_socket`, `effects::tests::dialog_feedback_names_the_panel_or_the_reason`, `bridge::tests` (the route); app vitest `multi-select.test.tsx` (9: the model, shift+arrows, cmd+click across palettes, the panel and the multi pick, `x`, the no-multi toast), `pick-level.test.tsx` (4), `dialog-hint.test.tsx` (2); host `files.test.ts` (+2: the multi picks, the dialog action and effect), `clipboard.test.ts` (+1), `windows.test.ts` (+1), `bookmarks.test.ts` (the multi open). Whole suites: cargo 137 + 214, vitest 165, host 831 pass with one pre-existing clock-dependent tela failure (`11 h ago` vs `12 h ago` in `research > asking`, a fixture dated against wall time; not this round's).

## Decided: lazy palettes (2026-09-16)

- **The rule** (Cagdas, 23:00): a `lazy` palette still restores from the index cache at startup so its rows stay at the root; its *refresh* moves from process start to the first panel show; from then on `ttl`/`live` apply as before; the only rowless moment is a first run before the panel was ever shown.
- **Where it lives**: `lazy` on `ManifestPalette` and `PaletteBase` (`sdk/src/protocol.ts`), merged manifest-over-code in `paletteMeta` with a disagreement warning like `ttl`'s (`sdk/src/manifest.ts`), `PaletteMeta.lazy` on the wire (`registry.rs`), and one pure decision in the core, `index::load_plan` (`Now | Fresh | AfterReady | AfterShow`): a fresh cache stands whatever else is true; lazy and not yet shown waits (`Registered::awaits_show`, log `index <ext>/<palette> lazy, waits for a show`), and it is checked before the expired-cache pass because that pass *is* process start as far as a prompt or the network is concerned. `on_shown` flips `SHOWN`, lists every waiting palette (one task each, the host's own timeout, `why` = `show`) and keeps a lazy live palette out of that show's live relist so it is not listed twice. An extension reloaded after the first show lists at once, as before.
- **Who declares it**: onepassword `items`; slack `unreads`, `channels`, `status`; hue's six list palettes; tela's eight; github `prs`, `issues`, `repos`, `notifications`; home-assistant `entities`, `services`, `areas`; otp; docker's three (measured on marko: `docker ps` 181-188 ms, `docker images` 203-221 ms, `docker compose ls` 145 ms, all three at every start; `launchctl list` is 4 ms and `systemctl list-units` 13 ms, so `services` is not lazy); calendar in code, `lazy: active() === "google"` at load (EventKit is local). Spotify: not touched this round (another agent's tree), listed under Open.
- **Tests**: `index::tests::lazy_palette_waits_for_the_first_show_and_keeps_its_cached_rows` (the plan for every case, the restored rows answering a query while stale, the flag on the wire), `host/test/manifest.test.ts` (the merge, the warning, the meta in `extension/loaded`), and the scratch instance (below).

## Decided: `settings.set` (2026-09-16)

- **The SDK**: `settings.set(id, value)` and `settings.set({ id: value, ... })` for `[extensions.<name>]`, `settings.setPalette(id, value)` / the object form for `[palettes.<id>.settings]`; `null` unsets. Not `settings.palette(name).set(...)`: `palette()` returns the values table itself, so a `.set` on it would collide with a setting named `set`. The object form is one `core/settings.set { extension, palette?, values }` and one file edit (`ConfigFile::set_many`, new: every key under one write, nothing written if any key fails), which is what Hue needs: `bridge` and `application_key` landing together, so no reader ever sees an address without its key (the first cut wrote them one by one and the intermediate push reconnected the bridge without its pinned certificate).
- **The core side** (`settings.rs`): `plan_write` per id (the manifest must declare it, `pal_core::config::specs::check` fits the value to the kind: string kinds, `select` against its options, `number` within `min`/`max`, `boolean`, `list` of strings; the declared default and `null` unset, as the settings window does; a `secret` that is not already a `keychain:`/`env:` reference goes to the OS store under `pal/<name>-<id>` (`pal/<pid>-settings-<id>` for a palette, the window's spelling) and the file gets the reference), then `write_settings` (store, one `set_many` with the window's one retry on `Contended`, then the extension's values from a fresh parse of the file, pushed as `settings/changed` ahead of the watcher and answered to the caller). The SDK puts the answer in the host's table before resolving (`Runtime.update`), so `settings.get()` right after is current; `host/src/settings.ts` `update` skips listeners for values it already has, so the answer, the push and the watcher's reload are one `onChange`.
- **Hue** now writes the pair on pairing (`adopt`): the record without the key to storage (the pinned certificate, the entertainment client key), then `settings.set({ bridge, application_key })`, and `load()` connects it from the settings with the record's certificate; a second bridge while the settings hold one keeps its key in storage; `x` (forget) clears both; the settings' `onChange` now reloads only when a connection setting changed (`loadKey`), not on every change. README and the paired view say so.
- **Tests**: `core/src/config/specs.rs` (each kind), `edit.rs` `extension_setting_round_trip_keeps_the_file_and_resolves_the_secret` (`set_many`, the comment kept, the secret resolved through a store, one bad key writes nothing), `settings::tests::plan_write_checks_the_manifest_and_names_the_key`, `host/test/host.test.ts` "settings.set" (the wire shape, the table current on resolve, the secret's reference, default/null unset, one `onChange` per change, the palette form, an undeclared id refusing the whole object), `hue.test.ts` (pairing writes the settings, storage keeps the record without the key, forget clears both). The harness answers `core/settings.set` in memory (`Host.written`, `Host.secrets`) and `changeSettings` now layers over what was written.
- **Not done**: Spotify's tokens (another agent's tree this round); its OAuth tokens live in storage and refresh themselves, which is arguably where they belong, so nothing is blocked there.

## Decided: updater install and relaunch (2026-09-16)

- **What landed** (`updater.rs`): `install` = `tauri-plugin-updater` `download_and_install` on the `Update` the last check found (`Found`; a check runs first when none is remembered), progress as `updater::Progress` (`downloading {downloaded, total?}`, `installing`, `restarting`, `failed {error}`) on `pal://update` and on the HUD (a line per phase and per ten percent, `Progress::hud_due`), then `commands::restart` (the shell that waits for this pid, then `open -n` the bundle with the profile env, or execs the AppImage: `restart` now prefers `$APPIMAGE` over `current_exe`, which inside an AppImage is the mount that goes with the process). Commands `update_install`, `update_progress`. `UpdateInfo` gained `installable` + `install_note` from `support()`: a debug build, a Linux `.deb`/`.rpm` (the manifest ships the AppImage; the plugin would refuse it with `InvalidUpdaterFormat` after the download) and a bare Linux binary are not installable, and every row says why.
- **Where it shows**: Settings > About's Version row ("Install 0.2.0", the progress line, the failure as an error line), the Overview's update row (Install inline, "Installing…" disabled while it runs; About with the reason when not installable), the root row "Install Update" listed only while a check found a release (`commands::sync_update_row`, called by `updater::check`; not installable, its pick opens the releases page), the tray's "Check for updates…" enabled (the answer on the HUD), the check toasts naming the row. `general.check_updates` still gates the automatic check only; nothing installs without a click.
- **Not verified end to end**: the real download needs a signed release. The plugin reads its endpoints from `tauri.conf.json` at build time and `Updater::endpoints` is a builder call the check does not take from the environment, so a local `latest.json` cannot be pointed at without a code path for it; the manual check is: publish a release with `latest.json`, run the previous version, About > Check for Updates, Install, watch `updater\tinstall`, the HUD, `updater\tinstalled`, `restart\t...` in the log, and the new version in About. What is tested: `support_for` per platform and bundle, `UpdateInfo`'s wire shape, `Progress`'s serialisation and HUD pacing, the rows/plan/toast, the About row and the Overview item in vitest.

## Decided: item hotkeys in Settings (2026-09-16)

- **The table** (`ItemHotkeys` in `SettingsPalettes.tsx`, in the selected palette's pane, every palette but a view): one row per `[palettes.<id>.item_hotkeys]` entry: the item id typed or picked (a `<datalist>` over the palette's indexed rows, `Settings.tsx` asks `query` scoped to the source with no cap, so the picker completes on the id and shows the row's name; an id the listing does not have now reads "not listed now" in amber, kept), the compact recorder, Remove; "Add hotkey" opens a draft row that writes once it has both an id and a combination; retyping a saved row's id moves its combination. Writes go through `Settings.tsx` `onPalette` one key at a time (`palettes.<id>.item_hotkeys.<item>`, quoted when the id needs it), so a hand-written table keeps its other lines; the last one gone unsets the table. The alias field writes as before (`palettes.<id>.alias`, verified on the scratch instance). The Overview's Palettes fact counts them ("6 of 7 on, 3 with a hotkey, 2 item hotkeys").
- **Tests**: `settings-item-hotkeys.test.tsx` (6), the Overview fact in `settings-overview.test.tsx`; the gallery's Bookmarks carries two so the page shows the table.


## Decided: instances, phases 0 and 1 (2026-09-17)

`docs/design/instances.md` as written: `[instances."gmail@work"]` next to `[extensions."gmail@work"]`, the key everywhere a name is used, one Bun Worker per instance of a `multi` extension, the default too.

- **Phase 0, core config** (`core/src/config/instance.rs`, new; `mod.rs`): `Instance { title, tint, badge, enabled }` under `Config.instances` (`[instances.<key>]`, the bare name titles the default); `instance::{split, name_of, is_key, valid_name, valid_suffix, palette_id, private_ids}` as pure helpers (the suffix `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}`, never `default`); `Config::instances_of(name)` (default first, disabled included) and `instance_keys(name, multi)` (the enabled ones; the bare name alone when not `multi`; `[instances.<name>] enabled = false` parks the default too). `extension_settings(key, defaults, specs)` and `palette_settings(key, palette, defaults, specs)` take the specs now and layer `defaults <- [extensions.<name>] minus secrets and scope: instance <- [extensions.<key>]`; the `_resolved` twins resolve secrets on the final table, so the reference is the instance's own. `palette_settings` takes `(key, palette)` rather than a palette id because `gmail@work-inbox` is ambiguous as a string (the suffix may carry `-`), and the core computes both ids itself; `registry::palette_id` delegates to `instance::palette_id`. `unknown_keys` warns per malformed `[instances.*]` key and per unknown key under one; the schema is regenerated. `storage::valid_name` takes one `@` (a name on each side; `../x@y` and `gmail@w@x` refused). `settings.rs`'s `resolved` resolves by key, `register` reads the directory from `params.name`, and `core/instances.get { extension }` is a bridge route answering `[{ key, title, tint, badge, enabled }]` from `Config::instances_of` (raw config; the host fills the defaults).
- **Phase 1, host** (`host/src/worker.ts`, `host/src/instances.ts`, `host/src/serve.ts`, new; `host.ts`, `bar.ts`, `sdk.ts`): `serve.ts` is the palette side of serving one extension (`paletteMethods(lookup, manifestOf)` for list/pick/view/detail/link, `sections(exts, manifestOf, kind, query, ms)` for inline/fallback/suggest, `log`/`timeout`/`describe`), shaped like `barMethods` so host.ts serves its inline extensions and worker.ts its one instance through the same code. `instances.ts`: `resolveInstances(name, answer, ownTint)` (the default first and always present, title = suffix capitalised, tint = a djb2 hash of the suffix over `TILE_COLORS` skipping the extension's own colour, badge = the title's first letter, a bad tint falls to the hash, keys of another name or malformed dropped), `rewriteEffect`/`rewriteBarItem`/`rewriteCall` (a `push` or a `menu: { palette }` naming the extension by manifest name or not at all lands on the key; an outgoing `effects.run`/`bar.update` the same), and `WorkerInstance` (the main thread's handle: `{ init }`, `{ req }`/`{ res }`, the worker's `{ call }` relayed through this thread's bridge and answered by `{ reply }`, `stop()` = `{ stop }` then `terminate()` after `STOP_GRACE_MS` 1000, pending requests rejected with "`<key> stopped`"). `worker.ts` binds the SDK in its own global (`caller` = the request's context else the key; the manifest name given explicitly means the instance), redirects `console.log` to stderr, imports the entry with `?t=`, runs `checkPalettes(manifest, ext, instanceMeta)` and `checkLinks`, answers everything inside `context.run`, `dispose` on `{ stop }`. `host.ts`: `reload(name)` branches on `manifest.multi` (`reloadInstances`: `core/instances.get`, the disabled logged and skipped, every instance restarted, `startInstance` per key with its own `settings.get`), `handle` routes any per-extension method whose `params.extension` is a worker key to that worker (`HOST_LEVEL` = hello, the three sections, `settings/changed`, `instances/changed` stay here), `sections()` merges the inline palettes with one ask per worker (`ROOT_TIMEOUT_MS` + 250 ms slack), `settings/changed` forwards per key, `instances/changed { extension }` reloads the name, `extension/loaded` carries `name` and `instance { key, title?, tint?, badge?, isDefault }` (an inline extension: `{ key: name, isDefault: true }`), `extension/removed` per key, `hello.extensions[]` and `host/ready known` list keys.
- **SDK** (`protocol.ts`, `manifest.ts`, `icon.ts`, `runtime.ts`, `api.ts`): `Manifest.multi`, `SettingSpec.scope: "instance"`, `InstanceInfo`, `BarCtx.instance` (set for a `multi` extension only), `ExtensionLoaded`, `TileIcon.badge`; `instanceTitle(title, { title, alone })` and `stripInstance` (the rule of the design doc: substitute `{instance}`, else append ` (Title)`; alone or an unnamed default strips `{instance}` with one pair of parentheses or one flanking space), applied in `paletteMeta` through `checkPalettes(manifest, ext, inst?)`, which also badges the tiles (`badged(icon, { tint, badge })`: the extension's tile takes the tint and the badge, a palette's own tile the badge only) and warns on `{instance}` in a non-`multi` manifest and on a tint off the palette; `checkIcon` allows a badge of one or two code points (`MAX_BADGE`); `Runtime.instance(extension)` and `instance()` in api.ts (`{ key, name, title, isDefault }`; the inline binding answers the extension as its own default). App side: `Icon.tsx` draws the badge as a pill in the tile's bottom-right corner (`.pal-icon__badge`, `--pal-bg-elevated` on `--pal-fg`, scaled off `--tile`), `items.ts` `iconOf` keeps `badge` (cut to two characters), `SettingsTypes.ts` mirrors `scope`.
- **Manifests**: `github`, `slack`, `home-assistant` are `multi: true`; `slack.workspace` and `home-assistant.url` are `scope: "instance"`. So the three run in a worker each on every profile from now on, alone or not.
- **Tests**: `instance::tests` (3), `config::tests::{instance_keys_default_first_and_disabled_skipped, inheritance_skips_secrets_and_instance_scope, unknown_keys_under_instances}`, `storage::tests::storage_accepts_one_at_sign`, `registry::tests::palette_id_of_an_instance` (with the suffix as a keyword), `bridge::tests` (the route); `host/test/instances.test.ts` (15: a fixture `counter` whose `state.ts` submodule holds a counter, two instances counting separately, `settings.get()` and `instance()` per key with `settings/changed` reaching one worker only, storage calls by key, push/link/menu rewrites and an inline extension's, `ctx.instance` in `render` and `bar.update` from inside, `instances/changed` disposing (a `storage.set disposed` by key) and terminating a removed instance, a `for (;;) {}` pick terminated after the grace with the other instance answering and the pending pick rejected, `hello`/`host/ready` by key, the title rule cases, `resolveInstances`, `tintOf`, the rewrites), `github.test.ts`/`slack.test.ts`/`home-assistant.test.ts` (+1 each: `multi`, the private ids, the lone default in a worker unmarked), `icon.test.tsx` (+2: the pill, `iconOf` with a badge).
- **Measured on hornet** (Bun 1.4.2, through the real host over the test harness, medians of five, RSS from `ps` 0.8 s after `host/ready`): a bare one-palette fixture inline 33.0 MB and `host/ready` 27 ms; the same as one worker 39.9 MB and 34 ms (+6.9 MB, +7 ms for the first worker); six workers 60.8 MB (4.2 MB per further instance), `host/ready` still 34 ms since they start in parallel; spawn to loaded 8 to 9 ms per worker of which the import itself is 4.5 ms. `github` alone inline 35.7 MB, its import 10.0 ms; as one worker 41.5 MB (+5.8 MB), 6.3 ms import inside the worker, 10.1 ms from spawn to loaded; two instances 46.1 MB, three 51.2 MB (4.6 to 4.8 MB per further github). In line with the design's estimate (0.3 to 0.8 ms and ~3 MB bare, ~5.7 MB with github); the per-worker startup here is a little higher because it includes the SDK binding, `settings.ts` and `bar.ts` imported afresh per worker and the `checkPalettes` pass. The host logs both times per instance: `in 6.3ms (10.1ms with the worker's start)`.
- **Two more renderer findings from the run.** A keyed `progress` node directly in a column grew to the free height (the `> .pal-view__progress` rule that pins a column's bar to 4 px did not see through the presence wrapper of a keyed child; now `> div:not(.pal-view__node) >` too). And a Tauri `listen` on the default target (`Any`) hears every window's `emit_to`, so the panel page was applying (and tracing) the popover's updates and vice versa: `useLiveViews` listens with `{ target: getCurrentWindow().label }` now; `pal://bar` never showed it because only the bar page listens to it.
- **Verified on hornet** with a scratch release instance (`target/scratch-inst`, identifier `io.cagdas.pal.inst` baked in, the staged host/sdk/extensions beside the binary, own `PAL_CONFIG` and `XDG_DATA_HOME`, hotkey ctrl+alt+shift+o, `[bar] target = "off"`, `menu_bar_icon = false`, every palette but github's, calc, emoji and apps off; `[instances.github] title = "Personal"`, `[instances."github@work"] title = "Work"`, `[extensions."github@work"] token = "ghp_fake..."`): `core/instances.get` answered three times at startup (github, slack, home-assistant), `loaded github@work (prs,issues,repos,notifications,search; bar notifications) in a worker ... in 2.4ms (6.2ms with the worker's start)` and `loaded github ... in 3.0ms (7.3ms ...)`, both bar items registered as `github@work/notifications` and `github/notifications`, `host/ready` 872 ms (the whole bundled root), `index/github@work/` next to `index/github/` in the profile's cache. The root for `pull req` (a screenshot of the panel window, looked at): "Palettes 2" with "Pull Requests (Work)" on a violet tile badged "W" (the tint hashed from `work` with `ink`, the extension's own, skipped) and "Pull Requests (Personal)" on the plain ink tile, both subtitled "GitHub", then "Notifications (Personal)" and "Repositories (Personal)" with his real rows. `pal://open/github@work/prs` (a screenshot): the crumb "Pull Requests (Work)" with the badged tile, the placeholder "Search Pull Requests (Work)...", the one row "GitHub rejected the token" with the pane "Bad credentials" and "Open token settings" as the action (the fake token; the Personal instance listed his 16 PRs through `gh`). Storage by key: `github@work.json` is not written by a rejected token (github caches successful answers only, `api.ts:150`), so a `demo` multi extension dropped into the instance's own root (`$XDG_DATA_HOME/pal/extensions/demo`, `list` writing `storage.set("seen", instance().key)`) after `[instances."demo@two"]` was appended to the live config: both instances loaded from the user's root in workers, and `storage/demo.json` read `{"seen":"demo"}`, `storage/demo@two.json` `{"seen":"demo@two"}`, next to `github.json`. Seen on the way: `pal://open/pal/palettes?q=` (the synthetic Palettes source) does nothing (`deeplink cli` logged, no paint; not this round's), so the root query was typed with CGEvents posted to the instance's pid alone. `pal quit` ended it; the daily instance was not touched.
- **Choices made on the way**: a change under `[instances]` restarts every instance of the name, not only the added or removed one, because the metas of the others change with the count (the "(Personal)" suffix appears with the second instance) and a restart is what a file change already does; `caller("gmail")` inside `gmail@work` answers the key, so code that names itself by manifest name means itself; `BarCtx.instance` is filled in the host for a `multi` extension (Phase 2 need not add it in `bar/mod.rs`); an unnamed default next to another instance keeps its plain titles ("Pull Requests", "Pull Requests (Work)") rather than reading "Pull Requests ()"; a `multi` extension whose every instance failed is listed once in `hello` under its bare name, unloaded.
- **Still to do (Phase 2)**: `settings.rs` `Ext.name` + key (`register` keys `Ext` by the instance key today, so Settings > Extensions lists `github@work` as its own row with no install time; `changed_extensions` already sees a `[extensions.gmail]` edit as a change to `gmail@work`, since `resolved` runs by key with the inheritance), the `instances` diff in `on_reload` sending `instances/changed` (today an added `[instances."github@work"]` is picked up at the next host restart, `pal reload`), `instances_remove`, `index.rs` reading `name`/`instance` (the palette row's subtitle is the extension title already), `deeplink.rs` `name_ok` for `pal://<key>/<route>` (the `open/<key>/<palette>` form works today since only the extension-route catch-all checks the name), `Confirm::asks` by name, the bar tooltip suffix, sketchybar names with `@`, the Launcher pop on a gone source, the "x does not support instances" config warning (needs the manifests). **Phase 3**: the Settings UI (Instances section, per-instance settings, the grouping on Palettes and Bar, the Overview rows), `docs/links.md`, `docs/cli.md`, `pal instance list|add|remove`, STORE-FIELDS.md.

## Decided: view updates (2026-09-17)

- **The contract, both halves, no new transport.** Push: `view.update(spec, { palette?, bar?, id?, extension? })` in the SDK (`sdk/src/api.ts`) is one `core/view.update { extension, palette | bar, id?, spec }` over the reverse RPC, like `bar.update`; `spec` is a whole `View` or `{ tree }` alone (the tree replaced, the level's actions, title, keys and input kept). The core (`app/src-tauri/src/views.rs`) forwards it as a `pal://view` event to every window whose page has that level on top and is visible, else drops it and logs once per target until one lands. The page (`Launcher.update`, `nav.patch`) puts the spec into the level in place: the keyed transitions run (`move`, enter, exit; the View component's FLIP is untouched), a kept key keeps its DOM, the text field and its caret are untouched (the query lives on the nav level, not in the spec). Rate limit in the SDK (`VIEW_UPDATE_MIN_MS` 33: pushes closer than that to one target coalesce, the first goes at once, the last within the window goes at its end, every caller's promise settles with it), so the pipe never sees more than ~30/s per level. The lifecycle: the page reports its top view level (`view_open`, on every change and again on every `start`), the shell reports each window's visibility (`set_visible` from the panel's show/hide and the popover's show/hide), and the core's `Table` announces the transitions of "reported and visible" as `view/shown { extension, palette | bar, id, compact? }` / `view/hidden` notifications to the host (`host/src/views.ts`), which runs `view.onShown`/`view.onHidden` listeners in the extension's context and answers `view.open()`. A level counts as open only on top: a palette pushed over it hides it, popping back shows it again; a hidden panel keeps its level but nothing ticks for it; a reload re-announces the open levels to the new module. Pull: `refresh: seconds` and `on: ["media" | "wake" | "network" | "show"]` on a view palette (manifest or code, the manifest wins, warned on a listing; `checkPalettes`) ride on `PaletteMeta` to `SourceInfo`; the page runs the timer while the level is on top (skipping a tick with a pick in flight) and re-asks on `pal://trigger { name }`, which `bar::trigger` now emits for every bar trigger. Docs: `docs/extensions.md` "Live views: push and pull" (when to use which) and the bar items section.
- **Bar popovers are view levels of the item's own** (Cagdas's addition, 2026-09-17 00:15). `{ view }` from `menu` or from `bar/open` was already drawn by the same Launcher; what it lacked: `{ palette }` naming a view palette listed it (the host refused: "a view palette has no list"), now `Launcher.start` opens it as a view level; every `BarCtx` the core sends carries `compact: true`, and a `view`/`list`/`pick` reached from the popover window gets `ctx.compact` (`host_request` and `run_pick_from` set it by the window label), so an extension lays out for 420 px; the popover's own `{ view }` level is addressed as `{ bar: <item id> }` in `view.update` and in `view/shown`/`view/hidden` (with `compact: true`), which is the "popover closed" signal `onShown` never had. `bar.update` still re-renders the whole item, popover included, for a change that reaches the strip too. The page tells a bar level from a palette level by whether its key is a source (`byKey`), so a palette and a bar item of one name never cross.
- **The renderer bug behind Cagdas's "lyrics pane displaying weirdly"** (before/after shots in the report): `.pal-view__stack[data-grow] { flex: 1 1 0 }` with `min-height: 0` collapses a grow column to nothing inside an auto-height parent, which the popover's root is (`.pal-bar-page .pal-view > .pal-view__stack { flex: 0 0 auto }`; a nested non-grow column in the panel likewise), so the lyrics column had no height and its `justify: center` children overflowed over the progress row and the badges, the lines after the current one under the hints and clipped; the popover measured the collapsed content and sized itself 291 px. Fixed in `ui.css`: a grow node's basis is `auto` (never less than its content; the free space still goes to it where there is any: the panel's views are unchanged). The compact Spotify tree was then redesigned (`extensions/spotify/view.ts`): the cover top-left with the titles cut to the width, a slim progress row, one line before and two after the current one (`window(lines, position, before, after)`), a transport row and a like/device/queue row as keycap hints, no glow band; 385 px tall for a synced track, under the 480 cap, scrolling inside past it.
- **Spotify** (`extensions/spotify/index.ts`): one 1 Hz loop serves both surfaces: the popover through `bar.update` from `view/shown { bar: "playing" }` (or `onShown`) until `view/hidden` (`TICK_WINDOW_MS` stays as the fallback), the panel's lyrics view through `view.update` of the wide tree from `view/shown { palette: "now-playing" }` until its hidden, pushing only a tree that differs from the last pushed (every second while playing, since the clock moves; nothing while paused). `refresh: 5` in the manifest is the safety net. **Hue**: every stream event that touches a resource redraws every open light/room view from the model (`view.open()`, keyed by the target id as the `View.id`) and pushes it, no GET.
- **Tests**: cargo `views::tests` (4: open only while reported and visible, the id narrowing, covered and uncovered, hidden and shown again, the clearing show; one level in two windows with the popover compact and a `{ bar }` target; the drop logged once; the notification params), `bridge::routes_core_methods_only` gains `view.update`; host `views.test.ts` (6: the meta, shown starting a loop in context and `view.open`, a `{ bar }` target, the coalescing burst (first and last of five), a bad tree and a target-less push refused in the SDK, a reload forgetting the old listeners), `manifest.test.ts` refresh/on, `spotify.test.ts` the panel view's pushes and the popover level ending the window, `hue.test.ts` the open view following the stream; vitest `view-live.test.tsx` (6: a tree push keeping DOM identity with a moved key the same element and a new key new, the text field and caret kept and a whole spec replacing the actions, pushes for another palette/id ignored, the report as levels come and go, a `{ bar }` level and a `{ palette }` start, `refresh` and `on`). Whole suites: cargo 143 + 221, vitest 181, host 859 pass.
- **Verified on hornet** with a scratch release instance (`target/scratch-view`, identifier `io.cagdas.pal.view`, own config with `[bar] target = "off"`, `PAL_SPOTIFY_API`/`ACCOUNTS`/`LRCLIB` pointed at a Bun mock of a playing track with synced lyrics and a painted cover, `extension_dirs` with a counter fixture that pushes every 500 ms): see the report for the frames and the DOM trace (`PAL_VIEW_TRACE=1` makes the core mark each update with the keyed nodes that mounted). Caveat met on the way: a hand-started release build hands itself to launchd unless `<data>/launchd/handover` is within the minute, and the launchd copy carries only `PAL_CONFIG`/`XDG_*`, so the mock env was lost on the first run.

## Decided: Obsidian (2026-09-17)

- `extensions/obsidian`: seven palettes over the vault as files (`notes` indexed primary with the manifest's `ttl` 300, `search` input, `daily` live, `tags` catalog, `recent` live, `backlinks` and `outgoing` indexed), three link routes (`open?path=`, `new?title=&body=&folder=`, `append-today?text=`), a violet tile with md-safe. Three files: `notes.ts` is pure (front matter as Obsidian writes it, inline and front-matter tags, wikilinks with `\|` unescaped as Obsidian escapes an alias inside a table cell, resolution as Obsidian does it: path, else the unique name, else the shortest path among namesakes, else an alias; moment's tokens for the daily name, `{{date}}`/`{{time}}`/`{{title}}` for a template, `obsidian.json` and `daily-notes.json`), `vault.ts` the index and the file system, `index.ts` the palettes.
- **The vault is found before it is asked for.** `vault` empty reads Obsidian's own `obsidian.json` (the vault marked `open`, else the newest `ts`); on hornet that file does not exist (no Obsidian installed here), so the setting is what he will fill; the hint row names it and Enter opens Settings. The daily plugin's `.obsidian/daily-notes.json` stands in for the three `daily_*` settings the same way; his vault has none, so `YYYY-MM-DD` at the top of the vault is the default there.
- **The index is the extension's, and the watcher invalidates it, not the core.** There is no core request an extension can send to relist an indexed palette (`bridge.rs` routes `clipboard`, `storage`, `effects`, `view`, ... and nothing for the index), so `fs.watch(root, { recursive: true })` marks the in-process index dirty (a 200 ms settle folds a burst) and the next `list` of any palette rebuilds it, per file by `mtimeMs` and size, so a rebuild re-reads what changed and nothing else (244 files in his vault; the smoke run indexed the fixture's 20 in single-digit ms). A 30 s age cap stands in where recursive watching is missing. What that means at the root: the `notes` rows the core holds refresh on `ttl`, `cmd+r` and a settings change as any indexed palette's do; every palette opened in the panel, and `search`, `daily`, `recent`, read the fresh index. A `writeNote` of our own invalidates too, and the picks that create a note pass `refresh` so the row they open exists in the index they answer from.
- **`daily` is `live`, not `list`.** Its rows are keyed on today's date; a listing cached across midnight would say "Today" of yesterday's note and offer to create a note that is there. Live relists on every show, cheap (a stat per day of the week). The `notes` palette carries the four command rows (Today's note, New note, Search notes, Random note) since it is primary and reached by name; `daily` lists the dated notes under their own names with "Today" / "Yesterday" in the subtitle, so the two never show the same row twice at the root.
- **Reads and writes.** Reads: the vault, the two Obsidian config files, the template. Writes, each on his action: today's note from its template (Create today's note asks first; the Today's note command and an append create silently, since the row said so), a line appended as the last line of today's note (`appendNote` adds a newline only when the file does not end on one, so a log list stays a list), a new note (the form refuses a title already there; `fileName` maps Obsidian's forbidden `* " \ / < > : | ?` to dashes), a `# Title` note from an unresolved link in Outgoing links. Verified against a temp vault only; his `~/Sync/vault` was read to learn the shape (kebab-case names, `# Title` heads, `_index.md` / `_log.md` / `_rules.md`, wikilinks, almost no front matter, no tags) and never opened by the extension.
- **Reuse.** `../tela/md.ts` renders Read in pal (headings, callouts as cards, tables as columns, `↗ label` link lines) and `../snippets/placeholders.ts` fills `{selection}`, `{clipboard}`, `{date}`, `{time}` in the append; both imported across extension directories like clipboard's `../colors/color.ts`. Obsidian's front matter needed its own parser (`tags: [a, b]` and block lists; md.ts's `frontmatter` reads one-line values only). The pane is markdown for `marked` (front matter off, `> [!TYPE]` a bold lead, `[[links]]` as `obsidian://open` links, one to nothing marked so), the same shape tela's `paneMarkdown` takes.
- **Keys.** `cmd+enter` is the other opener (Obsidian or the editor, per `open_with`), `cmd+c` the wikilink, `cmd+shift+r` Read in pal (`cmd+r` is the shell's Refresh), `cmd+b` Backlinks, `cmd+l` Outgoing links, `cmd+shift+c` Copy path; the view's `cmd+shift+c` is Copy markdown. A terminal editor cannot be launched from the panel (the github decision), so `editor` empty is the OS opener and a command off PATH is a toast naming the setting.
- **Search.** ripgrep when on PATH (`--fixed-strings --ignore-case --max-count 3 --glob *.md`, the excludes as `!globs`, 1 s then killed), else a scan of the index's notes in Bun that stops at 1 s or 60 hits; both feed one ranking (a note named like the query first, then by matching lines, then newest). Obsidian's `tag:` / `path:` operators are not read: the words are matched as typed, the README says so.
- Tests: `host/test/extensions/obsidian.test.ts` (32: the pure parsers and formats; over the wire against a temp vault with the rows, the openers with a fake editor on disk, the wikilink copy, the pane, the view, backlinks and outgoing, tags, recent, the daily create from the template, the append (prefilled from the harness's clipboard, placeholders filled, refusal), the new note form and its refusals, the watcher (a file written by hand appears in the next listing, a deleted one leaves), the search with ripgrep and again forced to the scan through `PAL_OBSIDIAN_SEARCH`, the three links, the two missing-vault hints, the vault found through a fake `obsidian.json` under `PAL_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG`). `tsc` over the host tree: nothing from this tree (the six errors it prints are in `extensions/images` and `translate.test.ts`, other agents' this round). Fixture `extensions/obsidian/fixture.ts` builds a temp vault in his vault's shape with invented notes and writes `app/src/gallery/shots/obsidian.json`; six shots rendered through `shots.mjs` and looked at, and four of them in dark through the same gallery url with `theme=dark`. Seen and fixed on the way: `\s*` in the callout regex crossed the line break and swallowed the callout's body into the bold lead; a wikilink alias inside a table cell split the cell (Obsidian escapes it `\|`, now read); the description line carried `[[brackets]]` (reduced to the label).

## Decided: Gmail (2026-09-17)

- **What landed** (`extensions/gmail`, `multi: true`): five palettes (`inbox` live and lazy, `search` input, `labels` hourly catalog, `compose` and `drafts` only where `send` is on) and the bar item `gmail/unread`, one instance per account through the instances design as written: `[extensions.gmail]` is the default, `[extensions."gmail@work"]` next to `[instances."gmail@work"]` the second; the titles are `"Inbox ({instance})"` and friends, so two accounts read "Inbox (Personal)" / "Inbox (Work)" with the badged tile, and the strip text of the bar item is the instance's title.
