Files: `api.ts` (the token command and the Gmail calls), `data.ts` (the mail model, the inbox, the label table, the writes), `mail.ts` (pure: addresses, MIME, HTML to text, the markdown escape, RFC 822, the links), `avatar.ts` (Gravatar probe, the initial tile), `index.ts` (rows, panes, forms, the bar).
- **The token is the calendar extension's pattern** (`token_command`, `scope: "instance"`): a shell command printing a bare token or the broker's JSON, cached to its expiry less a minute, one mint shared by concurrent callers, minted again once on a 401. The owner's identities: `ssh archer "curl -s 'http://127.0.0.1:8776/token?aud=gmail'"` and `aud=gmail-work`; the generic path is `gcloud auth application-default print-access-token` after a login carrying `gmail.modify`, documented and not tested. A failing command is one hint row carrying its last stderr line ("Token command exited 7: curl: (7) Failed to connect to 127.0.0.1 port 8776") with Open Gmail settings as the action; an empty command says where to set one; a rejected token says to check the scopes. The bar item hides on no token (the strip has no room for a hint) and throws on anything else (stale).
- **`send` is the work-account rule, per instance, off by default, and off means read and mark-read only**: no compose, no reply, no drafts, no archive, no star. The vault's rule for `cagdas@serpapi.com` names archive next to send/reply/draft, and the grant is the full scope because mark-read needs `gmail.modify` and no scope grants it without send (`~/Sync/vault/infra/token-broker.md`), so the control is this setting, not the scope: the token can, the extension does not. `scope: "instance"` on `send` is load-bearing: a `send = true` on `[extensions.gmail]` must never reach `gmail@work` through inheritance. With `send` off the row has Open in Gmail, Mark as read (or unread), Copy link; Compose and Drafts list nothing (not a hint row: a hint would sit at the root under "Compose (Work)"); every write path checks `canSend()` again at pick time and answers a failure toast naming the setting, so a stale row from before a settings change cannot write. Mark read stays on everywhere because it is the owner's daily write and the account's policy allows it.
- **Rows**: id = the message id (a pick after a restart is one `messages.get`); the subject, the sender and the snippet, the user labels as chips (two), a star tag, a paperclip, the date; the instance's title in the keywords (`work invoice` finds it at the root), not as a chip: the root's section header and the crumb already name the instance, and a chip on every row read as noise in the shot. The sender's mark: the Gravatar when a HEAD with `d=404` answers 200 (one probe per address per process; the `{ image }` icon has no error fallback in the renderer, so the probe is what keeps a broken image off the row), else the initial on a tile tinted from the address as a data-url SVG (the slack fixture's shape). `e` / `s` from the brief became `cmd+e` / `cmd+s`: a bare letter types into the search box in a list palette (`docs/keyboard.md`); `cmd+enter` marks read. `Action.multi` on Mark as read/unread and Archive, so marked rows go in one `batchModify`.
- **Requests**: `messages.list` `maxResults` 50 for `INBOX+UNREAD`, `INBOX`, and `<label>+UNREAD` per `labels` entry, then `messages.get format=metadata` with nine `metadataHeaders`, eight at a time (`pool`), cached by id for the process with the labels patched locally on every write (a header never changes); `format=full` only for the opened message, the last twenty kept; the count is the unread list's length under a page and `labels.get INBOX` past it (so the common case costs no extra call); the label table an hour in memory and in `storage` (`labels`), read before the rows so the chips have names; the inbox shared between the bar and the palette for 30 s; a 429 or a quota 403 remembered for `Retry-After` (60 s without) and refused locally. The attachment mark from `metadata` is a guess: `multipart/mixed` at the top level. Seen live: Gmail's `has:attachment` also matches `multipart/related` (inline images in newsletters), which the mark leaves alone on purpose; the pane counts every part with a filename.
- **The pane**: the plain part with the quotes folded (`On ... wrote:`, `-----Original Message-----`, a `From:` after a blank line, any run of `>` lines) else the HTML converted (`gmail_quote` / `blockquote` / `divRplyFwdMsg` folded by div depth, lists as `- `, links as `text (url)`, entities), then escaped for marked (a bare `<a@b>` would vanish as HTML, a leading `#` would be a heading; bare urls left whole so they link); From, To, Cc, Date, Labels, Attachments with sizes, Open in Gmail. Reply quotes the same text under the answer with the signature between, sends in the thread with `In-Reply-To` and `References`.
- **Bar popover**: each of the newest five is a submenu (the sender and the subject) with Open in Gmail and Mark as read, since a menu row has one action; then Open in pal and Open Gmail. No Mark all read: not asked for, and a wrong click over a work inbox is not undoable from here.
- **Verified live** (2026-09-17 01:15, hornet, both identities through the broker over ssh, read-only through the harness, counts only): personal inbox 52 rows (4 Unread, 48 Recent) in 2.3 s cold including the two token mints, 1 Gravatar among the senders, 17 label rows (5 yours, 12 Gmail's), `newer_than:7d has:attachment` 10 hits (8 Inbox, 2 Sent) in 1.1 s, the pane of the first row in 257 ms, the bar item badge 4 titled Personal with 4 menu nodes; work inbox 50 rows (0 Unread, 50 Recent) in 2.6 s, 26 Gravatars, 12 label rows, the same search 2 hits, the bar item hidden at zero; Compose and Drafts empty on both (`send` off). Storage: one `labels` set per instance (`gmail`, `gmail@work`). No write was made against either account: mark read, archive, star, reply, compose and drafts are verified against the mock only.
- **Tests**: `host/test/extensions/gmail.test.ts` (25: the pure helpers by import; then two instances through the harness against `gmail-mock.ts`, a Bun mock of the API with two mailboxes by bearer token, a small query parser, `batchModify`, `send`, drafts, a 401 for an unknown token, a 429 with `Retry-After: 1`, and a Gravatar stand-in: the metas and the title rule, the rows and actions per instance, the shared inbox, the pane, every write against the mock, the work instance refusing archive / star / reply / send / compose with no call and mark read going through, the reply's raw message decoded, search, labels persisted per key, compose and drafts, the bar item and its submenus per instance, `send` flipped off by `settings/changed` hiding every write, the 401 re-mint, the 429 refusal and recovery, the three token failures). Gallery: `app/src/gallery/shots/gmail.json` and `bar-gmail.json` generated from the extension running against the mock (invented mail, the avatar probe off so every sender is an initial), the six panel shots and four bar shots rendered with `shots.mjs` and looked at, the inbox, labels and drafts in dark too.
- **Not done**: threads (a row is a message opened at its thread); trash; labelling from here; attachment download; the exact attachment mark without `full` (a `has:attachment` list would cost one more call per refresh); the gcloud path is documented, not run.

## Decided: bar popovers (2026-09-17)

- **Every popover is a view of the item's own, drawn from a pure `render(state)`** (`extensions/<name>/view.ts`; Hue's `popover.ts` next to its light view), so the gallery draws the same tree from a fixture (`extensions/<name>/fixture.ts` writes `app/src/gallery/shots/bar-<name>.json`, `bar-shot.tsx` opens a `{ view }` menu as the item's view level now) and the tests assert on it. The shape they share: 420 px (396 of content inside `padding: 3`), `keys: "actions"`, the first listed action on Enter, a keycap hint row at the bottom, a cursor the arrows move and a click sets (`selected`), the height under the 480 cap for the common state (Hue with a room opened lands on it; GitHub budgets rows and says "and N more in pal"; Slack cut its row padding), both themes looked at. Every key and click from the popover is `onAction(id, ctx)`; a `{ view }` answer replaces the tree in place, `{ keep }` re-renders the item when the strip changes too.
- **The vocabulary grew where a control was missing, never faked** (`sdk/src/protocol.ts`, `checkView`, `View.tsx`, `ui.css`, `docs/extensions.md`): `action` on any node (a click runs that view action; the node is a control with a pointer and a hover lift; a hidden action some node runs needs no shortcut; the innermost control wins), `selected` (the accent ring; scrolled into view, nearest edge, on every tree), a hex `stack.surface` (a card in the extension's colour, alpha allowed; the ink black or white by contrast past alpha 0.5, the panel's under a faint tint), a hex `progress.color`, `slider` (a 6 px track with a thumb, `role="slider"`; a click carries the fraction as `ctx.values.value`), `switch` (the system pill), `image.dot` (a presence dot ringed by the panel). `bar_action` now carries `values` (BarPage sends `ctx.values`; the core puts them on the `BarCtx`, `values?: Record<string, string>`), which a `View.input` field in the popover needed (Timer's `25m tea`, Slack's reply) and a form in the popover would have needed too: before this a popover's field or form submitted nothing. `ManifestBar.keys` documents a popover's key table like a palette's. Vitest `view-controls.test.tsx` (3), `host/test/view.test.ts` +1 (the checks).
- **Hue** (the flagship): a status row (lights on, each motion sensor as a badge, green while it sees motion, the temperatures, a bridge away in red), the rooms as tiles two a row in their lit colour shaded by the brightness (`shade`: 62% at off-black, so a 30% room still reads as its colour), the name, a switch, the count and a thin brightness bar in the tile's ink (`darkInk` mirrors the renderer's contrast rule), an off room a plain card; a tap toggles, the chevron tile opens the room's lights inline (swatch, name, a slider in the light's colour a tap sets, the percentage, a switch), the scenes as swatch tiles sized to their count on the digits (the one playing a card with its name in the accent), `e` everything on, `x` all off, `p` pal, `r` refresh. The cursor (`PopoverState`: rooms or lights, the index, the opened room) lives in the module and survives the popover closing. A group write applies to the room's lights in the model at once (`setRoom` calls `applyLocally` per light), so the count and the tiles are right before the events land; `scheduleBar` after every action keeps the strip in step. `hue.test.ts` +1 rewritten (35 pass): the tree, a tile tap, the arrows over the grid, Enter opening a room, `bri+`, a slider tap through `values.value`, Enter toggling a light, backspace, a scene digit, All off / Everything on, the main-room setting.
- **The others**, each in its fork's report: Spotify's queue rows (`next` / `then`, a click skips, the queue read every 15 s while shown); Timer's cards with the field, the recent-duration tiles and a 1 Hz tick; Calendar's rows with the Join tile, tomorrow folded, a 30 s tick from the cache; Slack's sections with avatars as data urls (`avatarData`), the reply field, mark read; GitHub's repo groups with a colour rail per subject type and a reason badge for every API reason, a height budget; Now Playing's cover (the stream's, a url fetched once, else the app's icon) with a ticking position computed from the last look; OTP's digit tiles with the minute bar and the two codes before. Tests: hue 35, spotify 19, timer 18, calendar + google 110 (with manifest/host), slack 27, github 34, media + otp 32 (the counts each fork reported).
- **Seen on the way**: a `text` node cannot draw a Nerd Font glyph (no symbols font on view text), so GitHub's octicons became a colour rail and OTP's note a `♪`; a `text.style: "glyph"` in `--pal-font-symbols` would let a popover show them. `tile.text` is the UI font too. Gallery renders raced the Vite dev server other forks restarted (a strip drawn unstyled once); re-rendered clean.

## Decided: translate, shell, downloads (2026-09-17)

Three palettes from `notes/extension-wishlist.md` (Google Translate, Shell, Downloads Manager), each to the bundled standard: a tile, a keys table, store fields, a README, screenshots looked at in both themes, a host test. Fixtures `app/src/gallery/shots/{translate,shell,downloads}.json` from `extensions/<name>/fixture.ts`; tests `host/test/extensions/{translate,shell,downloads}.test.ts` (19, 15, 14 pass); `tsc` over the host tree clean for the three.

- **Translate** (`extensions/translate/`, blue tile, md-translate): one input palette plus a live history. The grammar is `lang.ts` (pure): `tr: hello`, `>de hello`, `german: hello` name the target, `en>tr merhaba` both ends, a prefix only counts when its word is a language code or English/native name (`todo: buy milk` is text), `>de` takes no space so it never clashes with the shell's `> ` root prefix; `matches` (the root's inline `match`) wants a prefix with text after it, since a bare prefix reads the selection and the root asks per keystroke. Typing is debounced 350 ms inside the palette (a list overtaken by a newer one answers a "Translating…" hint at once, so the order of replies holds), not for the root's inline ask, which the host debounces; results cached per backend, pair and text. Nothing typed: `selection.text()` else the newest clipboard text, read once per 2 s across empty listings. A text already in the target goes to `from` when concrete, else English, else the system language, else a hint saying how to fix it (`otherEnd`); set `from` and `to` to a pair and text in either lands in the other. **Google**: `translate_a/single` with `client=dict-chrome-ex` and `dj=1` (POST, `dt=t,at,rm,ld,bd`), which answers sentences, `src`, per-segment `alternative_translations`, `translit`/`src_translit` and `dict` in one JSON reply; **the `gtx` client every script uses was refused with Google's "Sorry..." page from Cagdas's network (both hornet and marko, one public IP) on 2026-09-17, while `dict-chrome-ex` answered**, so that is the client, the README says it is unofficial, and a refusal is one hint row naming DeepL. Alternatives for a several-sentence text are the segments' nth choices joined (`googleAlternatives`); a romanisation row only when the side's script is not Latin (`nonLatin`, `\p{Script=Latin}` set subtraction), so the IPA `src_translit` of an English word is not shown as one. **DeepL**: `/v2/translate` on `api-free.deepl.com` with `DeepL-Auth-Key`, the target in its spelling (`EN-US`, `PT-PT`, `ZH-HANS`), 403/456/429 each their own row. Speech: `say -v <voice>` with the first installed voice of the language from `say -v ?` (Yelda for Turkish), `spd-say`/`espeak-ng`/`espeak` on Linux, `PAL_TRANSLATE_SAY` for the tests. History records a translation on a pick (copy, paste, speak), never on a listing, so half-typed words never land; it is `live` so past translations are at the root by either text. `speak` is a boolean (also speak on Enter), not a voice: the voice follows the language. Rows: translation, translit, detected (inert, with the confidence; "From X" when the prefix named it), alternatives, source translit, dictionary, Swap (a push into the same palette with the pair reversed and the result as text). Apple's on-device Translation has no scriptable interface: skipped, as the wishlist said.
- **Shell** (`extensions/shell/`, slate tile, md-console_line): an input palette whose only row is `Run: <cmd>` until Enter, plus an input history. `run.ts` (pure but for the spawn): `shellArgv` from the `shell` setting (`$SHELL -lic` default: aliases need `-i`, PATH needs `-l`; Cagdas's zshrc prints `can't change option: zle` under `-i` without a tty, which shows in red, and `zsh -lc` is the documented way out), the process group killed on timeout (SIGTERM, SIGKILL 1.5 s later), stdout and stderr drained apart with a 256 KB tail each, `looksDestructive` on the **command word of every simple command** (split on `;`, `&&`, `||`, `|`, subshells; `VAR=x` and `xargs`/`time`/`nohup`/`exec` skipped): `rm mv dd mkfs shred truncate wipefs shutdown reboot halt poweroff dropdb sudo doas` always, `chmod -R`, `git push --force`/`reset --hard`/`clean -f`/`branch -D`, `kill -9`, `docker rm`/`system prune`, a package manager's uninstall, `diskutil erase`, `launchctl bootout`, `DROP TABLE`, a redirect onto a disk device by their arguments; an argument named `rm` (`grep rm *.txt`) is not a command (the first cut matched words anywhere and asked on `echo rm`). The output is a view level (`tree()`): the command in mono as the head row with `exit N` / `killed after` / `output cut` badges and the duration, the folder, then a sunken radius stack with stdout as one mono text node and stderr as one in red (the view scrolls: `.pal-view` is `overflow: auto`, text nodes pre-wrap), 64 KB drawn, the whole run on the clipboard. **The 10 s pick limit vs a longer timeout**: `pick` waits `PICK_GRACE_MS` (2.5 s) for the command, then answers a running view (blue badge, elapsed time) and the result is pushed with `view.update` when the run ends; a 1 Hz tick pushes the elapsed time meanwhile, and `view.onShown` re-pushes the current tree in case the level reported itself after the push (the core drops a push with no level open). The view's actions: Copy output (Enter), Open in terminal (cmd+Enter), Run again on **cmd+r** (the view level takes the keys the shell's Refresh has on a list, Launcher.tsx `actions` line ~636: a view's own actions replace the shell's), Copy command (cmd+c), Copy stderr (cmd+shift+e, off cmd+shift+c which is Copy deep link). A run started from the history palette pushes to that palette's level (`Live.palette`), and the view's picks route through `historyPick` to the same handler. Terminal: `terminalArgv` for Terminal/iTerm (AppleScript), kitty/Alacritty/WezTerm/Ghostty (`open -na … --args`), any name with `-e`, Linux `$TERMINAL` else `x-terminal-emulator`, the line `cd <cwd> && <cmd>; exec <shell>` so the window stays. `terminal` is a sixth setting beyond the asked five: the spec said cmd+Enter opens a terminal and the machine has several. History is `input`, not `live`: listed per keystroke so a just-run command is there, and never at the root where Enter would run it (translate's history is live for the opposite reason: its rows are safe to copy).
- **Downloads** (`extensions/downloads/`, cyan tile, md-download): one live, primary, multi palette with `suggest` and a lazy `detail`. `scan.ts` (pure): kinds by extension, the partial suffixes (`.crdownload .part .download .partial .tmp`) and `finalName`, `sectionOf` by local calendar day, `size`/`rate`, `browserDirsFrom` (Chrome-family `download.default_directory`, Firefox `browser.download.dir` when `folderList` is 2), `safariProgress` from the bundle's Info.plist. Rows newest first with Downloading first; a partial's rate comes from its size at the previous listing (the palette is live, so every show is a listing; Chrome and Firefox partials carry no total, Safari's does). Thumbnails: `sips -Z 64 -s format jpeg` (macOS) or `magick`/`convert` into `~/Library/Caches/pal/downloads/<sha1(path|size|mtime)>.jpg`, served as a `data:image/jpeg` row icon (~3 KB each), the newest 24 per listing so a 200-row folder lists in one pass (his real ~/Downloads, 49 files, through the host harness: 492 ms the first time with the thumbnails made, 11 ms with them on disk, 0.7 ms with them in memory). Actions: Open/Reveal/Copy file/Copy path/Trash `multi`, Quick Look, Move to folder and Rename as forms (`rename-submit`/`move-submit` picks, the form back with `errors` on a name in use, a slash, a blank folder; a cross-volume move falls to `mv`), Clear older than N (a confirm naming the count), Open Downloads. **Two keys moved off the spec**: `cmd+backspace` for Trash is the grammar's "pop a level" and never reaches an action (the polish pass moved timer's Stop off it for the same reason), so Trash is `cmd+d` as in Files; bare `m` and `r` in a list palette type into the search box (bare keys are actions only in a view with `keys: "actions"`), so Move is `cmd+m` and Rename `cmd+shift+r`. Trash is Finder's delete / `gio trash` as in Files, with `PAL_DOWNLOADS_TRASH` naming a stand-in for the tests (they move into a folder of their own; his ~/Downloads was only read). The detail's From/Page links come from `mdls -name kMDItemWhereFroms` (his real downloads carry both the file url and the page). The suggest row is `Downloaded: <name>` for a finished file under ten minutes old. The sixth planned shot (the Now row on the empty root) is not there: the gallery's Shot component (`app/src/gallery/shots.tsx`, another agent's tree this round) passes no `suggest` to the Launcher and its fixture Palette has no `multi`, which is also why the marked shot uses `shift+down` rather than Tab.
- **Seen on the way.** `bun test` runs in UTC while the spawned host runs in the box's zone, so a test that dates files by "yesterday" pins `process.env.TZ = "UTC"` before anything is dated (calc.test.ts does the same). The manifest's `match` string is compiled without the `u` flag (`regexOf` in manifest.ts), so `\p{L}` cannot be used there: translate's manifest carries an ASCII form for the store and the code's predicate decides. The `ls -la` fixture output is scrubbed of the temp path and the user name (`~/proj/demo`, `sam`).

## Decided: bar appearance and the Bar page (2026-09-17)

The queued "bar item appearance defaults and per-item overrides" (Cagdas, 00:25) and the Settings Bar page redesign (00:30), done together.

- **One look, three places.** `pal_core::config::BarLook` (`core/src/config/mod.rs`): `dim` (percent, 50), `size` (pt, 0 = the target's own), `spacing` (pt, 4), `show_icon`, `show_title`, `color` (a name or hex, unset = the extension's), `urgent_color` (`destructive`), `badge_style` (`count` | `dot` | `none`), `width` (pt, 0 = natural), `font` (`system` | `mono`), `max_chars` (32, moved in from `BarMenubar`). `#[serde(flatten)]` into `BarMenubar` and `BarSketchybar` (the same built-in defaults; `max_chars` now applies to sketchybar too through `label.max_chars`), and `BarLookOverride` (every key `Option`) flattened into `BarItemConfig`; `Bar::look(key, target)` is the target's look with the item's keys on top (`BarLook::with`, `dim` capped at 100, `max_chars` floored at 4). serde processes the flattens in order, so the `extra` map still catches unknown keys for the diagnostics (tested). Schema regenerated; the override fields carry their own descriptions.
- **The item is shaped before either renderer sees it.** `BarItem::shaped(&look)` (`app/src-tauri/src/bar/mod.rs`) drops the icon or the text, maps a count to a dot or drops the badge, puts the tint over the extension's colour (`muted` from the extension stays: it is a state), and sets `hidden` when nothing is left. `Draw` carries `look` instead of `max_chars`; `Draw::tint()` is `urgent_color` when urgent, `muted` when `BarItem::muted()` (stale, or coloured muted), else the item's. `color_name()` is gone.
- **Menu bar** (`bar/menubar.rs`, `bar/glyph.rs`). Measured against tray-icon 0.24.2: `set_title` is `button.setTitle(NSString)` with no attributes (`src/platform_impl/macos/mod.rs:179-191`), and an image of any width is scaled to 18 pt high keeping its aspect (`mod.rs:296-297`). So `size`, `font` and `width` prerender the whole item into the image (`menubar::prerendered`, `glyph::strip`): the glyph square as before, the gap (`spacing`), the text in SF Pro or SF Mono read from `/System/Library/Fonts` (Helvetica / Menlo fallbacks, `ab_glyph` parses the variable SFNS.ttf: probed, 13 of 15 sample characters inked), a Nerd glyph inside the text from the symbols font; a fixed `width` clips. The image stays a template unless a colour is set, so the system tints text and glyph alike; `dim` is the template's alpha (a stale item was a non-template grey at 50% before, now the template at `dim`, so it follows a light or dark bar); `color` / `urgent_color` are the glyph's ink (the title text keeps the bar's colour unless prerendered, as before); an emoji icon stays title text (Apple Color Emoji is a bitmap font); an image icon keeps its picture. `Style` gained `alpha` and `size` (`stale` gone), the cache keys them; `same_image` compares the look and the body, so a tick redraws a prerendered strip and a look change redraws everything.
- **sketchybar** (`bar/sketchybar.rs`): `size` → `icon.font.size` / `label.font.size`, `font = "mono"` → `label.font.family=Menlo`, `width` → `label.width` + `label.align=left`, `max_chars` → `label.max_chars`, `spacing` → `icon.padding_right` before a label or segment and a segment's `icon.padding_left` (its `label.padding_left` without an icon), `dim` → `Palette::muted_hex(dim)`, the `muted` colour (token or `[bar.sketchybar.colors]` override) at that alpha; `color` / `urgent_color` through `Palette::resolve` (a name, or `#rrggbb` / `0xAARRGGBB`). sketchybar has no unset, so `diff` removes and re-adds an item whose known properties lost a key (a size back to the bar's own takes the `--default`s again). Segments with a colour of their own keep it under the extension's `muted` (only stale mutes everything, as before).
- **Settings > Bar** (`app/src/ui/SettingsBar.tsx`, `settings.css`): a left column (the Defaults card: target with explanation and detection dot, peeks, sketchybar position, Appearance under a Menu bar / sketchybar segmented switch in four groups Placement / Text / Colour / Behaviour, each field with its file key, description and the target's caveat; then the items as a master list: badged tile, extension over the title, on, target, state line) and the selected item's pane (description, a preview strip of the last render in both themes on the target the item lands on, Placement, Appearance with "from the menu bar default" notes and Reset per overridden field, Popover: hotkey and peek, Reset to defaults). `settings.rs` `BarItemState` carries the whole strip (icon, segments, colour, progress, tooltip). Writes: `lookWrites` (`SettingsTypes.ts`) turns a moved look into per-key writes, unsetting a target key back at the built-in default and an item key back at its target's, and writing nothing for a key that was absent and stays at the default. Search anchors `bar:<target>:<key>` and `bar:<item>:<key>`; a hit selects the item (`barKey` in `Settings.tsx`).
- **The strip renderer is shared.** The band of `gallery/bar-shot.tsx` moved to `ui/BarStrip.tsx` (+ `ui/bar-strip.css`, imported from `ui/index.ts`; the gallery keeps the popover and HUD overlay) with the look applied the way the renderers apply it (`shapeItem`, `clipText` mirror `shaped` / `clip`; dim on the image only unless prerendered, the count pill kept as the gallery drew it). `BarStrip` takes `width`, `height`, `bare` for the pane.
- **Tests**: core `bar_look_resolves_item_over_target_default`; app `bar::tests` (`shaped_applies_the_look`, `tint_follows_urgent_then_muted_then_own`, the draw carrying the look), `glyph::tests` (size, alpha, `a_strip_prerenders_the_text_beside_the_glyph`: widths, mono, fixed width, the dot and the rule on a text-only strip), `menubar::tests::the_look_reaches_the_image_and_the_title`, `sketchybar::tests::props_follow_the_look` and the remove-and-re-add in `diff`, `colors::tests` for `resolve` / `muted_hex`; vitest `settings-bar.test.tsx` (8: the Defaults card, the groups, the master rows, the pane, the inheritance notes, the preview per theme and target, the index) and `settings-bar-writes.test.tsx` (4, happy-dom: a default edited under its target, an item key set / equal-to-default / Reset / Reset to defaults, the row's switch and target, `lookWrites`). Cargo 147 + 222 green, clippy clean.
- **Gallery**: the settings section gained a "Bar, the timer selected" state (`gallery/data.ts` `settingsBar` / `settingsBarItems`: a badge, a mono-width timer, a stale urgent calendar strip, a hidden OTP; `?gallery=settings:bar&theme=dark|light`), so the page has shots in both themes; that is what the screenshots in the report are (960 by 640 at 2x through headless Chrome).
- **Not verified live**: the scratch instance (`target/scratch-barset`, `io.cagdas.pal.barset`, `[bar] target = "menubar"`, `dim = 40`, the timer `font = "mono"` / `width = 72`, github `badge_style = "dot"`) ran and rendered, but the screen was locked the whole round (`CGSSessionScreenIsLocked`), so `screencapture -l` could not image its settings window or the real menu bar; the prerendered strip's look on the actual bar (text weight of the variable SFNS.ttf's default instance, the 18 pt scaling) is still to be eyeballed. The gallery's bar shots were not re-rendered (the band's default output is unchanged by design).

## Decided: gifs, maps, speedtest, youtube (2026-09-17)

Four palettes from `notes/extension-wishlist.md` (GIF Search, Google Maps Search, Speedtest, YouTube), each to the bundled standard: a tile, a keys table, store fields, a README, screenshots looked at in both themes, a host test against a mock. Fixtures `app/src/gallery/shots/{gifs,maps,speedtest,youtube}.json` from `extensions/<name>/fixture.ts`; tests `host/test/extensions/{gifs,maps,speedtest,youtube}.test.ts` (11, 12, 11, 12 pass) with the mocks `gifs-mock.ts` and `youtube-mock.ts`; `host/test/png.ts` is a shared PNG writer for a mock's pictures (the downloads fixture carries the same writer inline; that tree was another agent's). `tsc` over the host tree clean for the four.

- **GIFs** (`extensions/gifs/`, pink tile, md-file_gif_box): an input **grid** (`kind: "grid"` is what `kindOf` says for `view: "grid"` plus `input`, and the Launcher lists per keystroke and draws tiles regardless), trending from Tenor's `featured` while nothing is typed, a search 300 ms after the last key. **The preview is a data url**: Tenor's `nanogif` (Giphy's `fixed_height_small`) fetched once into `~/Library/Caches/pal/gifs` (`XDG_CACHE_HOME` on Linux) and inlined per row, the mime sniffed from the bytes (a backend may hand a webp or png under a .gif name); a listing of 24 is about a megabyte on the wire, which the pipe takes in stride and the webview animates; the detail pane shows the http preview url instead, so each preview crosses once. The grid's image tile is the UI's 56 px box with `object-fit: cover` (`{ image }` becomes `mask: "rounded"` in `items.ts`), so a 4:3 preview is cropped square; filling the cell would be a Grid change, left alone. Enter downloads the full GIF into the cache under `<hash>-<title>.gif` and answers `copy_files` (it pastes as a picture; `paste` takes text only, so no paste action), cmd+Enter the url, cmd+o the page, cmd+s a copy into `save_to`, cmd+f the Favourites grid (storage, 200, live so its rows are at the root). `backends.ts` is pure: `parseTenor`/`parseGiphy` on the documented shapes, `GifError` with a hint per refusal. **Keys**: Tenor v2 wants a Google Cloud API key with the Tenor API enabled (free, no billing; the README walks through the console), Giphy a developers.giphy.com app key; both endpoints were probed read-only on 2026-09-17 and answer 403 / 401 without one, as the mock does. `content_filter` maps Tenor's four `contentfilter` steps onto Giphy's `rating` (r, pg-13, pg, g).
- **Tenor retired (2026-09-17).** Google shut the Tenor API down on 2026-06-30 (new keys had stopped on 2026-01-13); enabling `tenor.googleapis.com` on a Google Cloud project now fails with "Service tenor.googleapis.com is not available to this consumer", verified today. The Tenor parser, fetcher, `PAL_GIFS_TENOR`, `tenor_api_key` and the `backend` select are gone; Giphy is the one backend, `content_filter` is its `rating` alone, and the mock, tests and fixture run against Giphy. `Backend`/`BACKEND_NAME` and the `<backend>:<id>` favourites key stay for a second backend.
- **Maps** (`extensions/maps/`, green tile, md-google_maps): an input palette over urls, no key: `google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=` (with `query_place_id` for a prediction), `maps/dir/?api=1&destination=&origin=&travelmode=` (Google spells the cycling mode `bicycling`), Apple's `maps://?q=` and `maps://?daddr=&saddr=&dirflg=d|r|w|c`; `webUrl` turns a `maps://` into `maps.apple.com` for Copy link. `maps.ts` is pure: `parse` strips `maps:`/`map:`/`go:` and splits a route on `>` / `->` always and on ` to ` **only when an end is home, work, here or a saved place** (the first cut split every ` to `, and "things to do in Moda" became a route); `parsePlaces` reads `Name = address` lines; `resolveEnd` maps home/work/here. Rows for a place: Search, Directions from here / from home / from work (named so, the route in the subtitle), the saved places that match; for a route: it and its reverse; for nothing: Home, Work, the commute both ways, the saved places, else two hints. The travel mode is `filters` (an input palette gets `ctx.filter` per keystroke; Tab cycles it), so every directions row reads it at pick time. **Places API (New)** autocomplete (`POST places:autocomplete`, `X-Goog-Api-Key`) only with `api_key`, 250 ms debounced, cached per input, never on the inline ask; `parsePredictions` reads `placePrediction.structuredFormat`. Actions: Open, Directions from here (cmd+enter), from home (cmd+h), from work (cmd+w), Copy address (cmd+c), Copy link (cmd+l), Open in the other app (cmd+shift+o). Verified read-only: `google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=coffee` answers 200.
- **Speedtest** (`extensions/speedtest/`, violet tile, md-speedometer): a **view palette** that never runs by itself: `view()` re-detects the tools on every open (three `which`es and one `--version`; a cache of the first look was wrong once a tool appeared or the `tool` setting changed) and draws the last result or the install lines; `pick("start")` spawns the tool in its own process group and answers the running view at once (no pick grace needed: every chunk the tool prints feeds `tools.ts`'s pure `feed` and pushes a whole `View` through `view.update`, a 1 Hz tick keeps the elapsed time moving, `onShown` re-pushes for a level that reported late). **`speedtest` on PATH is two programs**: Ookla's and sivel's pip alias; `detect` tells them apart by `--version` ("Speedtest by Ookla" vs "speedtest-cli"). Ookla: `--format=jsonl --progress=yes --accept-license --accept-gdpr` (`--server-id`), lines `testStart` / `ping` / `download` / `upload` / `result` with `bandwidth` in bytes per second (Mbps = × 8 / 1e6) and a per-phase `progress` (the shapes from Ookla's CLI as ddsol/speedtest.net documents them verbatim; no Ookla binary was on hornet to run); speedtest-cli: the human lines (`Testing from ISP (ip)`, `Hosted by X (City) [d km]: N ms`, `Download: N Mbit/s`, `Upload:`), since `--json` says nothing until the end; fast: `--upload --verbose`, the redrawn line read per chunk for the last `N Mbps ↓` / `↑` (either order) after stripping ANSI, the unloaded latency and the client line, done only with both figures and exit 0. The bar is the tool's fraction, else the elapsed time over 12 s capped at 0.95. Stop is Enter while running (the first listed action flips), SIGTERM to the group and SIGKILL a second later; **the run ends on the process exit, not on the streams**: a shell wrapper's `sleep` child kept the pipes open after the kill and the first cut never finished. The seeded runs in the fixture carry made-up server and ISP names. History: storage, `keep` runs, rows with copy/open/remove, a **Trend** row answering a view of the last twenty as `progress` bars, download and upload **each against their own best** (one scale made every upload bar a stub), the ping beside. `on: ["show"]` re-asks the view when the panel comes back with the level kept.
- **YouTube** (`extensions/youtube/`, red tile, md-youtube): `api.ts` answers one `Video`/`Channel` shape from the Data API v3 (`search` with `type=video`, 100 units, then `videos.list` with `contentDetails,statistics` for the durations and views, 1 unit; `chart=mostPopular` for trending; `type=channel` and `channelId&order=date` for channels) or an Invidious instance (`/api/v1/search?type=video|channel`, `/trending`, `/channels/<id>/videos`); the thumbnail is `i.ytimg.com/vi/<id>/default.jpg` for both, no key, and the detail pane's `mqdefault`. **Invidious is nearly gone as a keyless path**: of the instances api.invidious.io listed on 2026-09-17 only `invidious.f5.si` advertised the API, and it answered (search, trending, a channel's 60 videos: verified live through the palette, read-only); inv.nadeko.net says "Endpoint disabled", yewtu.be a captcha page, nerdvpn.de 401. The README and the setting say so and point at a self-hosted instance; an HTML or error answer is one hint row. The Data API answers 403 without a key (probed); `quotaExceeded` is its own hint. Rows: `channel · length · views · age` (`live now` alone for a stream, whose views and age are the stream's); Enter opens, cmd+Enter plays through `playerArgv` (IINA by `open -a` on macOS, else `mpv`, else VLC, per `player` or the first installed for `auto`; the browser when none), cmd+c the url, cmd+s Watch Later (storage, live, at the root), cmd+shift+o the channel. Channels: search by name, Enter pushes the search palette with `{ channel, title }` args, which lists that channel's videos once and filters them by the query; **subscriptions need OAuth and are skipped**, the empty channels palette says so. `yt:` matches at the root; the palette answers the inline ask only for a prefixed query (the first cut answered a plain one too, since the query was checked after the prefix was stripped).
- **Seen on the way.** A view fixture cannot show a pushed state: the gallery has no `view.update`, so the speedtest fixture catches the running tree from the harness's recorded `view.update` calls (a stand-in that pauses mid-download) and stores it as the `start` effect's `{ view }`. `bun test` runs every file in one process, so a stand-in tool's argv log written by one describe's detect probe was read by the next describe; and a spawn logs its argv a beat after `pick` answers, so a test waits for the line. The player and speed test binaries are found through `PAL_YOUTUBE_PATH` / `PAL_SPEEDTEST_PATH` (a directory searched first) rather than `PATH`, since the harness's host inherits the test's env at spawn and a later `process.env` change does not reach it. Four calendar-google and calendar tests fail in a full `bun test` at this hour (02:00 local) with Today and Tomorrow rows swapped; not this pass's files, not looked into.

## Decided: the calendar and snippets tests pin the clock (2026-09-17)

- **The failure.** `calendar.test.ts` built its fixtures off `Date.now()` (a standup ten minutes in, Dentist 42 minutes out, Concert tomorrow 19:00) and `calendar-google.test.ts` shifted its fixture's times onto the run's start with a fixed `Etc/GMT±N` zone; both held only while the run's hour left today today. Around midnight and at 02:00 six tests failed: the Today and Tomorrow rows swapped, "the standup over" wrong. `snippets.test.ts` had the same race on `isoDate(new Date())`.
- **A clock per extension, not a shared SDK module.** `extensions/calendar/clock.ts` and `extensions/snippets/clock.ts`: `now()` is `Date.now()` (a `Date` for snippets), or the instant `PAL_NOW` names (`2026-09-16T10:30:00`, local to `TZ`, read once at load). Every read of the time in calendar (`index.ts`, `source.ts`, `google.ts`, `schedule.ts`'s `parseDay` default; `today.ts` and `view.ts` take `now` as an argument already) goes through it: the rows' states, `popoverView(now = clock())` and the 30 s redraw, the bar's minute tick, the caches' ages, the token expiries. Snippets passes it as `Sources.now`. The harness passes `PAL_NOW` to the host by hand next to `TZ` (the spread does carry a runtime assignment on bun 1.4.2, checked; the by-hand pair guards the 1.3 quirk).
- **The fixtures.** `calendar.test.ts`: `TZ=UTC`, `PAL_NOW=2026-09-16T10:30:00`; the standup 10:20 to 10:50 (`now, 20 min left`), Earlier today 08:00 to 09:00 (`over`), Dentist 11:12 (`in 42 min`), Concert tomorrow 19:00 (`in 32 h`, `Concert in 32h 30m` at a 32.5 h horizon and hidden at 32), Review Sun 20 Sep 10:00, Republic Day Mon 21 Sep; the form's start defaults to `10:45`; the escalation boundaries through the host (42 minutes out: amber under warn 42 / urgent 41, red under urgent 42). `calendar-google.test.ts`: `TZ=UTC`, `PAL_NOW=2026-09-16T10:00:00`, the fixture served as written (no shifting, no `local()`): the standup `09:30 – 09:45` over, the sync `in 12 min`, tomorrow's 1:1 `in 24 h`, the day page `r/day/2026/9/16`; the token cache never ages under the pinned clock, so the 401 re-mint's `runs + 1` is the 401 path alone. `snippets.test.ts`: `PAL_NOW=2026-09-16T09:05:00`, `Reviewed 2026-09-16 09:05` literal (no `TZ`: the host parses and formats in one zone). Each file deletes `PAL_NOW` in `afterAll` (`bun test` runs every file in one process).
## Decided: WhatsApp (2026-09-17)

- **What landed** (`extensions/whatsapp`, the wishlist's WhatsApp row): four palettes (`chats` live, lazy and primary; `unread` live and lazy, the root's unread rows; `search` input over the archive's full-text search; `contacts` an hourly catalog), the bar item `whatsapp/unread`, two links (`open?chat=` by id, phone or name; `search?q=`), a green tile with md-whatsapp. Files: `api.ts` (the OpenWA client: the key, the session's UUID, the typed failures, the calls), `data.ts` (the model: chats with their unread run, a chat's conversation, contacts, search, pictures, the phone behind a LID; the pure parts by import), `view.ts` (the popover, pure), `index.ts` (rows, panes, forms, the bar, the links), `fixture.ts`. Over the owner's OpenWA on archer (`http://wp.lan`, session `main`, `~/Sync/vault/infra/whatsapp.md`), the routes read from `/api/docs-json` (OpenWA 0.10.10) and every shape checked live before a line was written.
- **The API, as it is.** Direct chat ids are `@lid` (227 of 228 on the owner's), so a chat's phone is `GET /contacts/<lid>/phone`, once per chat, memoised; `@c.us` ids carry the number. `GET /chats` is a bare list with `lastMessage` a plain string (absent for a media message and for 178 of 333 chats) and no sender, no muted, no pinned, no member count: muted/pinned glyphs asked for in the brief are not possible from this gateway and are left out, the group glyph and a `group` tag stand. Two message shapes that differ: the live history (`/messages/<chat>/history`: `fromMe`, oldest first, a group's sender as `author`, no names, no quotes) and the archive (`/messages?chatId=`: `direction`, newest first, `chatName` = the sender's saved name, `metadata.quotedMessage`); `conversation()` takes the live rows as what the phone shows and lays the archive's over them by message id for the name and the quote, the archive alone when the live history answers nothing (a LID-era chat the gateway never saw). The search hit shape came from the container's `builtin-fts.provider.js` (`messageId waMessageId sessionId chatId body snippet timestamp type direction from score`, `<mark>` in the snippet). A wrong session UUID is `404 "Session with id '…' not found"` (the UUID in storage is resolved again once on that message and the call retried), a name where a UUID goes is `400 Validation failed (uuid is expected)`, an unknown LID's phone is `200 {phone: null}`, a session that is not ready answers `409` on the chat list (mapped to a `SessionError` with the status: `qr_ready` says to scan at the url).
- **Pictures are off the listing's path.** `/contacts/profile-pictures` takes 50 ids but resolves them one by one at ~150 ms: 50 ids took 11.5 s live and the first cut's cold listing 12.1 s against the 2 s live-relist budget. Now a background pass asks for eight ids among the newest 48 chats, passes chain while any is wanted, the urls (pps.whatsapp.net, `oe` = expiry in hex seconds, about nine days) are kept in storage under `pictures` (200 at most) and refreshed a day before they expire; the listing sets `avatar` from the map and never waits. Cold listing after: 592 ms (the list 370 ms, the unread runs in parallel, the group senders' names one `GET /contacts/<id>` each). The popover fetches the picture bytes into data urls (`avatarData`, 96 KB cap, a miss remembered 15 min), since an `image` node takes no http url; a 42 KB JPEG each.
- **Unread rows are the ones that cost a history.** The unread chats (12 at most) get `history` with `limit = min(unread, 5)` memoised on the chat's timestamp, so their row says who said what and a `[photo]` is named; the rest carry the gateway's line. Chats and Unread overlap at the root for an unread chat (Slack's unreads/channels precedent): the primary Chats row leads, the Unread section repeats it.
- **`send` is the control, off by default**: no Send a message, no React, no reply field, and every write path checks again at pick time with a toast naming the setting. Mark read and unread stay on (`POST /chats/read`, `/chats/unread`). A send is a form (the text, a box to quote the latest incoming message through `/messages/reply`) or the popover's field on Enter; a reaction is a select of WhatsApp's six plus "Remove your reaction" on the latest incoming message, fetched at form time for a read chat. Sends, reactions and mark-read were verified against the mock only.
- **Settings**: `base_url`, `api_key` (secret), `session` (name, the UUID cached in storage `session:<name>`), `send`, `unread_only_bar` (read as "the bar item only while something is unread": off keeps the glyph as a way into the popover, which then lists the recent chats), `dm_urgent`, `open` (`auto` = the desktop app when `/Applications/WhatsApp.app` exists on macOS, else `web.whatsapp.com`; hornet has no app, so the owner gets the web client; a group has no link into it on either, so the app or the web opens at the top with a HUD line saying so).
- **Keys**: `cmd+enter` Mark as read (unread on a read chat, `multi`), `cmd+shift+r` Send a message, `cmd+shift+e` React, `cmd+shift+o` web, `cmd+c` number (a group's name; a hit's text), contacts `cmd+shift+c` a vCard 3.0 (the brief's "vCard-ish"; shadows the shell's Copy deep link on that row as bookmarks and apps do). Popover: Enter, `m`, `a`/`cmd+shift+a`, `r`, `o`, `p`, `cmd+shift+o`, arrows and `j`/`k`.
- **The pane is markdown**, so "sender tinted" became the sender in bold with "You" for the owner's, the quoted reply a blockquote, media an italic bracketed label; a tint would need a view level.
- **Verified live** (2026-09-17 02:20, hornet, the harness against `wp.lan`, read-only, counts only): session `main` ready; 332 chats (104 groups), 7 unread (2 direct, 5 groups) in 592 ms cold, 2 ms shared; the unread palette 7 rows in two sections; the pane of the first unread chat 40 paragraphs in 118 ms; the bar item badge 7, urgent, 15 popover actions; contacts 565 rows from 5,403 store entries (1,131 `isMyContact`, folded by number) in 1.6 s; the search answered `503 wsearch /search -> 500 Internal Server Error`, shown as the hint row (tan's knowledge.md already has the cause: wsearch's `bge-m3` embed model is gone from hornet's ollama, his call to fix). No write was made: the core calls the run made were `storage.*`, `settings.get`, `instances.get`.
- **Tests**: `host/test/extensions/whatsapp.test.ts` (22: the labels, both message shapes, the markdown, the links and the opener, the vCard and phone spelling, the picture expiry, the popover tree; then through the harness against `whatsapp-mock.ts`, a Bun mock of the API with a session, the two message shapes, paged contacts, the LID phone route, SVG pictures, `<mark>` search, the writes recorded, a 401 for a wrong key, a 429 with `Retry-After: 1`, a 503 for the search, a 409 for a session not ready: the metas, the chat rows and the background picture pass, the pane, the unread palette, open per setting, mark read and unread over marked rows, every send refused with `send` off and made with it on, the search and its 503, the contacts, the bar item and every popover key, the links, the 429, the 409, the 401, no key, a session nobody has, an unreachable url, a stale UUID re-resolved). `tsc` over the host tree clean. Gallery: `app/src/gallery/shots/whatsapp.json` and `bar-whatsapp.json` from `extensions/whatsapp/fixture.ts` (invented people; the mock's clock is relative to now so the times read as live), six panel shots and six bar shots rendered with `shots.mjs` against a Vite on 1431 (the one on 1430 served the main checkout) and looked at, the chats, the pane, the contacts and the popover in dark too.
- **Not done**: muted, pinned and member counts (not in the gateway's chat summary); a `suggest` for the root's Now section; sending media; WhatsApp Channels, the status feed and broadcast lists; a Linux box to check `whatsapp://` handling; the search against a live provider (down at the time).

## Decided: instances, phases 2 and 3 (2026-09-17)

`docs/design/instances.md` as written, on top of phases 0 and 1: the running app and the Settings window now know instances, and an added or removed `[instances.*]` table takes effect without a host restart.

- **Phase 2, core app** (`settings.rs`, `index.rs`, `deeplink.rs`, `cli.rs`, `bar/mod.rs`, `bar/popover.rs`, `bar/sketchybar.rs`, `commands.rs`; core `config/instance.rs`, `config/mod.rs`, `frecency.rs`, `storage.rs`): `Ext` is keyed by the instance key (`key`) and carries the manifest `name` and the host's `instance` (`InstanceInfo { key, title?, tint?, badge?, isDefault }`; `label(alone)` is the title of a non-default instance, or a named default's next to another); `register`/`retain`/`forget` by key, `manifest_of` by key falling back to the name (one directory serves every instance). `changed_instances(prev, next)` names the extensions whose `[instances.*]` tables differ, and `index::apply_config` sends one `instances/changed { extension }` per name after the settings push; the host reloads every instance of the name (phase 1), so a table added by hand, by the CLI or by Settings lists its palettes within the reload and a removed or parked one drops its rows and bar items (`extension/removed` per key). `changed_extensions` keeps resolving by key through the inheritance (`changed_extensions_follow_inheritance`), and leaves out an instance whose table `next` no longer has or parks (the host is stopping it; relisting it only logged `no extension gmail@work`). `instance_warnings` adds `x is not installed` / `x does not support instances` for a `[instances."x@y"]` the manifests cannot honour, appended to `View.diagnostics` (the strip and the Overview). The file edits are `pal_core::config::ConfigFile::instance_add(key, title?, tint?)` (refuses a malformed or existing key; an empty table is written as its explicit header, since the table existing is the instance) and `instance_remove(key)` (`[instances.<key>]`, `[extensions.<key>]`, every `[palettes.<id>]` with `owns_palette` (the key itself or `<key>-…`, so `gmail@workshop-inbox` is not `gmail@work`'s), every `[bar.items."<key>/*"]`, in one edit; the default is refused: `instances_remove_unsets_every_table`). The commands `instances_add` / `instances_rename` / `instances_remove`: each edits the file and calls `on_reload` with the fresh load at once (`reload_now`), so the diff and the host's reload do not wait for the watcher (whose later reload of the same bytes finds nothing changed); `instances_remove` then waits up to 3 s for the key to leave the registry (the host's `extension/removed` after the worker's `dispose`) before `Storage::forget(key)` (new: the file and what was loaded of it, so a later touch starts empty), `index/<key>/` and `Frecency::forget_extension` (new; the search history stays), then `pal://index`. Keychain items stay, as for a removed extension. `deeplink.rs`: `name_ok` is `instance::valid_name || is_key` for the first segment (`pal://gmail@work/inbox`, `%40` too), a route's own name stays plain; `Confirm::asks` trusts by name or key (`deeplink_confirm = ["gmail"]` covers `gmail@work`, `["github@work"]` that one alone); two routes `instance/add/{name}/{suffix}` (`?title=`, `?tint=`) and `instance/remove/{key}` behind the card always, like `install`. `bar/mod.rs`: `Entry.instance` is the label; `set` appends ` (Work)` to the tooltip before the compare (`labelled`; a re-registration strips a stale one), the popover's title reads "Unread (Work)" (`title_of`), the strip is unmarked. sketchybar names keep the `@` (`pal.gmail@work.unread`): read against the source 2026-09-17, `bar_item_set_name` refuses only an empty name, `bar_manager_get_item_index_for_name` is `strcmp`, the regex form needs the name wrapped in `/`, so the design's name map was not needed; a test pins the name. `pal doctor` lists instances by key. CLI: `pal instance list` (the file, in the calling process: `<key>\t<title>\t<state>`, default first), `pal instance add <name> <suffix> [--title T] [--tint C]` and `pal instance remove <key>` as link twins (a bad suffix or the default refused with exit 2 before anything is sent).
- **Phase 3, Settings UI** (`Settings.tsx`, `SettingsTypes.ts`, `SettingsExtensions.tsx`, `SettingsField.tsx`, `SettingsPalettes.tsx`, `SettingsOverview.tsx`, `settings.css`, `icons.css`): one `SettingsExtension` per instance key (`key`, `name`, `multi`, `instance`, `inherited`, `inheritedFrom`, `extTitle`), the title "Gmail (Work)" and the tile badged (`badgedIcon`) for a non-default instance; a parked instance the host did not load is synthesised from the default's report (`parkedInstances`) so it has a row. The Extensions page lists one row per name (`byName`, "2 instances" in the subtitle, the worst mark across instances) and the pane shows the **Instances** section (a row each: badged tile, title, `key`, "default", on/off (`[instances.<key>] enabled`), Rename inline (`instances_rename`), Remove armed on a second press (`instances_remove`), the default without Remove) and **Add another account**: an inline form with the title, the suffix slugged from it (`slugSuffix`) until typed by hand and checked live (`suffixProblem`: the grammar, `default`, a suffix in use), the tint as twelve swatches plus "auto" (the host's `tintOf` mirrored in `instanceTint` so the preview tile reads as the host will draw it), the note spelling the link the key will take; Create calls `instances_add` and selects the new key. The Settings section is per instance (a segmented control; the note `extensions."gmail@work"`), each field showing the effective value: an inherited one carries "From Gmail (Personal)" and Reset goes back to the inherited value (`SettingsField` `base`/`note`), a secret or `scope: "instance"` field empty reads "Set for this instance"; `writeDeclared` compares against the inherited value for a non-default instance (`leavesFile`), so an equal value leaves the file and the instance keeps following. `needsSetup` reads through the inheritance, so the Overview's rows are per instance ("Gmail (Work) needs token", anchor `extensions:gmail@work:token`; a manifest warning and an update once per name; the Extensions fact counts names and says "1 extra instance"). The Palettes page groups per key (the header "Gmail (Work)" with the badged tile; the row prefix stays the extension's plain title, "GitHub › Pull requests (Work)", since the palette title already carries the instance); a palette setting inherited from the default's table is noted the same way. The Bar page's `extTitle` is the instance's. Search anchors `extensions:<key>[:<setting>]` and `palettes:<id>`; `go` selects the extension and its instance tab. The gallery's GitHub is `multi` with a Work instance (`settingsExtensions`), with in-memory add/rename/park/remove, so `?gallery=settings:extensions` shows the section. The Launcher pops a level to the root with a toast ("Inbox (Work) is gone") when its source leaves `sources()` after having been known (a bar item's view level never was; the empty list at startup says nothing).
- **Docs**: `docs/config.md` (the live reload, the warnings, the Settings section and the removal's cleanup), `docs/extensions.md` (the bar tooltip, Settings and the CLI for authors, gmail among the bundled), `docs/links.md` (keys in every route, the two `instance/*` routes, the allowlist by name or key), `docs/cli.md` (`pal instance`), pal-site `STORE-FIELDS.md` (the `multi` row's chip and copy, `settings[].scope`).
- **Tests**: core `instance::tests::{instances_add_writes_the_table_and_refuses_a_second, instances_remove_unsets_every_table}`, `config::tests::confirm_trusts_by_name_or_key`, `frecency::tests::forget_extension_drops_its_keys_alone`, `storage_accepts_one_at_sign` (+ `forget`); app `settings::tests::{changed_extensions_follow_inheritance, changed_instances_names_the_extensions_whose_tables_moved, instance_warnings_name_the_missing_and_the_single, instance_label_reads_the_title_of_a_non_default_or_a_named_default_with_company}`, `deeplink::tests::{instance_keys_take_the_extensions_place_in_every_route, instance_add_and_remove}` (+ the table example test, the allowlist test), `cli::tests::instance_list_reads_the_file_and_the_twins_are_checked_first` (+ the twins), `bar::tests::keys_and_targets` (`@`, `labelled`, `strip_label`), `sketchybar::tests::names_and_rule`; vitest `settings-instances.test.tsx` (14: the suffix grammar and slug, the tint hash against djb2, `resolveInstance`, `badgedIcon`, `leavesFile`, `needsSetup` through the inheritance, `byName`/`instancesOf`/the indexes, the Overview's rows per instance, the Extensions page's rows and section, the per-instance settings with the notes and a write under the key, the add form's slug/validation/tint/Create, rename/park/remove, the Palettes page grouped) and `gone-source.test.tsx` (3); the settings-window and settings-overview tests updated for the gallery's two GitHub instances. All suites green (cargo 405, vitest 222, host `instances.test.ts` 15; the host's calendar and whatsapp failures in a full `bun test` run are another round's, not touched).
- **Verified on hornet** with a scratch release instance (`target/scratch-inst2`, `io.cagdas.pal.inst2`, its own `PAL_CONFIG` and `XDG_DATA_HOME`, hotkey ctrl+alt+shift+o, `[bar] target = "off"`, `[instances.github] title = "Personal"`): `pal instance add github work --title Work` from the CLI wrote `[instances."github@work"]\ntitle = "Work"` and the log read `config applied ... instances=[github]`, `core/instances.get`, then `loaded github` and `loaded github@work` in workers with `bar github@work/notifications registered` and `index/github@work/` created, no restart; `pal instance list` printed `github\tPersonal\tdefault` and `github@work\tWork\ton`. With a hand-written `[extensions."github@work"]`, `[palettes."github@work-prs"]`, `[bar.items."github@work/notifications"]` and a `storage/github@work.json`, `pal instance remove github@work` logged `instances removed github@work instances."github@work" extensions."github@work" palettes."github@work-prs" bar.items."github@work/notifications"`, the host's reload of `github` alone, then `instances cleaned github@work files=[storage,index] frecency=0`; the file had no `github@work` left and `list` showed the default alone. A hand edit `enabled = false` under the table logged `instance github@work is disabled` with `github` reloaded alone, and its removal brought `github@work` back. The screen was locked the whole round (`CGSSessionScreenIsLocked`), so the live Settings window could not be imaged; the Instances section, the per-instance settings with the inherited notes, the add form (title, slugged key, swatches, preview) and the Palettes page grouped per key were rendered from the gallery with the two GitHub instances (`?gallery=settings:extensions|palettes`, Chrome headless at 960 by 640, light and dark) and looked at; the preview tile's size and the key field's background were fixed from those shots. Not verified live: a click through the Settings window (its commands are the CLI's), the confirm card of `pal://instance/*` from a link, `pal://open/github@work/prs` under this build (the form worked in phase 1).

## Decided: expansion, folder browsing, store palette, theme file, screenshots, compact (2026-09-17)

The triage's "close behind" six (`notes/pit-triage.md`: 106, 339, 257, 43, 102, 3), one round; three of them by forks (store, screenshots, folder browsing) with the shell changes they needed made first.

- **Snippet expansion** (106; `core/src/expansion.rs`, `app/src-tauri/src/expansion.rs`, `extensions/snippets/pal.json` settings). Opt-in, `[extensions.snippets] expand = true`, macOS only. The watcher is an `NSEvent` global monitor on the main thread, not a `CGEventTap`: the popover's peek already established that both need the same Input Monitoring grant and a listen-only tap buys nothing over the monitor (bar/popover.rs), and the monitor's block gets `characters` with the layout applied and no run loop of its own to keep. The core is pure: `Matcher` (the last 64 characters typed in the app in front, emptied on a Cmd/Ctrl combo, an arrow, Enter, Tab, Escape, a change of app, and after a hit; Backspace pops one), the `Prefix` rule (`;sig` default, `:sig`, or the bare keyword at a word boundary), `fill` (`{clipboard} {date} {time} {datetime} {uuid}` and `{cursor}`, which is removed and counted from the end; `{selection}` left as written since the selection while typing a keyword is the keyword), `plan` (backspaces = the prefix and keyword's characters, the filled text, the left arrows), and `perform` (the backspaces 6 ms apart, the text through `write_text_concealed` so pal's history and other managers skip it, the paste key, the arrows after a 120 ms settle, and the previous clipboard put back 300 ms later through the same `restore_plan` the clear-after uses: `clipboard::{snapshot, restore, send_paste, send_keystroke}` are the new crate-level helpers, `Keystroke` gained the arrow codes). Never in the excluded apps (terminals and password managers by default, bundle ids, case-insensitive) nor while `IsSecureEventInputEnabled` (Carbon) says a secure field has the keyboard; pal's own keys are invisible to a global monitor anyway; a `INJECTING` flag keeps the plan's own keys out of the matcher. The snippets are read from the extension's storage file when its mtime moves. Turning the key on asks for Input Monitoring once (`permissions::request_once`); the Overview's Input Monitoring row names snippet expansion when the key is on (`permissionRows` `expand`), so the row shows even without bar items. HUD "Expanded <name>" (`expand_hud`). A paste from the panel now drops `{cursor}` instead of leaving it in the text. Linux: not available (no portable tap; Wayland gives keys to the focused client only), documented in the palette's section and the README; `wtype` would only cover the injection half. Tests: core `expansion::tests` (7: the prefix rule at the last character, the longest keyword, the boundary, backspace and resets, the spellings, the placeholders and the cursor count, the plan, the clock), app `expansion::tests` (5: the manifest defaults off, the file's overrides, the stored rows, the exclusion and secure-input gate, the `NSEvent` classification). **Verified live on hornet** (04:56, once the screen unlocked; the scratch instance `target/scratch-left`, `io.cagdas.pal.left`, own config and data dir, bar off, `expand = true`, two stored snippets; a bare binary started from the terminal is judged by the terminal's grants, and the log said `accessibility: true, input_monitoring: true`, `monitor installed`). A scratch TextEdit document, the keys typed by System Events: `abc ;sig` became `abc Best,\nAda !` with the caret before the `!` (an `X` typed next landed as `Ada X!`), then ` ;stamp` became `[2026-09-17 04:57]` at the caret; the log: `expanded Signature 4 back, 11 chars, 1 left`, `expanded Stamp 6 back, 18 chars, 0 left`; the clipboard was empty before and empty after (the restore's `Clear` arm). The document was closed unsaved and the instance quit. Two things seen: the first run's keystrokes, sent two seconds after `open -a TextEdit`, reached the monitor (`snippets 2 with a keyword` logged) but not the document, so the typing wants the window to have settled (`activate`, a beat, then type); and the screen had been locked from 01:24 to about 04:50, during which no key reaches a monitor and TextEdit cannot be activated, so a check like this needs an unlocked session.
- **Folder browsing in Files** (339; `extensions/files/browse.ts`, index.ts). A second palette `browse` ("Browse Folder", input, `multi`, filters Name / Date / Size as the sort) pushed with `args: { browse: path }` and `title: short(path)`: the crumb is the folder. `..` first (Go up on Enter, `←`, `⌫`; absent at `/`), then the entries through the same `item()` as the search rows (folders lead with Browse on Enter and `→`, then Open and the rest; images get `thumbnailUrl` icons), every row with Show / Hide hidden files on `cmd+.` (flips the `show_hidden` setting through `settings.set`, so it persists, as Finder's does), 500 rows then a hint. A folder row anywhere in Files (search, recent, the root's inline completions) leads with Browse; `~/` or `/` typed inside the palette lists that folder whole instead of five completions. What the shell needed: `Effect.push.title` (the pushed level's crumb; protocol.ts, items.ts, types.ts, the Launcher's `enter`), and `rowKey` in the Launcher: a bare `→`, `←` or `⌫` in a list level while nothing is typed runs the action carrying that key on the row under the cursor, and for `←`/`⌫` on any row of the level (the `..` row from anywhere), picked on that row after the cursor moves to it (not through `run`, which reads the render's `current`; a confirm waits for the cursor); with text in the box the keys keep their native effect, and a view level's own `backspace` key still reaches `viewCommand` (a first cut swallowed it, caught by view-level.test). Tests: `files.test.ts` (37: the sorts, `..`, the pushes with `title`, `cmd+.` through the harness's written settings, the `~/`-style listing, the cap), `app/src/__tests__/browse-keys.test.tsx` (4). Shot `5-browse` rendered and looked at.
- **Store palette** (257; `extensions/store/`, `app/src-tauri/src/extensions.rs`, `sdk/src/api.ts` `extensions`). One input palette over `https://pal.cagdas.io/api/extensions` (`PAL_STORE_API` for the tests), filters All / Installed / Updates / the seven categories, rows with the tile, the tagline, chips for bar items, links and accounts, an Installed or Update or bundled tag; a lazy detail with the description, the `store.features` bullets, the screenshots as markdown images (the Detail pane's `marked` loads http(s) images; `IMAGE_SRC` only gates view nodes) and a keys table per palette; Enter installs behind an `Action.confirm`, Update and Remove for store-installed ones, Open store page and Copy install command. The API answer is trimmed to what the rows and details need (~140 KB for the live 44) and cached in memory and `storage` for an hour (`cmd+r` forces); offline is a hint row, a stale cache leads with "Showing the list from N min ago". The new core capability `core/extensions.{list, install, update, remove}`: `list` classifies every registered extension (store dir, a configured dir, else bundled) and dedupes instances; the three writes hand the work to `deeplink::handle_trusted` (`pal://install/<spec>` and its siblings: the panel's Enter is the hand, so no card) and answer at once, since the store restarts the host the caller runs in. The "Extension Store" command row now pushes the palette when it is loaded (commands.rs `Plan::Store`), the website otherwise. Tests: `store.test.ts` (12, a `Bun.serve` fixture of five live entries), app `extensions::tests` (3). Shots rendered and looked at (md-storefront, teal: md-store rendered as a shield).
- **Theme file** (43; `core/src/theme.rs`, `app/src-tauri/src/theme.rs`, `app/src/theme.ts`, `app/src/ui/SettingsTheme.tsx`, `examples/themes/`). `[general] theme_file`: a name resolved as `<config dir>/themes/<name>.toml`, or a path. One TOML: `name`, keys shared by both schemes at the top level, `[light]` and `[dark]` sections; the allowed keys are `TOKENS` (the colours, the tag palette, the twelve brand tiles, the tile ink and ring, nine radii, the two font stacks; geometry, spacing and motion stay pal's), each `<key>` driving `--pal-<key>`. Validation in the core: an unknown key is a warning diagnostic with its dotted path, a bad value (a colour that is not `#hex` or an `rgb()`/`hsl()` call, a negative or non-numeric radius, a font stack with `;` or braces) is dropped with a warning, a file that does not parse is one error with its line; the sections win over the top level whatever the file's order. The shell loads it at startup and on a reload that changes the key, polls the file's mtime every second (one `stat`; no second notify watcher), and emits `pal://theme` (`Current { file?, theme: { name, light, dark }, diagnostics }`) to every window; `theme.ts` sets the section for the scheme in force (the pin, else the OS) as inline variables on `:root`, clears the previous set, and re-applies on a `data-theme` change or the OS's `prefers-color-scheme` flip, so the panel, the HUD, Settings and the popover agree. Settings > General > Theme file: a select over the folder's files by their `name` (plus "pal's own" and a path set by hand listed as itself), Edit theme file / Open themes folder (`theme_open`, `theme_open_dir`), the file's name and complaints under it; `theme_status` creates the themes dir with the two examples the first time the page looks for it (an existing dir, empty or not, is left alone, the folder button seeds an empty one). The two examples are Catppuccin Frappé (Latte as its light section) and Rosé Pine Dawn (Rosé Pine as its dark one), his kitty pair; the core test parses both clean. The gallery draws both examples over the same rows in both schemes from the files that ship (`gallery/theme-toml.ts`, a reader of the flat format for the gallery only: a first cut stripped `#` comments and with them every hex colour, seen as white text on a light panel) and the settings page carries the picker with a warning. Docs: the full token table in `docs/config.md` "Theme file". Tests: core `theme::tests` (6), app `theme::tests` (2), `app/src/__tests__/theme-file.test.tsx` (6).
- **Screenshots** (102; `extensions/screenshots/`). Capture area / window / screen rows (macOS `screencapture -i`, `-i -W`, whole; Linux `grim` with `slurp`, the window row taking the area path) with Capture after N s and the other destination as the second and third actions, the destination, folder (`defaults read com.apple.screencapture location`, else the Desktop), timer, sound, `all_files`, `limit` and `ocr_concealed` as settings; the pick hides first and the outcome reaches the HUD through `effects.run`. Then the recent shots newest first with `thumbnailUrl` icons and the PNG's dimensions off its IHDR bytes, actions Open, Reveal, Copy image (`copy_files`, multi), Copy path, Copy as markdown image (`![name](path)`), Copy text (OCR), Move to Trash (multi); a lazy detail with the picture itself; `suggest()` names a screenshot under two minutes old for the root's Now section. No OCR search over the folder (the pit card's other half): it would need text stored per file, as history's would. The shell change it needed: `icon://localhost/file?path=&size=` (icon.rs, `thumbnailUrl` in api.ts): a thumbnail of an image file on disk by absolute path, PNG / JPEG / GIF by extension only, the file itself for size 0, a 404 for anything else. Tests: `screenshots.test.ts` (13, a `PAL_SCREENCAPTURE_BIN` stand-in), `icon::tests` (1). Shots rendered and looked at.
- **Compact mode** (3; `app/src-tauri/src/compact.rs`, `tokens.css`, Launcher.tsx, Search.tsx, theme.ts). `[general] compact = true`, or `cmd+shift+m` in the panel (and "Compact panel" / "Full panel" under ⌘K), which writes the key through `settings_set`: the file stays the source of truth and the choice is per profile by construction. The page puts `data-density="compact"` on `<html>` (theme.ts, off the same config event as the theme) and tokens.css redefines the geometry under it (`--pal-panel-w` 560, rows 32, the search row 44, sections 24, the icon box 20, `--pal-density: compact`; the type sizes stay); the shell resizes the window to 560 wide at the configured height and re-places a visible panel (`compact.rs`, off the config reload). The Launcher folds the footer into the search row (`Search` gained `hint`, `onHint` and `count`, drawn with the footer's classes a size down), keeps the detail pane closed (`cmd+i` inert, the action not listed), and the row's subtitle goes as it does beside a detail pane. The gallery has a Compact section in both themes. Not Raycast's collapse-to-the-search-row: pal's root lists the frequent rows before anything is typed, so an empty panel would hide what the empty root is for. Tests: `app/src/__tests__/compact.test.tsx` (3), app `compact::tests` (1).
- **Seen on the way.** `bun test test/extensions/snippets.test.ts` alone fails its clock assertion between 21:00 and 24:00 UTC: the test process runs in UTC while the spawned host runs in the box's zone, so `isoDate(new Date())` and the host's `{date}` disagree for those hours; the six calendar failures in tonight's full host run (02:00 local, 23:00 UTC; rows dated by day, `standup@` where `next@` was expected) look like the same day-boundary split, untouched here; the suite pins `TZ` only in calc.test.ts. The worktree's `node_modules` is a symlink into the main checkout's, so the bundled extensions' `@zcag/pal` resolved to the main checkout's SDK (no `thumbnailUrl`) until the files fork added the ignored `extensions/node_modules/@zcag/pal -> ../../../sdk` link; a worktree needs that link (or its own install) for the host tests. `npm run build` gates on `tsc`, which another agent's in-flight test file failed; the scratch build ran `vite build` directly.

## Polish round 1 (2026-09-17)

Every panel fixture (54 extensions, 200 shots) and every bar fixture (11 items, 60 strips and popovers) rendered in both themes with `app/scripts/shots.mjs`'s logic (a scratch copy writing raw PNGs into the session scratchpad, `&theme=dark` through shots.tsx and bar-shot.tsx), plus the five Settings pages in both themes (`?gallery=settings:<page>`), and looked at on contact sheets of four, the suspects one by one. The store PNGs were then regenerated with the driver itself (`node app/scripts/shots.mjs`, then `bar`). Checked against the round's rules: nothing clipped, tiles and tinted glyphs of one weight per list, no grey on grey in dark, selected rows readable, AA on badges and muted text, keycap hints on one line, section headers and counts, hint rows naming the fix, sentence-case actions, tabular numbers, visible tracks, unstretched art, the 20 px crumb tile, popovers with a footer that fits, forms with aligned labels. What was off and what changed (PNG paths are the store's, `extensions/<name>/screenshots/`; the dark renders live only in the scratchpad):

| extension / page | what was off | what changed | PNG |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2048 | `3-over` was not over: the rigged board still had a 2,2 pair, so the shot showed a live board under the "over" caption; the crumb wore the old 🔢 emoji rather than the manifest's amber tile | `fixture.ts`: a board that up fills with no pair left (the Game over banner shows), `icon: manifest.icon` | `2048/3-over.png`, `2048/1-board.png` |
| apps, files, store | state tags capitalised (`Running`, `Default`, `Installed`, `Update to 0.1.0`) while every other state tag in the set is lowercase (`running`, `connected`, `pinned`, `failed`, 28 more) | lowercased in the code, tests, fixtures and docs/palettes.md; the rule: a state tag is lowercase, a tag that is a name keeps its case (`PR`, `TypeScript`, `Join`) | `apps/1-list.png`, `files/3-open-with.png`, `store/1-browse.png` |
| bookmarks | the file's rows sat above "Chrome 5" with no header of their own | the rows (and the file's hint row) take the file's basename as their section (`bookmarks.json 3`); test and fixture follow | `bookmarks/1-list.png` |
| shortcuts | the folder-less shortcuts sat above "Home 4" with no header | section `No folder`; test and fixture follow | `shortcuts/1-list.png` |
| colors | the manifest icon was still the hex dot (`#4F46D6`): a 6 px dot in the crumb, the palettes' rows and hint rows grey | `tile("indigo", md-palette)` in pal.json (the tile the icon-tiles note asked for); the palettes inherit it, so the four `icon: ICON` lines went; hint rows carry md-information_outline and take the indigo tint; `fixture.ts` writes the tile | `colors/1-picker.png`, `colors/5-history.png` |
| colors | `3-typing`: the keycap labels wrapped ("appl y", "clos e") when the long notation hint pushed the row | renderer: `.pal-kbd + .pal-view__text` never wraps (`ui.css`); the free text gives way instead | `colors/3-typing.png` |
| files | "Open with…" pushed a level that kept the Files crumb and "Search files by name" | `push.title` "Open README.md with"; tests and fixture follow | `files/3-open-with.png` |
| home-assistant | the attributes, brightness, volume and area levels kept the Home Assistant crumb and "Search entities" | `push.title`: the entity's name, "<name> brightness", "<name> volume", the area; tests and fixture follow | `home-assistant/3-detail.png` |
| docker, make (show levels) | a `show` level (Logs, a Makefile) drew no crumb tile and no footer icon | Launcher: the show level remembers the item's palette; crumb and footer draw its tile (`iconFor`) | `docker/3-logs.png`, `make/3-makefile.png` |
| bar popovers (all 11) | the popover's crumb and footer had no tile: a bar item's key (`calendar/upcoming`) is no source | Launcher `iconFor`: a key that is no source takes the tile of any palette of its extension (every palette wears the manifest's); bar-shot.tsx hands the manifest as the one source, as the app's popover has the extension's palettes | `*/bar-menubar-popover*.png` |
| bar popovers, dark (slack, whatsapp, timer done) | the urgent band was `--pal-destructive` with white ink: on the dark theme's salmon (#FF6E66) that is 2.5:1 | ink `--pal-accent-fg` (white on light's deep red, near-black on dark's salmon: 5.9:1 and 7:1), the crumb chip shaded toward `--pal-fg` with `color-mix` (darker in light, lighter in dark) | `slack/bar-menubar-popover-dark.png`, `timer/bar-menubar-popover-dark.png` |
| gmail, tela (popover footers) | the footer's primary hint repeats a menu row's whole title ("Open Mara Lind: Parser review before standup?"), leaving the context nothing | Footer: the hint's title is a span capped at a third of the footer (`32cqw`; the footer is an inline-size container: 243 px on the panel, 127 px in the popover) with an ellipsis | `gmail/bar-menubar-popover.png`, `tela/bar-menubar-popover.png` |
| slack (search), any long row | the footer's context title clipped mid-word ("…can you double chec") with no ellipsis: `text-overflow` sat on the flex container, not the span | `.pal-footer__title` min-width 0, hidden overflow, ellipsis | `slack/4-search.png` |
| clipboard, downloads, screenshots (thumbnails) | an `{ image }` row icon has no edge: the light placeholder vanished on the light panel and floated as a white square on dark | `.pal-icon__img[data-mask]` gets the tile's hairline (`outline` inset 1 px, which follows the mask's radius) | `clipboard/1-list.png`, `downloads/1-list.png` |
