| spotify | `2-lyrics-dark` is declared `theme: "dark"` in the fixture but shots.mjs ignored it: a rerun would have overwritten the dark PNG with a light one | shots.mjs: a shot's `theme` wins over the run's (`SHOTS_THEME`) | `spotify/2-lyrics-dark.png` |
| snippets | the form fixture's Text description still said "{cursor} is not supported" (expansion landed since) | the fixture carries the code's description | `snippets/4-form.png` |
| Settings > Bar | the appearance groups' titles (PLACEMENT, TEXT, COLOUR, BEHAVIOUR) were all caps in `--pal-fg-faint` on a sunken card; the brief has no all-caps labels and faint only on `--pal-bg` | 11 px medium, sentence case, `--pal-fg-muted` (`settings.css`) | scratchpad `settings/bar.png` |

Looked at and left alone, with the reason: the action panel's rows carry no icon except pal's own Refresh (a design question, not a defect); form field descriptions ending in a period (682 of 726 setting descriptions in the manifests do; full sentences keep theirs); gmail's Labels palette is `tier: "catalog"`, so its glyphs stay untinted by the rule; "Check for Updates" on About is the menu bar's Title Case item too; scripts' emoji rows are the data file's own; window-management's target level names the window in every subtitle; the slate tint in dark (#6F7B8E on #1C1C21, 3.9:1) is the brand as chosen, above the 3:1 for glyphs; the textarea resize grip and Chrome's spellcheck underline in forms are the webview's. Two renders raced under load and were re-rendered clean (generate `3-qr` typed before the palette opened, once). Tests after: host `bun test` 1091 pass, 1 skip (a first sweep under the screenshot run failed four on host timeouts, gifs 2 and home-assistant 2; both files pass alone and the sweep was rerun green once the renders finished), vitest 35 files, 222 pass, `tsc` clean over the app.

## Polish round 2 (2026-09-17)

The experience rather than the pixels: docs/getting-started.md walked as a new user on a fresh scratch profile (`io.cagdas.pal.fresh`, `target/scratch-fresh`, an empty `PAL_CONFIG` dir and `XDG_DATA_HOME`, `ctrl+alt+shift+n`, `[bar] target = "off"`, `menu_bar_icon = false`; a second profile with a broken extension in its store), the log as the record since the screen was locked (no screenshots of the panel; the gallery through the shots harness for the Settings visuals), the keyboard grammar as vitest interaction tests with real KeyboardEvents (`app/src/__tests__/keyboard-flows.test.tsx`: the walk root, palette, ⌘I, ⌘K, back, hide; Escape at every level; Tab with and without a filter; ⌘1..9; ⌘⌫; ↑ history; a confirm card; a toast; the HUD). Numbers from the fresh profile: first paint of the empty root 411 ms after start with nothing cached (`first paint (25)`; the first query answered at 369 ms), `hotkey->paint 0.8 ms`, host ready at 811 ms with 55 extensions (235 ms on the second profile), the Settings window built in 39 ms on its first open and painted 118 ms later. What disappointed and what changed:

| flow | what disappointed | what changed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| first run: the log | sixty `core core/settings.get 0.05ms` lines (every extension reads its settings at load) buried the startup; `[scripts] ` an empty line; the extensions' own prefixes were `[tela]` here and `gmail: ` there | host.rs logs a core call only when it failed or took 10 ms or more (`SLOW_CALL`); scripts logs its report only when it has one; the seven `name: ` extensions (calendar, quicklinks, bookmarks, home-assistant, hue, gmail, whatsapp) say `[name]`; four host tests followed |
| first run: the permission asks | the Wi-Fi palette is `live`, so the first show relisted it with the panel up, and `permissions::call` honoured any ask while a pal window was in front: the Location prompt fired on the first show next to the Accessibility ask (the 09-16 note accepted this) | an extension's ask is honoured only while the user is looking at it: the panel inside one of its palettes (`index::showing`, the page's last query scope) or a pick of its own in flight (`index::picking`, Enter on the row that offered it); the SDK sends the caller as `extension`; skipped asks say why in the log (`permissions location skipped the panel is at the root`, seen on both profiles). The Wi-Fi hint row now also stands while `not_determined` ("Enter shows the system prompt; pal reads no location, only the names", action Allow Location Access), so a user who lands in the palette after a held-back listing has a way in. docs/extensions.md, getting-started, troubleshooting, palettes.md and the wifi manifest say so |
| first run: the Overview | a fresh install with every bundled extension opened on "11 things to look at": Calendars and Location `not_determined`, Input Monitoring for the bar's peeks, and nine bundled extensions "need token" (gifs, home-assistant, hue, images, slack, tela, translate, whatsapp, youtube) | a permission is a row only when nothing else will ask for it: Accessibility while missing, Full Disk Access (no prompt exists), Input Monitoring once expansion is on (pal asked then; a peek only degrades), Calendars and Location once refused. A bundled extension never touched is not a thing to fix (its palette and its Extensions page say so); an installed one and an added instance keep their setup row. A fresh install now opens on Accessibility and Full Disk Access |
| first run: the Welcome tips | "Change the hotkey" opened Settings on the Overview and left the user to find the recorder | the row lands on General › Hotkey: `settings::open_at(page, anchor)` (the URL on a fresh window, the `pal://settings` payload on an open one; the page's `landing` state waits for the view, then `go` selects and flashes the row); subtitle "Enter opens the recorder in Settings" |
| keyboard: ⌘K | the shell's "pal" section came twice, split by Link: Reset ranking above Copy deep link, Refresh and Settings below | one pal section after Link: reset ranking, refresh, panel shape, Settings, tips |
| keyboard: a confirm card | with focus on the search box behind the card (a second question inside the previous one's exit motion, since Presence keeps the same Confirm mounted; or a stray click), Enter under "Quit Slack?" ran the row's Open | `useKeys` grew `modal`: a Confirm or ActionPanel handles a keydown that lands outside it (a capture listener on the window), swallowed as if inside, and stops once the scope is on its way out (`[data-exiting]`); the card refocuses its go-ahead on a new question |
| Settings: search | "dock", "crash", "welcome tips", "comments" found nothing: the General index carried hand-picked keywords, not the rows' descriptions | the General page's rows come from one text table that also builds its index (label, description and keywords), so a setting is found by any word of its description; a test walks the index against the page's anchors |
| Settings: Reset Ranking | forgot every ranking on one click (`data-destructive`) while Remove asks "Remove? Click again" | `ArmedButton` moved to SettingsField and shared: "Forget it all? Click again (5)" |
| Settings: a number setting | a value outside `min`/`max`, or not a number, went straight to the file (`specs::check` guards writes from code only) | `NumberControl`: what is typed stays in the box with the reason under it ("Between 10 and 5000", "At least 1", "Not a number"), nothing is written until it is fine, an emptied box unsets; `settings-field.test.tsx`, checked in the gallery's field showcase |
| states: a failing extension | said in Settings only; nothing at the root | one row per failed extension in the commands source (`commands::failed_rows`, `sync_failed_rows` on `extension/loaded`, `extension/error` and `host/ready`): "Broken failed to load", the error's first line, the manifest's icon, found by `failed`, `error` or its name; at the empty root it leads under "Needs attention" right after the tips (`index::query` splices it like Frequent, `rootHits` orders it); Enter opens Settings › Extensions on it (`Plan::SettingsExtension`, `open_at`); the row goes when the extension loads. Never a toast on a show. Seen on the second profile: `[host] failed broken: ...index.ts:33: Expected ";"`, `commands failed rows failed:broken`, `pal://commands/failed:broken` built the Settings window in 39 ms |
| states: the host restarting | a pick in the gap failed with "Failed: host not running" | "the extension host is restarting; try again in a moment" (`HOST_DOWN`, `HOST_EXITED` in host.rs); troubleshooting says the same |
| docs | getting-started said nothing but the config file is written into its directory; the first Settings open seeds `themes/` with the two examples (`theme seeded 2 examples`) | the paragraph says so and points at the theme-file section |

Looked at and left, with the reason: the Welcome tips' order and wording (Accessibility leads while missing, then the about row, the hotkey, the guide, GitHub, hide); the hotkey's first registration and the Spotlight guidance (unchanged, exercised by the settings tests); the tray hint (the first root hotkey as the accelerator); the diagnostics block's one `accessibility:` line (About lists every permission; the block stays short); the empty root's order (tips, attention, Now, Clipboard, Frequent, Recent Files, palettes and apps first); the "updating…" footer and the skeleton (round 1's tests); the crash announcer on a fresh profile (a report from another instance is logged and listed under About, never announced: `announce` wants a previous start). On the second profile the first two shows had the screenshots, downloads and media suggests time out at 1.5 s and the screenshots listing at 2 s, gone by the third show (`screenshots/screenshots 4 items 27.3ms`, `core/media.now_playing 185.75ms`) and absent on the first profile; a cargo build had just finished on the box, and it did not reproduce, so it is noted, not fixed. The scratch profiles read the daily machine (a `gh` token, Slack's local token), which is the machine, not pal. Tests after: vitest 37 files, 237 pass; `cargo test --workspace` 167 + 239 pass; host `bun test` 1091 pass, 1 skip; clippy clean; `tsc` clean; docs lint clean.

## Decided: images (2026-09-17)

The wishlist's TinyPNG and Image Modification rows as one bundled extension, `extensions/images/`, pink tile, md-image. One input palette (`images`), `multi`, the pane open by default.

- **A list, not a grid.** The brief asked for a gallery grid when several files are at hand; the grid cell's picture is capped at `--pal-grid-tile` (56 px) and a tile carries a name only, no subtitle or accessories, so the size and the pixel size the brief wants next to each thumbnail have no place in it, and `view` is a palette-level meta that cannot switch per listing anyway. The list with the pane is the gallery: a 24 px thumbnail on the row (the core's `icon://localhost/file` for PNG, JPEG and GIF, which decodes and caches them itself; `sips -Z` or ImageMagick into `~/Library/Caches/pal/images/thumbs/` as a `data:` url for WebP, AVIF, HEIC, TIFF, BMP, SVG, which the core's image crate does not read), size and dimensions on the right, the picture at 256 px over the info in the pane.
- **Sources** (`gather`, read once per 2.5 s window, not per keystroke): the Finder selection over AppleScript (`selection as alias list`, 115 ms on hornet; a selected folder lists its images, 200 at most; there is no core capability for it and `selection.text()` is text only), then `clipboard.current()` (an image entry's history file as "Clipboard image", or the images of a copied file list), then the session's Results. A typed path lists a file, a folder's images (with its own All row) or completions. The Files extension exposes no "open with extension" hook, so no row was added there.
- **Every operation is a job** (`ops.ts`, pure): `plan(job, input, { avail, quality, tmp, output, dims })` answers `{ tool, steps: [{ tool, argv }], output, lossless? }` or `{ missing, why }`; `exec.ts` resolves argv[0] to a binary (`magick` or `convert`, mozjpeg's keg-only `cjpeg` first) and runs the steps, a replace through a temp file so a failed step leaves the source whole. Output naming (`outputFor`): next to the source with the suffix (`-compressed`, `-web`, `@0.5x`, `-800w`, `-800x600`, `-2000max`, `-rotated90`, `-flipped-h`, `-square`, `-16x9`, `-padded`, `-stripped`, `-gray`, a `-icons` folder), a convert swaps the extension, `@2x` strips an existing `@Nx`, half of a `@2x` stem is the bare name, a taken name gets `-2`. A clipboard image's result goes to the cache's `clipboard/` folder (its source is pal's history file, not a place of the user's) and the image itself is copied back (`«class PNGf»`/`«class JPEG»` over AppleScript, `wl-copy`/`xclip` on Linux; other formats as `copy_files`).
- **The tools order is the user's** (`tools`, a list; one left out is never used; every result names the tool) with two rules on top: geometry is sips's on macOS whenever it is listed, whatever its place (native, colour-managed, 10 ms; sips reads WebP and SVG but writes neither, so those come out as PNG), and sips never compresses a PNG (measured: a 57 KB palette screenshot re-encoded to 93 KB RGBA; ImageMagick's `-define png:compression-level=9` kept it at 57 KB), so the PNG candidates are pngquant, oxipng, optipng, magick. A lossy ask on a machine without pngquant lands on the lossless tool and the row says "(lossless: no lossy encoder for it)". JPEG lossy goes `djpeg` to PPM then `cjpeg -quality -optimize -progressive` (cjpeg cannot read JPEG; a PNG source goes through a sips BMP); `jpegtran -copy none` is the lossless path. Strip is pal's own for PNG and JPEG (`stripJpeg`: APPn but APP0 and APP14 and COM dropped up to SOS; `stripPng`: tEXt iTXt zTXt tIME eXIf iCCP dSIG dropped), since `sips -d` cannot strip (`-d all` errors, `-d copyright` kept the field) and re-encoding keeps EXIF; other formats through `exiftool -all=` or `magick -strip`. Verified on hornet: sips 316 writes heic, avif, pdf, tiff, gif, bmp, ico (32 px) and icns is refused (iconutil does it); `cwebp` reads WebP; `avifenc -q -s 6`; `-p H W` pads, `-c H W` crops centred, `-m Generic Gray Profile.icc` greys.
- **No gain writes nothing**: a compress whose output is not smaller deletes it and answers a failure toast naming the tool ("photo.png is already small: pngquant could not make it smaller; nothing written"); pngquant's 98/99 exits are that, not errors.
- **One ending for every operation**: the result's path copied (paths one per line for a batch), the panel down, the HUD line "Compressed photo.png: 1.4 MB → 312 KB (−78%), pngquant · path copied" (Raycast's TinyPNG ends the same way). A batch runs three at a time and is waited on for 8 s (`BUDGET_MS`); past that the pick answers a progress toast and the end lands through `effects.run({ copy, hud })`, which reaches the HUD whether or not the panel is up (`hud::show` is its own window). Optimise for web is the exception: a view (`View.id` `web:<n>`) with a keyed row per image (thumbnail, before → after, the saving, a progress bar) and the total at the foot, pushed by `view.update` as each file lands, so a long batch is watched rather than waited for.
- **Undo without deleting**: `replace` copies the source to the cache's `originals/` before the output takes its place (the row then carries Restore original, `cmd+shift+z`, and never Trash); a result written next to its source carries Move result to Trash (`cmd+d`, asks, Finder's delete or `gio trash`). The icon set is a folder and never replaces.
- **Levels** for Resize, Convert, Rotate, Crop (a `push` with `{ op, files }` and a crumb "Resize hero.png" through `push.title`): choice rows whose subtitle names the pixels they land on, whose pane shows the source picture, the result size, the file it writes and the tool (or "Needs: cwebp (brew install webp) or magick" on an inert row), and typed sizes in Resize (`800`, `x600`, `800x600`, `50%`, `2x`); each level lists its own results under the choices.
- **Tools found on hornet**: sips 316, cjpeg/djpeg/jpegtran (libjpeg-turbo 3.2.0; no mozjpeg), cwebp/dwebp, avifenc/avifdec 1.4.2, ImageMagick 7.1.2, iconutil, tesseract, pdftoppm, heif-convert; missing: pngquant, oxipng, optipng, exiftool, gifsicle. So on this machine PNGs compress losslessly through ImageMagick until `brew install pngquant oxipng`, which the empty listing's hint rows say.
- **Not verified live**: Copy image over AppleScript (his clipboard held a picture during the round and was not overwritten; the idiom is the standard one and the test covers the fallback), the Linux paths (the plans' argv are tested; ImageMagick's `-crop WxH+0+0 +repage` with `-gravity center` and `-extent` are the documented forms), and TinyPNG against the real API (a mock answering the documented shape).
- **Tests**: `host/test/extensions/images.test.ts` (39: formats, naming, `parseResize`, `resized`/`cropped`, every operation's argv per tool and platform, the built-in strip on crafted bytes, the three info parsers, and over the wire on PNGs drawn by `images-png.ts` against stand-in tools (a temp bin on `PAL_IMAGES_PATH`, one POSIX sh script per tool: a copy, a compressor's 60% copy over 400 bytes, `sips -g` from the PNG header), so the argv, naming, HUD text and Results read the same on the CI runners; the real tools get three extras skipped where absent, and the file passes with `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin` plus bun alone: the sources and rows, the key table against the code, a query and typed paths, the clipboard, compress and `-2`, already small, strip/gray/resize/rotate/crop/pad/convert with a missing tool, replace and restore, the clipboard result, the icon set, the web view and its picks, the All batch and marked rows, TinyPNG against a mock with a bad key, OCR/info/copy, the hints). Fixture `extensions/images/fixture.ts` (drawn pictures, `~/Desktop/site`), six screenshots looked at in both themes, `manifest.test.ts` sweep clean, `tsc` clean over the host tree.

## Findings: performance round 2 (2026-09-17)

The same table as the 09-16 pass after the day's growth (55 bundled extensions, five workers, live views, the bar stream, the expansion monitor, the theme poll), measured on hornet with a scratch release build (`target/scratch-perf2`, identifier `io.cagdas.pal.perf2` baked in, the staged host/sdk/extensions beside the binary, the frontend from `vite build`), own `PAL_CONFIG` with the daily config's shape (the same `[extensions.*]` tables minus every token, `[instances."gmail@work"]`, scripts on the v1 config, bookmarks on the data file) plus `[bar] target = "off"`, `menu_bar_icon = false`, `check_updates = false`, hotkey ctrl+alt+shift+p, own `XDG_DATA_HOME`. The screen was locked the whole round (05:00 to 06:00), which matters twice below. Clocks: the `since start` marks in the log; `ps -o rss,cputime` and `footprint` at 5 s and 125 s into an idle stretch after three shows; `top -l` for wakeups; the host alone through `host/test/harness.ts` (a scratch script starting it on a root holding one, some or all of the staged extensions, `ps rss` 0.8 s after `host/ready`, then every non-input palette listed once); the core's `corpus_heads` on the profile's `index/` for the restore, the keystroke and the wire; the timer count is the log's line count over 60 s idle plus every `bar`, `refresh` and `view` line in it. "Before" is HEAD 220abf5, "after" the fixes below; both binaries ran against the same warm cache, one after the other.

| What | Round 1 after (09-16, 36 ext) | Before (55 ext) | After |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Cold start (warm cache) to cache restored | 190-204 ms (14 ms) | 186-207 ms (17.0-17.8 ms; 121 sources, 17293 items) | 194-205 ms (16.8-17.2 ms) |
| Cold start to first paint (200 hits) | 399-419 ms | 369-395 ms | 377-394 ms |
| Cold start to `host/ready` | 404-416 ms | 433-517 ms (host 208-310 ms after spawn) | 362-440 ms (137-217 ms after spawn; the first run after a build 848 ms, the signature check) |
| Fresh profile (no cache): first paint / `host/ready` | 411 / 811 ms | 362 / 442 ms (76 hits) | 444 / 598 ms (a cargo build was compiling in the background; not re-run) |
| `pal show` to paint, after the first show | 1.3-2.2 ms | 1.0-4.6 ms (six shows, both binaries back to back: 5.7 3.3 3.7 4.1 4.5 4.6) | 1.5-4.4 ms (5.0 2.1 1.5 4.0 4.0 4.4); the same within noise |
| Idle after 2 min, app: RSS / footprint / peak | 153-169 / 56-59 / 93 MB | 163 / 67 / 94 MB | 171 / 66 / 98 MB (unchanged) |
| Idle after 2 min, `pal-bun`: RSS / footprint / peak | 161 / 68 / 107 MB | 280 / 123 / 204 MB | 224 / 96 / 157 MB |
| Host alone, all extensions loaded, nothing listed / after every listing (RSS) | | 161 / 194 MB (bare host 35 MB; calc alone 99, icons 58, emoji 45, slack 43, unicode 42, gmail 42, hue 40, github 40, home-assistant 40, colors 39) | 106 / 150 MB (calc alone 38) |

| Idle CPU over 120 s, app / host | 0.02-0.03% / 0.02-0.1% | 0.04% (50 ms) / 0.25% (300 ms) | 0.07% (80 ms) / 0.13% (160 ms; the host alone with no extension is 80 ms a minute, bun's own loop and the root watchers, and a bare `bun -e` is 20) |
| Processes and threads at idle | | 10 processes at 5 s (app, `pal-bun`, 8 leaked `osascript`, see below), 4 at 125 s (app, `pal-bun`, the media stream's `sh` + `perl`; the `osascript`s gone at their two-minute event timeout); app 61 threads at 5 s and 33 at 125 s (blocking threads waiting on those), host 30 | 4 processes (app, `pal-bun`, `sh` + `perl`), app 51 then 31, host 32; 65 threads in all |
| Timers firing at idle over 60 s (log lines; `bar` / `refresh` / `view` events) | | 0 lines, 0 events (the popover state machine logs one `PopoverEnter Hidden -> Hidden` when the mouse crosses the bar's zone) | 0 / 0 |
| The theme file's 1 s mtime poll | | ticks with or without a file set: a `Config` clone (55 tables) + `theme::resolve` + `stat` per second; app cputime over 60 s 10 ms without a file and 10 ms with one (the `ps` resolution) | no tick without a file; one `stat` a second with one; 10 ms / 0-10 ms; a save while set still loads within a second, a save after the key is removed loads nothing (verified in the log) |
| The expansion monitor while typing 200 chars | | live `top -pid` while a script types into TextEdit: blocked, the screen was locked (no key reaches a global monitor, TextEdit cannot be activated); in-process the handler's work is `stat` + `Matcher::feed` with 20 snippets at 2.8 us per key (a throwaway release example), plus `frontmostApplication` and `IsSecureEventInputEnabled` per key | the snippet list is shared (`Arc`) per key rather than cloned; still to be measured live |
| A bar render storm: `[bar] target = "menubar"`, then `show` | | 9 items asked at once on the show trigger (hue, tela, spotify, slack, whatsapp, gmail, gmail@work 1-11 ms each; github 633 ms, `gh` over the network; media timed out at 5 s twice) | the same nine 1-10 ms, github 497 ms, media 181-237 ms |
| Every show relists the live palettes | 10 palettes, ~80 ms wall | 22 palettes (no `ttl`: system, audio, colors/history, downloads, spotify/devices, spotify/queue, browser-tabs, menu-bar, gifs/favourites, timer, translate/history, media, screenshots, wifi, bluetooth, youtube/later, speedtest/history, obsidian/daily, obsidian/recent, windows, scripts/otp, scripts/commands; the 6 with a `ttl` skipped while fresh), 2005 ms wall (media timing out; 305-360 ms once it answered), the rest 1-8 ms each but wifi 65-77, bluetooth 83, browser-tabs 47-61, windows 38, scripts/otp 372-379 (the v1 script) | 22 palettes, 310-390 ms wall; five shows cost the host 350 ms CPU (70 ms a show) and the app 600-700 ms (120-140 ms a show: the panel show itself and the WebKit UI-process work, spread thin in `sample`) |

| `emoji/emoji` (1906), `unicode/unicode` (1795) | 626 KB, 250 KB | 654 KB (271 KB of it the per-row actions, kept per row for `paste_by_default`), 260 KB | unchanged |
| `colors/colors` (995 rows, was 700) | 239 KB | 356 KB (211 KB of it the swatch icons: a percent-encoded SVG data url of 212 bytes per row) | 285 KB (117 bytes per swatch) |
| `downloads/downloads` (49) | | 65 KB (26 KB of it eight actions per row) | 39 KB |
| `bookmarks/bookmarks` (321) | 197 KB | 206 KB (71 KB of actions: the 7 file rows three each, the 314 browser rows four) | 205 KB (the file rows' three are the palette's; the browser rows keep their "Open in Chrome") |
| `obsidian/notes` (248), `github/repos` (137), `wifi/wifi` (95), `apps/apps` (132) | | 180 KB (102 KB actions, seven per row, their order set by `open_with`), 148 KB (38 KB actions, 62 KB inline detail), 43 KB (26 KB actions, the confirm names the network), 36 KB | unchanged (see below) |
| All default listings (120 files) | 3169 KB | 3827 KB | 3743 KB |
| Index restore (`corpus_heads`) | 3.4 MB in 23 ms (parse 15, fill 4.2) | 121 files, 3.8 MB in 57 ms (parse 27, fill 7.9); in the app 17.0 ms for the same files | 3.7 MB in 58 ms (parse 28, fill 7.6); in the app 17 ms |
| Per keystroke, core, 17.4k items: `c` / `chr` / `git` / `ha` / `""` | 1.08 / 0.83 / 0.89 / 1.17 / 0.10 ms | 1.31 / 1.03 / 1.08 / 1.33 / 0.107 ms | 1.28 / 1.05 / 1.08 / 1.34 / 0.109 ms (unchanged) |
| Root reply on the wire: `c` / `chr` / `git` / `ha` / `""` | 87.0 / 54.0 / 51.3 / - / 71.6 KB | 116.5 / 82.3 / 80.0 / 104.5 / 51.4 KB (github/prs 5-6 rows 7-8.4 KB, github/repos 6.6-7.5 KB, obsidian/notes 8 rows 6.4-7.3 KB, downloads 8 rows 10.3 KB) | 112.1 / 79.3 / 78.9 / 103.8 / 51.8 KB (downloads 8 rows 6.4 KB) |
| Workers: spawn to loaded, warm / cold | 8-10 ms | 14.0-14.7 ms (2-7 ms the import) / 117 ms | 22-26 ms in the fresh-profile run (the background build) |

What stood out, and the fix:

- **`osascript` had no deadline, and behind a locked screen Spotify and Music do not answer Apple events.** Every `media.now_playing` (the media listing on every show, the root's `suggest` on every show, the bar item on `[bar] target` other than off) ran two `osascript`s that sat on AppleScript's two-minute event timeout: eight of them alive at once on the scratch instance (24 MB RSS each, a blocking thread each: the app at 61 threads), four on the daily `/Applications/pal.app` at the same moment, the media listing 5.1 s and every show relist 2.0 s (the media relist timing out). `tool::run_timeout` was a plain `run` on macOS (coreutils' `timeout` is not stock); it now has a native deadline, `run_with` (`core/src/tool.rs:43`: the pipes drained on threads, `wait_until` at `:83` polling `try_wait` then `kill` + reap past the deadline; the reader threads are left to end with the pipes since a grandchild may hold them), `run_timeout` (`:103`) uses it on every platform, and `osascript` (`:115`) runs within `OSASCRIPT_SECS` = 5 (`:110`), as does the MediaRemote adapter's one-shot `get` (`core/src/media.rs:649`), which is what answers while the stream is down. Verified: `osascript("delay 60")` comes back "osascript did not answer within 5 s" at 5.0 s with no `osascript` left running; `run_timeout_kills_a_tool_that_never_answers` in `tool::tests`. The hang itself stopped reproducing at about 05:40 (both binaries' shows relisted media in 180-270 ms from then on), so the after column's show relist is the unblocked case; the deadline is what bounds the next locked night.
- **calc created mathjs at load: 66 MB of the host and 130 ms of its start.** `import("mathjs").then(create)` ran at import (`bun build` inlines a dynamic import, so the 1.3 MB was parsed with the entry too); `math.ts:13-14` now makes it on the first evaluation (`mathjs()`), and `build-extensions.sh:45` bundles with `--splitting`, so the dependency is a chunk beside `index.js` loaded on first use. calc alone in the host: 99 MB / 84 ms ready before, 45 MB / 30 ms lazy but inlined, 38 MB / 26 ms split; the host with everything: 161 to 106 MB before a listing, `host/ready` standalone 139 to 70 ms, in the app 310 to 137-217 ms after spawn. The first sum typed pays the 80 ms once.
- **The theme poll ticked once a second whether or not a theme file was set**, cloning the whole `Config` each time to resolve the path. `theme.rs:92` `install`: the loop reads the file `load` remembered, sleeps `POLL` only while one is set, and otherwise waits on a `Notify` that `load` fires when a file arrives (`:84`, `:100`); a save while set still loads within the second. The cost was below `ps`'s resolution either way (10 ms over 60 s in both states); what went is the wake and the clone.
- **The catalog rows that grew this round.** colors' swatch icon was `encodeURIComponent` of the whole SVG (212 bytes a row, 60% of the listing); `color.ts:384` escapes the `#` alone (117 bytes; the markup is legal in a data url). downloads carried its eight file actions on every row (`downloads/index.ts:329`: the palette's now, the in-progress and folder rows keep their own); bookmarks' file rows the three common ones (`bookmarks/index.ts:201`; a browser's rows add "Open in <browser>" and so keep theirs, which is 314 of the 321 rows here, hence the 1 KB). Tests follow (`downloads.test.ts`, `bookmarks.test.ts`, `colors.test.ts`).
- **The expansion handler cloned the snippet list on every key typed anywhere** (`expansion.rs:109`): an `Arc` now. Small (2.8 us a key in-process); the live cost with `top -pid` waits for an unlocked screen.

Left as measured, with the reason:

- **The four `multi` extensions run in a worker each, alone or not** (github, slack, home-assistant with one instance; gmail with two): 4.8 MB and under two threads per worker, 14 ms from spawn to loaded in parallel with the rest, ~24 MB of the host's 224 MB. An inline path for a lone default would save ~14 MB on this profile and give up what the worker is for (its own module registry, `terminate()` on a hang; `docs/design/instances.md`, "instances, phases 0 and 1"): not worth a second code path.
- **The 22 live palettes relist on every show** at 310-390 ms wall and ~70 ms of host CPU a show: each is 1-8 ms but wifi (65-77 ms, CoreWLAN), bluetooth (83), browser-tabs (47-61, CDP), windows (38) and scripts/otp (372-379: his v1 `run.sh`, `live = true` in that config). The rows are the reason they are live (a copied colour lands in history, a closed tab leaves the root); `live` + `ttl` already exists for a palette whose rows can be a minute old, and none of these declares one. The device palettes are open above ("device palettes").
- **github's rows are 1.1-1.4 KB each** (per-row actions that vary with state, draft, mergeability and a local clone; an inline `detail` beside the lazy pane) and still lead every typed reply; obsidian's seven actions a row follow `open_with`, so they cannot be the palette's without a meta refresh on a settings change (emoji's reason too); wifi's Forget confirm names the network. Each is its owner's call.
- **The per-show app CPU (120-140 ms)** is the panel show and the WebKit UI-process work, not the index (one `pal://index` per show relist batch, the query 0.1 ms); `sample` over four shows shows nothing but waits.
- **The host's idle floor** (80 ms a minute with no extension loaded, 160 with all 55 and the five workers) is bun's loop plus the recursive root watchers; a bare `bun -e` idles at 20.

Tests after: `cargo test --workspace` 167 + 240 pass; clippy clean; vitest 37 files, 237 pass; `tsc` clean over the app and the host; host `bun test` 1126 pass, 1 skip (72 files).

## Decided: pit partials round (2026-09-17)

`notes/pit-triage.md` re-read against the entries of the 16th and 17th: 25 of its `partial` rows had landed since (marked `done` with where), 14 keep a `partial` with a rewritten "missing" line, and the 20 highest-value remaining partials for a keyboard-driven daily launcher were pulled in one round, six forks on disjoint file sets, the docs merged by hand afterwards. Each item: code, tests, docs (`docs/palettes.md`, `docs/extensions.md`, `docs/keyboard.md`, `docs/config.md`, `docs/links.md`), the fixture and screenshots re-rendered and looked at where the UI changed, the triage row marked. `sdk/src/protocol.ts` was edited once up front (`WindowLayoutOptions.step`/`cycle`, every verb in `WindowLayout`, `"glyph"` in the text node's `style`) so no fork touched it.

## Polish round 3: code (2026-09-17)

The code itself, after a day of sixty-odd agents: one `@zcag/pal` for what the bundled extensions had each written, the relative imports between extensions gone, the casts, dead code, stale comments, message wording and test flakiness swept across `sdk/src`, `host/src`, `app/src`, both crates and all 55 extensions. Seven forks on disjoint file sets (terminal and file ops; markdown, tokens and the messaging extensions; tabs, colours and clipboard; the view extensions; the list extensions; Rust; app, host and the SDK's core files), the SDK modules and their tests written first so every fork imported the same thing. Every change small, proven by the extension's own tests (a helper that differs in output from the SDK's was not migrated, it is listed below).

**The SDK grew 12 modules** (`sdk/src/index.ts` re-exports them; `sdk/README.md` and docs/extensions.md "Shared helpers" document them; `sdk/src` is 4297 lines now). The bundler tree-shakes them per extension (`app/scripts/build-extensions.sh`: wifi's bundle grew 372 bytes, the 55 together 144 KB), and `host/tsconfig.json` and `sdk/tsconfig.json` now carry `noUnusedLocals` and `noUnusedParameters` like the app's.

| SDK export | what it replaced |
| --- | --- |
| `hint(id, name, subtitle?, { icon?, actions?, section? })`, `HINT_GLYPH` (`rows.ts:14`) | 33 local `hint` builders and inline `hint:` rows (every extension but the games); ids are `hint:` prefixed everywhere now (otp, onepassword, browser-tabs, network, hue, home-assistant, menu-bar, wifi, audio, bluetooth, shortcuts, calendar, media, screenshots gained the prefix; picks and tests follow); the glyph is md-information_outline in every list (gmail/whatsapp/slack/screenshots had the filled mark, github an octicon) |
| `toast(title, message?, style?)`, `failed(what, e)` (`rows.ts:24,27`) | 14 `failed`/`fail`/`failToast`/`failure` copies (apps, wifi, audio, bluetooth, calendar, onepassword, browser-tabs, home-assistant, windows, hue, clipboard, spotify, images, timer, screenshots) and 80-odd `{ keep: true, toast: {...} }` literals; `toast` omits `message` and `style` when not given (the UI's default is a success) |
| `text`, `row`, `column`, `keycap`, `keyHint(keys, what, { action?, size? })`, `POPOVER_W` (`rows.ts:30-44`) | the builders in 17 view files (2048, blackjack stays local: Gallery.tsx bundles it into the webview), 12 keycap-hint lines, `COMPACT_W = 396` in 9 popover views |
| `bytes`, `truncate`, `oneLine`, `slug`, `errorMessage`, `mdEscape` (`text.ts`) | screenshots' and downloads' `size` (byte-identical), 9 `short`/`cut`/`clip` truncations, tela's and clipboard's `slug`, 111 `(e as Error)?.message ?? e` / `e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)` expressions, gmail's `mdEscape` (gmail keeps the fold-italics step on top, `gmail/mail.ts:169`; whatsapp imported gmail's) |
| `tilde(path)` (`api.ts:24`) | `short` (`~` for home) in files/ops, screenshots, images, make, docker (docker's lacked the `p === HOME` case), clipboard keeps its own for the test's injected home |
| `exec(argv, { ms, cwd, stdin, env })`, `run(argv, opts)`, `EXEC_MS` (`exec.ts:20,31`) | 12 spawn-and-collect runners (files' `runTool`, apps', network's, services' version probe, onepassword's `op`, processes' `lsof`, speedtest's `versionOf`, github's, slack's cookies, images' (kept as a wrapper for its stderr rule), screenshots', timer's CLI); a timeout says "<program> did not finish in N s". Left local: shell's `run` (process-group kill, capped streams), files' `search` and hue's `runFor` (streaming), docker's and make's background runs, services' `WAIT_MS` race, shortcuts' (no timeout by design) |
| `parseToken`, `mintToken`, `TokenError` (`token.ts:19,42`) | calendar/google.ts and gmail/api.ts (identical `parseToken`, near-identical `mint`; calendar prefixes the account name) |
| `now()` (`clock.ts:10`) | calendar/clock.ts and snippets/clock.ts (the round-of-the-17th note chose a clock per extension; two identical `PAL_NOW` readers were one) |
| `pngSize(head)` (`png.ts:2`) | files/meta.ts (no IHDR tag check) and screenshots/shots.ts (`{ w, h }`); `{ width, height }` with the tag check |
| `imageData(url)`, `forgetImages` (`image.ts:11`) | slack's and whatsapp's `avatarData` (identical; whatsapp's 2.5 s fetch is the SDK's 2 s) |
| `terminal.linux/linuxArgv/argv/open/on/at/quote/Choice` (`terminal.ts`) | apps/terminal.ts (Linux chooser), docker/terminal.ts and make/terminal.ts (identical copies), ssh's own copy of the same table, shell/run.ts's `terminalOn`/`terminalAt`/`q` (files imported it). Two tables remain in the one module, on purpose: `argv`/`open` run a command in a new window (docker, make, ssh), `on`/`at` open a shell in a folder with a line typed in (shell, files); merging them changes argv shapes the tests pin, a round of its own |
| `files.*` (`files.ts`) | files/ops.ts whole (rename/move/copy forms and submits, archives), downloads imported it |
| `md.*` (`md.ts`) | tela/md.ts whole; obsidian imported it. `render` gained `where` for its closing line ("… the rest of the page is in tela" / "in Obsidian"; obsidian said "tela" before); test moved to `host/test/md.test.ts` |
| `colors.*` (`color.ts`) | colors/color.ts whole (the sampler capability stays `color`); clipboard imported `parse`/`toHex`/`toHslString`/`toRgb` |
| `tabs.*` (`tabs.ts`) | browser-tabs/cdp.ts and the source half of browser-tabs/index.ts (CDP, the JXA run, Firefox's session store, `gather`, `active`/`find`/`focus`/`samePage`/`close`/`mute`); it reads the `browser-tabs` settings by name as the code did; quicklinks imported the three functions. browser-tabs/index.ts is the rows, the filters and the picks (90 lines) |

Relative imports between extensions: 10 import lines in 9 files removed (docker, make, ssh, shell → apps/terminal; files → shell/run; downloads → files/ops; obsidian → tela/md; whatsapp → gmail/mail; quicklinks → browser-tabs; clipboard ×2 → colors/color); 9 files deleted (the two clocks, apps/terminal.ts, docker/terminal.ts, make/terminal.ts, files/ops.ts, tela/md.ts, colors/color.ts, browser-tabs/cdp.ts). Fixtures still import the test harness (`host/test/harness.ts`, the mocks, `png.ts`; downloads' inline PNG writer is that one now): they are not shipped.

**Counts** (the forks' own, units as they counted): helpers merged 156 (A 24 in apps/docker/make/ssh/shell/files/downloads; B 21 in tela/obsidian/gmail/whatsapp/slack/calendar/github; C 9 in browser-tabs/quicklinks/clipboard/colors; D1 31 definitions in the view extensions; D2 71 sites in the 27 list extensions); casts removed 46 in TS (`sdk/src/icon.ts:56,61` `badged`/`tinted` return `TileIcon`/`TintedIcon`; `host/src/serve.ts:41` a `Params` type for the six `p: any`; `host/src/host.ts:471` `"method" in req`; `app/src/ui/icons.ts:13` a `Window` augmentation in vite-env.d.ts; `app/src/Settings.tsx:196`; `extensions/menu-bar/index.ts:41` `{ keys }` is an `Accessory` now (`protocol.ts:32`, the app drew it already); `scripts/commands.ts:85` `tile()`; `icons/index.ts:28` typed `Data`; 26 non-null `!` in Launcher, selection, SettingsTypes, host/instances, gmail/data, slack, github, media/view, files, apps, clipboard) and in Rust 10 `unwrap()`s (nine `db.lock().unwrap()` in `core/src/clipboard.rs` through `Inner::db` at :172, poison-tolerant like icons.rs; `e.image.expect` at :502 is `Error::NotFound`) plus the one `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` (`index::Pick` at `app/src-tauri/src/index.rs:994`); dead code removed 100-odd (52 `export` keywords off names with no importer in the list extensions, 19 unused locals and parameters the new tsconfig flags found (`calc/dates.ts:145 locale`, `obsidian/vault.ts:179 root`, ...), 5 un-exported in host/src, ~90 lines of duplicated token code, the avatar caches, `clipboard/rows.ts KIND_EXT.code`, `hue/rows.ts EFFECT_GLYPH`, `images/exec.ts forgetTools`, `spotify/api.ts clearLimit`, the files above); messages unified 48 (`failed()` everywhere; "Settings › Extensions › X" in 14 extensions, their tests, READMEs and docs, replacing "Settings, Extensions, X" and "Settings > Extensions > X"; "<setting> is not set": gmail "Token command is not set", whatsapp "API key is not set", slack "Slack token is not set", github "Organisation is not set", spotify "Client id is not set", translate "DeepL key is not set", home-assistant "Token is not set", clipboard "URL shortener is not set"; "<thing> is not installed": onepassword, obsidian's editor, images "pngquant", screenshots "grim and slurp are", files "mdfind" / "fd (or locate)", `core/src/windows.rs:364`; "<service> did not answer within N s": `host/src/bridge.ts:11 tooLate()` for the bridge, `serve.ts timeout()` and instances' `ask` (were "timed out after N ms"), `app/src-tauri/src/host.rs:276` (was "host timed out on X"; troubleshooting.md follows), `core/src/extensions.rs:545`, whatsapp, youtube, iconify, network, home-assistant; the Launcher's pick failure is "Could not <action>" (`app/src/Launcher.tsx:709`) in place of "Failed"; calendar's "Settings > Calendar" (a page that does not exist) is "Settings › Extensions › Calendar"; one Rust `effects::toast()` builder at `app/src-tauri/src/effects.rs:58` for the four inline envelopes); stale comments fixed 25 (the "sibling import is inlined by bun build / a shared helper in @zcag/pal would replace both" headers, "ported from v1's X" provenance in 9 headers (scripts keeps its v1 mentions: it reads v1 configs), `sdk/src/api.ts:468` nowplaying-cli as the source (the adapter is; it is the fallback), `core/src/config/mod.rs:1026` `pali.toml` (dev.toml), `bar/popover.rs:38` "does not ask for Input Monitoring yet" (expansion does), the icon.ts "another pass owns protocol.ts" paragraph, "the gallery renders a fixture state with this same function" in five renderers where only blackjack's is); tests pinned 3 (`app/src/__tests__/root-sections.test.tsx` fake timers over `ROOT_DEBOUNCE`, `settings-window.test.tsx` "3h ago" on a fixed clock, 2048's spawn split on a seeded LCG). Naming: read across crates and packages and left: `instance_key`/`key`/`InstanceInfo.key` is the instance key everywhere, `palette_id` one function, `Source { extension, palette }` the wire shape (the six `ext:` params abbreviate its field), host `paletteKey` (an error label) and app `paletteId` (the config key) are different things, "level" is the UI stack throughout, `pid` only ever a process id.

**Looked at and left, with the reason.** The `ago`/`relative` helpers (screenshots, otp, tela, github, colors, generate, clipboard, calc: eight formats, `12 s ago` / `just now` / `4m` / `yesterday` / `in 3 weeks`), the `size` formatters that differ from `bytes` (files: KB always one decimal; clipboard: no GB; images: MB two decimals under 10 MB; gmail: KB rounded; gifs: undefined passthrough; processes: KB input), obsidian's `cut` (trims before the ellipsis), whatsapp's and calendar's `clock` (different outputs): unifying any of them changes what a row says, which a design round decides, not a code one; the candidates and their formats are in the fork reports above. Two QR encoders (clipboard's `qrcode-generator` package, generate's hand-written `qr.ts`): different SVG. hue/color.ts and spotify/color.ts (a Hue gamut, cover-art tints) are their own maths. Casts kept: `hue/model.ts:111` (`Omit` over a generic intersection), spotify/api.ts's lenient `any` readers, the test fakes in move.test.tsx and view.test.tsx, `codePointAt(0)!` inside `for (const ch of s)`, `i.palette!` on hits that always carry a source, `e.text!` behind `kind ===` checks in clipboard (the fix is a discriminated `ClipboardEntry`, a protocol change). Rust unwraps kept: `serde_json::to_value(struct)` on Serialize types, piped stdio after `Stdio::piped()`, static parses of bundled manifests, `Vec` seeded with one element. The three `#[allow(deprecated)]` carry their reasons; every `cfg_attr(..., allow(dead_code))` guards a Linux-only parser (notes/linux.md). Tests still on the wall clock: translate.test.ts:216 (a 300 ms bound on a 350 ms debounce) and home-assistant.test.ts:333 (2 s on a 1 s timeout) want an injectable clock in those extensions (now `now()` exists, a follow-up); obsidian's daily-note test reads `new Date()` (same fix); colors.test.ts:475 "just now" inside a 45 s window; spotify.test.ts's random `REDIRECT_PORT` avoids collisions between runs. The `Bun.sleep` waits in the media/otp/timer/spotify/hue tests exercise real 1 Hz pushes. Spotify's suite failed six tests once while the other forks' suites ran alongside (host timeouts, as round 1 saw) and is green alone.

Tests after: host `bun test` 1198 pass, 1 skip, 0 fail (75 files; `helpers.test.ts` 16, `terminal.test.ts` 5, `tabs.test.ts` 3, `md.test.ts` 9 new); vitest 37 files, 239 pass; `cargo test --workspace` 167 + 246 pass; clippy `-D warnings` clean; `tsc` clean in app, host (with the unused flags, over every extension) and sdk; `build-extensions.sh` stages all 55.

## Polish round 4: real data (2026-09-17)

Every palette of the credentialed extensions listed through the host harness (`host/test/harness.ts`, `Host.bundled`) with the owner's real accounts (his config's settings passed as the overlay, the keychain secrets read into the process and never printed, the storage files preloaded into the harness's `stored` map, `instances.get` answering the two Gmail instances), read-only against every service; the rows looked at as data (id, name, subtitle, accessories, section, icon kind for the first 15 of each, one detail per palette), every bar item rendered, and the counts compared with the services' own. What listed: github 4 palettes (14 PRs, 16 issues, 137 repos, 7 notification rows), slack 3 (10 unreads, 54 channels, 12 status rows), gmail 2 instances (52 and 50 inbox rows, 17 and 12 labels), calendar 2 (7 and 3 rows over two Google identities), tela 7 (54 pages, 14 spaces, 14 decks, 2 sheets), whatsapp 3 (332 chats, 7 unread, 565 contacts), home-assistant 3 (412 entities, 274 services, 10 areas), hue 6 (8 rooms, 9 lights, 58 scenes, 17 sensors, 3 automations, 2 entertainment areas), obsidian 6 (248 notes, 64 tags, 20 recent), plus apps (132), bookmarks (321), downloads (49), browser tabs (9), shortcuts (13), ssh (6), recent files (3), screenshots, snippets, quicklinks, spotify (no client id in the overlay, so its hint rows), media and menu-bar (the harness has no core for them: a null answer, noted). 1Password was not running, skipped. The root: every listable palette's rows dumped (118 sources, 19,085 rows) into a scratch `#[ignore]` test over `pal_core::index::Index` with the palette rows and pal's commands as the app builds them (`palette_row`, `commands::rows`), his frecency file loaded from a copy, `root_boost`/`root_tier`/`views` as `query` uses them, twenty queries typed; the test was removed after. Counts: Gmail 22 unread in the inbox (the API's `INBOX.messagesUnread` 22; 23 unread overall, one outside the inbox), work inbox 0 (the API's 0, the item hidden), GitHub 6 notifications (the API's 6: 2 author, 2 mention, 2 subscribed), Slack 1 DM + 9 quiet channels (the popover's "1 direct message; 9 channels unread"), WhatsApp 7 unread chats (5 direct, 2 groups). The longest real titles (a 325-char mail subject with quoted-printable residue, a 200-char download name, a 106-char tab title, a 60-char Turkish group name with an emoji, a note with three tags) rendered through the gallery's shot driver in both themes on a scratch fixture: every row ellipsizes cleanly, the footer's context title too, beside the pane the accessories drop in order and the titles keep their minimum. Looked at and left, with the reason: the WhatsApp popover is 359 KB per render (seven avatars as full-size JPEG data urls through `imageData`; Slack's are 28 px PNGs and its popover is 6 KB): a resize step is an image-pipeline decision; GitHub's Mentioned issues from 2016 and 2017 are open issues he is mentioned in (the queries are `is:open`); Home Assistant's services accessory repeats the service id under a title-cased name (`Apple Tv`): the id is what a HA user reads; a Hue lightstrip on at 0 % is what the bridge says; two lights named `ofis` are two entities; Gmail's 325-char subject with `=0A` residue is the sender's; the "Nothing today" hint row and the calendar bar hidden at 07:30 with nothing inside ten hours are the rules working; the harness's null answers for media and menu-bar are the harness, not the extensions (their rows say the JS error, which a real core never produces). Tests after: host `bun test` 1199 pass, 1 skip (75 files); vitest 37 files, 239 pass; `cargo test --workspace` 168 + 246 pass; clippy `-D warnings` clean; `tsc` clean in app, host and sdk; `build-extensions.sh` stages all 55.

## Decided: the `ago`/`size` format unification (2026-09-17)

One relative time and one byte size across the product, both from `@zcag/pal`. `ago(t, { now?, short? })` in `sdk/src/clock.ts` reads `now()` (so `PAL_NOW` pins it): `just now` under 5 s, then `23 s ago`, `5 min ago`, `2 h ago`, `3 d ago`, `2 w ago`, `5 mo ago`, `1 y ago`, a future moment `in 2 h`; `short` is the column form (`now` under a minute, `5m`, `2h`, `3d`, `2w`, `5mo`, `1y`), what github's age column takes. `bytes` as it was (`512 B`, `3.2 KB`, `24 KB` whole from 10 KB, `1.5 MB`, `2.25 GB`). Replaced: the `ago` copies in colors, github, otp, screenshots and tela, `relative` in clipboard and generate, the `size` copies in clipboard, files, gmail and images. What changed on a row: colors' `yesterday` is `1 d ago`; clipboard's `3 hours ago` / `in 2 days` are `3 h ago` / `in 2 d`; generate's `now` is `just now`; tela's date past 30 d is `2 mo ago`; clipboard's `12.1 KB` is `12 KB`, images' `1.44 MB` is `1.4 MB`, files' `2.0 KB` past 10 KB is whole. Left: calc's `relative` (`Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` in the user's locale, a calculator answer, not a row age), the app's `relativeDate` (the `{ date }` accessory renderer, app-side), processes' KB-input formatter, gifs and slack (other rounds). Tests: `helpers.test.ts` covers `ago`; the extensions' own `ago`/`relative`/`size` cases are gone with the copies.

## Decided: Settings > Bar is a master/detail like every other page (2026-09-17)

The page named "Bar items" showed none of them. Measured before the change: the left column was 588 px tall against a **1839 px scroll**, because the Defaults card sat above the list and pushed it under the fold; the item pane scrolled **2405 px** across 16 stacked fields and ~1620 characters of description, in a pane pinned at `width: 40%` (384 px). Extensions, the page it should have looked like, is a 220 px list beside a **740 px** pane with 3 fields and 179 characters. Bar was the only settings page not using the shared `SettingsList` + `pal-split__pane`.

It uses them now. The list is every item, with a **Defaults row pinned above a divider** (`BAR_DEFAULTS = "__defaults__"`, safe as a sentinel because an item key is always `ext/item`) — the global form is a selection, not a header, which is what got the items on screen. The landing selection is that row, so the page still opens on what the target and the appearance defaults are. The pane is `pal-split__pane` at 740 px.

What moved out of a row: the on/off switch and the target select. A `SettingsList` row is `role="option"` inside a `role="listbox"`, so a control does not belong in one; the target select was a duplicate of the pane's anyway. A row now carries only a `Tag`, and only when something is worth saying — `no code`, `off`, `stale`, `urgent`, `hidden` — so a healthy list is quiet rather than a wall of state lines.

Per item, the appearance overrides are folded into **"Override defaults"**, a new `SettingsDisclosure` primitive (a native `<details>` with a chevron, a count and a right-aligned hint naming which default it overrides). It sits after Popover, so the order is Preview → Placement → Popover → Override defaults → Reset. It **auto-opens when the count is not zero**: a departure from the defaults is never hidden, only the eleven untouched fields are. Item pane: **2405 px → 824 px**.

Two layout things worth keeping: `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(220px, 1fr))` is wrong for a settings form — extra width buys *more* skinny columns (three 171 px ones at 1440), not wider ones; fixed columns with a `max-width` is the shape. And the four appearance fieldsets are a **multi-column**, not a grid: a grid row is as tall as its tallest cell, which left 250-300 px of dead card under the shorter column; `columns: 2` with `break-inside: avoid` packs them to a 0 px gap. The `--flat` variant stays a grid — it holds bare fields, of even height, that should read across.

Also killed: the `<code>` chip when the key is just the label lowercased ("Spacing `spacing`", "Font `font`"), 22 lines of dead `.pal-btable*` CSS, and a specificity bug where `.pal-setting__control > .pal-field__input` (0,2,0) beat `.pal-bar__position` (0,1,0) and stretched every stacked text input the full width of the pane.

## Decided: what a bar popover owes its item (2026-09-17)

Nineteen bar items across sixteen extensions, each audited against the `menu`-form table in `docs/design/bar.md:108-122`. That table is the standard, and all three forms are legitimate: nodes for "up to a screenful of commands and toggles", `{ palette }` for "search, a detail pane, filters, more than a screenful", `{ view }` for "a dashboard or card". So the question per item is **fit**, not "is it a view yet".

Left alone on that reading: `audio/volume`, `github/issues`, `github/prs` and `bluetooth/battery` are palette-backed, and the doc names "a PR queue" as the palette's own example. `hue/home` is a nodes menu of room toggles, which is exactly "toggles".

Converted, because both were inbox-like lists that a flat menu could not serve: **gmail/unread** buried Open and the snippet behind a per-message submenu — two hops to read one line — and had no "mark all read"; **tela/inbox** was a flat list with no cursor and no keys. Both are now `view.ts` modules on the shape whatsapp and slack already used (`rowNode`/`hints`/`actions`/`render`, widths off `POPOVER_W`, the cursor a module-level `barFocus` in `index.ts`, hidden `focus:<id>`/`down`/`up` actions). Gmail's conversion hit a real constraint on the way: **a view's `image` src must be `icon://` or `data:`**, while a *menu node's* icon may load a URL — so gravatars that worked as nodes broke as a view, and `barState` had to become async and go through `imageData`, as whatsapp's does.

`network/status`, `power/battery` and `weather/weather` were already views but carried no `keys` table, so the popover could not say what its keys did; they have one now, and network's description still claimed it opened a palette, which it has not done for some time.

Two real overflow defects, both in `extensions/calendar/view.ts`: the all-day line is a single row of badges and **cannot wrap**, yet took every all-day event at full title — capped at three badges of 18 chars plus a `+N`; and the stale line carried a magic `width: 200` beside a wide badge in a 396 px popover, now computed off `POPOVER_W` with the message truncated.

Looked at and left, with the reason. **Media and network do not truncate wrongly** — both already pass an explicit `width` computed off `POPOVER_W` (`media/view.ts:45`, `network/view.ts:26`) and the renderer cuts at it; the audit's "truncation risk" there was unfounded. **Slack, whatsapp and weather do not need `view.update`**: `app/src/BarPage.tsx:57-61` restarts the same item's level **in place** with the new tree when it renders again while its popover shows, so their scheduled refresh already reaches an open popover. Calendar's extra 30 s tick is not the baseline everyone is missing — it exists because calendar shows *relative* time ("in 12 min") finer than its own 300 s refresh.

One thing that is not a repo bug: `pal bar json hue/home` reports a nodes menu while `extensions/hue/index.ts:588` says `menu: { view: popoverView() }`. There is an **older hue installed** at `~/Library/Application Support/pal/extensions/hue`, shadowing the bundled one. Worth remembering when a live item disagrees with its source.

Tests after: host `bun test` 1227 pass, 1 skip (77 files); vitest 37 files, 240 pass; host `tsc` at its 23-line baseline.

## Decided: a palette in a popover is a punt (2026-09-17)

The round above left four items palette-backed on the design doc's authority, which names "a PR queue" as the palette's own example. **Overruled**: a `{ palette }` popover is the item declining to answer its own question. Hovering the PR strip asks *what are my PRs doing* — a palette answers *here is a searchable list of everything*, which is a different, slower answer that also throws away the state the strip just finished computing. Five items converted to `{ view }`; the palette stays one keystroke away from each (`p`, `{ push: { extension, palette } }`), so nothing is lost, it is just no longer the front door.

What each now shows. **audio/volume** and **audio/microphone** are one `view.ts` parameterised by direction: the device in use is an elevated card (glyph, name, `builtin · 56%`, a `slider` and a mute `switch`), the other devices of that direction are rows that Enter switches to, and the opposite direction is one line. The cursor deliberately opens on the **first non-default device** — the default is the card, so Enter must mean "use this instead" rather than "use what you are already using". Two design bugs fell out: the cursor could land on a non-row, and the slider was dead for a device the OS reports no level for (Optical Out). **bluetooth/battery** puts the low devices first with coloured `progress` and AirPods-style per-part detail, then the other connected readings, then connected devices with no reading; Enter disconnects **with a confirm**, and a direct click on the bar still opens Bluetooth Settings. **github/prs** and **github/issues** show the queue bucketed with a status badge per PR (conflicting / checks failing / changes requested / review required / approved / draft); the new `prBuckets(lists)` is the **single source for both the strip's segments and the popover**, so the two can no longer disagree — which they could before, each bucketing separately. **weather/weather** was already a view but a thin one (one stats row, one action); it now has a current card, an 8-column hourly strip, a 4-day forecast and sunrise/sunset, which meant asking the API for `hourly=` and `daily=` it was not fetching.

The trap this round, hit by two of three agents independently and once pre-existing: `Space` is a **4px grid**, so the outer column's `padding: 3` costs **12 px a side**, and it must come off `POPOVER_W` before any columns are shared out. Forgetting it is invisible in review — the arithmetic *looks* like arithmetic — and overflows by 24 px, which for weather's 8-column strip was a whole column past the edge. Every view now derives from an explicit `INNER_W = POPOVER_W - 2 * OUTER_PAD - ROW_PAD`, and the number is checked numerically rather than trusted. `github/view.ts`'s `TITLE_W` had carried the same flaw since the notifications popover shipped; fixed in passing.

Also worth keeping, on delegating this kind of work: three agents produced four defects that review caught and tests did not — fake width derivations (`STEP = POPOVER_W / 99`) that overflowed, **153 lines of reformatting churn** in a compact `pal.json` that should have been a 2-line edit, and hand-rolled `hm()`/`weekday()` built on `Intl.DateTimeFormat` when the SDK already exports `clock` and `dayName`. None of it fails a test. The checklist that finds it: `diff --stat` first and challenge any file whose line count is out of proportion to the change, verify every width numerically, and grep the SDK for anything that looks hand-rolled.

Tests after: host `bun test` 1235 pass, 1 skip (77 files); vitest 37 files, 240 pass; host `tsc` **11 lines, down from 23** — the weather test rewrite took the stale weather errors with it; what remains is pre-existing in network (2) and bluetooth (1).

## Switcher and sidebar (2026-09-21)

The Contexts question, answered on product terms (pal is for other people, not
one desktop): the hold-modifier switcher and the edge sidebar both shipped,
built on what the tree had rather than as new surfaces. Spec:
`docs/design/switcher.md`. Branch `switcher`, three agents in worktrees
(`wt-mru`, `wt-hold`, `wt-side`) merged here, then the live pass with a
scratch instance on hornet (`target/scratch-sw`, the recipe under How to run it).

- **MRU order** (`pal_core::windows`): a bounded focus history
  (`note_focus`, 64 ids) sorts the macOS list most recently used first, the
  rest in CG's front to back; stamped on every app activation (the bar
  triggers' observer), on the panel's and the sidebar's show
  (`windows::stamp_focused`, a thread reading `focused()`), and by `focus`
  itself. The bridge's `list` waits up to 150 ms for a stamp in flight,
  else the relist the show triggers raced the stamp and the switcher's
  first frame had the window just left in CG order. `Window.hidden`
  (`isHidden` per pid) tells a hidden app from another space; `Show app`
  (`cmd+shift+h`) is `activate`, which already unhid.
- **Switcher**: `palettes.<id>.hold` (the windows manifest suggests
  `alt+tab`; the parser takes `alt` and `option`, not `opt`, so the spec's
  `opt+tab` is spelled `alt+tab`), `Target::Hold` registers the chord and
  its `shift+` variant, `switcher.rs` begins on the first press (`show_hold`,
  `hold: true` in `pal://shown`), steps on every further press (the OS
  hands the registered chord to `hotkey::pressed`, never to the webview),
  and commits when a 40 ms poll of `NSEvent.modifierFlags()` sees the
  chord's modifiers up; a commit waits on a relist in flight (`IN_FLIGHT`,
  up to 300 ms). No tap, no new permission. `pal switch
  [next|prev|commit|cancel]` drives the same machine from a compositor
  bind (Hyprland `bind`/`bindr` in docs/cli.md). **The page's cursor under
  a hold is an index, never a row followed by id**: the rows on screen at
  the begin are the last show's, and following their row 2 by id across the
  fresh relist committed the wrong window twice on hornet before the fix
  (`f9edd3c`); only a typed filter follows the row.
- **Sidebar**: `[sidebar]` (`palette`, `edge`, `display`, `width`, `peek`,
  `hotkey`), off until a palette is named (an edge that peeks when the
  pointer rests there is asked for, never shipped on). A second window of
  the popover's kind (`sidebar`, `index.html?bar&sidebar`) driven by
  `sidebar.rs` over the popover's pure `Machine`; docked placement; the
  peek is a 2 px transparent NSPanel of pal's own along the edge with a
  tracking area (`panel::strip`), one per display under `display =
  "cursor"`, no Input Monitoring needed; engage by click (the page's
  `bar_engage` on mousedown), the hotkey, or a key during a peek (the
  popover's monitor, Input Monitoring or nothing). Ordinals drawn always;
  `cmd+N` runs row N. Two bugs the live pass found that no test could: the
  `sidebar` window label was missing from `capabilities/default.json`, so
  the page's `listen()` was denied while app commands still passed (the
  event landed in the popover's page instead, whose listener is on the
  `Any` target: Tauri delivers every `emit_to` to `Any` listeners in every
  webview); the bar page's `pal://bar` listener is now named to its own
  window. And `Vec::drain(n..)` on the first `strip_place` panicked in an
  observer callback (length 0, `drain(1..)`).
- **Measured on hornet** (scratch instance, synthetic keys through System
  Events): Slack then kitty, `alt+tab` released: Slack, 89 ms from release
  to focus; Chrome, kitty, Slack then `alt+tab`, `tab` released: Chrome.
  Sidebar: hotkey engages, `cmd+8` focused row 8, the strip's
  `mouseEntered` fired at x = 1799 on the 1800 pt display and the peek
  showed after the hover delay. `screencapture` resigns an engaged sidebar
  (it takes key), which is not a bug. skhd on hornet holds
  `ctrl+alt+shift+s` (Spotify), which is why the scratch used `+9`.
- Not done: Settings › Palettes does not edit `hold` yet
  (`SettingsPalettes.tsx`); the sidebar's `show_when`/`hide_when`; an
  `AXObserver` for focus changes inside one app; replacing Cmd+Tab itself
  (an event tap, Input Monitoring, gated like expansion); a Linux sidebar.

## Switcher round 2: the tap, cmd+tab, Settings (2026-09-22)

His three corrections on the first cut, all Cmd+Tab semantics, plus "pal is
drivable from the UI": nothing may exist only as a config line.

- **A tap switches at once and paints nothing.** `switcher::press` when idle
  stamps the front window, starts the release poll and arms a 150 ms
  `SHOW_AFTER`; the panel shows only if the chord is still held then.
  Presses before the show accumulate as `steps` (`Shown.steps` places the
  cursor at row `2 + steps`); a release before it is the tap: the shell
  lists (`await_stamp` first) and raises row `2 + steps` itself through
  `windows::raise` (the focus effect's raise, moved out of effects.rs so
  the two share it). A commit that reaches the page before its rows are in
  is parked and runs once they land. The tap applies to `windows/windows`;
  another held palette shows at once.
- **No pop back into the windows palette**: `pop::forget()` from a shown
  commit and from `on_hidden`, so the next show is the root whatever
  `pop_to_root` says.
- **cmd+tab.** Measured: a Carbon registration of cmd+tab is accepted and
  the Dock still takes the press. So `hotkey::dock_owned` chords (Tab with
  exactly cmd or cmd+shift) go through a session `CGEventTap` (active,
  head-insert, keyDown swallowed for the chord, autorepeats dropped,
  re-enabled on `TapDisabledByTimeout`), which needs Input Monitoring:
  asked once (`request_once`, as expansion does), an Overview row with
  Grant until then (`Outcome.hold_blocked`), re-applied when the grant
  lands (`permissions::watch`). Verified on hornet: cmd+tab tap raised the
  previous window, cmd+tab tab raised row 3, the App Switcher never opened.
- **Settings**: General has a Window switcher card (the windows palette's
  chord; Clear writes `hold = ""`, Reset the manifest's suggestion; Grant
  for Input Monitoring only when the chord is cmd+tab and the grant is
  missing) and the whole Sidebar card (switch, palette, edge, display from
  the OS names, width, peek, hotkey; Bar has a pointer row); Palettes has
  the per-palette Switcher chord; Welcome has two tips; the Overview
  lists both as facts.
- **Not the switcher's, seen twice on the scratch instance**: a fresh
  profile's first show lists the lazy palettes, and one of them steals key
  (1Password's authorisation dialog for `onepassword/items`, a
  Bluetooth/Location prompt from `permissions.request`), so the panel
  resigns and hides mid-hold. A daily profile answered those long ago; a
  first-run pal should defer such prompts until the panel is down.

## Decided: odak (2026-09-22)

- `extensions/odak`: four palettes (`odak` Todos, live and lazy with a 60 s ttl, `multi`; `add` input with the root fallback row; `search` input; `done` Completed, live) and the bar item `odak/today`, over one client (`api.ts`: the bearer, one REST call, an in-memory cache with `patch`/`peek`), one data layer (`data.ts`), the add grammar and the day words (`when.ts`, pure, 13 tests), the popover (`view.ts`, pure). No instances: odak is one file per server and the API has no workspaces.
- **The model, read against `~/proj/odak` (`internal/parser`, `internal/store`).** An id is sha256 of the line after its checkbox, first four bytes, tags rendered first; so a toggle keeps it (only the checkbox flips) and every other write gives a new one, and the server's answer to `PATCH`/`move` still carries the old one (the store returns the item before the next parse). The extension computes the new id the way odak does (`idOf`), patches the cache with it, uses it for the move that follows an edit (the web UI's own edit-then-move goes to the old id and would 404: seen while building the mock) and refetches behind every write so the server's view wins. `PATCH` keeps an empty `text`/`section`/`deadline`/`trigger` and always writes `urgent`: Edit refuses an emptied due day with the reason, and the current flag rides on every patch. A `POST` to a section the file lacks lands under the last heading, so a section is always resolved against `GET /sections` (`targetSection`: the setting's, else Inbox, else the first).
- **The grammar** is the web UI's add bar (`#tag`, a bare `!`, `/section` by prefix, `d:`), plus `w:` and a day in words at the end read by `parseWhen` (today, tomorrow, weekdays, `next x`, `in N days/weeks/months`, day-month forms, ISO, dots and slashes, `by`/`on`/`due` lead-ins). `next fri` is the coming Friday, as the calendar's Quick Add reads it. A time after the day stays in the text: odak keeps a day only. Overlaps the calendar's `parseDay` (`extensions/calendar/schedule.ts`); a candidate for the SDK, left in the lane so the calendar is untouched.
- **Sections.** The palette follows the file (Focus, Today, Next, ... in heading order) with Overdue and Due today hoisted on top and Not yet (a future `[w:]`) at the end; no filter dropdown, the section and the tags are row keywords so typing `work` or `overdue` narrows. The bar counts due-today plus the sections named by `today_sections` (Focus, Today), since his Today is a section rather than a date. Presentation is the manifest's rules over `odak/overdue`, `odak/today`, `odak/open` (hidden at zero, red while overdue), as the states design asks; `show = "always"` keeps the `empty` shape.
- **Feel.** A write patches the cache before the relist (`keep`), so a complete is instant, with an Undo row at the top for a minute and the completion time kept in `storage` for Completed; Snooze and Move are forms (a pushed level would have nowhere to pop back to), Snooze to tomorrow and Mark urgent are one key. Add Todo's Enter hides with the HUD line, `cmd+enter` keeps the line for a variation (a push with an empty query would stack a level). The prefill rows (selection, clipboard) are rows of the empty listing rather than a pre-typed query, since an input palette cannot set its own text.
- **Verified**: `host/test/extensions/odak.test.ts` (29, against `odak-mock.ts`: the routes with odak's quirks mirrored, a refused key, a 502, an unreachable port) and `odak-when.test.ts` (13); `bun run extensions/odak/fixture.ts` writes the gallery fixtures (no PNGs rendered: the shots need a Vite server and Chrome). Live against `http://archer:8761` with the key from `~/Sync/.secrets/odak.env` through the harness: the listing, the bar item, then one todo "pal test <timestamp>" added, completed and deleted, nothing else touched.
- **Not done**: screenshots; reordering; the WebSocket feed (the cache refetches on show and after writes); a "clear completed" (the file is his history).
